# Loom PWA Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-21-loom-pwa-design.md`

**Goal:** An installable web app, served by the daemon through a Cloudflare tunnel, that shows loom's fleet on an iPhone, replies through the loom mod, runs the lifecycle actions, pushes a notification when a session starts waiting or finishes, and hands off to the session's Remote Control.

**Architecture:** A new package, `internal/web`, runs inside `loom serve` when `web.json` enables it. It is a `core.Core` client over `net.Pipe` to the daemon's own `rpc.Server` (hello role `web`), plus `core.WebHost` for device records, which the model owns in `web.json`. It serves an embedded no-build PWA, a JSON API and a server-sent event stream on a loopback port, and sends Web Push itself. `core`, `core/rpc`, `session` and `internal/cli` gain the small pieces it needs.

**Tech Stack:** Go 1.25 standard library (`net/http`, `embed`, `crypto/ecdh`, `crypto/ecdsa`, `crypto/hkdf`, `crypto/aes`, `crypto/cipher`), cobra, testify; ES modules (`.mjs`) with one vendored Preact + htm file; Web Push (RFC 8030, RFC 8291, RFC 8292).

---

## Execution

- **Where.** This worktree, on branch `aidanb/loom-pwa`, which loom manages: never switch branches. Every path is relative to the repo root. Commit by pathspec, `git add -- <files>` then `git commit -m "<message>" -- <files>`, with Conventional Commit types scoped by package (`core`, `rpc`, `session`, `cli`, `web`, `daemon`).
- **Before the run.** The spike ran on 2026-10-22 and passed; its outcome is in the spec ("Spike, as a gate", "Outcome, 2026-10-22"). The design it changed was written into Stories 8, 9, 11 and 12 by hand: the push `Topic`'s form (11, 12), `DELETE /api/v1/device/push` (8) and the launch's push check (9, 12), the waiting subscribe button (12), and the stream's refresh when the page becomes visible (9).
- **Order.** Stories 1 to 13, one at a time: the profile makes every Go build exclusive, and each story builds on the ones before it.
- **Gates.** The profile's story gates run after each story: gofmt, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`. Stories 6 and 8 change the daemon and name the e2e suite in their Verify lines; it needs tmux.
- **Generated files.** Story 2 describes the hello's new field in the prose `TestProtocolReference` writes (`core/rpc/wire_test.go`), then regenerates `docs/specs/protocol.md` (`CGO_ENABLED=0 go test ./core/rpc -run TestProtocolReference -update`). Story 3 regenerates `core/rpc/methods_gen.go` (`go generate ./core/rpc`) and `protocol.md` again. Never edit either by hand.
- **CLAUDE.md budgets.** `core/CLAUDE.md` has 93 bytes free (19907/20000), so Stories 3 and 4 add no rule to it: Story 13 writes the web layer's rules in `internal/web/CLAUDE.md` and a guide. Story 6 creates `internal/web/CLAUDE.md` with its own rules, since the coverage lint fails a Go package without one.
- **Tests.** No test touches the real `~/.loom`, `~/.claude` or the user's tmux server: `internal/web` gets a `TestMain` that calls `testenv.IsolateLoomDirs`, and real-tmux tests run on a private server. Fixtures go through the production path: a booted model, the real `rpc.Server`, a client over `net.Pipe`.
- **Running loom.** Never run loom itself (`./loom`, `go run .`) or `loom serve stop` in a loom pane. Manual checks use the `loom-dev` skill (`go run ./tools/loomdev`).
- **JavaScript.** The app's modules are `.mjs`, so the Verify lines can check their syntax with `node --check` (Node 22 is on PATH here; CI has no Node, so no Go test depends on it).

## Names contract

Every name one story defines and another uses. A story may add names of its own; a name another story needs and this section lacks goes back to the manager.

**session (Story 1)**

- `session.RosterEntry` gains `PID int` and `RemoteSession string`. `RemoteSession` is the `bridgeSessionId` of `<config dir>/sessions/<PID>.json`, empty unless that file's `sessionId` equals the entry's (spec, "Changes outside `internal/web`").
- `hooks.Event` gains `ToolSummary string`, set for `PermissionRequest` only, read from the payload's `tool_input` and kept in the compact form under the key `tool_summary`.
- `(*session.Instance).ClaudeWaitDetail() string`: the current observation's tool summary, empty unless it is Prompting.
- `(*session.Instance).WaitDetail() string` and `(*session.Instance).SetWaitDetail(detail string)`, beside `WaitReason`/`SetWaitReason`; `adoptClaudeStatus` sets and clears both together.
- `core.InstanceView` gains `RemoteSession string` and `WaitDetail string`.

**core/rpc (Story 2)**

- `rpc.RoleWeb = "web"`; `rpc.Hello` gains `Role string` (`json:"role,omitempty"`).
- `func rpc.DialAs(nc io.ReadWriteCloser, role string) (*rpc.Client, error)`; `rpc.Dial(nc)` is `DialAs(nc, "")`.
- The server counts a connection whose hello's role is `RoleWeb` as no client for the notice backlog: a notice is kept while no other connection exists, and a joining web connection never takes the backlog. A role other than exactly `web` counts as an ordinary client.
- A notice tied to the web client's own request goes to the web connection and is also handled as if no web connection existed: it reaches the other connections, or waits in the backlog when there is none. The phone may never see it, and a kill's or resume's stash notice can be the only record of its SHA.

**core: web state (Story 3)**, in `core/web.go`:

```go
type WebView struct {
	Listen  string      // "127.0.0.1:7420"; "" when off
	Origin  string      // "https://loom.example.com"
	Serving bool        // the listener is up
	Addr    string      // what it listens on, while Serving
	Error   string      // why it is not serving, or why web.json was left alone
	Devices []WebDevice // sorted by Name; no token hashes
	Rev     uint64      // bumped by every ConfigureWeb; the layer rebinds when it changes
}

type WebDevice struct {
	Name     string
	Paired   time.Time
	LastSeen time.Time
	Notify   NotifySettings
	Push     PushState
}

type NotifySettings struct {
	Waiting     bool
	Finished    bool
	PausedUntil time.Time // zero: not paused
}

type Pairing struct {
	Code    string    // PairingCodeLen characters of PairingAlphabet, no dash
	Expires time.Time
}

type PushSubscription struct {
	Endpoint string
	P256DH   string // unpadded base64url of a 65-byte uncompressed P-256 point
	Auth     string // unpadded base64url of 16 bytes
}

type PushTarget struct {
	Device       string
	Notify       NotifySettings
	Subscription PushSubscription
}

type PushState string

const (
	PushNone    PushState = ""
	PushOK      PushState = "ok"
	PushExpired PushState = "expired"
)

func PushFailing(status int) PushState // "failing: <status>"

type VAPIDKey struct {
	Private []byte // the 32-byte P-256 scalar
	Public  []byte // the 65-byte uncompressed point
}

const (
	PairingAlphabet   = "23456789ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTUVWXYZ"
	PairingCodeLen    = 8
	PairingTTL        = 10 * time.Minute
	PairingMaxPending = 4
	PairingMaxMisses  = 5
	MaxWebDevices     = 16
	WebFile           = "web.json" // in the global dir
)

var (
	ErrWebOff         = errors.New("web: not enabled; run loom web enable first")
	ErrPairingCode    = errors.New("pairing: the code is wrong, used or expired")
	ErrPairingLocked  = errors.New("pairing: too many wrong codes; run loom web pair again")
	ErrDeviceExists   = errors.New("pairing: a device has that name already")
	ErrTooManyDevices = errors.New("pairing: 16 devices are paired; revoke one first")
	ErrNoDevice       = errors.New("no device has that name")
)

func ValidateWebListen(listen string) error
func ValidateWebOrigin(origin string) error
func ValidateDeviceName(name string) (string, error) // the trimmed name
func NormalizePairingCode(code string) (string, bool) // upper case, dashes and spaces dropped; false unless PairingCodeLen characters of PairingAlphabet
```

- Errors that name a device wrap the sentinel: `fmt.Errorf("%w: %q", ErrDeviceExists, name)`.
- `core.ModelView` gains `Web WebView`, deep-copied by `Clone`.
- `core.Core` gains, in `core/iface.go`:
  - `Web() WebView // rpc:local`
  - `ConfigureWeb(listen, origin string) error`: validates both; an empty `listen` turns the web off and keeps `origin`; the first enable makes the VAPID key.
  - `PairDevice(name string) (Pairing, error)`: `ErrWebOff`, a `ValidateDeviceName` error, `ErrDeviceExists` or `ErrTooManyDevices`.
  - `RevokeDevice(name string) error`: `ErrNoDevice` for an unknown name.
- `web.json` (version 1, mode 0600, through `config.AtomicWriteFile`):

```json
{"version": 1, "listen": "127.0.0.1:7420", "origin": "https://loom.example.com",
 "vapid": {"private": "<base64url>", "public": "<base64url>"},
 "devices": [{"name": "iPhone", "token_sha256": "<hex>", "paired": "<RFC 3339>", "last_seen": "<RFC 3339>",
              "notify": {"waiting": true, "finished": true, "paused_until": "<RFC 3339 or empty>"},
              "push": {"endpoint": "https://web.push.apple.com/…", "p256dh": "<base64url>", "auth": "<base64url>"},
              "push_state": "ok"}]}
```

- The model's web state: Story 3 defines it in `core/web_store.go`, holds it as the field `web webState` on `Model` (`core/model.go`; the zero value is off, with no devices) and loads it in `Boot`; Story 4's `WebHost` reads and changes it in `core/web_host.go`, and a change reaches clients through `ModelView.Web` on the next publish:

```go
type webState struct {
	path           string           // <global dir>/web.json once loaded; "" on a model that never booted, where saveWeb looks the dir up itself
	listen, origin string
	vapid          VAPIDKey         // zero until the first enable
	devices        []*webDevice     // sorted by name
	pending        []pendingPairing // oldest first, at most PairingMaxPending; memory only
	misses         int              // wrong codes since the last PairDevice, which resets it
	loadErr        string           // why web.json was left alone (unreadable, or a newer version); keeps the web off and saveWeb refusing
	rev            uint64           // ConfigureWeb's count, published as WebView.Rev
	serving        bool             // set by WebServing
	addr           string           // set by WebServing
	servingErr     string           // set by WebServing; WebView.Error is loadErr when set, else this
	now            func() time.Time // nil means time.Now; tests set it
}

type webDevice struct {
	name             string
	tokenHash        [32]byte
	paired, lastSeen time.Time
	notify           NotifySettings
	sub              PushSubscription // zero when none
	push             PushState
}

type pendingPairing struct {
	code    string // in NormalizePairingCode's form
	name    string
	expires time.Time
}

func (w *webState) clock() time.Time            // w.now(), or time.Now when nil
func (w *webState) find(name string) *webDevice // nil when no device has the name
func (w *webState) prune()                      // drops expired pending codes; Story 4 may call it too
func (m *Model) saveWeb() error                 // web.json through config.AtomicWriteFile at path, or under config.GetGlobalConfigDir() when path is empty (a NewForTest model); refuses while loadErr is set
```

- `MaxWebDevices` counts paired devices, not pending codes; `PairDevice` checks it, and `RedeemPairing` checks it again. A new device starts with `Waiting` and `Finished` on.

- `PairDevice` resets `misses` and replaces the name's pending code. Story 4's `RedeemPairing` counts each wrong code in `misses`, voids every pending code at the fifth, and answers `ErrPairingLocked` to every code until the next `PairDevice`.
- Every `ConfigureWeb` that succeeds bumps `rev` and clears `serving`, `addr` and `servingErr`, so the layer acts on it afresh (binding, rebinding or, for an empty `listen`, closing) and reports through `WebServing`, and `loom web enable` never reads a stale `Error`. While `loadErr` is set, `ConfigureWeb` and `RevokeDevice` refuse, and `PairDevice` answers `ErrWebOff`. A stored `listen`, `origin`, device name, token hash or VAPID key that fails validation counts as a file that fails to load, and `Boot` raises a notice for such a file (stricter than the spec, which names only an unreadable or newer file).

**core: the web layer's host (Story 4)**, in `core/web_host.go`, implemented by `*core.Loop` in `core/loop_web.go` (`var _ WebHost = (*Loop)(nil)`):

```go
type WebHost interface {
	RedeemPairing(code string) (name, token string, err error) // token: unpadded base64url of 32 random bytes
	DeviceByToken(hash [32]byte) (name string, ok bool)       // hash: SHA-256 of the 32 bytes the cookie's base64url token decodes to
	TouchDevice(name string)                                   // last seen, written at most once a minute
	SetNotify(name string, c NotifyChange) (WebDevice, error)                 // applies the fields c sets; the device as stored
	SetPushSubscription(name string, sub PushSubscription) (WebDevice, error) // validates; sets PushOK
	PushTargets() []PushTarget // devices with a subscription that isn't expired
	PushResult(device string, state PushState) // PushExpired also drops the subscription
	VAPIDKey() (VAPIDKey, error) // ErrWebOff before the first enable
	WebServing(addr string, err error) // sets WebView.Serving, Addr and Error; a non-nil err also raises a Notice, once per distinct error, which waits for the next TUI
}

// NotifyChange changes a device's notification settings; a nil field is left as it is.
type NotifyChange struct {
	Waiting     *bool
	Finished    *bool
	PausedUntil *time.Time // a zero time ends a pause
}

func ValidatePushSubscription(sub PushSubscription) error // spec, "Limits"
```

- `core.OriginWeb Origin = "web"`: `Send` treats it as `OriginCLI` (refused when empty, for a program other than Claude, and without a live mod; never pasted; refused at the mods' stop) except that it has no "From" line, is submitted as the user's own words, as `OriginScript` is, and its refusals name no CLI command, since the phone shows them as they are.
- Story 6's `authed` base64url-decodes the `__Host-loom` cookie, refuses one that doesn't decode to 32 bytes, and passes the SHA-256 of those bytes to `DeviceByToken`.

**loom web (Story 5)**

- `cli.WebCmd` (`loom web`), added to the root in `main.go`. Subcommands: `enable --listen <addr> --origin <url>` (both required), `disable`, `pair [--name <device>]` (default `iPhone`), `devices [--json]`, `revoke <name>` or `revoke --all`, and `status`.
- `enable` waits up to 5 s for `Web().Serving` or `Web().Error` and says which; `pair` prints the code as `XXXX-XXXX` with its expiry and the origin.
- `enable` with the stored values while `Web().Serving` is true changes nothing and says it serves. Any other `enable` calls `ConfigureWeb` once, which bumps `Rev`, so the layer binds afresh: running `enable` again retries a failed bind (Story 13's USAGE.md says so).

**internal/web (Stories 6 to 8, 12)**, package `web`:

```go
type Options struct {
	Server   *rpc.Server  // the daemon's server, dialled over net.Pipe with rpc.DialAs(…, rpc.RoleWeb)
	Host     core.WebHost // the loop
	Hostname string       // shown in the app's header
	Build    string       // the daemon's build ID: in the snapshot and in sw.js
}

func Start(o Options) (*Layer, error)
func (l *Layer) Close() // sends bye on every stream, closes the listener and the client
```

- `daemon.Serve` calls `web.Start` after `modserver.Listen`, and `Layer.Close` first when `Options.Stop` closes, before `Server.Bye`.
- Seams between the stories of one package (Story 6 defines them):
  - `type sink interface{ apply(evs []core.Event) }` and `(*Layer).addSink(s sink)`, called in `Start` before the drain begins. The drain hands every `Sync`'s events to each sink in turn, on its own goroutine; a sink never blocks and never does network I/O.
  - `func (l *Layer) mux() *http.ServeMux` calls one registrar per story: `l.routePair(m)` (6), `l.routeStream(m)` (7), `l.routeAPI(m)` (8), `l.routeStatic(m)` (9), `l.routePush(m)` (12).
  - `func (l *Layer) guard(next http.Handler) http.Handler`: the Host and Origin checks, the security headers, the 1 MiB body cap. It passes the `ResponseWriter` on unwrapped, or wraps it with an `Unwrap() http.ResponseWriter`, so the stream can flush through `http.ResponseController`.
  - `func (l *Layer) fail(err error)`: stops the layer after a panic in one of its goroutines. It returns at once and stops the layer on a goroutine of its own, so a part may call it from the goroutine that panicked. It ends every stream, closes the listener and the client, and reports `err` through `WebServing("", err)`, whose notice waits for the next TUI. The drain (6), the hub's flusher (7) and the push sender (12) each recover a panic into it.
  - `func (l *Layer) recoverPanic(where string)`: each goroutine the layer starts defers it first; it recovers a panic, logs the stack under `subsystem=web`, and calls `l.fail` with an error naming `where`.
  - Story 6's tests define the package's test harness: `webStack`, `newWebStack`, `newWebStackIn` (a booted model, the real `rpc.Server` and the layer on `httptest`), `httpDo`, `httpProbe`, `bodyText`, `deviceToken`, `scriptedHost`, `funcSink`, `freeLoopbackAddr`, `requireNoListener`, `portOf` and `guardOrigin`. Stories 7, 8 and 12 test the layer through `webStack`, as the spec's "Testing" asks, keep fakes and literals for pure functions, and declare none of these names. They use `webStack`'s fields `loop`, `srv`, `layer` and `tui` and its methods `start`, `enable`, `pair`, `connect` and `waitNotice` as Story 6 defines them, and Story 6 renames none of them.
  - `type deviceHandler func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, device string)` and `func (l *Layer) authed(h deviceHandler) http.Handler`: 401 unless the cookie names a device; touches it.
  - `writeJSON(w http.ResponseWriter, status int, v any)`, `writeError(w http.ResponseWriter, status int, msg string)` (body `{"error": msg}`), `readJSON(r *http.Request, v any) error` (unknown fields refused).
  - `cookieName = "__Host-loom"`, `setDeviceCookie(w http.ResponseWriter, token string)`, `clearDeviceCookie(w http.ResponseWriter)`.
  - `Layer` fields: `client *rpc.Client`, `host core.WebHost`, `hostname`, `build string`, `now func() time.Time` (the clock tests replace).
  - `Start` builds the `Layer`, calls `l.startParts()`, then starts the drain and the listener; `Close` calls `l.stopParts()` first, then closes the listener and the client (the pinned texts are under "Files more than one story writes").
  - The layer acts on `WebView.Rev`: whenever `Rev` differs from the last it acted on, it closes its listener, binds `Listen` when it isn't empty, and reports through `WebServing`. Two `ConfigureWeb`s that one `Sync` coalesces still differ, and `WebServing` doesn't bump `Rev`, so a failed bind is retried only by the next `ConfigureWeb`. The layer starts having acted on no revision, so its first view binds a stored `Listen` although a booted model's `Rev` is 0.
- Story 7 defines: `type sessionJSON`, `type workspaceJSON`, `type deviceJSON`, `type snapshotJSON`, `type noticeJSON struct { Text string; Error bool; At time.Time }` (JSON keys `text`, `error`, `at`), `func groupOf(v core.InstanceView) (group, label string)`, `func deviceOf(d core.WebDevice) deviceJSON`, `func noticeOf(n core.Notice, at time.Time) noticeJSON`, and `l.hub` with `publish(event string, data any)`, `sendTo(device, event string, data any)` and `closeDevice(device string)`.
- Notices: Story 7's sink publishes to every stream the notices not tied to a web request; Story 8's `reqs` takes those tied to one of the web client's requests.
- Story 8's `routeAPI` serves every `/api/v1` route except `/pair` (6), `/stream` (7) and `/push-key` (12).
- Story 8 defines `l.reqs` with `next() core.ReqID` and `wait(ctx context.Context, req core.ReqID, device string) (core.Reply, []noticeJSON, bool)`: false after 30 s, after which that request's notices go to the device's stream.
- Constants: `maxBody = 1 << 20`, `maxStreamsPerDevice = 4`, `maxStreams = 32`, `replyWait = 30 * time.Second`, `pingEvery = 20 * time.Second`, `publishEvery = 250 * time.Millisecond`, `recentNotices = 20`. Each is declared once, by the story that first uses it: `maxBody` by Story 6; `maxStreamsPerDevice`, `maxStreams`, `pingEvery`, `publishEvery` and `recentNotices` by Story 7 in `stream.go`; `replyWait` by Story 8 in `reqs.go`.
- The hub's recent ring holds every notice the hub sends to a stream, by `publish` or `sendTo`, so a phone that missed a late notice finds it in its next snapshot. Notices returned in a response stay out of it.

**The JSON the app reads (Stories 7 and 8)**

- Stream events: `snapshot`, `sessions`, `workspaces`, `device`, `notice`, `ping`, `bye`.
- `notices` in the snapshot is oldest first, at most 20. The app appends each `notice` event's notice at the end and keeps the last 20, and the settings screen shows them newest first.
- `snapshot`: `{"build", "host", "workspaces": [workspace], "sessions": {"<workspace id>": [session]}, "device": device, "badge": <the number of sessions in needs_you>, "notices": [notice]}`; `sessions`: `{"workspace": <id>, "sessions": [session]}`; `workspaces`: `{"workspaces": [workspace]}`.
- workspace: `{"id", "name", "label"}`. device: `{"name", "notify": {"waiting", "finished", "paused_until"}, "push"}`.
- session: `{"id", "workspace", "title", "branch", "group", "label", "status", "since", "wait": {"reason", "detail"}, "last_message", "diff": {"added", "removed"}, "ahead", "behind", "parity_known", "pr": {"number", "state", "checks"} | null, "account", "agents", "remote_url"}`.
  - `group`: `needs_you`, `your_turn`, `working` or `paused`. `label`: `Needs you`, `Your turn`, `Working`, `Loading`, `Deleting`, `Paused`, `Recoverable` or `Not started`. `status`: `session.Status.String()`.
  - `pr.state`: `draft`, `open`, `merged` or `closed`; `pr.checks`: `none`, `pending`, `passing` or `failing`.
  - `remote_url`: `https://claude.ai/code/<RemoteSession>`, or empty.
  - `agents`: `[{"name", "description", "idle"}]`, one per running subagent.
  - Times (`since`, `at`, a device's `paused_until`) are RFC 3339 strings; `since` is `""` when unknown and `paused_until` is `""` when not paused. IDs are JSON numbers, and the snapshot's `sessions` is keyed by the workspace's ID.
- API bodies: `POST /api/v1/pair` `{"code"}` answers `{"device": "<name>"}`, or the error's text with 400 for a wrong, used or expired code (`ErrPairingCode`), 429 once locked (`ErrPairingLocked`), 409 for a name taken or 16 devices paired since the code was made, 503 while the web is off, and 500 with a fixed text for any other failure; `POST /api/v1/sessions` `{"workspace", "title", "prompt", "account", "model", "permission_mode", "effort"}` answers 201 `{"id", "notices"}`; `send` `{"text"}`; `merge` `{"source": <session id>}` (a JSON number); `PUT /api/v1/device` `{"waiting", "finished", "pause": "1h" | "none"}`, each field optional and a missing one left as it is (`"1h"` pauses until an hour from `l.now()`, `"none"` ends a pause, any other value is 400), answers the device; `POST /api/v1/device/push` takes `PushSubscription.toJSON()` (`{"endpoint", "keys": {"p256dh", "auth"}}`), strips any trailing `=` from the keys (`SetPushSubscription` takes only unpadded base64url), and answers the device; `DELETE /api/v1/device/push` drops the device's subscription through `WebHost.PushResult(device, core.PushExpired)` (which changes nothing for a device with none) and answers 204, for the app's launch check, since Apple answers 201 to a subscription the phone has dropped; `GET /api/v1/launch-defaults?workspace=<id>` answers `{"account", "accounts", "model", "models", "permission_mode", "permission_modes", "effort", "efforts"}`, where `accounts` is the registry's account names, `account` the default one or `""`, and `models`, `permission_modes` and `efforts` are `config.ClaudeModelAliases()`, `config.ClaudePermissionModes` and `config.ClaudeEfforts`, so the app hard-codes none of them; `GET /api/v1/push-key` answers `{"key"}`; `DELETE /api/v1/device` answers 204. Actions answer `{"notices"}`, 202 `{"pending": true}`, or `{"error"}` with 400, 401, 404, 409, 429 or 503.
- Push payload (Story 12): `{"kind": "waiting" | "finished", "session": <id>, "title", "body", "badge": <n>, "url": "/#s/<id>"}`.

**webpush (Story 11)**, package `webpush` in `internal/web/webpush`:

```go
type Subscription struct {
	Endpoint string
	P256DH   []byte // 65 bytes
	Auth     []byte // 16 bytes
}

func ParseSubscription(s core.PushSubscription) (Subscription, error)
func Encrypt(sub Subscription, payload []byte) ([]byte, error) // RFC 8291 aes128gcm, one record, rs 4096
func VAPIDAuth(key core.VAPIDKey, endpoint, subject string, now time.Time) (string, error) // "vapid t=<JWT>, k=<public key>"; ES256, aud = the endpoint's origin, exp = now + 12 h

type Message struct {
	Payload []byte
	TTL     time.Duration
	Urgency string // "high" or "normal"
	Topic   string
}

func NewRequest(ctx context.Context, sub Subscription, key core.VAPIDKey, subject string, msg Message, now time.Time) (*http.Request, error)
```

- `NewRequest` refuses a `Topic` over 32 characters, outside `A-Za-z0-9_-`, or whose length is 1 mod 4 (Apple answers 400 `BadWebPushTopic` to such a length, which unpadded base64url never has: the spike), a `subject` that starts with neither `https://` nor `mailto:` (Story 12 passes the configured origin, so an `http://` development origin sends no push and logs why), an `Urgency` other than empty, `very-low`, `low`, `normal` or `high`, and a negative `TTL`.
- Story 12's topic is the session ID's 8 bytes, big-endian, as unpadded base64url: 11 characters for every ID (`AAAAAAAAACo` for 42).
- `ParseSubscription` requires an `https` endpoint; `VAPIDAuth` and `NewRequest` also take `http`, so Story 12's tests can post to an `httptest` server through a hand-built `Subscription`.

**The app (Stories 9, 10, 12)**, in `internal/web/static/`:

- `lib/preact-htm.mjs`: htm 3.1.1's `preact/standalone.module.js` from `https://unpkg.com/htm@3.1.1/preact/standalone.module.js`, 13194 bytes, SHA-256 `72284e8e9079c87817145df1110f74e8a2aa040b2fc384922e18dfcb46fc1fd7` (a test pins it), with a header comment naming both. Modules import `html`, `render`, `useState`, `useEffect` and the rest from it. Not under a `vendor/` dir: Go's module zips drop files below a nested `vendor/` (`isVendoredPackage` in `golang.org/x/mod/zip`), so a `go install` build would embed no such file.
- `js/api.mjs` (9): `export async function call(method, path, body)` resolves `{status, data}` and never throws on an HTTP status; `path` is the whole path, such as `/api/v1/sessions/12/pause`, and nothing is prepended to it; `export function connect(handlers)` opens the stream, calls `handlers[event](data)` per event, `handlers.open()` / `handlers.error()`, and `handlers.unauthorized()` on a 401 (after which it stops reconnecting), reconnects with backoff from 1 s to 30 s, drops a stream silent for 45 s (two pings and a margin), and when the page becomes visible again drops a live stream and opens a fresh one without calling `handlers.error()` (a page iOS resumes can sit on a stream that delivers nothing: the spike), and returns `{close()}`.
- `js/store.mjs` (9): `loadSnapshot()`, `saveSnapshot(snap)`, `clearSnapshot()` (IndexedDB database `loom`, store `snapshot`; all three return promises and never reject), and `pref(key, value)` (localStorage `loom.<key>`, JSON-encoded, every access in try/catch; a read when `value` is undefined, which returns `null` for a missing or unreadable key; `pref(key, null)` removes the key).
- `js/app.mjs` (9): the routes `#` (fleet), `#s/<id>` (session), `#new`, `#settings`, `#pair`. It renders `screens[route.name]` with the props `{snap, stale, route, go, act, toast}`, where `route` is `{name, id}`: `name` is `fleet`, `session`, `new`, `settings` or `pair`, `id` is the session's ID as a number for `session` and undefined otherwise, and an unknown hash is the fleet; `go(hash)` navigates; `act(method, path, body)` calls the API, shows the notices and errors as toasts, and resolves `{ok, status, data}`, where `ok` is true for every 2xx (200, 201, 202, 204) and `data` is null for a 204; `act` toasts nothing for a 202, and the screen says the outcome will follow; `toast(text, isError, sticky)`, where `sticky` is optional and keeps any toast until tapped. An error toast and a notice's toast stay until tapped, since a kill's or resume's stash notice may be the only record of a SHA; any other toast fades after 4 s. Any 401 clears the snapshot and goes to `#pair`. Once per launch, after the first snapshot, it calls the module-level hook `launched(snap, toast)`, which Story 9 leaves empty and Story 12 fills with the push check.
- `js/screens.mjs`: Story 9 creates `export const screens = {}`; Story 10 fills it as `{fleet, session, new, settings}`.
- `js/sheets.mjs` (10): `export function ConfirmSheet({title, body, confirm, danger, onConfirm, onCancel})`.
- `js/push.mjs`: Story 10 writes a stub, `export function PushButton({ device, act, toast })`, which renders nothing, and the settings screen renders it where notifications are turned on; Story 12 replaces the file whole, keeping that export and its props, with the "Turn on notifications" button, `export async function enablePush(act, env)`, `export async function pushState(env)` and `export async function checkPush(device, toast, post, env)` (`env` defaults to the browser's), which Story 12's `launched` calls with the app's `call` as `post`.
- `app.css` (9): the tokens `--bg`, `--panel`, `--fg`, `--dim`, `--rule`, `--accent`, `--attention`, `--ok`, `--error`, `--workspace`, from loom's theme (`ui/theme.go`) in both themes.
- `sw.js` (9) holds `const BUILD = "__LOOM_BUILD__";` and `const SHELL = ["__LOOM_SHELL__"];`, which `routeStatic` replaces when serving it with the build ID and the JSON list of every embedded file's URL path plus `/`. Story 12 adds its `push` and `notificationclick` handlers.
- `index.html` (9) holds `<meta name="loom-build" content="__LOOM_BUILD__">`, which `routeStatic` fills the same way. `app.mjs` compares it with the snapshot's `build`, and on a difference updates the service worker's registration and reloads.
- `tools/webicons` (9): `go run ./tools/webicons` writes `icons/icon-180.png`, `icon-192.png` and `icon-512.png`.

**Test names Story 13's docs cite** (the lint fails a cited test that doesn't exist): `TestPreactHTM_IsThePinnedFile`, `TestStatic_IndexHasNoInlineScriptOrStyle` and `TestStatic_ModulesSetNoStyleAttribute` (Story 9), and `TestEncryptWith_RFC8291Example` (Story 11).

```go
}
```

  | 8 | `reqs *reqs` | `l.routeAPI(m)` | `l.reqs = newReqs(l)`, then `l.addSink(l.reqs)` | none |

## After the run

The owner, on the iPhone, through the fresco tunnel, with a dev build from the `loom-dev` skill or a released build:

- [ ] `loom web enable --listen 127.0.0.1:7420 --origin https://<host>` reports serving.
- [ ] Safari shows the Add to Home Screen steps; the installed app shows pairing.
- [ ] `loom web pair --name iPhone`, then the code, pairs the phone.
- [ ] The fleet matches the TUI, groups included, and updates live.
- [ ] Turning on notifications works; a waiting and a finished push arrive with the app open and closed, and the badge counts what needs you.
- [ ] Open in Claude reaches the session, and a permission prompt can be answered there.
- [ ] A reply reaches Claude through the mod, and is refused with a reason for a session without one.
- [ ] New session, pause, resume, push and merge work; kill asks first.
- [ ] `loom web revoke iPhone` ends the app's stream and returns it to pairing.

**Design stage:** /flow-draft session f82e87ca-7e3b-4587-9e72-849eec28b5ac, 2026-10-21T15:53:41Z to 2026-10-21T18:09:14Z UTC: $87.87 logged, $104.75 with estimated thinking; 24,333 plan lines, $3.61 per 1,000 lines.

## Stories

- **Story 1** — The session facts the phone shows: the Remote Control link and the permission's tool summary.

  The roster query keeps each interactive entry's `pid` and reads that session's `sessions/<pid>.json` for its bridge ID, failing closed; a permission request keeps a short summary of its tool's input beside the wait reason. Both reach `core.InstanceView`, so every client sees them and the web layer can show them later.

- **Story 2** — The hello's role, and a web connection never holds the notice backlog.

  A connection can name its role in its hello; one named `web` doesn't count as a client when the server decides to keep a notice for the next one, and doesn't take the backlog when it joins. Without this the always-connected web layer would swallow the notices meant for the next TUI.

  - **Interfaces:** exposes `rpc.RoleWeb`, `rpc.Hello.Role` and `rpc.DialAs`; uses none

- **Story 3** — The web settings and devices in the model: `web.json`, the requests and the published view.

  The model loads and writes `web.json`, keeping a file it can't read or one from a newer version untouched; serves `ConfigureWeb`, `PairDevice` and `RevokeDevice`; validates every setting and name; and publishes `WebView` in `ModelView`, answered locally by every replica.

- **Story 4** — The web layer's host on the loop, and `OriginWeb` for `Send`.

  `core.WebHost` gives the web layer what it needs without the wire: redeeming a code, finding a device by token hash, touching it, its notification settings and push subscription, the push targets and results, the VAPID key, and the listener's state. `OriginWeb` lets a reply from the phone go through the mod as the user's own words.

- **Story 6** — The web layer: its client, its listener, the daemon's wiring, the guard, pairing and auth.

  `web.Start` dials the daemon's server over `net.Pipe` as a `web` client, drains it, and listens on `Web().Listen` when set, rebinding when the settings change and reporting through `WebServing`. Every request passes the Host and Origin checks and the security headers; `POST /api/v1/pair` sets the device cookie; `authed` guards every later route. The daemon starts it after the mod socket and closes it before its bye. The package's `CLAUDE.md` and `README.md` start here.

- **Story 7** — The fleet projection and the live stream.

  A sink turns `ViewsChanged` and `WorkspacesChanged` into display-ready sessions, grouped and labelled; `GET /api/v1/stream` sends a snapshot, then each changed workspace's list at most four times a second, notices not tied to a request, a ping every 20 s, and `bye` at the end. It enforces the stream limits, sets the cookie again on connect, and ends a revoked device's streams.

- **Story 8** — The API: sessions and their actions, launch defaults, the device, and the requests' replies and notices.

  Each route makes its `Core` request with the web client's next request ID and answers from the `Reply`: 200, 409 with the model's text, 404, 503, or 202 after 30 s, with the request's notices (late ones go to the device's stream, and a `Started` whose first prompt went unsent becomes one). Create checks and resolves as `loom session new` does. The e2e scenario drives it over HTTP.

  A pure package: parsing a stored subscription, the aes128gcm body (checked against RFC 8291's own example), the ES256 VAPID header (checked by verifying the JWT with its public key), and the push request with its TTL, Urgency and Topic headers.

- **Story 12** — Push: the triggers, the sender, the push key, and notifications on the phone.

  A sink compares each session with its last state (the first projection is a baseline) and queues waiting and finished pushes for the devices whose settings allow them; the sender posts them off the drain goroutine within the limits, retrying and recording results through `PushResult`. `GET /api/v1/push-key` serves the key; the service worker shows each notification tagged by session and sets the badge; the settings screen turns notifications on; and each launch checks the phone's subscription against loom's, since Apple answers 201 to one the phone has dropped.

## Story 1: The session facts the phone shows: the Remote Control link and the permission's tool summary

- **Developer:** intermediate-dev
- **Files:** `session/claude_roster.go`, `session/claude_roster_test.go`, `session/hooks/event.go`, `session/hooks/event_test.go`, `session/claude_state.go`, `session/claude_state_test.go`, `session/instance.go`, `core/claude_status.go`, `core/claude_status_test.go`, `core/view.go`, `core/views.go`, `core/hook_scan.go`, `core/hook_scan_test.go`, `core/CLAUDE.md`, `docs/specs/protocol.md`, `session/README.md`
- **Interfaces:** exposes `session.RosterEntry.PID`, `RosterEntry.RemoteSession`, `hooks.Event.ToolSummary`, `(*session.Instance).ClaudeWaitDetail`, `WaitDetail`, `SetWaitDetail`, and `core.InstanceView.RemoteSession` and `WaitDetail`; uses none
- **Verify:** `CGO_ENABLED=0 go test ./session/... ./core/...`
- **Review:** deep

The roster query keeps each interactive entry's `pid` and reads that session's `<config dir>/sessions/<pid>.json` for its bridge ID (the ID in its `https://claude.ai/code/<id>` link), failing closed on every doubt; a `PermissionRequest` hook event keeps a one-line summary of its tool's input, which the instance holds beside the wait reason and sets and clears with it. Both reach `core.InstanceView` (`RemoteSession`, `WaitDetail`), so every client sees them and the web layer can show them later. Three edits are easy to miss, and the story is wrong without them: `core/hook_scan.go` with `core/hook_scan_test.go` (a hook scan decides whether an instance changed from a hand-written list of fields, which must include the summary, or a second permission request for the same tool leaves the old command on the phone and wakes no client); `docs/specs/protocol.md` (the wire reference holds `InstanceView`'s zero value, so `TestProtocolReference` fails until the file is regenerated with `-update`, never edited by hand); and `core/CLAUDE.md` (its `adoptClaudeStatus` rule says the adopt records `Instance.WaitReason`, and must name `WaitDetail`, which is set and cleared with it; the file has 93 bytes free of its 20,000, so only that phrase changes). `session/README.md` also changes, in the tasks that add each fact: its roster paragraph lists what an entry keeps (the `pid` and the Remote Control ID, Task 1.3), and its hooks paragraph describes the compact `.ev` form (`tool_summary`, Task 1.1). `core/README.md` is left alone: a later story updates its `adoptClaudeStatus` line.

### Task 1.1: Keep a permission request's tool summary on the hook event

**Files:**

`hooks.Event` gains `ToolSummary`: for a `PermissionRequest` only, one line read from the payload's `tool_input` (Bash's `command`, the `file_path` of Edit, Write and MultiEdit, NotebookEdit's `notebook_path`, WebFetch's `url`), at most 200 characters, cut with "…". The compact form the scan keeps on disk holds it as `tool_summary`, so a replay after a restart still has it.

```go
// ToolSummary is one line saying what a permission request's tool will do,
// read from the payload's tool_input.
func TestParseEvent_ToolSummary(t *testing.T) {
	perm := func(tool, input string) string {
		return `{"hook_event_name":"PermissionRequest","tool_name":"` + tool + `","tool_input":` + input + `}`
	}
	cases := []struct {
		name    string
		payload string
		want    string
	}{
		{"bash: the command", perm("Bash", `{"command":"go test ./...","description":"Run the tests"}`), "go test ./..."},
		{"bash: only the first line", perm("Bash", `{"command":"cat <<EOF\nsecret\nEOF"}`), "cat <<EOF"},
		{"bash: blank lines and indentation before the command are skipped", perm("Bash", `{"command":"\n\n   ls -la\nrm -rf x"}`), "ls -la"},
		{"edit: the file", perm("Edit", `{"file_path":"/w/a.go","old_string":"a","new_string":"b"}`), "/w/a.go"},
		{"write: the file", perm("Write", `{"file_path":"/w/b.go","content":"package b"}`), "/w/b.go"},
		{"multiedit: the file", perm("MultiEdit", `{"file_path":"/w/c.go","edits":[]}`), "/w/c.go"},
		{"notebookedit: the notebook", perm("NotebookEdit", `{"notebook_path":"/w/n.ipynb","new_source":"x"}`), "/w/n.ipynb"},
		{"webfetch: the url", perm("WebFetch", `{"url":"https://example.com/a?b=1","prompt":"summarize"}`), "https://example.com/a?b=1"},
		{"a tool without a summary: Read", perm("Read", `{"file_path":"/w/secret"}`), ""},
		{"a tool without a summary: an MCP tool", perm("mcp__db__query", `{"command":"drop table x"}`), ""},
		{"only the tool's own key counts", perm("Bash", `{"file_path":"/w/a.go"}`), ""},
		{"no tool_input", `{"hook_event_name":"PermissionRequest","tool_name":"Bash"}`, ""},
		{"tool_input null", perm("Bash", `null`), ""},
		{"tool_input a string", perm("Bash", `"ls"`), ""},
		{"tool_input a list", perm("Bash", `["ls"]`), ""},
		{"a command that is not a string", perm("Bash", `{"command":7}`), ""},
		{"a command of only whitespace", perm("Bash", `{"command":"  \n "}`), ""},
		{"tool_input wins over a tool_summary the payload should not carry", `{"hook_event_name":"PermissionRequest","tool_name":"Bash","tool_input":{"command":"real"},"tool_summary":"forged"}`, "real"},
	}
	for _, tc := range cases {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			ev, err := ParseEvent([]byte(tc.payload))
			require.NoError(t, err)
			assert.Equal(t, tc.want, ev.ToolSummary)
		})
	}
}

// Only a PermissionRequest keeps a summary: another event's tool_input is
// not what a prompt asks for.
func TestParseEvent_ToolSummaryOnlyForPermissionRequests(t *testing.T) {
	for _, name := range []string{EventStop, EventNotification, EventSessionStart, EventUserPromptSubmit, EventSubagentStart} {
		ev, err := ParseEvent([]byte(`{"hook_event_name":"` + name + `","tool_name":"Bash","tool_input":{"command":"ls"},"tool_summary":"ls"}`))
		require.NoError(t, err)
		assert.Empty(t, ev.ToolSummary, name)
	}
}

// The cap is MaxToolSummaryRunes characters, a multi-byte one counting once,
// and a cut summary ends in "…" within the cap.
func TestParseEvent_ToolSummaryIsCapped(t *testing.T) {
	summary := func(n int) string {
		ev, err := ParseEvent([]byte(`{"hook_event_name":"PermissionRequest","tool_name":"Bash","tool_input":{"command":"` + strings.Repeat("é", n) + `"}}`))
		require.NoError(t, err)
		return ev.ToolSummary
	}

	assert.Equal(t, strings.Repeat("é", MaxToolSummaryRunes), summary(MaxToolSummaryRunes), "exactly the cap stays whole")

	cut := summary(MaxToolSummaryRunes + 1)
	assert.Equal(t, strings.Repeat("é", MaxToolSummaryRunes-1)+"…", cut)
	assert.Equal(t, MaxToolSummaryRunes, utf8.RuneCountInString(cut))
	assert.True(t, utf8.ValidString(cut))
}

func TestParseEvent_ProbeToolSummary(t *testing.T) {
	parent, err := ParseEvent(probeFixture(t, "1790179972989150631"))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, "date > probe.txt", parent.ToolSummary)

	sub, err := ParseEvent(probeFixture(t, "1790180017571376478"))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, "touch sub.txt", sub.ToolSummary)
}

// The compact form keeps the one line, not the input it came from, and
// reads back unchanged.
func TestEventCompact_KeepsTheToolSummary(t *testing.T) {
	raw, err := ParseEvent([]byte(`{"hook_event_name":"PermissionRequest","tool_name":"Bash","tool_input":{"command":"date > probe.txt","description":"Write the date"}}`))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, "date > probe.txt", raw.ToolSummary)

	compact, err := raw.Compact()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Contains(t, string(compact), `"tool_summary"`)
	assert.NotContains(t, string(compact), "tool_input")
	assert.NotContains(t, string(compact), "description")

	again, err := ParseEvent(compact)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, raw, again)
}
```

```go
// toolSummaryKeys names, per tool, the tool_input field that says what the
// tool will do. A tool not listed has no summary: its input may hold
// anything, and the phone shows only what loom knows to be one line of
// intent.
var toolSummaryKeys = map[string]string{
	"Bash":         "command",
	"Edit":         "file_path",
	"Write":        "file_path",
	"MultiEdit":    "file_path",
	"NotebookEdit": "notebook_path",
	"WebFetch":     "url",
}

// toolSummary reads the summary of tool's request from a payload's
// tool_input. It fails closed: a tool without a key, an input that is not
// an object, and a key that is missing or not a string all give "".
func toolSummary(tool string, input json.RawMessage) string {
	key, ok := toolSummaryKeys[tool]
	if !ok {
		return ""
	}
	var fields map[string]json.RawMessage
	if err := json.Unmarshal(input, &fields); err != nil {
		return ""
	}
	var s string
	if err := json.Unmarshal(fields[key], &s); err != nil {
		return ""
	}
	return capSummary(s)
}

// capSummary reduces s to its first line that has any text, trimmed, and
// cuts that to MaxToolSummaryRunes characters, ending in "…" when it cut.
// It is idempotent, which ParseEvent relies on when it reads a compact
// event's tool_summary back.
func capSummary(s string) string {
	s = strings.TrimSpace(s)
	if i := strings.IndexAny(s, "\r\n"); i >= 0 {
		s = strings.TrimSpace(s[:i])
	}
	if utf8.RuneCountInString(s) <= MaxToolSummaryRunes {
		return s
	}
	return string([]rune(s)[:MaxToolSummaryRunes-1]) + "…"
}
```

### Task 1.2: Hold the tool summary beside the wait reason on the instance

**Files:**
- Modify: `session/claude_state.go:23`, `:49-72`, `:93-94`, `:134-151`, `:208-209`
- Modify: `session/instance.go:239-241`, `:1954-1959`
- Test: `session/claude_state_test.go`

The observation (a hook's or the roster's report of the session's status) gains `detail`, the tool summary of the permission request behind a Prompting status. A new request replaces it and counts as a change; a Notification for the same prompt, and every roster answer that agrees on the status, keep it; any other outcome clears it, because the observation is replaced. `(*Instance).ClaudeWaitDetail` reads it; `WaitDetail` and `SetWaitDetail` hold the copy the model adopts beside `WaitReason`.

Add at the end of `session/claude_state_test.go`:

```go
func TestClaudeState_PermissionRequestKeepsItsToolSummary(t *testing.T) {
	var s claudeState
	s.applyEvent(hooks.Event{Name: hooks.EventPermissionRequest, ToolName: "Bash", ToolSummary: "date > probe.txt", At: t0})
	assert.Equal(t, "Prompting: permission: Bash", describe(s))
	assert.Equal(t, "date > probe.txt", s.waitDetail())
}

// The summary belongs to the wait: whatever replaces the Prompting
// observation takes it along.
func TestClaudeState_ToolSummaryEndsWithTheWait(t *testing.T) {
	cases := []struct {
		name string
		end  func(s *claudeState)
	}{
		{"a new prompt", func(s *claudeState) {
			s.applyEvent(hooks.Event{Name: hooks.EventUserPromptSubmit, At: t0.Add(time.Second)})
		}},
		{"a stop", func(s *claudeState) {
			s.applyEvent(hooks.Event{Name: hooks.EventStop, HasTasks: true, At: t0.Add(time.Second)})
		}},
		{"a session end", func(s *claudeState) {
			s.applyEvent(hooks.Event{Name: hooks.EventSessionEnd, At: t0.Add(time.Second)})
		}},
		{"a roster answer that the session is running", func(s *claudeState) {
			s.offer(rosterObs(Running, "", time.Second))
		}},
		{"a relaunch", func(s *claudeState) { s.newLaunch(t0.Add(time.Second)) }},
		{"the hooks folder vanishing", func(s *claudeState) { s.folderGone(t0.Add(time.Second)) }},
	}
	for _, tc := range cases {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			var s claudeState
			s.applyEvent(hooks.Event{Name: hooks.EventPermissionRequest, ToolName: "Bash", ToolSummary: "go test ./...", At: t0})
			require.Equal(t, "go test ./...", s.waitDetail(), "precondition")

			tc.end(&s)

			assert.Empty(t, s.waitDetail())
		})
	}
}

// Two requests for one tool leave the status and the reason as they were,
// so only the summary says which command the prompt is for.
func TestClaudeState_ANewToolSummaryIsAChange(t *testing.T) {
	var s claudeState
	perm := func(summary string, dt time.Duration) bool {
		return s.applyEvent(hooks.Event{Name: hooks.EventPermissionRequest, ToolName: "Bash", ToolSummary: summary, At: t0.Add(dt)})
	}

	assert.True(t, perm("ls", 0))
	assert.True(t, perm("rm -r build", time.Second), "the same status and reason, another command")
	assert.Equal(t, "rm -r build", s.waitDetail())
	assert.False(t, perm("rm -r build", 2*time.Second), "the same request again changes nothing")
}

// The Notification for a prompt arrives about six seconds after its
// PermissionRequest; the specific reason stays, and so does its summary.
func TestClaudeState_NotificationKeepsTheToolSummary(t *testing.T) {
	var s claudeState
	s.applyEvent(hooks.Event{Name: hooks.EventPermissionRequest, ToolName: "Bash", ToolSummary: "date > probe.txt", At: t0})
	s.applyEvent(hooks.Event{Name: hooks.EventNotification, NotificationType: "permission_prompt",
		Message: "Claude needs your permission", At: t0.Add(6 * time.Second)})
	assert.Equal(t, "Prompting: permission: Bash", describe(s))
	assert.Equal(t, "date > probe.txt", s.waitDetail())
}

func TestClaudeState_ANotificationAloneHasNoToolSummary(t *testing.T) {
	var s claudeState
	s.applyEvent(hooks.Event{Name: hooks.EventNotification, NotificationType: "elicitation_dialog",
		Message: "Claude needs your input", At: t0})
	assert.Equal(t, "Prompting: Claude needs your input", describe(s))
	assert.Empty(t, s.waitDetail())
}

// A roster answer names no tool, so it never sets a summary; one that
// agrees on the status keeps the hook's, and so does the next, which
// follows an observation that is the roster's own by then.
func TestClaudeState_RosterAnswersKeepTheToolSummaryWhileTheWaitLasts(t *testing.T) {
	var s claudeState
	s.applyEvent(hooks.Event{Name: hooks.EventPermissionRequest, ToolName: "Bash", ToolSummary: "date > probe.txt", At: t0})

	assert.False(t, s.offer(rosterObs(Prompting, "permission prompt", time.Second)))
	s.offer(rosterObs(Prompting, "permission prompt", 2*time.Second))
	assert.Equal(t, "date > probe.txt", s.waitDetail())

	assert.True(t, s.offer(rosterObs(Running, "", 3*time.Second)))
	assert.Empty(t, s.waitDetail(), "the answer ended the wait")
	s.offer(rosterObs(Prompting, "sandbox request", 4*time.Second))
	assert.Empty(t, s.waitDetail(), "a wait only the roster reports names no tool")
}

func TestClaudeWaitDetail_ReadsTheCurrentObservation(t *testing.T) {
	inst := hooksInstance(t, "claude")
	require.True(t, inst.ApplyHookScan(HookScanResult{LaunchID: "L", Replayed: true, Events: []hooks.Event{
		{Name: hooks.EventUserPromptSubmit, At: t0},
		{Name: hooks.EventPermissionRequest, ToolName: "Bash", ToolSummary: "go test ./...", At: t0.Add(time.Second)},
	}}))
	assert.Equal(t, "go test ./...", inst.ClaudeWaitDetail())

	require.True(t, inst.ApplyHookScan(HookScanResult{LaunchID: "L", Replayed: true, Events: []hooks.Event{
		{Name: hooks.EventStop, HasTasks: true, At: t0.Add(2 * time.Second)},
	}}))
	assert.Empty(t, inst.ClaudeWaitDetail())
}

func TestWaitDetail_SetAndClear(t *testing.T) {
	inst := hooksInstance(t, "claude")
	assert.Empty(t, inst.WaitDetail())

	inst.SetWaitDetail("rm -r build")
	assert.Equal(t, "rm -r build", inst.WaitDetail())

	inst.SetWaitDetail("")
	assert.Empty(t, inst.WaitDetail())
}
```

Make `offer` carry the summary and count it as a change.

Current (`session/claude_state.go:49-72`):

```go
// offer applies o if it is newer than the current observation, and reports
// whether the reported status or reason changed. Newest wins whichever
// source either came from: the roster publishes a change before Claude
// runs the hook, so a roster answer stamped after a hook event already
// reflects it, and one stamped before is older and dropped. The comparison
// uses at even when the current observation is no-opinion, so an older
// answer delivered late never revives a status a SessionEnd cleared.
//
// A roster observation with the same status as a current hook one keeps
// the hook's reason: the hook names the tool ("permission: Bash"), the
// roster only the kind of wait ("permission prompt").
func (s *claudeState) offer(o observation) bool {
	if !s.obs.at.IsZero() && !o.at.After(s.obs.at) {
		return false
	}
	if o.source == obsRoster && o.valid && s.obs.valid && s.obs.source == obsHook &&
		o.status == s.obs.status && s.obs.reason != "" {
		o.reason = s.obs.reason
	}
	changed := o.valid != s.obs.valid ||
		(o.valid && (o.status != s.obs.status || o.reason != s.obs.reason))
	s.obs = o
	return changed
}
```

Replace with:

```go
// offer applies o if it is newer than the current observation, and reports
// whether the reported status, reason or tool summary changed. Newest wins
// whichever source either came from: the roster publishes a change before
// Claude runs the hook, so a roster answer stamped after a hook event
// already reflects it, and one stamped before is older and dropped. The
// comparison uses at even when the current observation is no-opinion, so an
// older answer delivered late never revives a status a SessionEnd cleared.
//
// A roster observation with the same status as a current hook one keeps
// the hook's reason: the hook names the tool ("permission: Bash"), the
// roster only the kind of wait ("permission prompt"). The tool summary
// belongs to the permission request, not to whoever reports the wait, so
// every roster answer that agrees on the status keeps it, the one after the
// hook's and the ones after that, until a new request or the end of the wait
// replaces the observation. The roster names no tool, so it never sets one.
func (s *claudeState) offer(o observation) bool {
	if !s.obs.at.IsZero() && !o.at.After(s.obs.at) {
		return false
	}
	if o.source == obsRoster && o.valid && s.obs.valid && s.obs.source == obsHook &&
		o.status == s.obs.status && s.obs.reason != "" {
		o.reason = s.obs.reason
	}
	if o.source == obsRoster && o.valid && s.obs.valid && o.status == s.obs.status {
		o.detail = s.obs.detail
	}
	changed := o.valid != s.obs.valid ||
		(o.valid && (o.status != s.obs.status || o.reason != s.obs.reason || o.detail != s.obs.detail))
	s.obs = o
	return changed
}
```

```go
// waitDetail returns the current observation's tool summary: "" unless it
// is a Prompting one a permission request gave.
func (s *claudeState) waitDetail() string {
	if !s.obs.valid || s.obs.status != Prompting {
		return ""
	}
	return s.obs.detail
}
```

```go
// SetWaitReason records (or with "" clears) Claude's reason for blocking.
func (i *Instance) SetWaitReason(reason string) {
	i.mu.Lock()
	defer i.mu.Unlock()
	i.waitReason = reason
}

// WaitDetail returns the tool summary of the permission request behind the
// wait reason, or "" when none is known (see ClaudeWaitDetail).
func (i *Instance) WaitDetail() string {
	i.mu.RLock()
	defer i.mu.RUnlock()
	return i.waitDetail
}

// SetWaitDetail records (or with "" clears) the wait's tool summary. The
// model sets it with SetWaitReason, in core.Model.adoptClaudeStatus.
func (i *Instance) SetWaitDetail(detail string) {
	i.mu.Lock()
	defer i.mu.Unlock()
	i.waitDetail = detail
}
```

### Task 1.3: Keep each roster entry's pid and read its Remote Control ID

**Files:**
- Modify: `session/claude_roster.go:3-13`, `:37-43`, `:61-70`, `:103-108`, `:142-160`

`RosterEntry` gains `PID` and `RemoteSession`. The query reads `<config dir>/sessions/<pid>.json` beside each entry it keeps (the dir it ran under: the account's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, else the daemon's own, else `~/.claude`) and takes `bridgeSessionId` only when that file's `sessionId` is the entry's own. Every doubt gives no ID, and none of them fails the query: the roster's status is what drives the session's state.

Add at the end of `session/claude_roster_test.go`:

```go
// bridgeRosterJSON is one interactive entry, and bridgeSessionFile the
// sessions/41.json Claude keeps for it while Remote Control is connected.
const (
	bridgeRosterJSON  = `[{"pid":41,"cwd":"/w/rc","kind":"interactive","sessionId":"s-rc","name":"rc-1","status":"idle"}]`
	bridgeSessionFile = `{"pid":41,"sessionId":"s-rc","cwd":"/w/rc","status":"idle","bridgeSessionId":"session_01AbC-9"}`
)

// bridgeFileFor is the session file of bridgeRosterJSON's entry holding id.
func bridgeFileFor(id string) string {
	return `{"sessionId":"s-rc","bridgeSessionId":` + strconv.Quote(id) + `}`
}

// writeSessionFile writes <dir>/sessions/<pid>.json.
func writeSessionFile(t *testing.T, dir string, pid int, content string) {
	t.Helper()
	require.NoError(t, os.MkdirAll(filepath.Join(dir, "sessions"), 0o755))
	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(dir, "sessions", strconv.Itoa(pid)+".json"), []byte(content), 0o600))
}

// bridgeOf runs the roster query as an account whose config dir is dir and
// returns the entry for /w/rc.
func bridgeOf(t *testing.T, dir, roster string) RosterEntry {
	t.Helper()
	got, err := QueryClaudeRosterEnv("claude", account.EnvFor(dir), &fakeRosterExecutor{out: []byte(roster)})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Contains(t, got, "/w/rc")
	return got["/w/rc"]
}

func TestQueryClaudeRoster_KeepsEachEntrysPID(t *testing.T) {
	got, err := QueryClaudeRoster("claude", &fakeRosterExecutor{out: []byte(rosterJSON)})

	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, 1, got["/w/busy"].PID)
	assert.Equal(t, 2, got["/w/idle"].PID)
	assert.Equal(t, 3, got["/w/waiting"].PID)
}

// The bridge ID is a convenience read from a file Claude owns: it is taken
// only when every check holds, and no failure of one touches the entry's
// status, which drives the session.
func TestQueryClaudeRoster_BridgeIDRules(t *testing.T) {
	const capBytes = 64 << 10 // the limit on one of Claude's session files
	padded := func(n int) string { return bridgeSessionFile + strings.Repeat(" ", n-len(bridgeSessionFile)) }
	type bridgeCase struct {
		name   string
		roster string // bridgeRosterJSON when empty
		file   string // the contents of sessions/41.json
		noFile bool
		want   string
	}
	cases := []bridgeCase{
		{name: "Remote Control is connected", file: bridgeSessionFile, want: "session_01AbC-9"},
		{name: "the file is missing", noFile: true},
		{name: "another session's file: the pid was reused", file: `{"sessionId":"s-other","bridgeSessionId":"session_01Other"}`},
		{name: "the file names no session", file: `{"bridgeSessionId":"session_01AbC"}`},
		{name: "the entry names no session", roster: `[{"pid":41,"cwd":"/w/rc","kind":"interactive","status":"idle"}]`, file: `{"bridgeSessionId":"session_01AbC"}`},
		{name: "Remote Control is not connected", file: `{"sessionId":"s-rc","status":"idle"}`},
		{name: "the file is not JSON", file: `{"sessionId":"s-rc",`},
		{name: "the bridge ID is not a string", file: `{"sessionId":"s-rc","bridgeSessionId":7}`},
		{name: "the entry has no pid", roster: `[{"cwd":"/w/rc","kind":"interactive","sessionId":"s-rc","status":"idle"}]`, file: bridgeSessionFile},
		{name: "the file is exactly the size cap", file: padded(capBytes), want: "session_01AbC-9"},
		{name: "the file is a byte over the cap", file: padded(capBytes + 1)},
		{name: "an ID of the longest length", file: bridgeFileFor(strings.Repeat("a", 128)), want: strings.Repeat("a", 128)},
	}
	for _, id := range []string{strings.Repeat("a", 129), "../session_01AbC", "session_01 AbC", "session_01?x=1", "a/b", "séssion", ""} {
		cases = append(cases, bridgeCase{name: "an ID that cannot be a path segment: " + id, file: bridgeFileFor(id)})
	}
	for _, tc := range cases {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			dir := t.TempDir()
			if !tc.noFile {
				writeSessionFile(t, dir, 41, tc.file)
			}
			roster := tc.roster
			if roster == "" {
				roster = bridgeRosterJSON
			}

			got := bridgeOf(t, dir, roster)

			assert.Equal(t, tc.want, got.RemoteSession)
			assert.Equal(t, RosterStatusIdle, got.Status, "the roster's own answer stands whatever the file says")
		})
	}
}

// Only a regular file is read: anything else at the path, a FIFO included,
// could block the query that the whole fleet's status waits on.
func TestQueryClaudeRoster_BridgeIDNeedsARegularFile(t *testing.T) {
	dir := t.TempDir()
	require.NoError(t, os.MkdirAll(filepath.Join(dir, "sessions", "41.json"), 0o755))

	got := bridgeOf(t, dir, bridgeRosterJSON)

	assert.Empty(t, got.RemoteSession)
	assert.Equal(t, RosterStatusIdle, got.Status)
}

// A pid this build cannot read costs its entry the bridge ID and nothing
// else, not the whole query.
func TestQueryClaudeRoster_AnUnreadablePIDCostsOnlyTheBridgeID(t *testing.T) {
	dir := t.TempDir()
	writeSessionFile(t, dir, 41, bridgeSessionFile)
	for _, pid := range []string{`"41"`, `true`, `-41`, `41.5`, `null`, `[41]`, `{}`, `0`} {
		roster := `[{"pid":` + pid + `,"cwd":"/w/rc","kind":"interactive","sessionId":"s-rc","status":"idle"}]`

		got := bridgeOf(t, dir, roster)

		assert.Zero(t, got.PID, pid)
		assert.Empty(t, got.RemoteSession, pid)
		assert.Equal(t, RosterStatusIdle, got.Status, pid)
	}
}

// The files are read in the dir the query ran under: the account's, else the
// daemon's own CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR, else ~/.claude. An account whose dir lacks
// the file is not answered from another's.
func TestQueryClaudeRoster_BridgeIDIsReadUnderTheDirTheQueryRanUnder(t *testing.T) {
	acctDir, emptyDir, daemonDir, home := t.TempDir(), t.TempDir(), t.TempDir(), t.TempDir()
	writeSessionFile(t, acctDir, 41, bridgeFileFor("session_01Account"))
	writeSessionFile(t, daemonDir, 41, bridgeFileFor("session_01Daemon"))
	writeSessionFile(t, filepath.Join(home, ".claude"), 41, bridgeFileFor("session_01Home"))
	t.Setenv("CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR", daemonDir)
	t.Setenv("HOME", home)
	t.Setenv("USERPROFILE", home)
	query := func(env []string) string {
		got, err := QueryClaudeRosterEnv("claude", env, &fakeRosterExecutor{out: []byte(bridgeRosterJSON)})
		require.NoError(t, err)
		return got["/w/rc"].RemoteSession
	}

	assert.Equal(t, "session_01Account", query(account.EnvFor(acctDir)), "an account's sessions are in its own dir")
	assert.Empty(t, query(account.EnvFor(emptyDir)), "and never in the daemon's")
	assert.Equal(t, "session_01Daemon", query(nil), "the default account runs under the daemon's own CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR")

	t.Setenv("CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR", "")
	assert.Equal(t, "session_01Home", query(nil), "and without one under ~/.claude")
}
```

Add at the end of `session/claude_roster.go`:

```go
// parsePID reads a roster entry's pid: a positive JSON integer, else 0.
func parsePID(raw json.RawMessage) int {
	var pid int
	if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &pid); err != nil || pid < 0 {
		return 0
	}
	return pid
}

// rosterConfigDir is the config dir `claude agents --json` ran under, whose
// sessions/ dir holds its sessions' files: the CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR in env (an
// account's; the last value wins, as os/exec resolves a duplicated key),
// else the one loom itself inherited, else ~/.claude. It is "" when none can
// be found.
func rosterConfigDir(env []string) string {
	dir := ""
	for _, kv := range env {
		if v, found := strings.CutPrefix(kv, "CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR="); found {
			dir = v
		}
	}
	if dir != "" {
		return dir
	}
	return account.MainDir(account.Identity{})
}

// maxSessionFileBytes is the largest sessions/<pid>.json readBridgeID reads.
// Claude's file is a few hundred bytes; the cap keeps a stray big file from
// being read on every roster query.
const maxSessionFileBytes = 64 << 10

// maxBridgeIDLen bounds a bridge ID, which becomes a path segment of the
// session's https://claude.ai/code/<id> link.
const maxBridgeIDLen = 128

// claudeSessionFile is the part of <config dir>/sessions/<pid>.json this
// package reads. Claude writes the file for each interactive session and
// adds bridgeSessionId while Remote Control is connected.
type claudeSessionFile struct {
	SessionID       string `json:"sessionId"`
	BridgeSessionID string `json:"bridgeSessionId"`
}

// readBridgeID returns the bridgeSessionId in dir/sessions/<pid>.json when
// that file's sessionId is sessionID, else "". It fails closed on every
// doubt: no config dir, pid or session ID; a file that is missing,
// unreadable, not a regular file, over maxSessionFileBytes or malformed; a
// file of another session (a reused pid); and an ID that could not be a
// path segment of the link built from it.
func readBridgeID(dir string, pid int, sessionID string) string {
	if dir == "" || pid <= 0 || sessionID == "" {
		return ""
	}
	path := filepath.Join(dir, "sessions", strconv.Itoa(pid)+".json")
	// Lstat first: opening a FIFO a stranger left here would block the query.
	if info, err := os.Lstat(path); err != nil || !info.Mode().IsRegular() || info.Size() > maxSessionFileBytes {
		return ""
	}
	f, err := os.Open(path)
	if err != nil {
		return ""
	}
	defer func() { _ = f.Close() }()
	data, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(f, maxSessionFileBytes+1))
	if err != nil || len(data) > maxSessionFileBytes {
		return ""
	}
	var sf claudeSessionFile
	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &sf); err != nil || sf.SessionID != sessionID || !validBridgeID(sf.BridgeSessionID) {
		return ""
	}
	return sf.BridgeSessionID
}

// validBridgeID reports whether id is 1 to maxBridgeIDLen letters, digits,
// underscores and hyphens.
func validBridgeID(id string) bool {
	if id == "" || len(id) > maxBridgeIDLen {
		return false
	}
	for _, r := range id {
		switch {
		case r >= 'a' && r <= 'z', r >= 'A' && r <= 'Z', r >= '0' && r <= '9', r == '_', r == '-':
		default:
			return false
		}
	}
	return true
}
```

```go
// The tool summary of the permission request behind a wait lives exactly as
// long as the wait reason: adoptClaudeStatus sets and clears both, and the
// instance's view publishes it.
func TestAdoptClaudeStatus_TheToolSummaryLivesAsLongAsTheWaitReason(t *testing.T) {
	m := NewForTest(Options{})
	inst := activeInst(t, m, "detail-lives")
	now := time.Now()

	applyHookEvents(t, inst, hooks.Event{Name: hooks.EventPermissionRequest, ToolName: "Bash", ToolSummary: "date > probe.txt", At: now.Add(-2 * time.Second)})
	m.applyClaudeStatus(inst)

	require.Equal(t, session.Prompting, inst.GetStatus())
	assert.Equal(t, "permission: Bash", inst.WaitReason())
	assert.Equal(t, "date > probe.txt", inst.WaitDetail())
	assert.Equal(t, "date > probe.txt", m.viewOf(inst).WaitDetail)

	applyHookEvents(t, inst, hooks.Event{Name: hooks.EventStop, HasTasks: true, At: now.Add(-time.Second)})
	m.applyClaudeStatus(inst)

	require.Equal(t, session.Ready, inst.GetStatus())
	assert.Empty(t, inst.WaitReason())
	assert.Empty(t, inst.WaitDetail(), "the summary goes with the wait")
	assert.Empty(t, m.viewOf(inst).WaitDetail)
}

func TestAdoptClaudeStatus_ARosterOnlyWaitHasNoToolSummary(t *testing.T) {
	m := NewForTest(Options{})
	inst := activeInst(t, m, "detail-roster")

	deliverRoster(m, rosterFor(inst, session.RosterStatusWaiting))

	deliverRoster(m, rosterFor(inst, session.RosterStatusWaiting))
```

```go
// TestDeliverHookScan_ANewToolSummaryReplacesTheOldOne: a second permission
// request for one tool changes neither the status nor the wait reason, so
// the summary alone says which command the prompt is for. The scan must
// count it as a change: the instance follows it and the clients wake.
func TestDeliverHookScan_ANewToolSummaryReplacesTheOldOne(t *testing.T) {
	m := NewForTest(Options{})
	inst := activeInst(t, m, "two-prompts")
	base := time.Now().Add(-time.Minute)
	scan := func(summary string, after time.Duration) (woke bool) {
		m.noWake = false
		m.Deliver(hookScanResults{results: []hookScanResult{{instance: inst, result: session.HookScanResult{
			LaunchID: "L1", Replayed: true,
			Events: []hooks.Event{{Name: hooks.EventPermissionRequest, ToolName: "Bash", ToolSummary: summary, At: base.Add(after)}},
		}}}})
		return !m.noWake
	}

	require.True(t, scan("ls", time.Second), "the first request")
	require.Equal(t, session.Prompting, inst.GetStatus())
	require.Equal(t, "ls", inst.WaitDetail())

	assert.True(t, scan("rm -r build", 2*time.Second), "another command for the same tool wakes the clients")
	assert.Equal(t, "permission: Bash", inst.WaitReason(), "the wait reason is the same")
	assert.Equal(t, "rm -r build", inst.WaitDetail(), "the summary follows the newest request")

	assert.False(t, scan("rm -r build", 3*time.Second), "the same request again moves nothing")
}
```

Current (`core/claude_status.go:207-231`):

```go
// adoptClaudeStatus is the single place a Claude session's reported status
// is applied to an instance. It returns the instance's merged hook and
// roster observation (see session.Instance.ClaudeStatus) and, as a side
// effect, records Claude's reason for blocking so the card can render it.
//
// The reason lives exactly as long as the reported wait: any other
// outcome clears it. Only the model writes a reported status, and every
// path that does (deliverHealth, and applyClaudeStatus for a hook scan or
// a roster answer) must go through here; the TUI's pane ladder
// (statusDetectedMsg, the snapshot scan) only reads StatusReported and
// overlays its own status. Duplicating the set/clear at each call site is
// how they drift apart, which is the lockstep hazard called out in
// CLAUDE.md.
func (m *Model) adoptClaudeStatus(inst *session.Instance) (session.Status, bool) {
	if inst == nil {
		return session.Ready, false
	}
	status, reason, ok := inst.ClaudeStatus()
	if ok && status == session.Prompting {
		inst.SetWaitReason(reason)
	} else {
		inst.SetWaitReason("")
	}
	return status, ok
}
```

Replace with:

```go
// adoptClaudeStatus is the single place a Claude session's reported status
// is applied to an instance. It returns the instance's merged hook and
// roster observation (see session.Instance.ClaudeStatus) and, as a side
// effect, records Claude's reason for blocking so the card can render it,
// and the tool summary of the permission request behind it
// (session.Instance.ClaudeWaitDetail), which the phone shows.
//
// The reason and the summary live exactly as long as the reported wait: any
// other outcome clears both. Only the model writes a reported status, and
// every path that does (deliverHealth, and applyClaudeStatus for a hook scan
// or a roster answer) must go through here; the TUI's pane ladder
// (statusDetectedMsg, the snapshot scan) only reads StatusReported and
// overlays its own status. Duplicating the set/clear at each call site is
// how they drift apart, which is the lockstep hazard called out in
// CLAUDE.md.
func (m *Model) adoptClaudeStatus(inst *session.Instance) (session.Status, bool) {
	if inst == nil {
		return session.Ready, false
	}
	status, reason, ok := inst.ClaudeStatus()
	if ok && status == session.Prompting {
		inst.SetWaitReason(reason)
		inst.SetWaitDetail(inst.ClaudeWaitDetail())
	} else {
		inst.SetWaitReason("")
		inst.SetWaitDetail("")
	}
	return status, ok
}
```

```go
// hookFace is what a hook scan can change of an instance: what its view
// shows (its status and Claude's, the wait reason, the last message, the
// subagent rows) and the conversation the model saves.
type hookFace struct {
	status, claude   session.Status
	why, wait        string
	reported         bool
	conv, transcript string
	lastMsg          string
	hasLastMsg       bool
	subagents        []subagent.View
}

// hookFaceOf reads inst's hookFace.
func hookFaceOf(inst *session.Instance) hookFace {
	f := hookFace{status: inst.GetStatus(), wait: inst.WaitReason(), subagents: inst.Subagents()}
	f.claude, f.why, f.reported = inst.ClaudeStatus()
	f.conv, f.transcript = inst.ClaudeSession()
	f.lastMsg, f.hasLastMsg = inst.LastMessage()
	return f
}
```

```go
// hookFace is what a hook scan can change of an instance: what its view
// shows (its status and Claude's, the wait reason and the tool summary
// behind it, the last message, the subagent rows) and the conversation the
// model saves. detail is Claude's observation's tool summary: a second
// permission request for one tool changes it and nothing else.
type hookFace struct {
	status, claude   session.Status
	why, wait        string
	detail           string
	reported         bool
	conv, transcript string
	lastMsg          string
	hasLastMsg       bool
	subagents        []subagent.View
}

// hookFaceOf reads inst's hookFace.
func hookFaceOf(inst *session.Instance) hookFace {
	f := hookFace{status: inst.GetStatus(), wait: inst.WaitReason(), subagents: inst.Subagents()}
	f.claude, f.why, f.reported = inst.ClaudeStatus()
	f.detail = inst.ClaudeWaitDetail()
	f.conv, f.transcript = inst.ClaudeSession()
	f.lastMsg, f.hasLastMsg = inst.LastMessage()
	return f
}
```

```go
func TestViewOf_RemoteSessionComesFromTheRoster(t *testing.T) {
	m := NewForTest(Options{})
	inst := activeInst(t, m, "rc")
	other := activeInst(t, m, "rc-other")

		inst.GetWorktreePath(): {Status: session.RosterStatusIdle, PID: 41, RemoteSession: "session_01AbC"},
	}

	assert.Equal(t, "session_01AbC", m.viewOf(inst).RemoteSession)
	assert.Empty(t, m.viewOf(other).RemoteSession, "a worktree the roster has no entry for")

	failRoster(m)

	assert.Empty(t, m.viewOf(inst).RemoteSession, "a failed query clears the roster, and the link with it")

func TestViewOf_RemoteSessionIsJoinedToTheInstancesAccountRoster(t *testing.T) {
	m := NewForTest(Options{})
	inst := activeInst(t, m, "rc-acct")
	inst.SetAccount("max-2")
	wt := inst.GetWorktreePath()
	m.roster = map[string]session.RosterEntry{wt: {RemoteSession: "session_01Default"}}
	m.rosterByAccount = map[string]map[string]session.RosterEntry{"max-2": {wt: {RemoteSession: "session_01Account"}}}

	assert.Equal(t, "session_01Account", m.viewOf(inst).RemoteSession)

	m.rosterByAccount = nil

	assert.Empty(t, m.viewOf(inst).RemoteSession, "the default account's roster never answers for another account's session")
```

In `core/claude_status.go`, split the lookup out of `rosterStatusFor`.

Current (`core/claude_status.go:188-205`):

```go
func (m *Model) rosterStatusFor(inst *session.Instance) (session.Status, string, bool) {
	if inst == nil || !session.IsClaudeProgram(inst.Program()) {
		return session.Ready, "", false
	}
	roster := m.roster
	if acct := inst.Account(); acct != "" {
		roster = m.rosterByAccount[acct]
	}
	if len(roster) == 0 {
		return session.Ready, "", false
	}
	entry, ok := roster[inst.GetWorktreePath()]
	if !ok {
		return session.Ready, "", false
	}
	status, authoritative := entry.LoomStatus()
	return status, entry.WaitingFor, authoritative
}
```

Replace with:

```go
func (m *Model) rosterStatusFor(inst *session.Instance) (session.Status, string, bool) {
	entry, ok := m.rosterEntryFor(inst)
	if !ok {
		return session.Ready, "", false
	}
	status, authoritative := entry.LoomStatus()
	return status, entry.WaitingFor, authoritative
}

// rosterEntryFor returns the roster's entry for inst: the one in m.roster,
// or in its account's roster, for the worktree it runs in. ok is false when
// the roster has none to give, which is every case rosterStatusFor lists as
// no opinion: a non-Claude agent, an empty or failed roster, no entry for
// this worktree. What the entry carries beyond the status (the Remote
// Control session) therefore clears when the roster does.
func (m *Model) rosterEntryFor(inst *session.Instance) (session.RosterEntry, bool) {
	if inst == nil || !session.IsClaudeProgram(inst.Program()) {
		return session.RosterEntry{}, false
	}
	roster := m.roster
	if acct := inst.Account(); acct != "" {
		roster = m.rosterByAccount[acct]
	}
	entry, ok := roster[inst.GetWorktreePath()]
	return entry, ok
}
```

## Story 2: The hello's role, and a web connection never holds the notice backlog

- **Developer:** intermediate-dev
- **Files:** `core/rpc/rpc.go`, `core/rpc/client.go`, `core/rpc/server.go`, `core/rpc/server_conn.go`, `core/rpc/server_backlog_test.go`, `core/rpc/wire_test.go`, `core/rpc/README.md`, `docs/specs/protocol.md`, `docs/claude/adding-a-core-event.md`
- **Interfaces:** exposes `rpc.RoleWeb`, `rpc.Hello.Role` and `rpc.DialAs`; uses none
- **Verify:** `CGO_ENABLED=0 go test ./core/rpc/`
- **Review:** deep

A client can name its role in its hello (`Hello.Role`, set by the new `DialAs`; `Dial` names none). The server reads the role once, when the hello arrives, and a connection that says `web` (`RoleWeb`, the daemon's own web layer, which stays connected for as long as the daemon runs) counts as no client for the notice backlog: a `Notice` raised while only web connections exist is sent to them and also kept for the next other connection, and a web connection that joins is sent none of the kept notices. The `Notice` of a web connection's own request is sent to it and is also handled as if no web connection existed: the other connections are sent it, and it is kept for the next client when none is connected (a kill's or resume's stash notice can be the only record of its SHA, and the phone may never see it). A TUI's own request's `Notice` still goes to that TUI alone. Without this the web layer, always connected, would swallow the notices meant for the next TUI. The field is optional on the wire, so it needs no `Protocol` bump; a role this build does not know is an ordinary client. Task 2.1 adds the field and `DialAs` (the server still ignores the role); Task 2.2 adds the server's rule. Both regenerate `docs/specs/protocol.md` from the prose `TestProtocolReference` writes.

```go
// TestHello_RoleIsOptionalOnTheWire: a hello that names no role encodes
// without the key, so a build from before the field reads the same frame,
// and a hello from such a build decodes with no role.
func TestHello_RoleIsOptionalOnTheWire(t *testing.T) {
	assert.NotContains(t, compact(t, Frame{Hello: &Hello{Protocol: Protocol, Build: "b"}}), "role")
	assert.Contains(t, compact(t, Frame{Hello: &Hello{Protocol: Protocol, Build: "b", Role: RoleWeb}}), `"role":"web"`)

	var old Frame
	require.NoError(t, json.Unmarshal([]byte(`{"hello":{"protocol":2,"build":"b"}}`), &old))
	require.NotNil(t, old.Hello)
	assert.Empty(t, old.Hello.Role)

	var web Frame
	require.NoError(t, json.Unmarshal([]byte(`{"hello":{"protocol":2,"build":"b","role":"web"}}`), &web))
	require.NotNil(t, web.Hello)
	assert.Equal(t, RoleWeb, web.Hello.Role)
}

// TestDialAs_NamesItsRoleInTheHello: DialAs sends the role it is given in
// its hello, and Dial (and DialAs with "") sends none. A fake server reads
// the hello and hangs up, so each dial fails after the hello went out.
func TestDialAs_NamesItsRoleInTheHello(t *testing.T) {
	for _, tc := range []struct {
		name string
		dial func(io.ReadWriteCloser) (*Client, error)
		role string
	}{
		{"web", func(nc io.ReadWriteCloser) (*Client, error) { return DialAs(nc, RoleWeb) }, RoleWeb},
		{"an empty role", func(nc io.ReadWriteCloser) (*Client, error) { return DialAs(nc, "") }, ""},
		{"Dial", Dial, ""},
	} {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			a, b := net.Pipe()
			got := make(chan Hello, 1)
			go func() {
				defer a.Close()
				var f Frame
				if json.NewDecoder(a).Decode(&f) == nil && f.Hello != nil {
					got <- *f.Hello
				}
			}()
			_, err := tc.dial(b)
			require.Error(t, err, "the fake server answers no hello")
			select {
			case h := <-got:
				assert.Equal(t, tc.role, h.Role)
				assert.Equal(t, Protocol, h.Protocol)
			case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
				t.Fatal("no hello arrived")
			}
		})
	}
}
```
