### Task 2.2: A web connection is no client for the notice backlog

**Files:**
- Modify: `core/rpc/rpc.go` (the `RoleWeb` comment)
- Modify: `core/rpc/server_conn.go:23-34`
- Modify: `core/rpc/server.go:55-58`, `:243-246`, `:395-448`, `:450-453`, `:473-476`
- Modify: `core/rpc/wire_test.go` (the reference's prose, after the example Task 2.1 added)
- Modify: `core/rpc/README.md:45`, `:47`, `:57`, `:86`
- Modify: `docs/claude/adding-a-core-event.md:35`
- Modify: `docs/specs/protocol.md` (regenerated, never by hand)
- Test: `core/rpc/server_backlog_test.go`

The design: `serverConn` gets a `web` flag, set from the hello before the connection joins `Server.conns`, so every read of it (under `Server.mu`, over `conns`) follows the write. A new `Server.clientsLocked` counts the connections that are not web. Wherever `sendLocked` decided "no connection, keep the notice" it now decides "no client, keep the notice", and it still sends the notice to the web connections that are there. `flushBacklogLocked` sends a web connection nothing and leaves the backlog alone. A notice that names a request goes to its requester with the request, as ever. When that requester is a web connection (or has gone), the notice is also handled as if no web connection existed: the connections it did not reach are sent it, with no request named, and it is kept when none of the connections is a client. A TUI's own request's notice still goes to that TUI alone and is not kept. The guide `docs/claude/adding-a-core-event.md` states the backlog rule in its "Who receives an event" trap, so the same task rewrites that line to the new rule.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests and the reference's prose**

In `core/rpc/server_backlog_test.go`, add after `dialTo`:

```go
// dialAsTo is dialTo for a client that names its role.
func dialAsTo(t *testing.T, srv *Server, role string) *Client {
	t.Helper()
	a, b := net.Pipe()
	srv.Serve(a)
	c, err := DialAs(b, role)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	t.Cleanup(c.Close)
	return c
}

// raiseNotice has the model raise a Notice no request is behind: the kill of
// the instance failingKill returned fails, and its job lands.
func raiseNotice(t *testing.T, loop *core.Loop, id core.InstanceID) {
	t.Helper()
	loop.Kill(id, 0)
	for _, j := range loop.JobsForTest() {
		loop.DeliverForTest(j())
	}
}

// raiseRequestNotice has c make the failing kill of id as its request 3, and
// the job lands: the model raises a Notice that names c's request.
func raiseRequestNotice(t *testing.T, loop *core.Loop, c *Client, id core.InstanceID) {
	t.Helper()
	c.Kill(id, 3)
	require.NoError(t, c.FlushForTest())
	for _, j := range loop.JobsForTest() {
		loop.DeliverForTest(j())
	}
}
```

Append these tests at the end of `core/rpc/server_backlog_test.go`:

```go
// The web layer is always connected, so a notice raised while it is the only
// connection must still reach the next TUI: the web layer is told, and the
// notice is kept for whoever else comes.
func TestServe_ANoticeRaisedWhileOnlyTheWebLayerIsConnectedWaitsForTheNextTUI(t *testing.T) {
	loop, id := failingKill(t)
	srv := NewServer(loop)
	t.Cleanup(srv.Close)
	web := dialAsTo(t, srv, RoleWeb)

	raiseNotice(t, loop, id)
	require.NoError(t, web.FlushForTest())
	told := noticesOf(web.Sync())
	require.Len(t, told, 1, "the web layer is told, as any connection is")
	assert.Error(t, told[0].Err)

	tui := dialTo(t, srv)
	kept := noticesOf(tui.Sync())
	require.Len(t, kept, 1, "and the next TUI is sent it too")
	assert.Error(t, kept[0].Err)
	assert.Empty(t, noticesOf(dialTo(t, srv).Sync()), "a kept notice is sent once")
}

// The other side of the rule: with a TUI connected beside the web layer the
// notice reached a client, so both are told and nothing is kept.
func TestServe_ANoticeIsNotKeptWhileATUIIsConnectedBesideTheWebLayer(t *testing.T) {
	loop, id := failingKill(t)
	srv := NewServer(loop)
	t.Cleanup(srv.Close)
	web := dialAsTo(t, srv, RoleWeb)
	tui := dialTo(t, srv)

	raiseNotice(t, loop, id)
	require.NoError(t, web.FlushForTest())
	require.NoError(t, tui.FlushForTest())
	assert.Len(t, noticesOf(web.Sync()), 1)
	assert.Len(t, noticesOf(tui.Sync()), 1)
	assert.Empty(t, noticesOf(dialTo(t, srv).Sync()), "told to the TUI, so not kept as well")
}

// A web connection that joins takes none of the backlog: the notices a
// daemon's boot kept wait for the TUI, and the TUI gets them once.
func TestServe_AWebConnectionIsNotSentTheBacklog(t *testing.T) {
	loop := core.StartForTest(core.NewForTest(core.Options{}))
	t.Cleanup(loop.Stop)
	srv := NewServer(loop)
	t.Cleanup(srv.Close)
	srv.Keep(core.Notice{Info: "from the boot"})

	web := dialAsTo(t, srv, RoleWeb)
	assert.Empty(t, noticesOf(web.Sync()), "the web layer takes none of the backlog")

	tui := dialTo(t, srv)
	got := noticesOf(tui.Sync())
	require.Len(t, got, 1, "it waits for the TUI")
	assert.Equal(t, "from the boot", got[0].Info)
	assert.Empty(t, noticesOf(dialTo(t, srv).Sync()), "and is sent once")
}

// Only the role `web` is exempt: a client naming none, or a role this build
// does not know (a newer peer's), takes the backlog as ever.
func TestServe_AConnectionWithAnyOtherRoleIsAClient(t *testing.T) {
	for _, tc := range []struct{ name, role string }{
		{"none", ""},
		{"one this build does not know", "tui"},
	} {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			loop := core.StartForTest(core.NewForTest(core.Options{}))
			t.Cleanup(loop.Stop)
			srv := NewServer(loop)
			t.Cleanup(srv.Close)
			srv.Keep(core.Notice{Info: "from the boot"})

			c := dialAsTo(t, srv, tc.role)
			got := noticesOf(c.Sync())
			require.Len(t, got, 1, "an ordinary client takes the backlog")
			assert.Equal(t, "from the boot", got[0].Info)
		})
	}
}

// A request's Notice whose client has gone is told to the connections still
// there; when the only one left is the web layer's, it is kept for the next
// TUI as well.
func TestServe_AGoneClientsNoticeWaitsForTheNextTUIWhenOnlyTheWebLayerStays(t *testing.T) {
	loop, id := failingKill(t)
	srv := NewServer(loop)
	t.Cleanup(srv.Close)

	stays := dialAsTo(t, srv, RoleWeb)
	gone := dialTo(t, srv)
	gone.Kill(id, 3)
	require.NoError(t, gone.FlushForTest())
	jobs := loop.JobsForTest()
	gone.Close()
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool {
		srv.mu.Lock()
		defer srv.mu.Unlock()
		return len(srv.conns) == 1
	}, 5*time.Second, time.Millisecond)
	for _, j := range jobs {
		loop.DeliverForTest(j())
	}

	require.NoError(t, stays.FlushForTest())
	told := noticesOf(stays.Sync())
	require.Len(t, told, 1, "the web layer is told")
	assert.Zero(t, told[0].Req, "the request was another client's")

	next := dialTo(t, srv)
	kept := noticesOf(next.Sync())
	require.Len(t, kept, 1, "and the next TUI is sent it")
	assert.Error(t, kept[0].Err)
	assert.Zero(t, kept[0].Req)
}

// A Notice naming a request of the web layer's own is the web layer's, with
// its request, and also handled as if no web connection existed: a TUI
// connected beside it is told too, since a stash notice may be the only
// record of a SHA and the phone may never see it. It reached a client, so it
// is not kept as well.
func TestServe_ANoticeOfAWebRequestAlsoReachesTheTUI(t *testing.T) {
	loop, id := failingKill(t)
	srv := NewServer(loop)
	t.Cleanup(srv.Close)
	web := dialAsTo(t, srv, RoleWeb)
	tui := dialTo(t, srv)

	raiseRequestNotice(t, loop, web, id)
	require.NoError(t, web.FlushForTest())
	require.NoError(t, tui.FlushForTest())
	told := noticesOf(web.Sync())
	require.Len(t, told, 1, "the web layer is told, once")
	assert.Equal(t, core.ReqID(3), told[0].Req, "it names the web layer's own request")
	seen := noticesOf(tui.Sync())
	require.Len(t, seen, 1, "and the TUI is told too")
	assert.Error(t, seen[0].Err)
	assert.Zero(t, seen[0].Req, "the request was the web layer's")
	assert.Empty(t, noticesOf(dialTo(t, srv).Sync()), "told to the TUI, so not kept as well")
}

// With only the web layer connected, the Notice of its own request is told
// to it and also kept: the next TUI is sent it from the backlog, once.
func TestServe_ANoticeOfAWebRequestWaitsForTheNextTUIWhenOnlyTheWebLayerIsConnected(t *testing.T) {
	loop, id := failingKill(t)
	srv := NewServer(loop)
	t.Cleanup(srv.Close)
	web := dialAsTo(t, srv, RoleWeb)

	raiseRequestNotice(t, loop, web, id)
	require.NoError(t, web.FlushForTest())
	told := noticesOf(web.Sync())
	require.Len(t, told, 1, "the web layer is told, once")
	assert.Equal(t, core.ReqID(3), told[0].Req, "it names the web layer's own request")

	tui := dialTo(t, srv)
	kept := noticesOf(tui.Sync())
	require.Len(t, kept, 1, "and the next TUI is sent it from the backlog")
	assert.Error(t, kept[0].Err)
	assert.Zero(t, kept[0].Req, "the request was the web layer's")
	assert.Empty(t, noticesOf(dialTo(t, srv).Sync()), "a kept notice is sent once")
}

// The other side: a TUI's own request's Notice goes to that TUI alone, the
// web layer connected beside it is not told, and nothing is kept.
func TestServe_ANoticeOfATUIsRequestIsTheTUIsAlone(t *testing.T) {
	loop, id := failingKill(t)
	srv := NewServer(loop)
	t.Cleanup(srv.Close)
	web := dialAsTo(t, srv, RoleWeb)
	tui := dialTo(t, srv)

	raiseRequestNotice(t, loop, tui, id)
	require.NoError(t, tui.FlushForTest())
	require.NoError(t, web.FlushForTest())
	got := noticesOf(tui.Sync())
	require.Len(t, got, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, core.ReqID(3), got[0].Req, "it names the TUI's own request")
	assert.Empty(t, noticesOf(web.Sync()), "a TUI's request's notice is its requester's alone")
	assert.Empty(t, noticesOf(dialTo(t, srv).Sync()), "and it is not kept")
}
```

   ```
   **The notice backlog.** A `Notice` published while no client is connected, and those a daemon hands over with `Keep` (its boot's, raised before any client could be), wait for the next client; a request's `Notice` whose client has gone goes to the connections still there, and is kept too when none of them is a client. A connection whose hello names the role `web` (`rpc.RoleWeb`, the daemon's web layer, which stays connected for as long as the daemon runs) is no client here (`Server.clientsLocked`): a `Notice` raised while only web connections exist is sent to them and also kept for the next other connection, and a web connection that joins is sent none of the kept ones (`TestServe_ANoticeRaisedWhileOnlyTheWebLayerIsConnectedWaitsForTheNextTUI`, `TestServe_AWebConnectionIsNotSentTheBacklog`). A `Notice` naming a request goes to its requester, with the request; one of a web connection's own request is also handled as if no web connection existed (the other connections are sent it, and it is kept for the next client when none is connected), since a kill's or resume's stash notice can be the only record of a SHA and the phone may never see it (`TestServe_ANoticeOfAWebRequestAlsoReachesTheTUI`, `TestServe_ANoticeOfAWebRequestWaitsForTheNextTUIWhenOnlyTheWebLayerIsConnected`); a TUI's own request's goes to that TUI alone (`TestServe_ANoticeOfATUIsRequestIsTheTUIsAlone`). ```
   ```
```
- **Who receives an event depends on its kind.** A state event needs no backlog: a client that connects later gets the state in its snapshot. Any other event published while no client is connected goes nowhere, except a `Notice`, which the server's backlog keeps for the next connection. A routed event whose client has gone (a `Reply`) is dropped; a `Notice` in that position goes to the clients still connected instead (`core/rpc/server.go`).
```

with

```
- **Who receives an event depends on its kind.** A state event needs no backlog: a client that connects later gets the state in its snapshot. Any other event published while no client is connected goes nowhere, except a `Notice`, which the server's backlog keeps for the next client. A web connection (the daemon's web layer, whose hello names the role `web`) is no client for that: a `Notice` raised while only web connections are connected is sent to them and also kept, and a web connection that joins is sent none of the kept ones. A routed event whose client has gone (a `Reply`) is dropped; a `Notice` in that position goes to the clients still connected instead, and is kept as well when none of them is a client. The `Notice` of a web connection's own request goes to that connection and is handled the same way, so the other connections are sent it too (`core/rpc/server.go`).
```

## Story 3: The web settings and devices in the model: `web.json`, the requests and the published view

- **Developer:** intermediate-dev
- **Files:** `core/web.go`, `core/web_store.go`, `core/web_test.go`, `core/web_store_test.go`, `core/model.go`, `core/iface.go`, `core/loop_core.go`, `core/state_views.go`, `core/state_views_test.go`, `core/state_publish.go`, `core/rpc/replica.go`, `core/load.go`, `core/loop_test.go`, `core/rpc/client_test.go`, `core/rpc/methods_gen.go`, `docs/specs/protocol.md`
- **Interfaces:** exposes the core web state names of the contract (types, constants, errors, validators, `ModelView.Web`, and `Core.Web`, `ConfigureWeb`, `PairDevice`, `RevokeDevice`); uses none
- **Verify:** `CGO_ENABLED=0 go test ./core/... ./app/...`
- **Review:** deep

The model loads `web.json` from the global config dir at `Boot`, keeping a file it can't read, one that names a newer version, or one holding a value that fails validation untouched: the web stays off, `WebView.Error` says why, and `Boot` hands back a notice for the next TUI. This is stricter than the spec, which names only an unreadable or newer file: a stored `listen`, `origin`, device name, token hash or VAPID key that fails validation counts as a file that fails to load, as `account.LoadRegistry` treats an entry it would never have written, and so do a few structural faults of the same kind (a device or token listed twice, more than 16 devices, a push state or pause that no build writes). The model serves `ConfigureWeb`, `PairDevice` and `RevokeDevice`, validates every setting and name (`ValidateWebListen`, `ValidateWebOrigin`, `ValidateDeviceName`, `NormalizePairingCode`), and publishes `WebView` in `ModelView.Web`, so `Core.Web` is answered locally by every replica; a revoke therefore reaches clients as a `ModelChanged` and the web layer ends that device's streams from it. Every `ConfigureWeb` that succeeds bumps `WebView.Rev` (the model's `rev`) and clears what the layer reported (`serving`, `addr`, `servingErr`), whatever it changed, so a layer that rebinds when `Rev` changes retries a failed bind after the next `loom web enable`, and still sees a disable and an enable that one `Sync` coalesces into a single `ModelChanged`; a refused or failed `ConfigureWeb` changes nothing, `Rev` included. The model's web state (`webState`, `webDevice`, `pendingPairing`, `clock`, `find`, `prune`, `saveWeb`) is pinned in the names contract for Story 4, which reads and changes it; `saveWeb` writes at the model's `path`, or under the global config dir when it has none (a `NewForTest` model that never ran `Boot`, which Story 4's tests rely on), and fails only when neither is known; `MaxWebDevices` counts paired devices only, never the pending codes; the loader `initWeb` stays internal, and a test in another package loads a fixture through the seam `InitWebForTest`. Five tasks, each leaving the tree green: the types and validators; the state and the file; the three requests; the published view and `Boot`; the `Core` methods with their forwarders, replica and generated wire. No new event is added (`ModelChanged` already carries the view), so there is no `EventTypes` entry, coalescing key, `forConn` routing or TUI applier; the requests name no instance, so no `admit` or `precondition`; they carry no `ReqID`, since the CLI waits on the reply; only additive wire changes, so `rpc.Protocol` stays. None of the code holds a landmine marker, so the developer needs no extra effort.

### Task 3.1: The web types, constants, errors and validators

**Files:**
- Create: `core/web.go`
- Test: `core/web_test.go`

Create `core/web_test.go`:

```go
package core

import (
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
)

func TestValidateWebListen(t *testing.T) {
	for _, listen := range []string{"127.0.0.1:7420", "[::1]:7420", "127.0.0.1:1", "127.0.0.1:65535", "127.0.0.2:8080"} {
		assert.NoError(t, ValidateWebListen(listen), listen)
	}
	for _, listen := range []string{
		"", "localhost:7420", "0.0.0.0:7420", ":7420", "192.168.1.5:7420", "[::]:7420", "[fe80::1]:7420",
		"127.0.0.1", "127.0.0.1:", "127.0.0.1:0", "127.0.0.1:65536", "127.0.0.1:-1", "127.0.0.1:+80",
		"127.0.0.1:07420", "127.0.0.1:http", "[127.0.0.1]:7420", "[0:0:0:0:0:0:0:1]:7420",
		"[::ffff:127.0.0.1]:7420", "[::1%lo]:7420", "::1:7420", " 127.0.0.1:7420", "127.0.0.1:7420 ",
		"127.0.0.1:7420/x", "unix:/tmp/loom.sock", "/tmp/loom.sock",
	} {
		assert.Error(t, ValidateWebListen(listen), "%q", listen)
	}
	assert.ErrorContains(t, ValidateWebListen("localhost:7420"), "loopback IP literal", "a host name is refused for what it is")
}

func TestValidateWebOrigin(t *testing.T) {
	long := "https://" + strings.Repeat("a", maxWebOrigin-len("https://"))
	for _, origin := range []string{
		"https://loom.example.com", "https://loom.example.com:8443", "https://x",
		"http://127.0.0.1:7420", "http://localhost:3000", long,
	} {
		assert.NoError(t, ValidateWebOrigin(origin), origin)
	}
	for _, origin := range []string{
		"", "loom.example.com", "//loom.example.com", "ftp://loom.example.com", "https://", "https:///x",
		"https://loom.example.com/", "https://loom.example.com/app", "https://loom.example.com?",
		"https://loom.example.com?a=1", "https://loom.example.com#", "https://loom.example.com#top",
		"https://u@loom.example.com", "https://u:p@loom.example.com", "HTTPS://loom.example.com",
		"https://Loom.Example.com", "https://loom.example.com:", "https://loom.example.com:0",
		"https://loom.example.com:65536", "https://loom.example.com:08443", "https://loom.example.com:+1",
		" https://loom.example.com", "https://loom.example.com ", "https://lo om.example.com",
		"http://loom.example.com:80", "http://localhost", "http://127.0.0.1", "http://[::1]:7420",
		"https:loom.example.com", long + "a",
	} {
		assert.Error(t, ValidateWebOrigin(origin), "%q", origin)
	}
}

func TestValidateDeviceName(t *testing.T) {
	for _, tc := range []struct{ in, want string }{
		{"iPhone", "iPhone"},
		{"  iPhone \t", "iPhone"},
		{"Aidan's iPhone 15", "Aidan's iPhone 15"},
		{"x", "x"},
		{strings.Repeat("x", 32), strings.Repeat("x", 32)},
		{strings.Repeat("é", 32), strings.Repeat("é", 32)},
	} {
		got, err := ValidateDeviceName(tc.in)
		require.NoError(t, err, "%q", tc.in)
		assert.Equal(t, tc.want, got)
	}
	for _, in := range []string{
		"", "   ", "\t\n", strings.Repeat("x", 33), strings.Repeat("é", 33),
		"a\tb", "a\nb", "bad\x00", "\x7f", "a\xffb",
	} {
		_, err := ValidateDeviceName(in)
		assert.Error(t, err, "%q", in)
	}
}

func TestNormalizePairingCode(t *testing.T) {
	for in, want := range map[string]string{
		"K7QM-2XPA":       "K7QM2XPA",
		"k7qm2xpa":        "K7QM2XPA",
		" k7qm 2xpa ":     "K7QM2XPA",
		"K-7-Q-M-2-X-P-A": "K7QM2XPA",
		"K7QM2XPA":        "K7QM2XPA",
	} {
		got, ok := NormalizePairingCode(in)
		assert.True(t, ok, "%q", in)
		assert.Equal(t, want, got, "%q", in)
	}
	for _, in := range []string{
		"", "----", "K7QM2XP", "K7QM2XPAA", "K7QM-2XPA-K7QM",
		"K7QM2XP0", "K7QM2XP1", "K7QMIXPA", "K7QM2XPO", "K7QM2XPL",
		"K7QM2XPÁ", "K7QM2XP\t", "ſ7QM2XPA",
	} {
		got, ok := NormalizePairingCode(in)
		assert.False(t, ok, "%q", in)
		assert.Empty(t, got, "%q", in)
	}
}

func TestPairingAlphabet_HasNoLookAlikes(t *testing.T) {
	assert.Len(t, PairingAlphabet, 31)
	seen := map[rune]bool{}
	for _, r := range PairingAlphabet {
		assert.False(t, seen[r], "%q twice", r)
		seen[r] = true
	}
	for _, r := range "01ILO" {
		assert.NotContains(t, PairingAlphabet, string(r))
	}
}

func TestPushFailing(t *testing.T) {
	assert.Equal(t, PushState("failing: 503"), PushFailing(503))
	assert.Equal(t, PushState("failing: 0"), PushFailing(0))
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `core/web.go`:

```go
package core

import (
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"net"
	"net/netip"
	"net/url"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
	"time"
	"unicode"
	"unicode/utf8"
)

// The web layer's settings and devices (internal/web). The model keeps them
// in web.json (web_store.go) and publishes them in ModelView.Web, so every
// client reads them from its replica (Core.Web); the requests that change
// them are ConfigureWeb, PairDevice and RevokeDevice.

// WebView is the web settings as a client sees them, and who is paired.
type WebView struct {
	Listen  string      // "127.0.0.1:7420"; "" when off
	Origin  string      // "https://loom.example.com"
	Serving bool        // the listener is up
	Addr    string      // what it listens on, while Serving
	Error   string      // why it is not serving, or why web.json was left alone
	Devices []WebDevice // sorted by Name; no token hashes
	Rev     uint64      // bumped by every ConfigureWeb; the layer rebinds when it changes
}

// WebDevice is a paired device as a client sees it: its token hash and its
// push subscription stay in the model.
type WebDevice struct {
	Name     string
	Paired   time.Time
	LastSeen time.Time
	Notify   NotifySettings
	Push     PushState
}

// NotifySettings are the kinds of push a device takes, and until when it
// takes none.
type NotifySettings struct {
	Waiting     bool
	Finished    bool
	PausedUntil time.Time // zero: not paused
}

// Pairing is the one-time code PairDevice makes, and when it stops working.
type Pairing struct {
	Code    string // PairingCodeLen characters of PairingAlphabet, no dash
	Expires time.Time
}

// PushSubscription is the browser's push subscription, as the app posts it.
type PushSubscription struct {
	Endpoint string
	P256DH   string // unpadded base64url of a 65-byte uncompressed P-256 point
	Auth     string // unpadded base64url of 16 bytes
}

// PushTarget is a device the web layer may push to, with what it takes.
type PushTarget struct {
	Device       string
	Notify       NotifySettings
	Subscription PushSubscription
}

// PushState is how a device's push subscription stands: none, ok, expired
// or failing (PushFailing).
type PushState string

// The states of a push subscription besides failing (PushFailing).
const (
	PushNone    PushState = ""
	PushOK      PushState = "ok"
	PushExpired PushState = "expired"
)

// PushFailing is the state of a subscription whose last push the push
// service answered with status: "failing: <status>".
func PushFailing(status int) PushState { return PushState("failing: " + strconv.Itoa(status)) }

// VAPIDKey is the P-256 key pair the web layer signs its pushes with, made
// when the web is first enabled.
type VAPIDKey struct {
	Private []byte // the 32-byte P-256 scalar
	Public  []byte // the 65-byte uncompressed point
}

// The pairing code's alphabet (no 0, 1, I, L or O) and length, how long a
// code lives, how many wait at once and how many wrong ones lock redeeming,
// the most devices that can be paired, and web.json's name in the global dir.
const (
	PairingAlphabet   = "23456789ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTUVWXYZ"
	PairingCodeLen    = 8
	PairingTTL        = 10 * time.Minute
	PairingMaxPending = 4
	PairingMaxMisses  = 5
	MaxWebDevices     = 16
	WebFile           = "web.json" // in the global dir
)

const (
	maxWebOrigin     = 255 // bytes
	maxDeviceNameLen = 32  // characters
)

// The errors the web requests answer with; those that name a device wrap
// the sentinel (fmt.Errorf("%w: %q", ErrDeviceExists, name)).
var (
	ErrWebOff         = errors.New("web: not enabled; run loom web enable first")
	ErrPairingCode    = errors.New("pairing: the code is wrong, used or expired")
	ErrPairingLocked  = errors.New("pairing: too many wrong codes; run loom web pair again")
	ErrDeviceExists   = errors.New("pairing: a device has that name already")
	ErrTooManyDevices = errors.New("pairing: 16 devices are paired; revoke one first")
	ErrNoDevice       = errors.New("no device has that name")
)

// validPort reports whether port is a port number written plainly: digits
// only, 1 to 65535, no sign and no leading zero.
func validPort(port string) bool {
	n, err := strconv.ParseUint(port, 10, 16)
	return err == nil && n >= 1 && strconv.FormatUint(n, 10) == port
}

// ValidateWebListen checks a listen address: a loopback IP literal and a
// port, written as net.JoinHostPort writes them (127.0.0.1:7420 or
// [::1]:7420). A host name such as localhost, any other address, a unix
// socket and a port outside 1 to 65535 are refused: only a process on this
// machine, cloudflared through the tunnel, may reach the listener.
func ValidateWebListen(listen string) error {
	host, port, err := net.SplitHostPort(listen)
	if err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("listen %q: want a loopback address and a port, like 127.0.0.1:7420 or [::1]:7420", listen)
	}
	addr, err := netip.ParseAddr(host)
	if err != nil || addr.Zone() != "" || addr.Is4In6() || !addr.IsLoopback() || addr.String() != host {
		return fmt.Errorf("listen %q: the address must be a loopback IP literal, 127.0.0.1 or ::1", listen)
	}
	if !validPort(port) {
		return fmt.Errorf("listen %q: the port must be a number from 1 to 65535", listen)
	}
	if want := net.JoinHostPort(host, port); listen != want {
		return fmt.Errorf("listen %q: write it as %s", listen, want)
	}
	return nil
}

// ValidateWebOrigin checks the origin the phone reaches loom at:
// https://<host>[:<port>] in lower case with no user, path, query or
// fragment, or for development http://127.0.0.1:<port> or
// http://localhost:<port>, at most 255 bytes. The web layer compares the
// Host and Origin headers with it byte for byte, so a spelling a browser
// would not send is refused here.
func ValidateWebOrigin(origin string) error {
	if len(origin) > maxWebOrigin {
		return fmt.Errorf("origin is longer than %d bytes", maxWebOrigin)
	}
	bad := func(why string) error { return fmt.Errorf("origin %q: %s", origin, why) }
	u, err := url.Parse(origin)
	if err != nil || u.String() != origin {
		return bad("write it as https://<host>[:<port>], in lower case")
	}
	if u.Scheme != "https" && u.Scheme != "http" {
		return bad("the scheme must be https")
	}
	if u.Opaque != "" || u.User != nil || u.Path != "" || u.RawPath != "" || u.RawQuery != "" || u.ForceQuery || u.Fragment != "" || u.RawFragment != "" {
		return bad("it has no user, path, query or fragment")
	}
	host, port := u.Hostname(), u.Port()
	if host == "" || host != strings.ToLower(host) {
		return bad("the host must be named, in lower case")
	}
	if strings.HasSuffix(u.Host, ":") || (port != "" && !validPort(port)) {
		return bad("the port must be a number from 1 to 65535")
	}
	if u.Scheme == "http" && (port == "" || (host != "127.0.0.1" && host != "localhost")) {
		return bad("http is for development only: 127.0.0.1 or localhost, with a port")
	}
	return nil
}

// ValidateDeviceName checks a device name and returns it trimmed: 1 to 32
// characters, none of them a control character. Whether a device has it
// already is for the model to say (ErrDeviceExists).
func ValidateDeviceName(name string) (string, error) {
	name = strings.TrimSpace(name)
	if !utf8.ValidString(name) {
		return "", errors.New("device name: not valid UTF-8")
	}
	if n := utf8.RuneCountInString(name); n < 1 || n > maxDeviceNameLen {
		return "", fmt.Errorf("device name: want 1 to %d characters, got %d", maxDeviceNameLen, n)
	}
	for _, r := range name {
		if unicode.IsControl(r) {
			return "", errors.New("device name: no control characters")
		}
	}
	return name, nil
}

// NormalizePairingCode puts a code as typed into the form the model keeps:
// upper case, with dashes and spaces dropped. It reports false unless what
// is left is PairingCodeLen characters of PairingAlphabet.
func NormalizePairingCode(code string) (string, bool) {
	out := make([]byte, 0, PairingCodeLen)
	for i := 0; i < len(code); i++ {
		c := code[i]
		if c == '-' || c == ' ' {
			continue
		}
		if c >= 'a' && c <= 'z' {
			c -= 'a' - 'A'
		}
		if len(out) == PairingCodeLen || strings.IndexByte(PairingAlphabet, c) < 0 {
			return "", false
		}
		out = append(out, c)
	}
	if len(out) != PairingCodeLen {
		return "", false
	}
	return string(out), true
}
```

```go
package core

import (
	"crypto/ecdh"
	"encoding/base64"
	"encoding/hex"
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"strings"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/config"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
)

var (
	tokenA = strings.Repeat("ab", 32)
	tokenB = strings.Repeat("cd", 32)
)

// webJSON is an enabled web.json without a VAPID key, holding two devices
// listed out of name order: iPhone, which takes waiting pushes only, until
// a time, and iPad, whose push subscription expired.
var webJSON = `{"version":1,"listen":"127.0.0.1:7420","origin":"https://loom.example.com","devices":[` +
	`{"name":"iPhone","token_sha256":"` + tokenA + `","paired":"2026-10-01T09:00:00Z","last_seen":"2026-10-02T10:30:00Z",` +
	`"notify":{"waiting":true,"finished":false,"paused_until":"2026-10-03T08:00:00Z"},` +
	`"push":{"endpoint":"https://web.push.apple.com/abc","p256dh":"pk","auth":"au"},"push_state":"ok"},` +
	`{"name":"iPad","token_sha256":"` + tokenB + `","paired":"2026-10-04T09:00:00Z","last_seen":"0001-01-01T00:00:00Z",` +
	`"notify":{"waiting":false,"finished":true,"paused_until":""},"push_state":"expired"}]}`

// webDeviceJSON is one device of a web.json, with no push subscription.
func webDeviceJSON(name, token string) string {
	return `{"name":"` + name + `","token_sha256":"` + token + `","paired":"2026-10-01T09:00:00Z","last_seen":"2026-10-01T09:00:00Z",` +
		`"notify":{"waiting":true,"finished":true,"paused_until":""}}`
}

// webWith is an enabled web.json holding devices (webDeviceJSON).
func webWith(devices ...string) string {
	return `{"version":1,"listen":"127.0.0.1:7420","origin":"https://loom.example.com","devices":[` + strings.Join(devices, ",") + `]}`
}

// webDevices is n devices with distinct names and tokens, d0 to d<n-1>.
func webDevices(n int) []string {
	var out []string
	for i := 0; i < n; i++ {
		out = append(out, webDeviceJSON(fmt.Sprintf("d%d", i), fmt.Sprintf("%064x", i)))
	}
	return out
}

// loadWebInto points the global config dir at a fresh temp dir holding a
// web.json with body (none when body is empty) and loads it into m with
// initWeb, as Boot does.
func loadWebInto(t *testing.T, m *Model, body string) {
	t.Helper()
	dir := t.TempDir()
	t.Setenv(config.EnvGlobalDir, dir)
	if body != "" {
		require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(dir, WebFile), []byte(body), 0o600))
	}
	m.initWeb()
}

// webModel is a model whose global dir holds a web.json with body (none
// when body is empty), loaded by initWeb.
func webModel(t *testing.T, body string) *Model {
	t.Helper()
	m := NewForTest(Options{})
	loadWebInto(t, m, body)
	return m
}

// webFileBytes is web.json's content in m's global dir.
func webFileBytes(t *testing.T, m *Model) []byte {
	t.Helper()
	data, err := os.ReadFile(m.web.path)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	return data
}

func TestInitWeb_NoFileIsOffAndEmpty(t *testing.T) {
	m := webModel(t, "")

	assert.Equal(t, WebView{}, m.Web())
	dir, err := config.GetGlobalConfigDir()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, filepath.Join(dir, WebFile), m.web.path)
	assert.Empty(t, m.web.loadErr)
	assert.Empty(t, m.Drain().Events, "nothing to tell anyone")
	assert.NoFileExists(t, m.web.path, "loading writes nothing")
}

func TestInitWeb_LoadsTheFile(t *testing.T) {
	m := webModel(t, webJSON)

	v := m.Web()
	assert.Equal(t, "127.0.0.1:7420", v.Listen)
	assert.Equal(t, "https://loom.example.com", v.Origin)
	assert.False(t, v.Serving, "nothing listens until the layer says so")
	assert.Empty(t, v.Error)
	require.Len(t, v.Devices, 2)
	ipad, iphone := v.Devices[0], v.Devices[1]
	assert.Equal(t, "iPad", ipad.Name, "sorted by name")
	assert.Equal(t, PushExpired, ipad.Push)
	assert.True(t, ipad.LastSeen.IsZero(), "never seen")
	assert.True(t, ipad.Notify.Finished)
	assert.False(t, ipad.Notify.Waiting)
	assert.True(t, ipad.Notify.PausedUntil.IsZero(), "an empty paused_until is no pause")
	assert.Equal(t, "iPhone", iphone.Name)
	assert.Equal(t, PushOK, iphone.Push)
	assert.True(t, time.Date(2026, 10, 1, 9, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC).Equal(iphone.Paired))
	assert.True(t, time.Date(2026, 10, 2, 10, 30, 0, 0, time.UTC).Equal(iphone.LastSeen))
	assert.True(t, iphone.Notify.Waiting)
	assert.False(t, iphone.Notify.Finished)
	assert.True(t, time.Date(2026, 10, 3, 8, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC).Equal(iphone.Notify.PausedUntil))

	d := m.web.find("iPhone")
	require.NotNil(t, d)
	assert.Equal(t, tokenA, hex.EncodeToString(d.tokenHash[:]))
	assert.Equal(t, PushSubscription{Endpoint: "https://web.push.apple.com/abc", P256DH: "pk", Auth: "au"}, d.sub)
	assert.Nil(t, m.web.find("iphone"), "names match exactly")
	assert.Zero(t, m.web.vapid, "no key until the first enable")
	assert.Empty(t, m.Drain().Events)

	data, err := json.Marshal(v)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.NotContains(t, string(data), tokenA, "the view carries no token hash")
	assert.NotContains(t, string(data), "web.push.apple.com", "and no push subscription")
}

func TestInitWeb_LeavesAFileItCannotUseAlone(t *testing.T) {
	for _, tc := range []struct{ name, body, why string }{
		{"not JSON", `{not json`, "parse"},
		{"a newer version", `{"version":2,"listen":"127.0.0.1:7420","origin":"https://loom.example.com","devices":[]}`, "newer"},
		{"no version", `{"listen":"127.0.0.1:7420","origin":"https://loom.example.com"}`, "version 0"},
		{"a listen address that is not loopback", `{"version":1,"listen":"0.0.0.0:7420","origin":"https://loom.example.com"}`, "listen"},
		{"a listen address without an origin", `{"version":1,"listen":"127.0.0.1:7420"}`, "origin is empty"},
		{"an unusable origin", `{"version":1,"listen":"127.0.0.1:7420","origin":"http://example.com"}`, "origin"},
		{"a device without a name", webWith(webDeviceJSON("", tokenA)), "device"},
		{"a name with spaces around it", webWith(webDeviceJSON(" a", tokenA)), "spaces"},
		{"a name listed twice", webWith(webDeviceJSON("a", tokenA), webDeviceJSON("a", tokenB)), "twice"},
		{"a short token hash", webWith(webDeviceJSON("a", "abcd")), "token_sha256"},
		{"a token hash that is not hex", webWith(webDeviceJSON("a", strings.Repeat("zz", 32))), "token_sha256"},
		{"a token shared by two devices", webWith(webDeviceJSON("a", tokenA), webDeviceJSON("b", tokenA)), "shares a token"},
		{"too many devices", webWith(webDevices(MaxWebDevices + 1)...), "devices"},
		{"an unknown push state", strings.Replace(webJSON, `"push_state":"expired"`, `"push_state":"bogus"`, 1), "push_state"},
		{"a pause that is no time", strings.Replace(webJSON, `"paused_until":"2026-10-03T08:00:00Z"`, `"paused_until":"tomorrow"`, 1), "paused_until"},
		{"a key that is not P-256", `{"version":1,"vapid":{"private":"AAAA","public":"AAAA"}}`, "vapid"},
	} {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			m := webModel(t, tc.body)

			assert.Contains(t, m.web.loadErr, tc.why)
			assert.Equal(t, WebView{Error: m.web.loadErr}, m.Web(), "off, with the reason shown and nothing of the file held")
			var notices []string
			for _, ev := range m.Drain().Events {
				if n, ok := ev.(Notice); ok && n.Err != nil {
					notices = append(notices, n.Err.Error())
				}
			}
			require.Len(t, notices, 1, "a notice for the next TUI")
			assert.Contains(t, notices[0], "web:")
			assert.Contains(t, notices[0], tc.why)
			assert.Error(t, m.saveWeb(), "and nothing writes over it")
			assert.Equal(t, tc.body, string(webFileBytes(t, m)), "the file is as it was")
		})
	}
}

func TestInitWeb_AnUnreadableFileIsLeftAlone(t *testing.T) {
	dir := t.TempDir()
	t.Setenv(config.EnvGlobalDir, dir)
	require.NoError(t, os.Mkdir(filepath.Join(dir, WebFile), 0o755), "a directory where the file should be")
	m := NewForTest(Options{})

	m.initWeb()

	assert.Contains(t, m.web.loadErr, "read")
	assert.Error(t, m.saveWeb())
	info, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(dir, WebFile))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.True(t, info.IsDir(), "untouched")
}

func TestInitWeb_AKeyWhosePublicHalfIsNotItsOwnIsLeftAlone(t *testing.T) {
	a, err := newVAPIDKey()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	b, err := newVAPIDKey()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	enc := base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString
	body := fmt.Sprintf(`{"version":1,"vapid":{"private":%q,"public":%q}}`, enc(a.Private), enc(b.Public))

	m := webModel(t, body)

	assert.Contains(t, m.web.loadErr, "vapid")
	assert.Equal(t, body, string(webFileBytes(t, m)))
}

func TestInitWeb_PushStates(t *testing.T) {
	device := func(state string) string {
		return strings.Replace(webDeviceJSON("a", tokenA), `"paused_until":""}}`, `"paused_until":""},"push_state":"`+state+`"}`, 1)
	}
	for state, want := range map[string]PushState{
		"": PushNone, "ok": PushOK, "expired": PushExpired, "failing: 503": PushFailing(503), "failing: 0": PushFailing(0),
	} {
		m := webModel(t, webWith(device(state)))
		require.Empty(t, m.web.loadErr, "%q", state)
		assert.Equal(t, want, m.Web().Devices[0].Push, "%q", state)
	}
	for _, state := range []string{"OK", "failing", "failing: ", "failing: -1", "failing: 05", "failing: x", "failed: 503"} {
		m := webModel(t, webWith(device(state)))
		assert.Contains(t, m.web.loadErr, "push_state", "%q", state)
	}
}

func TestSaveWeb_RoundTripsWhatWasLoaded(t *testing.T) {
	m := webModel(t, webJSON)
	key, err := newVAPIDKey()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	m.web.vapid = key

	require.NoError(t, m.saveWeb())

	info, err := os.Stat(m.web.path)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, os.FileMode(0o600), info.Mode().Perm(), "it holds a token hash and a key")
	m2 := NewForTest(Options{})
	m2.initWeb()
	require.Empty(t, m2.web.loadErr)
	assert.Equal(t, m.Web(), m2.Web())
	assert.Equal(t, m.web.devices, m2.web.devices, "tokens, subscriptions and states too")
	assert.Equal(t, key, m2.web.vapid)

	var f map[string]any
	require.NoError(t, json.Unmarshal(webFileBytes(t, m), &f))
	assert.EqualValues(t, 1, f["version"])
	assert.Equal(t, "127.0.0.1:7420", f["listen"])
	assert.Equal(t, "https://loom.example.com", f["origin"])
	assert.Equal(t, map[string]any{
		"private": base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(key.Private),
		"public":  base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(key.Public),
	}, f["vapid"], "unpadded base64url")
	devices := f["devices"].([]any)
	require.Len(t, devices, 2)
	ipad, iphone := devices[0].(map[string]any), devices[1].(map[string]any)
	assert.Equal(t, "iPad", ipad["name"], "sorted by name")
	assert.NotContains(t, ipad, "push", "no subscription, no key")
	assert.Equal(t, "", ipad["notify"].(map[string]any)["paused_until"])
	assert.Equal(t, "iPhone", iphone["name"])
	assert.Equal(t, tokenA, iphone["token_sha256"])
	assert.Equal(t, "2026-10-03T08:00:00Z", iphone["notify"].(map[string]any)["paused_until"])
	assert.Equal(t, map[string]any{"endpoint": "https://web.push.apple.com/abc", "p256dh": "pk", "auth": "au"}, iphone["push"])
	assert.Equal(t, "ok", iphone["push_state"])
}

func TestSaveWeb_ReportsAWriteThatFails(t *testing.T) {
	m := webModel(t, webJSON)
	blocker := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "file")
	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(blocker, nil, 0o600))
	m.web.path = filepath.Join(blocker, WebFile)

	assert.Error(t, m.saveWeb(), "a path below a regular file")
}

func TestSaveWeb_ALoadedModelWritesToItsPath(t *testing.T) {
	m := webModel(t, "")
	loaded := m.web.path
	require.NoFileExists(t, loaded, "fixture: loading wrote nothing")
	elsewhere := t.TempDir()
	t.Setenv(config.EnvGlobalDir, elsewhere)

	require.NoError(t, m.saveWeb())

	assert.FileExists(t, loaded, "the path it loaded from")
	assert.NoFileExists(t, filepath.Join(elsewhere, WebFile), "it does not look the global dir up again")
}

// TestSaveWeb_AModelWithNoPathWritesUnderTheGlobalDir: a NewForTest model
// that never ran initWeb (or Boot) has no path, and the tests of the web
// layer (Story 4) change such a model's web state and expect it kept. It is
// kept under the global dir TestMain isolates, so the test leaves no file
// behind.
func TestSaveWeb_AModelWithNoPathWritesUnderTheGlobalDir(t *testing.T) {
	require.NotEmpty(t, os.Getenv(config.EnvGlobalDir), "TestMain isolates the global dir: never write into the real one")
	dir, err := config.GetGlobalConfigDir()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	path := filepath.Join(dir, WebFile)
	_ = os.Remove(path)
	t.Cleanup(func() { _ = os.Remove(path) })
	m := NewForTest(Options{})
	require.Empty(t, m.web.path, "fixture: initWeb never ran")
	m.web.listen, m.web.origin = "127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"

	require.NoError(t, m.saveWeb())

	info, err := os.Stat(path)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, os.FileMode(0o600), info.Mode().Perm())
	m2 := NewForTest(Options{})
	m2.initWeb()
	require.Empty(t, m2.web.loadErr)
	assert.Equal(t, "127.0.0.1:7420", m2.Web().Listen, "what it wrote is what a boot reads")
	assert.Empty(t, m.web.path, "it looked the dir up and kept no path")
}

func TestSaveWeb_FailsOnlyWhenNeitherAPathNorAGlobalDirIsKnown(t *testing.T) {
	loaded := webModel(t, "")
	t.Setenv(config.EnvGlobalDir, "not/absolute")
	unbooted := NewForTest(Options{})

	err := unbooted.saveWeb()

	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, errWebNoDir)
	assert.ErrorContains(t, err, "must be an absolute path", "and says why the dir is unknown")
	assert.NoError(t, loaded.saveWeb(), "a model that has a path needs no dir")
}

func TestSaveWeb_StillRefusesWhileTheFileIsLeftAlone(t *testing.T) {
	dir := t.TempDir()
	t.Setenv(config.EnvGlobalDir, dir)
	m := NewForTest(Options{})
	m.web.loadErr = "parse web.json: bad"

	err := m.saveWeb()

	assert.ErrorContains(t, err, "left alone")
	assert.NotErrorIs(t, err, errWebNoDir, "the load error comes first, before any dir is looked up")
	assert.NoFileExists(t, filepath.Join(dir, WebFile), "and nothing was written")
}

func TestWebView_ShowsTheLoadErrorBeforeTheServingError(t *testing.T) {
	var w webState
	w.servingErr = "listen tcp 127.0.0.1:7420: address already in use"
	assert.Equal(t, w.servingErr, w.view().Error)
	w.loadErr = "parse web.json: bad"
	assert.Equal(t, "parse web.json: bad", w.view().Error)
}

func TestWebView_CarriesTheRevisionAndKeepsItOutOfTheFile(t *testing.T) {
	m := webModel(t, webJSON)
	assert.Zero(t, m.Web().Rev, "a model that only loaded has seen no ConfigureWeb")
	m.web.rev = 5
	assert.EqualValues(t, 5, m.Web().Rev)

	require.NoError(t, m.saveWeb())

	assert.NotContains(t, strings.ToLower(string(webFileBytes(t, m))), `"rev`, "memory only: web.json does not hold it")
	m2 := NewForTest(Options{})
	m2.initWeb()
	assert.Zero(t, m2.Web().Rev, "a boot starts the count afresh")
}

func TestPrune_DropsTheCodesThatExpired(t *testing.T) {
	now := time.Date(2026, 10, 21, 12, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
	w := webState{now: func() time.Time { return now }}
	w.pending = []pendingPairing{
		{code: "AAAAAAAA", name: "old", expires: now.Add(-time.Second)},
		{code: "BBBBBBBB", name: "due", expires: now},
		{code: "CCCCCCCC", name: "live", expires: now.Add(time.Second)},
	}

	w.prune()

	require.Len(t, w.pending, 1, "a code expires at its time, not after it")
	assert.Equal(t, "live", w.pending[0].name)
}

func TestClock_IsTimeNowUnlessSet(t *testing.T) {
	var w webState
	assert.WithinDuration(t, time.Now(), w.clock(), time.Minute)
	set := time.Date(2020, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 0, time.UTC)
	w.now = func() time.Time { return set }
	assert.True(t, set.Equal(w.clock()))
}

func TestNewPairingCode_IsEightCharactersOfTheAlphabet(t *testing.T) {
	seen := map[string]bool{}
	symbols := map[byte]bool{}
	for i := 0; i < 500; i++ {
		code, err := newPairingCode()
		require.NoError(t, err)
		norm, ok := NormalizePairingCode(code)
		require.True(t, ok, code)
		require.Equal(t, code, norm, "already in the form the model keeps")
		seen[code] = true
		for j := 0; j < len(code); j++ {
			symbols[code[j]] = true
		}
	}
	assert.Greater(t, len(seen), 495, "random: no repeats to speak of")
	assert.Len(t, symbols, len(PairingAlphabet), "every symbol turns up in 4000 draws")
}

func TestNewVAPIDKey_IsAP256PairAndFresh(t *testing.T) {
	a, err := newVAPIDKey()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Len(t, a.Private, 32)
	assert.Len(t, a.Public, 65)
	assert.EqualValues(t, 4, a.Public[0], "an uncompressed point")
	priv, err := ecdh.P256().NewPrivateKey(a.Private)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, a.Public, priv.PublicKey().Bytes(), "the public half belongs to the private")
	b, err := newVAPIDKey()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.NotEqual(t, a.Private, b.Private)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `core/web_store.go`:

```go
package core

import (
	"bytes"
	"crypto/ecdh"
	"crypto/rand"
	"crypto/sha256"
	"encoding/base64"
	"encoding/hex"
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"slices"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
	"time"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/config"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/log"
)

// webFileVersion is the web.json version this build reads and writes. A
// file naming a newer one is left alone, as is one that can't be read or
// holds a value that fails validation: the web stays off, WebView.Error
// says why, a notice tells the next TUI, and nothing writes over the file.
const webFileVersion = 1

// errWebNoDir is why a change can't be kept when the model has no web.json
// path and the global config dir can't be found either.
var errWebNoDir = errors.New("web: the global config dir can't be found, so web.json has no place")

// webState is the model's web settings and devices: web.json as loaded and
// changed, and what the model keeps in memory only (the pairing codes and
// the count of wrong ones, and what the web layer reported through
// WebServing). The zero value is off, with no devices. Loop-goroutine only,
// like the rest of the model.
type webState struct {
	path           string           // <global dir>/web.json; "" on a model that never ran initWeb or when the global dir can't be found, and saveWeb then looks the dir up itself
	listen, origin string           // an empty listen is off; the origin is kept while off
	vapid          VAPIDKey         // zero until the first enable
	devices        []*webDevice     // sorted by name
	pending        []pendingPairing // oldest first, at most PairingMaxPending; memory only
	misses         int              // wrong codes since the last PairDevice, which resets it
	loadErr        string           // why web.json was left alone (unreadable, a newer version, or a value that fails validation); keeps the web off and saveWeb refusing
	rev            uint64           // ConfigureWeb's count, published as WebView.Rev; memory only, not in web.json
	serving        bool             // set by WebServing
	addr           string           // set by WebServing
	servingErr     string           // set by WebServing; WebView.Error is loadErr when set, else this
	now            func() time.Time // nil means time.Now; tests set it
}

// webDevice is a paired device. Its token is never kept, only the SHA-256
// of it.
type webDevice struct {
	name             string
	tokenHash        [32]byte
	paired, lastSeen time.Time
	notify           NotifySettings
	sub              PushSubscription // zero when none
	push             PushState
}

// pendingPairing is a code PairDevice made and nobody has redeemed.
type pendingPairing struct {
	code    string // in NormalizePairingCode's form
	name    string
	expires time.Time
}

// clock is the web state's time: w.now, or time.Now when nil.
func (w *webState) clock() time.Time {
	if w.now != nil {
		return w.now()
	}
	return time.Now()
}

// find is the device called name, nil when no device has the name.
func (w *webState) find(name string) *webDevice {
	for _, d := range w.devices {
		if d.name == name {
			return d
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// prune drops the pending codes that expired: one is good until its
// expiry, not at it.
func (w *webState) prune() {
	now := w.clock()
	w.pending = slices.DeleteFunc(w.pending, func(p pendingPairing) bool { return !now.Before(p.expires) })
}

// leftAlone is why a change is refused while web.json is left alone.
func (w *webState) leftAlone() error {
	return fmt.Errorf("web.json is left alone: %s", w.loadErr)
}

// view is the state as a client sees it: no token hash, no subscription.
func (w *webState) view() WebView {
	v := WebView{Listen: w.listen, Origin: w.origin, Serving: w.serving, Addr: w.addr, Error: w.servingErr, Rev: w.rev}
	if w.loadErr != "" {
		v.Error = w.loadErr
	}
	for _, d := range w.devices {
		v.Devices = append(v.Devices, WebDevice{Name: d.name, Paired: d.paired, LastSeen: d.lastSeen, Notify: d.notify, Push: d.push})
	}
	return v
}

// Web is the web settings and devices as a client sees them (Core.Web).
func (m *Model) Web() WebView { return m.web.view() }

// webFile is web.json, version 1.
type webFile struct {
	Version int             `json:"version"`
	Listen  string          `json:"listen"`
	Origin  string          `json:"origin"`
	VAPID   *webFileVAPID   `json:"vapid,omitempty"`
	Devices []webFileDevice `json:"devices"`
}

// webFileVAPID is the key pair, each half as unpadded base64url.
type webFileVAPID struct {
	Private string `json:"private"`
	Public  string `json:"public"`
}

// webFileDevice is one device of web.json. A pause is an RFC 3339 time, or
// empty when there is none.
type webFileDevice struct {
	Name        string        `json:"name"`
	TokenSHA256 string        `json:"token_sha256"`
	Paired      time.Time     `json:"paired"`
	LastSeen    time.Time     `json:"last_seen"`
	Notify      webFileNotify `json:"notify"`
	Push        *webFilePush  `json:"push,omitempty"`
	PushState   string        `json:"push_state,omitempty"`
}

type webFileNotify struct {
	Waiting     bool   `json:"waiting"`
	Finished    bool   `json:"finished"`
	PausedUntil string `json:"paused_until"`
}

type webFilePush struct {
	Endpoint string `json:"endpoint"`
	P256DH   string `json:"p256dh"`
	Auth     string `json:"auth"`
}

// initWeb loads web.json from the global config dir, as Boot does. A
// missing file is the web off with no devices. A file that can't be read,
// names a newer version or holds a value that fails validation is left
// exactly as it is: the web stays off, loadErr says why, and a notice
// tells the next TUI (Boot returns it).
func (m *Model) initWeb() {
	now := m.web.now
	m.web = webState{now: now}
	dir, err := config.GetGlobalConfigDir()
	if err != nil {
		log.For("web").Warn("web.no_global_dir", "err", err.Error())
		return
	}
	path := filepath.Join(dir, WebFile)
	w, err := loadWeb(path)
	if err != nil {
		log.For("web").Error("web.load_failed", "path", path, "err", err.Error())
		m.web = webState{path: path, loadErr: err.Error(), now: now}
		m.notifyErr(fmt.Errorf("web: %s; the web stays off and web.json is left as it is", err))
		return
	}
	w.now = now
	m.web = w
}

// loadWeb reads the web.json at path into a fresh state.
func loadWeb(path string) (webState, error) {
	w := webState{path: path}
	data, err := os.ReadFile(path)
	if errors.Is(err, os.ErrNotExist) {
		return w, nil
	}
	if err != nil {
		return webState{}, fmt.Errorf("read %s: %w", path, err)
	}
	var f webFile
	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &f); err != nil {
		return webState{}, fmt.Errorf("parse %s: %w", path, err)
	}
	switch {
	case f.Version > webFileVersion:
		return webState{}, fmt.Errorf("%s is version %d, newer than the version %d this build reads", path, f.Version, webFileVersion)
	case f.Version < 1:
		return webState{}, fmt.Errorf("%s names version %d, which no build wrote", path, f.Version)
	}
	if err := w.adopt(f); err != nil {
		return webState{}, fmt.Errorf("validate %s: %w", path, err)
	}
	return w, nil
}

// adopt takes the settings, key and devices of f into w, refusing a value
// this build would never have written itself.
func (w *webState) adopt(f webFile) error {
	if f.Listen != "" {
		if err := ValidateWebListen(f.Listen); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		if f.Origin == "" {
			return errors.New("listen is set but origin is empty")
		}
	}
	if f.Origin != "" {
		if err := ValidateWebOrigin(f.Origin); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	w.listen, w.origin = f.Listen, f.Origin
	if f.VAPID != nil {
		key, err := f.VAPID.key()
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		w.vapid = key
	}
	if len(f.Devices) > MaxWebDevices {
		return fmt.Errorf("%d devices; at most %d", len(f.Devices), MaxWebDevices)
	}
	tokens := map[[32]byte]bool{}
	for _, fd := range f.Devices {
		d, err := fd.device()
		if err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("device %q: %w", fd.Name, err)
		}
		if w.find(d.name) != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("device %q is listed twice", d.name)
		}
		if tokens[d.tokenHash] {
			return fmt.Errorf("device %q shares a token with another device", d.name)
		}
		tokens[d.tokenHash] = true
		w.devices = append(w.devices, d)
	}
	slices.SortFunc(w.devices, func(a, b *webDevice) int { return strings.Compare(a.name, b.name) })
	return nil
}

// key decodes the key pair and checks that the public half is the private
// one's own.
func (v webFileVAPID) key() (VAPIDKey, error) {
	priv, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(v.Private)
	if err != nil {
		return VAPIDKey{}, fmt.Errorf("vapid private key: %w", err)
	}
	pub, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(v.Public)
	if err != nil {
		return VAPIDKey{}, fmt.Errorf("vapid public key: %w", err)
	}
	k, err := ecdh.P256().NewPrivateKey(priv)
	if err != nil {
		return VAPIDKey{}, fmt.Errorf("vapid private key: %w", err)
	}
	if !bytes.Equal(k.PublicKey().Bytes(), pub) {
		return VAPIDKey{}, errors.New("vapid public key is not the private key's")
	}
	return VAPIDKey{Private: priv, Public: pub}, nil
}

// device checks one stored device and builds it.
func (fd webFileDevice) device() (*webDevice, error) {
	name, err := ValidateDeviceName(fd.Name)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	if name != fd.Name {
		return nil, errors.New("the name has spaces around it")
	}
	raw, err := hex.DecodeString(fd.TokenSHA256)
	if err != nil || len(raw) != sha256.Size {
		return nil, errors.New("token_sha256 is not 64 hex digits")
	}
	if !validPushState(fd.PushState) {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("push_state %q is not ok, expired or failing: <status>", fd.PushState)
	}
	d := &webDevice{
		name:     name,
		paired:   fd.Paired,
		lastSeen: fd.LastSeen,
		notify:   NotifySettings{Waiting: fd.Notify.Waiting, Finished: fd.Notify.Finished},
		push:     PushState(fd.PushState),
	}
	copy(d.tokenHash[:], raw)
	if fd.Notify.PausedUntil != "" {
		until, err := time.Parse(time.RFC3339, fd.Notify.PausedUntil)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, fmt.Errorf("paused_until: %w", err)
		}
		d.notify.PausedUntil = until
	}
	if fd.Push != nil {
		d.sub = PushSubscription{Endpoint: fd.Push.Endpoint, P256DH: fd.Push.P256DH, Auth: fd.Push.Auth}
	}
	return d, nil
}

// validPushState reports whether s is a state this build writes: none, ok,
// expired, or "failing: <status>".
func validPushState(s string) bool {
	if s == "" || s == string(PushOK) || s == string(PushExpired) {
		return true
	}
	status, ok := strings.CutPrefix(s, "failing: ")
	if !ok {
		return false
	}
	n, err := strconv.Atoi(status)
	return err == nil && n >= 0 && strconv.Itoa(n) == status
}

// file is the device as web.json stores it.
func (d *webDevice) file() webFileDevice {
	f := webFileDevice{
		Name:        d.name,
		TokenSHA256: hex.EncodeToString(d.tokenHash[:]),
		Paired:      d.paired.UTC(),
		LastSeen:    d.lastSeen.UTC(),
		Notify:      webFileNotify{Waiting: d.notify.Waiting, Finished: d.notify.Finished},
		PushState:   string(d.push),
	}
	if !d.notify.PausedUntil.IsZero() {
		f.Notify.PausedUntil = d.notify.PausedUntil.UTC().Format(time.RFC3339)
	}
	if d.sub != (PushSubscription{}) {
		f.Push = &webFilePush{Endpoint: d.sub.Endpoint, P256DH: d.sub.P256DH, Auth: d.sub.Auth}
	}
	return f
}

// saveWeb writes the state to web.json through config.AtomicWriteFile,
// mode 0600: it holds a token hash per device and the VAPID private key.
// It writes at the path the model loaded from, or, when it has none (a
// NewForTest model that never ran initWeb), under the global config dir,
// and fails with errWebNoDir only when neither is known. It refuses while
// the file is left alone (loadErr), so nothing overwrites what the model
// could not read. A caller changes the state first and puts it back when
// saveWeb fails.
func (m *Model) saveWeb() error {
	w := &m.web
	if w.loadErr != "" {
		return w.leftAlone()
	}
	path := w.path
	if path == "" {
		dir, err := config.GetGlobalConfigDir()
		if err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("%w: %s", errWebNoDir, err)
		}
		path = filepath.Join(dir, WebFile)
	}
	f := webFile{Version: webFileVersion, Listen: w.listen, Origin: w.origin, Devices: []webFileDevice{}}
	if len(w.vapid.Private) > 0 {
		f.VAPID = &webFileVAPID{
			Private: base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(w.vapid.Private),
			Public:  base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(w.vapid.Public),
		}
	}
	for _, d := range w.devices {
		f.Devices = append(f.Devices, d.file())
	}
	data, err := json.MarshalIndent(f, "", "  ")
	if err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("marshal %s: %w", path, err)
	}
	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(path), 0o755); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("create %s: %w", filepath.Dir(path), err)
	}
	if err := config.AtomicWriteFile(path, append(data, '\n'), 0o600); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("write %s: %w", path, err)
	}
	return nil
}

// newVAPIDKey makes the P-256 key pair the first enable keeps for good:
// push subscriptions are bound to its public half.
func newVAPIDKey() (VAPIDKey, error) {
	k, err := ecdh.P256().GenerateKey(rand.Reader)
	if err != nil {
		return VAPIDKey{}, fmt.Errorf("make the VAPID key: %w", err)
	}
	return VAPIDKey{Private: k.Bytes(), Public: k.PublicKey().Bytes()}, nil
}

// newPairingCode makes a code of PairingCodeLen characters of
// PairingAlphabet from crypto/rand. A byte at or above the largest
// multiple of the alphabet's length is skipped, so no symbol is likelier.
func newPairingCode() (string, error) {
	const limit = 256 - 256%len(PairingAlphabet)
	code := make([]byte, 0, PairingCodeLen)
	buf := make([]byte, 2*PairingCodeLen)
	for len(code) < PairingCodeLen {
		if _, err := rand.Read(buf); err != nil {
			return "", fmt.Errorf("make a pairing code: %w", err)
		}
		for _, b := range buf {
			if int(b) < limit && len(code) < PairingCodeLen {
				code = append(code, PairingAlphabet[int(b)%len(PairingAlphabet)])
			}
		}
	}
	return string(code), nil
}
```

```go
// webT0 is the instant an enabledWeb model's clock stands at.
var webT0 = time.Date(2026, 10, 21, 12, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)

// enabledWeb is a model loaded from an enabled web.json holding devices
// (webDeviceJSON), its clock fixed at webT0.
func enabledWeb(t *testing.T, devices ...string) *Model {
	t.Helper()
	m := webModel(t, webWith(devices...))
	require.Empty(t, m.web.loadErr)
	m.web.now = func() time.Time { return webT0 }
	return m
}

// blockWebPath moves m's web.json to a place that can't be written: below a
// regular file.
func blockWebPath(t *testing.T, m *Model) {
	t.Helper()
	blocker := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "file")
	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(blocker, nil, 0o600))
	m.web.path = filepath.Join(blocker, WebFile)
}

// pendingNames is the names the waiting codes are for, oldest first.
func pendingNames(m *Model) []string {
	var names []string
	for _, p := range m.web.pending {
		names = append(names, p.name)
	}
	return names
}

func TestConfigureWeb_EnablesWritesAndMakesTheKeyOnce(t *testing.T) {
	m := webModel(t, "")

	require.NoError(t, m.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))

	v := m.Web()
	assert.Equal(t, "127.0.0.1:7420", v.Listen)
	assert.Equal(t, "https://loom.example.com", v.Origin)
	assert.Empty(t, v.Devices)
	assert.EqualValues(t, 1, v.Rev, "the layer rebinds on a new revision")
	v.Rev = 0 // memory only: a boot starts the count afresh
	info, err := os.Stat(m.web.path)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, os.FileMode(0o600), info.Mode().Perm())
	key := m.web.vapid
	require.Len(t, key.Private, 32, "the first enable makes the key")
	require.Len(t, key.Public, 65)
	m2 := NewForTest(Options{})
	m2.initWeb()
	require.Empty(t, m2.web.loadErr)
	assert.Equal(t, v, m2.Web(), "on disk")
	assert.Equal(t, key, m2.web.vapid, "the key too")

	require.NoError(t, m.ConfigureWeb("[::1]:7421", "https://loom.example.com"))
	assert.Equal(t, key, m.web.vapid, "a second enable keeps the key: push subscriptions are bound to it")
	require.NoError(t, m.ConfigureWeb("", ""))
	require.NoError(t, m.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))
	assert.Equal(t, key, m.web.vapid, "so does an enable after a disable")
}

func TestConfigureWeb_OffKeepsTheOriginAndTheDevices(t *testing.T) {
	m := webModel(t, webJSON)

	require.NoError(t, m.ConfigureWeb("", "https://other.example.com"))

	v := m.Web()
	assert.Empty(t, v.Listen, "off")
	assert.Equal(t, "https://loom.example.com", v.Origin, "the origin is kept; the argument is not read")
	assert.Len(t, v.Devices, 2, "and the devices")
	assert.EqualValues(t, 1, v.Rev, "turning off bumps the revision too: the layer closes its listener on it")
	v.Rev = 0 // memory only: a boot starts the count afresh
	assert.Zero(t, m.web.vapid, "turning off makes no key")
	m2 := NewForTest(Options{})
	m2.initWeb()
	assert.Equal(t, v, m2.Web(), "on disk too")
}

func TestConfigureWeb_DisablingWhatWasNeverEnabledWritesNothing(t *testing.T) {
	m := webModel(t, "")

	require.NoError(t, m.ConfigureWeb("", ""))

	assert.NoFileExists(t, m.web.path)
}

func TestConfigureWeb_RefusesWhatItValidates(t *testing.T) {
	for _, tc := range []struct{ name, listen, origin, why string }{
		{"a host name", "localhost:7420", "https://loom.example.com", "loopback"},
		{"all interfaces", "0.0.0.0:7420", "https://loom.example.com", "loopback"},
		{"no port", "127.0.0.1", "https://loom.example.com", "listen"},
		{"no origin", "127.0.0.1:7420", "", "origin"},
		{"a plain http origin", "127.0.0.1:7420", "http://loom.example.com", "origin"},
		{"an origin with a path", "127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com/app", "origin"},
	} {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			m := webModel(t, webJSON)
			m.web.rev = 3
			m.web.serving, m.web.addr = true, "127.0.0.1:7420"
			before := m.Web()

			err := m.ConfigureWeb(tc.listen, tc.origin)

			assert.ErrorContains(t, err, tc.why)
			assert.Equal(t, before, m.Web(), "nothing changed: the revision (3) and the layer's report too")
			assert.Equal(t, webJSON, string(webFileBytes(t, m)), "nothing was written")
		})
	}
}

// TestConfigureWeb_EverySuccessBumpsTheRevisionAndClearsWhatTheLayerReported:
// the layer rebinds when WebView.Rev changes, so every ConfigureWeb that
// succeeds bumps it, whatever it changed (even the stored values, which is how
// running loom web enable again retries a failed bind), and clears what the
// layer reported, so the next enable never reads a stale error.
func TestConfigureWeb_EverySuccessBumpsTheRevisionAndClearsWhatTheLayerReported(t *testing.T) {
	for _, tc := range []struct{ name, listen, origin string }{
		{"the stored settings", "127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"},
		{"another port", "127.0.0.1:7421", "https://loom.example.com"},
		{"another origin", "127.0.0.1:7420", "https://other.example.com"},
		{"off", "", ""},
	} {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			m := webModel(t, webJSON)
			m.web.rev = 4
			m.web.serving, m.web.addr, m.web.servingErr = true, "127.0.0.1:7420", "stale"

			require.NoError(t, m.ConfigureWeb(tc.listen, tc.origin))

			assert.EqualValues(t, 5, m.Web().Rev, "one more than before")
			assert.False(t, m.web.serving)
			assert.Empty(t, m.web.addr)
			assert.Empty(t, m.web.servingErr, "so loom web enable never reads a stale error")
			assert.Empty(t, m.Web().Error)
			assert.False(t, m.Web().Serving)
		})
	}
}

// TestConfigureWeb_ADisableThenAnEnableLeavesTheRevisionChanged: a Sync
// coalesces the two ModelChanged events into the newest, whose Listen and
// Origin equal the first view's, so only Rev tells the layer to bind again.
func TestConfigureWeb_ADisableThenAnEnableLeavesTheRevisionChanged(t *testing.T) {
	m := webModel(t, webJSON)
	before := m.Web()

	require.NoError(t, m.ConfigureWeb("", ""))
	require.NoError(t, m.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))

	after := m.Web()
	assert.Equal(t, before.Listen, after.Listen)
	assert.Equal(t, before.Origin, after.Origin)
	assert.EqualValues(t, before.Rev+2, after.Rev, "one per call")
}

// the key already made, leave web.json alone, and still bump the revision.
func TestConfigureWeb_TheSameSettingsWriteNothing(t *testing.T) {

	require.NoError(t, m.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))
	require.NoError(t, os.Remove(m.web.path), "fixture: the file is gone, so a write would bring it back")
	rev := m.Web().Rev

	require.NoError(t, m.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))

	assert.NoFileExists(t, m.web.path, "nothing to write")

}
```

In `core/web.go`, add `slices` to the imports. Replace:


and append at the end of the file:

```go
// ConfigureWeb sets where the web listens and the origin the phone reaches
// it at, and writes web.json (Core.ConfigureWeb). Both are validated. An
// empty listen turns the web off and keeps the origin (its argument is not
// read), the devices and the key. The first enable makes the VAPID key,
// which every later enable keeps: push subscriptions are bound to it. Every
// call that succeeds, whatever it changed, bumps the revision (WebView.Rev)
// and clears what the web layer reported (WebServing): the layer rebinds
// whenever the revision changes, so running enable again retries a bind that
// failed, and a disable and an enable that one Sync coalesces still differ.
// Settings already in force, with the key made, write nothing. It is refused
// while web.json is left alone, and when the write fails (errWebNoDir when
// the model has no path and the global dir is unknown) nothing has changed,
// the revision included.
func (m *Model) ConfigureWeb(listen, origin string) error {
	w := &m.web
	if w.loadErr != "" {
		return w.leftAlone()
	}
	if listen == "" {
		origin = w.origin
	} else {
		if err := ValidateWebListen(listen); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		if err := ValidateWebOrigin(origin); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	needKey := listen != "" && len(w.vapid.Private) == 0
	needWrite := listen != w.listen || origin != w.origin || needKey
	prev := *w
	w.rev++
	w.serving, w.addr, w.servingErr = false, "", ""
	w.listen, w.origin = listen, origin
	if needKey {
		key, err := newVAPIDKey()
		if err != nil {
			*w = prev
			return err
		}
		w.vapid = key
	}
	if needWrite {
		if err := m.saveWeb(); err != nil {
			*w = prev
			return err
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// PairDevice makes the one-time code that pairs a device under name
// (Core.PairDevice): PairingCodeLen characters of PairingAlphabet, good for
// PairingTTL. The code lives in memory only, so a daemon restart voids it,
// and is not in the view. A second code for the same name replaces the
// first, and at most PairingMaxPending wait: one more voids the oldest.
// Starting a pairing also resets the count of wrong codes, which locks
// redeeming at PairingMaxMisses. The web must be on (ErrWebOff, which is
// also what a web.json left alone answers), the name valid and not a
// paired device's (ErrDeviceExists), and fewer than MaxWebDevices paired
// (ErrTooManyDevices): the codes waiting are not devices and don't count,
// and redeeming a code checks the limit again.
func (m *Model) PairDevice(name string) (Pairing, error) {
	w := &m.web
	if w.loadErr != "" || w.listen == "" {
		return Pairing{}, ErrWebOff
	}
	name, err := ValidateDeviceName(name)
	if err != nil {
		return Pairing{}, err
	}
	if w.find(name) != nil {
		return Pairing{}, fmt.Errorf("%w: %q", ErrDeviceExists, name)
	}
	if len(w.devices) >= MaxWebDevices {
		return Pairing{}, ErrTooManyDevices
	}
	code, err := newPairingCode()
	if err != nil {
		return Pairing{}, err
	}
	w.prune()
	p := pendingPairing{code: code, name: name, expires: w.clock().Add(PairingTTL)}
	w.pending = slices.DeleteFunc(w.pending, func(q pendingPairing) bool { return q.name == name })
	w.pending = append(w.pending, p)
	if n := len(w.pending) - PairingMaxPending; n > 0 {
		w.pending = slices.Delete(w.pending, 0, n)
	}
	w.misses = 0
	return Pairing{Code: p.code, Expires: p.expires}, nil
}

// RevokeDevice unpairs the device called name (Core.RevokeDevice): its
// record, token hash and push subscription go from web.json, and its
// cookie stops working. The next publish tells the web layer, which ends
// the device's streams. It works while the web is off. ErrNoDevice for a
// name no device has; refused while web.json is left alone; when the write
// fails the device stays paired.
func (m *Model) RevokeDevice(name string) error {
	w := &m.web
	if w.loadErr != "" {
		return w.leftAlone()
	}
	i := slices.IndexFunc(w.devices, func(d *webDevice) bool { return d.name == name })
	if i < 0 {
		return fmt.Errorf("%w: %q", ErrNoDevice, name)
	}
	prev := w.devices
	w.devices = slices.Delete(slices.Clone(prev), i, i+1)
	if err := m.saveWeb(); err != nil {
		w.devices = prev
		return err
	}
	return nil
}
```

In `core/state_views_test.go`, insert a new test after `TestPublishState_CarriesTheState` (after the line `}` that closes it, the one following `assert.Equal(t, map[string]string{"/r": "boom"}, ev.View.Errs)`), before the `// TestSnapshot_IsTheWholeStateAndLeavesTheDiffAlone` comment:

```go
// TestPublishState_WebChangesReachClients: the web settings and devices
// travel in the model view, so a revoke, an enable and a disable are
// published, and a pairing code, which lives in memory only, is not.
func TestPublishState_WebChangesReachClients(t *testing.T) {
	m := webModel(t, webJSON)
	model, _, _ := stateEvents(m.Sync().Events)
	assert.Equal(t, 1, model, "the first Sync publishes it")
	model, _, _ = stateEvents(m.Sync().Events)
	assert.Zero(t, model, "and then only what changes")

	_, err := m.PairDevice("Pixel")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	model, _, _ = stateEvents(m.Sync().Events)
	assert.Zero(t, model, "a pairing code is in no view")

	require.NoError(t, m.RevokeDevice("iPad"))
	events := m.Sync().Events
	model, _, _ = stateEvents(events)
	assert.Equal(t, 1, model, "a revoke is published")
	for _, ev := range events {
		if mc, ok := ev.(ModelChanged); ok {
			require.Len(t, mc.View.Web.Devices, 1)
			assert.Equal(t, "iPhone", mc.View.Web.Devices[0].Name)
		}
	}

	require.NoError(t, m.ConfigureWeb("", ""))
	model, _, _ = stateEvents(m.Sync().Events)
	assert.Equal(t, 1, model, "so is turning the web off")
	var snap WebView
	for _, ev := range m.Snapshot() {
		if mc, ok := ev.(ModelChanged); ok {
			snap = mc.View.Web
		}
	}
	assert.Equal(t, m.Web(), snap, "and the snapshot a client joins with carries it")
	assert.Empty(t, snap.Listen)

	rev := m.Web().Rev
	require.NoError(t, m.ConfigureWeb("", ""))
	events = m.Sync().Events
	model, _, _ = stateEvents(events)
	assert.Equal(t, 1, model, "a configure that changes no setting is published too: its revision is what makes the layer act")
	for _, ev := range events {
		if mc, ok := ev.(ModelChanged); ok {
			assert.Equal(t, rev+1, mc.View.Web.Rev)
		}
	}
}

```

Append to `core/web_store_test.go`:

```go
// Boot loads web.json after the account registry, and the web is on from
// the moment it boots.
func TestBoot_LoadsTheWebFile(t *testing.T) {
	m := bootModel(t)
	global, err := config.GetGlobalConfigDir()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(global, WebFile), []byte(webJSON), 0o600))

	notices := m.Boot()

	assert.Empty(t, notices)
	assert.Equal(t, "127.0.0.1:7420", m.Web().Listen)
	assert.Len(t, m.Web().Devices, 2)
}

// A web.json Boot cannot use is left as it is, and Boot hands back the
// notice since no client is connected yet to be sent it; it leaves none
// behind for the first Sync.
func TestBoot_ReturnsTheNoticeOfAWebFileItLeavesAlone(t *testing.T) {
	m := bootModel(t)
	global, err := config.GetGlobalConfigDir()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	path := filepath.Join(global, WebFile)
	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(path, []byte(`{"version":2}`), 0o600))

	notices := m.Boot()

	require.Len(t, notices, 1)
	n, ok := notices[0].(Notice)
	require.True(t, ok)
	assert.Contains(t, n.Err.Error(), "web:")
	assert.Contains(t, n.Err.Error(), "newer")
	assert.Contains(t, m.Web().Error, "newer", "and the view says why the web is off")
	data, err := os.ReadFile(path)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, `{"version":2}`, string(data), "the file is untouched")
	for _, ev := range m.Sync().Events {
		assert.NotEqual(t, notices[0], ev, "nothing left for the first Sync")
	}
}
```

```go
// ModelView is the model's own state: the default account's remote-control
// auth and the workspace registry (published as ModelChanged).
type ModelView struct {

	Registry RegistryView

// Clone deep-copies v's slices; every other field is a value already.
func (v ModelView) Clone() ModelView {
```

```go
	Web      WebView
```

add:

```go

// TestWebRequests_ReachTheModelOverTheWire: ConfigureWeb, PairDevice and
// RevokeDevice are requests, so their effect is in the replica when they
// return, and an error crosses as its message.
func TestWebRequests_ReachTheModelOverTheWire(t *testing.T) {
	loop := core.StartForTest(webModel(t))
	c := pair(t, loop)

	require.NoError(t, c.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7421", "https://loom.example.com"))
	assert.Equal(t, "127.0.0.1:7421", c.Web().Listen, "the request's ModelChanged reached the replica before its reply")
	assert.EqualValues(t, 1, c.Web().Rev, "and the revision with it: the fixture's model had seen no ConfigureWeb")
	code, err := c.PairDevice("Pixel")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Len(t, code.Code, core.PairingCodeLen)
	assert.WithinDuration(t, time.Now().Add(core.PairingTTL), code.Expires, time.Minute)
	_, err = c.PairDevice("iPhone")
	assert.ErrorContains(t, err, core.ErrDeviceExists.Error())
	require.NoError(t, c.RevokeDevice("iPhone"))
	require.Len(t, c.Web().Devices, 1, "the revoke is in the replica when it returns")
	assert.Equal(t, "iPad", c.Web().Devices[0].Name)
	assert.ErrorContains(t, c.RevokeDevice("iPhone"), core.ErrNoDevice.Error())
	assert.ErrorContains(t, c.ConfigureWeb("localhost:7420", "https://loom.example.com"), "loopback")
	assert.Equal(t, "127.0.0.1:7421", c.Web().Listen, "a refused request changes nothing")
	assert.EqualValues(t, 1, c.Web().Rev, "the revision included; a pairing and a revoke never bumped it")
}
```

Modify `core/iface.go:122-123`. The current lines:

```go
	SetDefaultAccount(name string) error
}
```

become:

```go
	SetDefaultAccount(name string) error

	// The web layer (internal/web): its settings and the devices paired to
	// it, and the requests that change them. Web answers from the replica
	// (the view travels in ModelView, so no event of its own); a pairing
	// code, the one secret a request returns, lives in the model's memory
	// only.
	Web() WebView // rpc:local
	ConfigureWeb(listen, origin string) error
	PairDevice(name string) (Pairing, error)
	RevokeDevice(name string) error
}
```

Modify `core/loop_core.go:162-164`. The current lines:

```go
func (l *Loop) SetDefaultAccount(name string) error {
	return get(l, func(m *Model) error { return m.SetDefaultAccount(name) })
}
```

become:

```go
func (l *Loop) SetDefaultAccount(name string) error {
	return get(l, func(m *Model) error { return m.SetDefaultAccount(name) })
}

// Web runs Model.Web on the loop.
func (l *Loop) Web() WebView { return get(l, (*Model).Web) }

// ConfigureWeb runs Model.ConfigureWeb on the loop.
func (l *Loop) ConfigureWeb(listen, origin string) error {
	return get(l, func(m *Model) error { return m.ConfigureWeb(listen, origin) })
}

// PairDevice runs Model.PairDevice on the loop.
func (l *Loop) PairDevice(name string) (Pairing, error) {
	return get2(l, func(m *Model) (Pairing, error) { return m.PairDevice(name) })
}

// RevokeDevice runs Model.RevokeDevice on the loop.
func (l *Loop) RevokeDevice(name string) error {
	return get(l, func(m *Model) error { return m.RevokeDevice(name) })
}
```

Regenerate the wire and its reference. Never edit either file by hand:

```bash
CGO_ENABLED=0 go generate ./core/rpc
CGO_ENABLED=0 go test ./core/rpc -run TestProtocolReference -update
git diff --stat core/rpc/methods_gen.go docs/specs/protocol.md
```

`methods_gen.go` gains `WebParams`/`WebResult`, `ConfigureWebParams` (`Listen`, `Origin`)/`ConfigureWebResult`, `PairDeviceParams`/`PairDeviceResult` (`Value core.Pairing`) and `RevokeDeviceParams`/`RevokeDeviceResult`, four rows in the method table (`Web` as `kindLocal`, the others `kindRequest`), four cases in `dispatch`, and four `Client` methods, of which `Web` is `c.local(func(r *replica) { v0 = r.Web() })`. `protocol.md` gains, after `### SetDefaultAccount (request)` and before `## Events`:

```
### Web (local)

Params: `{}`

Result: `{"value":{"Listen":"","Origin":"","Serving":false,"Addr":"","Error":"","Devices":null,"Rev":0}}`

### ConfigureWeb (request)

Params: `{"listen":"","origin":""}`

Result: `{}`

### PairDevice (request)

Params: `{"name":""}`

Result: `{"value":{"Code":"","Expires":"0001-01-01T00:00:00Z"}}`

### RevokeDevice (request)

Params: `{"name":""}`

Result: `{}`
```

Adding methods and a view field is compatible (an older server answers `unsupported`, an older client ignores `Web`), so `rpc.Protocol` stays.
