## Story 4: The web layer's host on the loop, and `OriginWeb` for `Send`

- **Developer:** intermediate-dev
- **Files:** `core/web_host.go`, `core/loop_web.go`, `core/web_host_test.go`, `core/mod.go`, `core/mod_test.go`
- **Interfaces:** exposes `core.WebHost`, `core.NotifyChange`, `core.ValidatePushSubscription` and `core.OriginWeb`; uses the core web state names (Story 3)
- **Verify:** `CGO_ENABLED=0 go test ./core/...`
- **Review:** deep

`core.WebHost` gives the web layer what it needs without the wire: redeeming a pairing code, finding a device by token hash, touching it, its notification settings and push subscription, the push targets and results, the VAPID key, and the listener's state. The model methods live in `core/web_host.go` and work on Story 3's `Model.web`; `*Loop` runs each on the loop in `core/loop_web.go` and wakes the client after a change that reaches `ModelView.Web`. `OriginWeb` lets a reply from the phone go through the mod as the user's own words. Choices the spec leaves open, fixed here and pinned by tests: an `OriginWeb` refusal (an empty message, a program other than Claude, no live mod, the mods' stop) reads in words of its own that name no `loom` command and no terminal to attach to, since the phone shows the model's text as it is, while the CLI's refusals keep their text; the stored token hash is the SHA-256 of the 32 raw bytes, so the web layer must base64url-decode the cookie and hash those bytes for `DeviceByToken` (hashing the cookie's text finds no device); a new device's notifications start with `Waiting` and `Finished` on; a code that matches is consumed before the device-name and device-count checks, so a refusal there costs the code; and `ValidatePushSubscription` is stricter than the spec's "Limits" in two ways, it refuses an endpoint with a user in it and one with a port other than 443. The story's code holds none of the profile's landmine markers.

### Task 4.1: `OriginWeb` for `Send`

**Files:**
- Modify: `core/mod.go:61-70`, `core/mod.go:83-90`, `core/mod.go:98-122`, `core/mod.go:346-355`, `core/mod.go:391` (insert before), `core/mod.go:486-498`
- Test: `core/mod_test.go` (append)

A web message is the person's own words (`asUser`, no "From" line, as `OriginScript`'s) but is refused wherever a CLI message is: empty, for a program other than Claude, without a live mod, and when the daemon's mods stop; it is never pasted. `Origin.needsMod` names the CLI-and-web group so the refusals read one term. The phone shows the model's text as it is, so each of the web's four refusals has words of its own that name no `loom` command and no terminal to attach to (`Origin.emptyReason`, `webNoModReason`, and `withoutMod`'s web case); the CLI's text, `noModReason` and the tests that pin them stay as they are.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Append at the end of `core/mod_test.go`. The file's imports already hold everything these use.

```go
// OriginWeb is the phone's reply: the person's own words with no "From" line,
// submitted through the mod as a script's message is.
func TestSend_AWebMessageIsThePersonsOwnWordsWithNoFromLine(t *testing.T) {
	withMod(t)
	m := NewForTest(Options{})
	inst := modInst(t, m, "x")

	m.Send(m.idOf(inst), Message{Text: "go on", From: "ignored", Origin: OriginWeb}, 5)

	assert.Empty(t, replies(m.Drain()), "the Reply comes once the mod has queued it")
	msgs, _, err := m.ModInbox("L1", "")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Len(t, msgs, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, ModMessage{ID: msgs[0].ID, Text: "go on", AsUser: true}, msgs[0])
}

// refusalOf sends text from origin to inst and returns the refusal that
// answers it, checking that nothing is pasted in its place.
func refusalOf(t *testing.T, m *Model, inst *session.Instance, origin Origin, text string) error {
	t.Helper()
	m.Send(m.idOf(inst), Message{Text: text, Origin: origin}, 5)
	out := m.Drain()
	rs := replies(out)
	require.Len(t, rs, 1, "%s message %q", origin, text)
	require.ErrorIs(t, rs[0].Err, ErrRefused)
	assert.Empty(t, out.Jobs, "a %s message is never pasted", origin)
	return rs[0].Err
}

// webMessage is the web app's message, as cliMessage is the CLI's.
func webMessage(req ReqID, text string) *modMessage {
	return &modMessage{text: text, asUser: true, origin: OriginWeb, req: req}
}

// assertWebWords checks that err is a refusal worded for the phone, which shows
// the model's text as it is: exactly want, naming no `loom` command and no
// terminal to attach to.
func assertWebWords(t *testing.T, err error, want string) {
	t.Helper()
	assert.EqualError(t, err, want)
	assert.NotContains(t, err.Error(), "loom session", "the phone has no loom command to run")
	assert.NotContains(t, err.Error(), "attach", "the phone has no terminal to attach to")
}

// A web message is refused wherever a CLI message is, and unlike a script's it
// is never pasted into the pane instead. Each refusal reads in the web's own
// words, and the CLI's refusal beside it keeps its own.
func TestSend_AWebMessageIsRefusedWhereTheCLIsIsInItsOwnWords(t *testing.T) {
	t.Run("an empty message", func(t *testing.T) {
		withMod(t)
		m := NewForTest(Options{})
		inst := modInst(t, m, "x")
		for _, text := range []string{"", " \n\t"} {
			assertWebWords(t, refusalOf(t, m, inst, OriginWeb, text), "send to x: there is nothing to send")
			assert.EqualError(t, refusalOf(t, m, inst, OriginCLI, text), "send to x: the message is empty")
		}
		assert.Empty(t, m.mods, "nothing reaches the mod")
	})
	t.Run("another program than Claude", func(t *testing.T) {
		withMod(t)
		m := NewForTest(Options{})
		inst := startedInst(t, "x", "aider --model sonnet")
		hold(m, inst)

		assertWebWords(t, refusalOf(t, m, inst, OriginWeb, "hi"),
			"send to x: it runs aider, not Claude: only a Claude session takes a reply from the app")
		assert.EqualError(t, refusalOf(t, m, inst, OriginCLI, "hi"),
			"send to x: it runs aider, not Claude: loom session send reaches only Claude")
	})
	t.Run("a launch with no live mod", func(t *testing.T) {
		m := NewForTest(Options{}) // no withMod: the daemon serves none
		inst := activeInst(t, m, "x")

		assertWebWords(t, refusalOf(t, m, inst, OriginWeb, "hi"),
			"send to x: this loom daemon serves no loom mod, so it cannot deliver a reply")
		assert.EqualError(t, refusalOf(t, m, inst, OriginCLI, "hi"),
			"send to x: this loom daemon serves no loom mod (on Windows, or where its socket path would be too long): attach and type instead")
	})
}

// A web message to a launch with no mod is refused saying why, as the CLI's is
// (TestMod_ARefusalSaysWhyTheLaunchHasNoMod, which pins the CLI's words), in
// words for the phone: the same five causes, no `loom` command, no terminal to
// attach to. A session that predates the daemon is told to pause and resume
// it, which the app can do.
func TestMod_AWebRefusalSaysWhyTheLaunchHasNoMod(t *testing.T) {
	refusal := func(t *testing.T, m *Model, inst *session.Instance, now time.Time) error {
		t.Helper()
		m.enqueue(inst, webMessage(5, "hi"))
		m.modTick(now)
		rs := replies(m.Drain())
		require.Len(t, rs, 1)
		return rs[0].Err
	}

	t.Run("another program, where the daemon serves no mod", func(t *testing.T) {
		m := NewForTest(Options{})
		inst := startedInst(t, "x", "aider")
		hold(m, inst)
		assertWebWords(t, refusal(t, m, inst, time.Now()),
			"send to x: it runs aider, not Claude: only a Claude session takes a reply from the app")
	})
	t.Run("the daemon serves no mod", func(t *testing.T) {
		m := NewForTest(Options{})
		inst := activeInst(t, m, "x")
		assertWebWords(t, refusal(t, m, inst, time.Now()),
			"send to x: this loom daemon serves no loom mod, so it cannot deliver a reply")
	})
	t.Run("launched before this daemon", func(t *testing.T) {
		withMod(t)
		m := NewForTest(Options{})
		inst := activeInst(t, m, "x")
		adoptLaunch(t, m, inst, "L1", time.Now().Add(-time.Hour))
		require.Zero(t, inst.Launches(), "fixture: restored, not launched by this daemon")
		assertWebWords(t, refusal(t, m, inst, time.Now()),
			"send to x: it was launched before this daemon started and its loom mod has not said hello: pause and resume it to relaunch it with the mod")
	})
	t.Run("a launch without hooks", func(t *testing.T) {
		withMod(t)
		m := NewForTest(Options{})
		inst := relaunchableInstIn(t, m, "x", "")
		require.NoError(t, inst.Restart())
		_, hooked := inst.HookLaunch()
		require.False(t, hooked, "fixture: no config dir, no hooks")
		assertWebWords(t, refusal(t, m, inst, time.Now()),
			"send to x: it runs without loom's hooks, so it has no loom mod")
	})
	t.Run("a launch whose Claude never said hello", func(t *testing.T) {
		withMod(t)
		m := NewForTest(Options{})
		inst := relaunchableInst(t, m, "x")
		require.NoError(t, inst.Restart())
		_, hooked := inst.HookLaunch()
		require.True(t, hooked, "fixture: launched by this daemon, with the mod")
		assertWebWords(t, refusal(t, m, inst, time.Now().Add(modStartCap+time.Second)),
			"send to x: its Claude runs without the loom mod (an older Claude, or the mod did not load)")
	})
}

// A web message a silent mod never took is refused in the mod's own words, as
// the CLI's is: only a launch with no mod and the daemon's stop have words of
// the web's own, so any other reason reaches the phone as the model gives it.
func TestMod_AWebMessageASilentModNeverTookIsRefusedAsTheCLIsIs(t *testing.T) {
	withMod(t)
	m := NewForTest(Options{})
	inst := modInst(t, m, "x")
	require.NoError(t, m.ModHello("L1", ModHello{Mod: 1}))
	m.mods[inst].heard = time.Now().Add(-time.Hour) // greeted long ago; only its polls keep it live
	m.enqueue(inst, webMessage(5, "never taken"))
	m.Drain()

	m.modTick(time.Now())

	out := m.Drain()
	rs := replies(out)
	require.Len(t, rs, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, ReqID(5), rs[0].Req)
	assertWebWords(t, rs[0].Err, "send to x: the loom mod stopped answering")
	assert.Empty(t, out.Jobs, "a web message is never pasted")
}

// A web message waiting for a mod that never took it is refused when the
// daemon stops, as a CLI message is, and not pasted: in the web's own words,
// beside the CLI's "the loom daemon stopped".
func TestModsStop_RefusesAWebMessageNoModTook(t *testing.T) {
	withMod(t)
	m := NewForTest(Options{})
	inst := modInst(t, m, "x")
	m.Send(m.idOf(inst), Message{Text: "from the phone", Origin: OriginWeb}, 5)
	m.Send(m.idOf(inst), Message{Text: "from the cli", Origin: OriginCLI}, 6)
	m.Drain()

	m.ModsStop()

	out := m.Drain()
	refusals := map[ReqID]error{}
	for _, r := range replies(out) {
		refusals[r.Req] = r.Err
	}
	require.Len(t, refusals, 2, "each is refused once")
	assertWebWords(t, refusals[5], "send to x: the loom daemon is stopping, so the message was not sent")
	assert.EqualError(t, refusals[6], "send to x: the loom daemon stopped")
	assert.Empty(t, out.Jobs, "neither message is pasted")
}
```

```go
// noModReasonFor is noModReason for a message of origin o: a web message gets
// webNoModReason's words, the others the CLI's.
func noModReasonFor(o Origin, inst *session.Instance) string {
	if o == OriginWeb {
		return webNoModReason(inst)
	}
	return noModReason(inst)
}

// webNoModReason is noModReason's reason in words for the phone, which shows
// the model's text as it is: the same causes in the same order, but naming no
// `loom` command and no terminal to attach to. A session that predates the
// daemon is told to pause and resume it, which the app can do.
func webNoModReason(inst *session.Instance) string {
	launch, hooked := inst.HookLaunch()
	switch {
	case !session.IsClaudeProgram(inst.Program()):
		return fmt.Sprintf("it runs %s, not Claude: only a Claude session takes a reply from the app", programName(inst.Program()))
	case session.ModSocket() == "":
		return "this loom daemon serves no loom mod, so it cannot deliver a reply"
	case inst.Launches() == 0:
		return "it was launched before this daemon started and its loom mod has not said hello: pause and resume it to relaunch it with the mod"
	case launch != "" && !hooked:
		return "it runs without loom's hooks, so it has no loom mod"
	default:
		return "its Claude runs without the loom mod (an older Claude, or the mod did not load)"
	}
}

// modsStoppedWhy is the reason ModsStop gives modGone: the daemon is stopping.
// withoutMod words it again for a web message.
const modsStoppedWhy = "the loom daemon stopped"

```


### Task 4.2: The `WebHost` interface and push subscription validation

**Files:**
- Create: `core/web_host.go`
- Create: `core/web_host_test.go`

`core.WebHost`, `NotifyChange` and `ValidatePushSubscription` are the pieces with no state behind them. The daemon posts to whatever endpoint a stored subscription names, so the validator is the only thing between a paired phone and a request to an arbitrary host: it allows the three push services' hosts and Windows' `.notify.windows.com`, `https` only, no user, no port but 443, at most 2048 bytes, and keys that decode to exactly a P-256 point and 16 bytes in their canonical spelling.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `core/web_host_test.go`:

```go
package core

import (
	"crypto/ecdh"
	"crypto/rand"
	"encoding/base64"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
)

// webHostSub is a push subscription the daemon accepts, for the push service
// at host: a real P-256 point and 16 bytes of auth secret.
func webHostSub(t *testing.T, host string) PushSubscription {
	t.Helper()
	key, err := ecdh.P256().GenerateKey(rand.Reader)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	auth := make([]byte, 16)
	_, err = rand.Read(auth)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	return PushSubscription{
		Endpoint: "https://" + host + "/push/abc123",
		P256DH:   base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(key.PublicKey().Bytes()),
		Auth:     base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(auth),
	}
}

// The daemon posts to the endpoint a paired phone supplies, so only the push
// services' hosts are taken, over https, on their port, with no user.
func TestWebHost_ValidatePushSubscription_Endpoints(t *testing.T) {
	apple := "https://web.push.apple.com/"
	exactly2KiB := apple + strings.Repeat("a", webPushEndpointMax-len(apple))
	cases := []struct {
		name     string
		endpoint string
		want     string // "" accepts the endpoint; else part of the error
	}{
		{"Apple", "https://web.push.apple.com/wpush/v2/abc", ""},
		{"Google", "https://fcm.googleapis.com/fcm/send/abc", ""},
		{"Mozilla", "https://updates.push.services.mozilla.com/wpush/v2/abc", ""},
		{"Windows", "https://db5.notify.windows.com/?token=abc", ""},
		{"an explicit port 443", "https://web.push.apple.com:443/abc", ""},
		{"a host in capitals", "https://WEB.PUSH.APPLE.COM/abc", ""},
		{"exactly 2 KiB", exactly2KiB, ""},
		{"one byte over 2 KiB", exactly2KiB + "a", "longer than"},
		{"http", "http://web.push.apple.com/abc", "not https"},
		{"no scheme", "web.push.apple.com/abc", "not https"},
		{"empty", "", "not https"},
		{"a control character", "https://web.push.apple.com/a\x7fb", "no URL"},
		{"another host", "https://push.example.com/abc", "no push service"},
		{"a host that only ends like Apple's", "https://evil-web.push.apple.com/abc", "no push service"},
		{"Apple's host as a prefix", "https://web.push.apple.com.evil.example/abc", "no push service"},
		{"the bare Windows domain", "https://notify.windows.com/abc", "no push service"},
		{"a look-alike Windows domain", "https://evilnotify.windows.com/abc", "no push service"},
		{"a loopback address", "https://127.0.0.1/abc", "no push service"},
		{"a user naming the real host", "https://web.push.apple.com@evil.example/abc", "names a user"},
		{"a user before the real host", "https://evil.example@web.push.apple.com/abc", "names a user"},
		{"another port", "https://web.push.apple.com:8443/abc", "port other than 443"},
	}
	good := webHostSub(t, "web.push.apple.com")
	for _, tc := range cases {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			sub := good
			sub.Endpoint = tc.endpoint

			err := ValidatePushSubscription(sub)

			if tc.want == "" {
				assert.NoError(t, err)
				return
			}
			assert.ErrorContains(t, err, tc.want)
		})
	}
}

// The keys must decode to a P-256 point and 16 bytes, spelled as the browser
// writes them (unpadded base64url, nothing else in the text).
func TestWebHost_ValidatePushSubscription_Keys(t *testing.T) {
	good := webHostSub(t, "web.push.apple.com")
	require.NoError(t, ValidatePushSubscription(good))
	enc := base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString
	offCurve := make([]byte, 65)
	offCurve[0] = 4
	for i := 1; i < len(offCurve); i++ {
		offCurve[i] = 1
	}
	cases := []struct {
		name   string
		mutate func(s *PushSubscription)
		want   string
	}{
		{"no p256dh", func(s *PushSubscription) { s.P256DH = "" }, "p256dh is not a 65-byte"},
		{"p256dh of 64 bytes", func(s *PushSubscription) { s.P256DH = enc(make([]byte, 64)) }, "p256dh is not a 65-byte"},
		{"p256dh of 66 bytes", func(s *PushSubscription) { s.P256DH = enc(make([]byte, 66)) }, "p256dh is not a 65-byte"},
		{"p256dh with padding", func(s *PushSubscription) { s.P256DH += "=" }, "p256dh is not a 65-byte"},
		{"p256dh with a newline in it", func(s *PushSubscription) { s.P256DH = s.P256DH[:10] + "\n" + s.P256DH[10:] }, "p256dh is not a 65-byte"},
		{"p256dh that is not base64", func(s *PushSubscription) { s.P256DH = "!!!!" }, "p256dh is not a 65-byte"},
		{"p256dh that is the point at infinity", func(s *PushSubscription) { s.P256DH = enc(make([]byte, 65)) }, "no point on P-256"},
		{"p256dh off the curve", func(s *PushSubscription) { s.P256DH = enc(offCurve) }, "no point on P-256"},
		{"no auth", func(s *PushSubscription) { s.Auth = "" }, "auth is not a 16-byte"},
		{"auth of 15 bytes", func(s *PushSubscription) { s.Auth = enc(make([]byte, 15)) }, "auth is not a 16-byte"},
		{"auth of 17 bytes", func(s *PushSubscription) { s.Auth = enc(make([]byte, 17)) }, "auth is not a 16-byte"},
		{"auth with padding", func(s *PushSubscription) { s.Auth += "=" }, "auth is not a 16-byte"},
		{"auth with a newline in it", func(s *PushSubscription) { s.Auth = s.Auth[:10] + "\n" + s.Auth[10:] }, "auth is not a 16-byte"},
		{"auth that is not base64", func(s *PushSubscription) { s.Auth = "!!!!" }, "auth is not a 16-byte"},
	}
	for _, tc := range cases {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			sub := good
			tc.mutate(&sub)

			assert.ErrorContains(t, ValidatePushSubscription(sub), tc.want)
		})
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `core/web_host.go`:

```go
package core

import (
	"crypto/ecdh"
	"encoding/base64"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"net/url"
	"slices"
	"strings"
	"time"
)

// WebHost is the model as the web layer (internal/web) reaches it, beside the
// Core it dials over the wire: the device records and push state a client may
// not set for itself. *Loop implements it (loop_web.go), in process, and
// nothing about it crosses the wire. Each call runs on the loop's goroutine.
type WebHost interface {
	// RedeemPairing trades a pairing code for a device named as the code was
	// made for. The token is the unpadded base64url of 32 random bytes, which
	// the model keeps only as its SHA-256 and returns here once. The errors
	// are ErrWebOff, ErrPairingCode, ErrPairingLocked, ErrDeviceExists and
	// ErrTooManyDevices.
	RedeemPairing(code string) (name, token string, err error)
	// DeviceByToken finds the device whose token hashes to hash: the SHA-256
	// of the 32 raw bytes the cookie's token decodes to, not of its text.
	DeviceByToken(hash [32]byte) (name string, ok bool)
	// TouchDevice records that the device was seen, written at most once a
	// minute.
	TouchDevice(name string)
	// SetNotify applies the fields c sets to the device's notification
	// settings and returns the device as stored; ErrNoDevice for an unknown
	// name.
	SetNotify(name string, c NotifyChange) (WebDevice, error)
	// SetPushSubscription validates sub (ValidatePushSubscription), stores it
	// as the device's and sets PushOK; ErrNoDevice for an unknown name.
	SetPushSubscription(name string, sub PushSubscription) (WebDevice, error)
	// PushTargets are the devices with a subscription that isn't expired.
	PushTargets() []PushTarget
	// PushResult records how a push to the device went. PushExpired also
	// drops the subscription.
	PushResult(device string, state PushState)
	// VAPIDKey is the push key pair; ErrWebOff before the first enable.
	VAPIDKey() (VAPIDKey, error)
	// WebServing records that the listener listens on addr, or why it does
	// not: WebView.Serving, Addr and Error. A non-nil err also raises a
	// Notice, once per distinct error, which waits for the next TUI.
	WebServing(addr string, err error)
}

// NotifyChange changes a device's notification settings; a nil field is left
// as it is.
type NotifyChange struct {
	Waiting     *bool
	Finished    *bool
	PausedUntil *time.Time // a zero time ends a pause
}

// webPushEndpointMax is the longest push endpoint a device may store.
const webPushEndpointMax = 2048

// webPushHosts are the push services whose endpoints the daemon posts to;
// Windows' end in webPushWindowsSuffix instead.
var webPushHosts = []string{"web.push.apple.com", "fcm.googleapis.com", "updates.push.services.mozilla.com"}

const webPushWindowsSuffix = ".notify.windows.com"

// webPushHostAllowed reports whether host is a push service the daemon posts
// to.
func webPushHostAllowed(host string) bool {
	h := strings.ToLower(host)
	if slices.Contains(webPushHosts, h) {
		return true
	}
	return strings.HasSuffix(h, webPushWindowsSuffix) && len(h) > len(webPushWindowsSuffix)
}

// webPushKey decodes s, an unpadded base64url value of exactly n bytes, and
// accepts only its canonical spelling: the decoder skips newlines and stray
// bits, and the daemon encrypts to the decoded bytes.
func webPushKey(s string, n int) ([]byte, bool) {
	b, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(s)
	if err != nil || len(b) != n || base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(b) != s {
		return nil, false
	}
	return b, true
}

// ValidatePushSubscription checks a push subscription before the model keeps
// it. The daemon posts to its endpoint, so only the push services' hosts are
// taken: an https endpoint of at most 2 KiB on web.push.apple.com,
// fcm.googleapis.com, updates.push.services.mozilla.com or a host ending in
// .notify.windows.com, with no user and no port but 443 (stricter than the
// spec's limits, which name the host only). p256dh is a 65-byte uncompressed
// P-256 point and auth 16 bytes, both unpadded base64url. The errors never
// quote the endpoint: it is a capability.
func ValidatePushSubscription(sub PushSubscription) error {
	if len(sub.Endpoint) > webPushEndpointMax {
		return fmt.Errorf("push subscription: the endpoint is longer than %d bytes", webPushEndpointMax)
	}
	u, err := url.Parse(sub.Endpoint)
	switch {
	case err != nil:
		return errors.New("push subscription: the endpoint is no URL")
	case u.Scheme != "https":
		return errors.New("push subscription: the endpoint is not https")
	case u.User != nil:
		return errors.New("push subscription: the endpoint names a user")
	case u.Port() != "" && u.Port() != "443":
		return errors.New("push subscription: the endpoint names a port other than 443")
	case !webPushHostAllowed(u.Hostname()):
		return fmt.Errorf("push subscription: %q is no push service the daemon posts to", u.Hostname())
	}
	p256dh, ok := webPushKey(sub.P256DH, 65)
	if !ok {
		return errors.New("push subscription: p256dh is not a 65-byte unpadded base64url value")
	}
	if _, err := ecdh.P256().NewPublicKey(p256dh); err != nil {
		return errors.New("push subscription: p256dh is no point on P-256")
	}
	if _, ok := webPushKey(sub.Auth, 16); !ok {
		return errors.New("push subscription: auth is not a 16-byte unpadded base64url value")
	}
	return nil
}
```

### Task 4.3: Pairing and finding a device

**Files:**
- Modify: `core/web_host.go` (the import block, then append)
- Test: `core/web_host_test.go` (the import block, then append)

`RedeemPairing`, `DeviceByToken` and `TouchDevice` on `*Model`, over Story 3's `Model.web`. Redeeming counts each wrong code in `misses`, voids every pending code at the fifth and answers `ErrPairingLocked` to every code until the next `PairDevice` resets the count; a match consumes the code, then checks the name and the device count, makes the device and writes `web.json`, and puts the code back if the write fails. `TouchDevice` returns whether it wrote, which Task 4.5's wrapper uses to wake the client only then. Story 3's `saveWeb` writes `web.json` under `config.GetGlobalConfigDir()` for a model that never booted, so a test on a bare `NewForTest` model that reaches a write would leave the file in the package's shared `TestMain` dir for a later test to load. Every test of this story that can write it therefore runs over its own global dir: `webHostModel` sets `LOOM_GLOBAL_DIR` to a `t.TempDir()`, and so do the two tests that build a bare model (`never enabled` here, `before the first enable` in Task 4.4), though they only read; Task 4.1's tests never reach `saveWeb`.

```go
// webHostClock is the clock a web model reads (webState.now): a test moves it
// by hand.
type webHostClock struct{ at time.Time }

func (c *webHostClock) now() time.Time { return c.at }

// webHostModel is a model with the web enabled through the production path
// (ConfigureWeb), over an isolated global dir, reading a clock the test moves.
func webHostModel(t *testing.T) (*Model, *webHostClock) {
	t.Helper()
	t.Setenv(config.EnvGlobalDir, t.TempDir())
	m := NewForTest(Options{})
	clk := &webHostClock{at: time.Date(2026, 10, 21, 12, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)}
	m.web.now = clk.now
	require.NoError(t, m.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))
	return m, clk
}

// webHostPair pairs a device through the production path (PairDevice, then
// RedeemPairing) and returns its cookie token and the hash DeviceByToken takes.
func webHostPair(t *testing.T, m *Model, name string) (token string, hash [32]byte) {
	t.Helper()
	p, err := m.PairDevice(name)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	got, token, err := m.RedeemPairing(p.Code)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.Equal(t, name, got)
	raw, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(token)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	return token, sha256.Sum256(raw)
}

// webHostWrongCode is a well-formed code that is not right.
func webHostWrongCode(right string) string {
	if right == "22222222" {
		return "33333333"
	}
	return "22222222"
}

// webHostFile is the part of web.json the tests read back.
type webHostFile struct {
	Devices []struct {
		Name        string `json:"name"`
		TokenSHA256 string `json:"token_sha256"`
		LastSeen    string `json:"last_seen"`
		Notify      struct {
			Waiting     bool   `json:"waiting"`
			Finished    bool   `json:"finished"`
			PausedUntil string `json:"paused_until"`
		} `json:"notify"`
		Push struct {
			Endpoint string `json:"endpoint"`
		} `json:"push"`
		PushState string `json:"push_state"`
	} `json:"devices"`
}

// webHostFileOf reads web.json back from the isolated global dir, parsed and
// as text.
func webHostFileOf(t *testing.T) (webHostFile, string) {
	t.Helper()
	dir, err := config.GetGlobalConfigDir()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	data, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(dir, WebFile))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	var f webHostFile
	require.NoError(t, json.Unmarshal(data, &f))
	return f, string(data)
}

// webHostBreakWrites makes web.json unwritable by putting a directory where
// the file goes: the atomic write's rename onto it fails, whoever runs the
// test. The returned func puts the file back.
func webHostBreakWrites(t *testing.T) (restore func()) {
	t.Helper()
	dir, err := config.GetGlobalConfigDir()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	path := filepath.Join(dir, WebFile)
	saved, err := os.ReadFile(path)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.NoError(t, os.Remove(path))
	require.NoError(t, os.Mkdir(path, 0o700))
	return func() {
		require.NoError(t, os.Remove(path))
		require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(path, saved, 0o600))
	}
}

func TestWebHost_RedeemPairingTradesACodeForADevice(t *testing.T) {
	m, clk := webHostModel(t)
	p, err := m.PairDevice("iPhone")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	typed := strings.ToLower(p.Code[:4] + "-" + p.Code[4:]) // as typed: any case, with a dash
	clk.at = clk.at.Add(time.Minute)

	name, token, err := m.RedeemPairing(typed)

	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, "iPhone", name, "the device takes the name the code was made for")
	raw, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(token)
	require.NoError(t, err, "the token is unpadded base64url")
	require.Len(t, raw, 32)
	found, ok := m.DeviceByToken(sha256.Sum256(raw))
	assert.True(t, ok)
	assert.Equal(t, "iPhone", found)

	require.Len(t, m.Web().Devices, 1)
	dev := m.Web().Devices[0]
	assert.Equal(t, "iPhone", dev.Name)
	assert.True(t, dev.Paired.Equal(clk.at))
	assert.True(t, dev.LastSeen.Equal(clk.at))
	assert.Equal(t, NotifySettings{Waiting: true, Finished: true}, dev.Notify)
	assert.Equal(t, PushNone, dev.Push)

	onDisk, text := webHostFileOf(t)
	require.Len(t, onDisk.Devices, 1)
	sum := sha256.Sum256(raw)
	assert.Equal(t, hex.EncodeToString(sum[:]), onDisk.Devices[0].TokenSHA256, "the file holds the token's hash")
	assert.NotContains(t, text, token, "and never the token")
}

func TestWebHost_RedeemPairingGivesACodeOneUse(t *testing.T) {
	m, _ := webHostModel(t)
	p, err := m.PairDevice("iPhone")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	_, _, err = m.RedeemPairing(p.Code)
	require.NoError(t, err)

	_, _, err = m.RedeemPairing(p.Code)

	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, ErrPairingCode)
	assert.Len(t, m.Web().Devices, 1)
}

func TestWebHost_RedeemPairingRefusesAnExpiredCode(t *testing.T) {
	for _, tc := range []struct {
		name  string
		after time.Duration
		want  error
	}{
		{"a second before it expires", PairingTTL - time.Second, nil},
		{"a second after it expires", PairingTTL + time.Second, ErrPairingCode},
	} {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			m, clk := webHostModel(t)
			p, err := m.PairDevice("iPhone")
			require.NoError(t, err)
			clk.at = clk.at.Add(tc.after)

			_, _, err = m.RedeemPairing(p.Code)

			if tc.want == nil {
				assert.NoError(t, err)
				return
			}
			assert.ErrorIs(t, err, tc.want)
			assert.Empty(t, m.Web().Devices)
		})
	}
}

// Five wrong codes since the last pair void every pending code, and the app is
// told to pair again: the right code is refused too, until the next PairDevice.
func TestWebHost_FiveWrongCodesLockPairingUntilTheNextPair(t *testing.T) {
	m, _ := webHostModel(t)
	p, err := m.PairDevice("iPhone")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	wrong := webHostWrongCode(p.Code)
	wrongs := []string{wrong, "abc", "", "0000-0000", wrong} // a malformed code is a wrong one
	require.Len(t, wrongs, PairingMaxMisses)

	for i, code := range wrongs[:PairingMaxMisses-1] {
		_, _, err = m.RedeemPairing(code)
		require.ErrorIs(t, err, ErrPairingCode, "wrong code %d", i+1)
	}
	_, _, err = m.RedeemPairing(wrongs[PairingMaxMisses-1])
	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, ErrPairingLocked, "the fifth voids every pending code")
	_, _, err = m.RedeemPairing(p.Code)
	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, ErrPairingLocked, "the right code is refused too")
	assert.Empty(t, m.Web().Devices)

	again, err := m.PairDevice("iPhone")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	name, _, err := m.RedeemPairing(again.Code)
	require.NoError(t, err, "the next pair lifts the lock")
	assert.Equal(t, "iPhone", name)
}

func TestWebHost_FourWrongCodesLockNothing(t *testing.T) {
	m, _ := webHostModel(t)
	p, err := m.PairDevice("iPhone")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	for i := 1; i < PairingMaxMisses; i++ {
		_, _, err = m.RedeemPairing(webHostWrongCode(p.Code))
		require.ErrorIs(t, err, ErrPairingCode, "wrong code %d", i)
	}

	name, _, err := m.RedeemPairing(p.Code)

	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, "iPhone", name)
}

func TestWebHost_RedeemPairingWhileTheWebIsOff(t *testing.T) {
	t.Run("never enabled", func(t *testing.T) {
		t.Setenv(config.EnvGlobalDir, t.TempDir()) // a bare model's saveWeb would write under the global dir
		m := NewForTest(Options{})

		_, _, err := m.RedeemPairing("23456789")

		assert.ErrorIs(t, err, ErrWebOff)
	})
	t.Run("turned off with a code pending", func(t *testing.T) {
		m, _ := webHostModel(t)
		p, err := m.PairDevice("iPhone")
		require.NoError(t, err)
		require.NoError(t, m.ConfigureWeb("", "https://loom.example.com"))

		_, _, err = m.RedeemPairing(p.Code)

		assert.ErrorIs(t, err, ErrWebOff)
		assert.Empty(t, m.Web().Devices)
	})
}

// A code made while there was room can be redeemed after the room is gone,
// and a name taken in between is not given twice. No PairDevice rule reaches
// either state alone, so the fixture fills the device list directly.
func TestWebHost_RedeemPairingChecksTheDeviceListAgain(t *testing.T) {
	for _, tc := range []struct {
		name    string
		devices int
		want    error
	}{
		{"one place left", MaxWebDevices - 1, nil},
		{"none left", MaxWebDevices, ErrTooManyDevices},
	} {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			m, _ := webHostModel(t)
			p, err := m.PairDevice("over")
			require.NoError(t, err)
			for i := 0; i < tc.devices; i++ {
				m.web.devices = append(m.web.devices, &webDevice{name: fmt.Sprintf("d%02d", i), tokenHash: [32]byte{byte(i + 1)}})
			}

			_, _, err = m.RedeemPairing(p.Code)

			if tc.want == nil {
				assert.NoError(t, err)
				assert.Len(t, m.web.devices, tc.devices+1)
				return
			}
			assert.ErrorIs(t, err, tc.want)
			assert.Len(t, m.web.devices, tc.devices)
		})
	}
	t.Run("a name taken in between", func(t *testing.T) {
		m, _ := webHostModel(t)
		p, err := m.PairDevice("iPad")
		require.NoError(t, err)
		taken := [32]byte{1}
		m.web.devices = append(m.web.devices, &webDevice{name: "iPad", tokenHash: taken})

		_, _, err = m.RedeemPairing(p.Code)

		assert.ErrorIs(t, err, ErrDeviceExists)
		assert.Len(t, m.web.devices, 1, "no second device")
		got, ok := m.DeviceByToken(taken)
		assert.True(t, ok, "the first device keeps its token")
		assert.Equal(t, "iPad", got)
	})
}

// A write that fails leaves no device behind and keeps the code, so the phone
// can try again once the disk answers.
func TestWebHost_RedeemPairingKeepsTheCodeWhenTheWriteFails(t *testing.T) {
	m, _ := webHostModel(t)
	p, err := m.PairDevice("iPhone")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	restore := webHostBreakWrites(t)

	_, _, err = m.RedeemPairing(p.Code)

	require.Error(t, err)
	assert.NotErrorIs(t, err, ErrPairingCode, "the code was right")
	assert.Empty(t, m.Web().Devices, "no device without its record")

	restore()
	name, _, err := m.RedeemPairing(p.Code)
	require.NoError(t, err, "the code was kept")
	assert.Equal(t, "iPhone", name)
}

func TestWebHost_DeviceByToken(t *testing.T) {
	m, _ := webHostModel(t)
	phoneToken, phone := webHostPair(t, m, "iPhone")
	_, pad := webHostPair(t, m, "iPad")

	name, ok := m.DeviceByToken(phone)
	assert.True(t, ok)
	assert.Equal(t, "iPhone", name)
	name, ok = m.DeviceByToken(pad)
	assert.True(t, ok)
	assert.Equal(t, "iPad", name)
	_, ok = m.DeviceByToken(sha256.Sum256([]byte("nobody")))
	assert.False(t, ok)
	_, ok = m.DeviceByToken(sha256.Sum256([]byte(phoneToken)))
	assert.False(t, ok, "the hash is of the 32 raw bytes, not of the cookie's text")

	// A record with no hash (a hand-edited web.json) answers to no one's.
	m.web.devices = append(m.web.devices, &webDevice{name: "blank"})
	_, ok = m.DeviceByToken([32]byte{})
	assert.False(t, ok)

	require.NoError(t, m.RevokeDevice("iPhone"))
	_, ok = m.DeviceByToken(phone)
	assert.False(t, ok, "a revoked device is found no more")
}

func TestWebHost_TouchDeviceWritesAtMostOnceAMinute(t *testing.T) {
	m, clk := webHostModel(t)
	webHostPair(t, m, "iPhone")
	paired := clk.at
	lastSeen := func() time.Time { return m.Web().Devices[0].LastSeen }

	clk.at = paired.Add(time.Minute - time.Second)
	assert.False(t, m.TouchDevice("iPhone"))
	assert.True(t, lastSeen().Equal(paired), "within a minute of the last time nothing changes")

	clk.at = paired.Add(time.Minute)
	assert.True(t, m.TouchDevice("iPhone"))
	assert.True(t, lastSeen().Equal(clk.at))
	onDisk, _ := webHostFileOf(t)
	seen, err := time.Parse(time.RFC3339, onDisk.Devices[0].LastSeen)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.True(t, seen.Equal(clk.at), "the file holds it")

	touched := clk.at
	clk.at = clk.at.Add(30 * time.Second)
	assert.False(t, m.TouchDevice("iPhone"), "a minute counts from the last write")
	assert.True(t, lastSeen().Equal(touched))
	assert.False(t, m.TouchDevice("nobody"), "an unknown name is no one")
}
```

```go
const (
	// webTokenBytes is the size of a device's random token before encoding.
	webTokenBytes = 32
	// webTouchInterval is the least time between two writes of a device's
	// last-seen time.
	webTouchInterval = time.Minute
)

// RedeemPairing trades a pairing code for a device (WebHost). A code matches
// ignoring case, dashes and spaces, once, and until it expires. Each wrong
// code counts in misses; the fifth voids every pending code, and every code
// then gets ErrPairingLocked until the next PairDevice resets the count. A
// match is consumed before the name and the device count are checked, so a
// refusal there costs the code; a failed write puts it back. Loop goroutine
// only.
func (m *Model) RedeemPairing(code string) (name, token string, err error) {
	w := &m.web
	if w.listen == "" {
		return "", "", ErrWebOff
	}
	if w.misses >= PairingMaxMisses {
		return "", "", ErrPairingLocked
	}
	now := w.clock()
	w.pending = slices.DeleteFunc(w.pending, func(p pendingPairing) bool { return !now.Before(p.expires) })
	at := -1
	if norm, ok := NormalizePairingCode(code); ok {
		for i, p := range w.pending {
			if subtle.ConstantTimeCompare([]byte(p.code), []byte(norm)) == 1 {
				at = i
			}
		}
	}
	if at < 0 {
		w.misses++
		if w.misses < PairingMaxMisses {
			return "", "", ErrPairingCode
		}
		w.pending = nil
		log.For("core").Warn("web.pairing_locked", "misses", w.misses)
		return "", "", ErrPairingLocked
	}
	p := w.pending[at]
	w.pending = slices.Delete(w.pending, at, at+1)
	if w.find(p.name) != nil {
		return "", "", fmt.Errorf("%w: %q", ErrDeviceExists, p.name)
	}
	if len(w.devices) >= MaxWebDevices {
		return "", "", ErrTooManyDevices
	}
	raw := make([]byte, webTokenBytes)
	_, _ = rand.Read(raw)
	d := &webDevice{
		name:      p.name,
		tokenHash: sha256.Sum256(raw),
		paired:    now,
		lastSeen:  now,
		notify:    NotifySettings{Waiting: true, Finished: true},
	}
	w.devices = append(w.devices, d)
	slices.SortFunc(w.devices, func(a, b *webDevice) int { return strings.Compare(a.name, b.name) })
	if err = m.saveWeb(); err != nil {
		w.devices = slices.DeleteFunc(w.devices, func(x *webDevice) bool { return x == d })
		w.pending = slices.Insert(w.pending, at, p)
		return "", "", fmt.Errorf("pairing: %w", err)
	}
	log.For("core").Info("web.paired", "device", p.name)
	return p.name, base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(raw), nil
}

// DeviceByToken finds the device whose token hashes to hash, the SHA-256 of
// the token's 32 raw bytes (WebHost). A record with no hash (a hand-edited
// web.json) answers to no hash. Loop goroutine only.
func (m *Model) DeviceByToken(hash [32]byte) (string, bool) {
	found := ""
	for _, d := range m.web.devices {
		if d.tokenHash != [32]byte{} && subtle.ConstantTimeCompare(d.tokenHash[:], hash[:]) == 1 {
			found = d.name
		}
	}
	return found, found != ""
}

// TouchDevice records that the device was seen, writing web.json at most once
// a minute per device (WebHost), and reports whether it did, so the loop
// wakes its client only then. A write that fails is logged and left to the
// next minute: the time stays in memory. Loop goroutine only.
func (m *Model) TouchDevice(name string) bool {
	d := m.web.find(name)
	if d == nil {
		return false
	}
	now := m.web.clock()
	if now.Sub(d.lastSeen) < webTouchInterval {
		return false
	}
	d.lastSeen = now
	if err := m.saveWeb(); err != nil {
		log.For("core").Warn("web.touch_unsaved", "device", name, "err", err.Error())
	}
	return true
}
```

### Task 4.4: Notification settings, push state, the VAPID key and the listener's state

**Files:**
- Modify: `core/web_host.go` (append)
- Test: `core/web_host_test.go` (the import block, then append)

`SetNotify`, `SetPushSubscription`, `PushTargets`, `PushResult`, `VAPIDKey` and `WebServing` on `*Model`. The two setters write `web.json` and put the old value back if the write fails, since their caller hears the error. `PushResult` and `TouchDevice` have no caller to tell, so a failed write is logged and the value stays in memory. A result for a device with no subscription changes nothing: a push still in flight when another answered 410 would otherwise overwrite "expired" with "failing". `PushResult`, `WebServing` and the setters return whether a client would see a change, for Task 4.5's wake. `WebServing` is the layer's report, not a setting: it leaves `rev` alone (only `ConfigureWeb` bumps it, which is what makes the layer rebind), so a failed bind is retried only by the next `ConfigureWeb`; the `WebServing` tests pin `WebView.Rev` unchanged.

```go
func webHostPtr[T any](v T) *T { return &v }

func TestWebHost_SetNotifyAppliesOnlyTheFieldsSet(t *testing.T) {
	m, clk := webHostModel(t)
	webHostPair(t, m, "iPhone")
	until := clk.at.Add(time.Hour)

	dev, err := m.SetNotify("iPhone", NotifyChange{Waiting: webHostPtr(false), PausedUntil: &until})

	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.False(t, dev.Notify.Waiting)
	assert.True(t, dev.Notify.Finished, "a nil field is left as it is")
	assert.True(t, dev.Notify.PausedUntil.Equal(until))
	assert.Equal(t, m.Web().Devices[0], dev, "the device as stored")
	onDisk, _ := webHostFileOf(t)
	assert.False(t, onDisk.Devices[0].Notify.Waiting)
	assert.True(t, onDisk.Devices[0].Notify.Finished)
	assert.NotEmpty(t, onDisk.Devices[0].Notify.PausedUntil)

	dev, err = m.SetNotify("iPhone", NotifyChange{Finished: webHostPtr(false)})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.False(t, dev.Notify.Waiting, "a setting not named stands")
	assert.False(t, dev.Notify.Finished)
	assert.True(t, dev.Notify.PausedUntil.Equal(until), "so does a pause not named")

	dev, err = m.SetNotify("iPhone", NotifyChange{Waiting: webHostPtr(true), PausedUntil: &time.Time{}})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.True(t, dev.Notify.Waiting)
	assert.True(t, dev.Notify.PausedUntil.IsZero(), "a zero time ends the pause")
	onDisk, _ = webHostFileOf(t)
	assert.Empty(t, onDisk.Devices[0].Notify.PausedUntil)
}

func TestWebHost_SetNotifyRefusesAnUnknownDeviceAndChangesNothingWhenTheWriteFails(t *testing.T) {
	m, _ := webHostModel(t)
	webHostPair(t, m, "iPhone")
	before := m.Web().Devices[0]

	_, err := m.SetNotify("nobody", NotifyChange{Waiting: webHostPtr(false)})
	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, ErrNoDevice)

	webHostBreakWrites(t)
	_, err = m.SetNotify("iPhone", NotifyChange{Waiting: webHostPtr(false)})
	require.Error(t, err)
	assert.NotErrorIs(t, err, ErrNoDevice)
	assert.Equal(t, before, m.Web().Devices[0], "the setting is put back")
}

func TestWebHost_SetPushSubscriptionStoresAValidOne(t *testing.T) {
	m, _ := webHostModel(t)
	webHostPair(t, m, "iPhone")
	sub := webHostSub(t, "web.push.apple.com")

	dev, err := m.SetPushSubscription("iPhone", sub)

	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, PushOK, dev.Push)
	assert.Equal(t, m.Web().Devices[0], dev, "the device as stored")
	assert.Equal(t, []PushTarget{{Device: "iPhone", Notify: NotifySettings{Waiting: true, Finished: true}, Subscription: sub}}, m.PushTargets())
	onDisk, _ := webHostFileOf(t)
	assert.Equal(t, sub.Endpoint, onDisk.Devices[0].Push.Endpoint)
	assert.Equal(t, "ok", onDisk.Devices[0].PushState)
}

func TestWebHost_SetPushSubscriptionRefusesWhatItShouldNotKeep(t *testing.T) {
	m, _ := webHostModel(t)
	webHostPair(t, m, "iPhone")
	good := webHostSub(t, "web.push.apple.com")

	elsewhere := good
	elsewhere.Endpoint = "https://push.example.com/abc"
	_, err := m.SetPushSubscription("iPhone", elsewhere)
	assert.ErrorContains(t, err, "no push service")
	assert.Equal(t, PushNone, m.Web().Devices[0].Push, "nothing is stored")
	assert.Empty(t, m.PushTargets())

	_, err = m.SetPushSubscription("nobody", good)
	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, ErrNoDevice)

	_, err = m.SetPushSubscription("iPhone", good)

}

func TestWebHost_PushTargetsAreTheDevicesWithALiveSubscription(t *testing.T) {
	m, _ := webHostModel(t)
	for _, name := range []string{"a-ok", "b-none", "c-expired", "d-failing"} {
		webHostPair(t, m, name)
	}
	for _, name := range []string{"a-ok", "c-expired", "d-failing"} {
		_, err := m.SetPushSubscription(name, webHostSub(t, "fcm.googleapis.com"))
		require.NoError(t, err)
	}
	m.PushResult("c-expired", PushExpired)
	m.PushResult("d-failing", PushFailing(503))

	targets := m.PushTargets()

	require.Len(t, targets, 2, "no subscription, no target; an expired one is not tried")
	assert.Equal(t, "a-ok", targets[0].Device)
	assert.Equal(t, "d-failing", targets[1].Device, "a failing one is tried again")
}

func TestWebHost_PushResult(t *testing.T) {
	m, _ := webHostModel(t)
	webHostPair(t, m, "iPhone")
	_, err := m.SetPushSubscription("iPhone", webHostSub(t, "web.push.apple.com"))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	push := func() PushState { return m.Web().Devices[0].Push }

	assert.True(t, m.PushResult("iPhone", PushFailing(503)))
	assert.Equal(t, PushFailing(503), push())
	assert.Len(t, m.PushTargets(), 1, "a failing subscription is tried again")
	assert.False(t, m.PushResult("iPhone", PushFailing(503)), "the same result changes nothing")

	assert.True(t, m.PushResult("iPhone", PushOK))
	assert.Equal(t, PushOK, push())

	assert.True(t, m.PushResult("iPhone", PushExpired))
	assert.Equal(t, PushExpired, push())
	assert.Empty(t, m.PushTargets(), "an expired subscription is dropped")
	onDisk, _ := webHostFileOf(t)
	assert.Empty(t, onDisk.Devices[0].Push.Endpoint)
	assert.Equal(t, "expired", onDisk.Devices[0].PushState)

	assert.False(t, m.PushResult("iPhone", PushFailing(500)), "a late result of another push leaves the expiry standing")
	assert.Equal(t, PushExpired, push())
	assert.False(t, m.PushResult("nobody", PushOK), "a revoked device is no one")

	_, err = m.SetPushSubscription("iPhone", webHostSub(t, "web.push.apple.com"))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, PushOK, push(), "a new subscription revives the device")
	assert.Len(t, m.PushTargets(), 1)
}

func TestWebHost_VAPIDKey(t *testing.T) {
	t.Run("before the first enable", func(t *testing.T) {
		t.Setenv(config.EnvGlobalDir, t.TempDir()) // a bare model's saveWeb would write under the global dir
		m := NewForTest(Options{})

		_, err := m.VAPIDKey()

		assert.ErrorIs(t, err, ErrWebOff)
	})
	t.Run("after it", func(t *testing.T) {
		m, _ := webHostModel(t)

		key, err := m.VAPIDKey()

		require.NoError(t, err)
		require.Len(t, key.Private, 32)
		require.Len(t, key.Public, 65)
		priv, err := ecdh.P256().NewPrivateKey(key.Private)
		require.NoError(t, err)
		assert.Equal(t, key.Public, priv.PublicKey().Bytes(), "the pair belongs together")

		want := append([]byte(nil), key.Private...)
		key.Private[0] ^= 0xff
		again, err := m.VAPIDKey()
		require.NoError(t, err)
		assert.Equal(t, want, again.Private, "a caller's edit does not reach the model")
	})
	t.Run("kept when the web is turned off", func(t *testing.T) {
		m, _ := webHostModel(t)
		before, err := m.VAPIDKey()
		require.NoError(t, err)
		require.NoError(t, m.ConfigureWeb("", "https://loom.example.com"))

		after, err := m.VAPIDKey()

		require.NoError(t, err)
		assert.Equal(t, before, after)
	})
}

func TestWebHost_WebServingSetsWhatTheViewShows(t *testing.T) {
	m, _ := webHostModel(t)
	m.Drain()
	rev := m.Web().Rev
	require.NotZero(t, rev, "fixture: the ConfigureWeb that enabled the web bumped it")

	assert.True(t, m.WebServing("127.0.0.1:7420", nil))
	v := m.Web()
	assert.True(t, v.Serving)
	assert.Equal(t, "127.0.0.1:7420", v.Addr)
	assert.Empty(t, v.Error)
	assert.Equal(t, rev, v.Rev, "the report is no new setting: Rev is the layer's cue to rebind, and only ConfigureWeb moves it")
	raised, _ := noticesAndStarts(m.Drain().Events)
	assert.Empty(t, raised, "listening is no news")

	assert.False(t, m.WebServing("127.0.0.1:7420", nil), "the same state changes nothing")

	assert.True(t, m.WebServing("", nil), "the listener closed")
	v = m.Web()
	assert.False(t, v.Serving)
	assert.Empty(t, v.Addr)
	assert.Equal(t, rev, v.Rev, "closing is a report too")
}

func TestWebHost_WebServingRaisesOneNoticePerDistinctError(t *testing.T) {
	m, _ := webHostModel(t)
	m.Drain()
	rev := m.Web().Rev
	require.NotZero(t, rev, "fixture: the ConfigureWeb that enabled the web bumped it")
	inUse := errors.New("listen tcp 127.0.0.1:7420: bind: address already in use")
	denied := errors.New("listen tcp 127.0.0.1:80: bind: permission denied")

	assert.True(t, m.WebServing("", inUse))
	v := m.Web()
	assert.False(t, v.Serving)
	assert.Empty(t, v.Addr)
	assert.Contains(t, v.Error, "address already in use")
	assert.Equal(t, rev, v.Rev, "a failed bind does not bump Rev, or the layer would retry it in a loop")
	raised, _ := noticesAndStarts(m.Drain().Events)
	require.Len(t, raised, 1)
	assert.ErrorContains(t, raised[0].Err, "address already in use")

	assert.False(t, m.WebServing("", inUse), "the same error again changes nothing")
	raised, _ = noticesAndStarts(m.Drain().Events)
	assert.Empty(t, raised, "and is told once")

	assert.True(t, m.WebServing("", denied))
	raised, _ = noticesAndStarts(m.Drain().Events)
	require.Len(t, raised, 1, "a different error is told")
	assert.ErrorContains(t, raised[0].Err, "permission denied")

	assert.True(t, m.WebServing("127.0.0.1:7420", nil))
	assert.Empty(t, m.Web().Error, "serving clears the reason")
	m.Drain()

	m.WebServing("", inUse)
	raised, _ = noticesAndStarts(m.Drain().Events)
	require.Len(t, raised, 1, "an error that comes back after serving is told again")
	assert.Equal(t, rev, m.Web().Rev, "none of the reports moved Rev")
}

// The model's web state reaches clients as ModelChanged on the next Sync, with
// no event of its own.
func TestWebHost_AChangeReachesClientsThroughTheModelView(t *testing.T) {
	m, _ := webHostModel(t)
	m.Sync() // the first publish

	m.WebServing("127.0.0.1:7420", nil)

	var view *ModelView
	for _, ev := range m.Sync().Events {
		if mc, ok := ev.(ModelChanged); ok {
			v := mc.View
			view = &v
		}
	}
	require.NotNil(t, view, "the Sync after the change carries the model view")
	assert.True(t, view.Web.Serving)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Append at the end of `core/web_host.go` (the imports Task 4.3 left already cover it):

```go
// webHostDeviceView is d as a client sees it: the record without its hash or
// its subscription's keys.
func webHostDeviceView(d *webDevice) WebDevice {
	return WebDevice{Name: d.name, Paired: d.paired, LastSeen: d.lastSeen, Notify: d.notify, Push: d.push}
}

// SetNotify applies the fields c sets to the device's notification settings
// and returns the device as stored (WebHost). A write that fails puts the
// settings back. Loop goroutine only.
func (m *Model) SetNotify(name string, c NotifyChange) (WebDevice, error) {
	d := m.web.find(name)
	if d == nil {
		return WebDevice{}, fmt.Errorf("%w: %q", ErrNoDevice, name)
	}
	prev := d.notify
	if c.Waiting != nil {
		d.notify.Waiting = *c.Waiting
	}
	if c.Finished != nil {
		d.notify.Finished = *c.Finished
	}
	if c.PausedUntil != nil {
		d.notify.PausedUntil = *c.PausedUntil
	}
	if err := m.saveWeb(); err != nil {
		d.notify = prev
		return WebDevice{}, fmt.Errorf("notify settings: %w", err)
	}
	return webHostDeviceView(d), nil
}

// SetPushSubscription validates sub, stores it as the device's and sets
// PushOK (WebHost). A write that fails puts the old subscription back. Loop
// goroutine only.
func (m *Model) SetPushSubscription(name string, sub PushSubscription) (WebDevice, error) {
	d := m.web.find(name)
	if d == nil {
		return WebDevice{}, fmt.Errorf("%w: %q", ErrNoDevice, name)
	}
	if err := ValidatePushSubscription(sub); err != nil {
		return WebDevice{}, err
	}
	prevSub, prevPush := d.sub, d.push
	d.sub, d.push = sub, PushOK
	if err := m.saveWeb(); err != nil {
		d.sub, d.push = prevSub, prevPush
		return WebDevice{}, fmt.Errorf("push subscription: %w", err)
	}
	return webHostDeviceView(d), nil
}

// PushTargets are the devices with a subscription that isn't expired, in
// device order (WebHost). A subscription that failed is tried again. Loop
// goroutine only.
func (m *Model) PushTargets() []PushTarget {
	var targets []PushTarget
	for _, d := range m.web.devices {
		if d.sub.Endpoint == "" || d.push == PushExpired {
			continue
		}
		targets = append(targets, PushTarget{Device: d.name, Notify: d.notify, Subscription: d.sub})
	}
	return targets
}

// PushResult records how a push to the device went; PushExpired also drops
// the subscription (WebHost). It reports whether a client would see a
// change. A result for a device with no subscription changes nothing: a push
// still in flight when another one's 410 dropped it would otherwise turn
// "expired" into "failing". A write that fails is logged and the state stays
// in memory. Loop goroutine only.
func (m *Model) PushResult(device string, state PushState) bool {
	d := m.web.find(device)
	if d == nil || d.sub.Endpoint == "" {
		return false
	}
	prevSub, prevPush := d.sub, d.push
	d.push = state
	if state == PushExpired {
		d.sub = PushSubscription{}
	}
	if d.sub == prevSub && d.push == prevPush {
		return false
	}
	if err := m.saveWeb(); err != nil {
		log.For("core").Warn("web.push_result_unsaved", "device", device, "err", err.Error())
	}
	return true
}

// VAPIDKey is a copy of the push key pair; ErrWebOff before the first enable
// made one (WebHost). It outlives a disable. Loop goroutine only.
func (m *Model) VAPIDKey() (VAPIDKey, error) {
	k := m.web.vapid
	if len(k.Private) == 0 || len(k.Public) == 0 {
		return VAPIDKey{}, ErrWebOff
	}
	return VAPIDKey{Private: slices.Clone(k.Private), Public: slices.Clone(k.Public)}, nil
}

// WebServing records where the listener listens, or why it does not (WebHost):
// serving means no error and an address. An error also raises a Notice, once
// per distinct error text (the one already recorded is not told again), which
// the server keeps for the next TUI while only the web layer is connected. It
// reports whether the view changed. It leaves rev alone: only ConfigureWeb
// bumps it, and the layer rebinds when it moves, so a failed bind is not
// retried by its own report. Loop goroutine only.
func (m *Model) WebServing(addr string, err error) bool {
	w := &m.web
	serving := err == nil && addr != ""
	if !serving {
		addr = ""
	}
	errText := ""
	if err != nil {
		errText = err.Error()
	}
	told := errText != "" && errText != w.servingErr
	changed := serving != w.serving || addr != w.addr || errText != w.servingErr
	w.serving, w.addr, w.servingErr = serving, addr, errText
	if told {
		log.For("core").Warn("web.not_serving", "err", errText)
		m.notifyErr(fmt.Errorf("web: %w", err))
	}
	return changed
}
```

### Task 4.5: The loop implements `WebHost`

**Files:**
- Create: `core/loop_web.go`
- Test: `core/web_host_test.go` (append)

`*Loop` runs each model method on the loop's goroutine, as `loop_mod.go` does for the mod, and wakes the client after a call that changed what a client sees (the `Web` view) or queued an event (`WebServing`'s Notice). A wake has the server build and diff every view, so a call that changed nothing wakes none: `TouchDevice` runs on every authenticated request but writes once a minute. The wake is raised on the loop's goroutine, inside the call, since `Stop` closes `Wakes` only after that goroutine has exited. A call after `Stop` runs nothing, so the methods that return an error start from `ErrWebOff`: a stopped loop pairs no one, and hands out no key.

Append at the end of `core/web_host_test.go` (no new imports):

```go
// webHostLoop runs a loop over a web model with one device, "iPhone", paired
// and published, and no wake waiting.
func webHostLoop(t *testing.T) (*Loop, *webHostClock, [32]byte) {
	t.Helper()
	m, clk := webHostModel(t)
	_, hash := webHostPair(t, m, "iPhone")
	l := StartForTest(m)
	t.Cleanup(l.Stop)
	l.Sync() // the first publish
	webHostDrainWakes(l)
	return l, clk, hash
}

func webHostDrainWakes(l *Loop) {
	for len(l.Wakes()) > 0 {
		<-l.Wakes()
	}
}

func webHostDevice(v WebView, name string) WebDevice {
	for _, d := range v.Devices {
		if d.Name == name {
			return d
		}
	}
	return WebDevice{}
}

// Each call that changes what a client sees wakes the client, and the change
// reaches it as ModelChanged on the Sync that follows.
func TestWebHostLoop_AChangeWakesTheClientAndReachesTheModelView(t *testing.T) {
	inUse := errors.New("listen tcp 127.0.0.1:7420: bind: address already in use")
	cases := []struct {
		name string
		prep func(t *testing.T, l *Loop, clk *webHostClock) (act func())
		want func(t *testing.T, v WebView, events []Event)
	}{
		{
			name: "WebServing",
			prep: func(t *testing.T, l *Loop, clk *webHostClock) func() {
				return func() { l.WebServing("127.0.0.1:7420", nil) }
			},
			want: func(t *testing.T, v WebView, _ []Event) { assert.True(t, v.Serving) },
		},
		{
			name: "WebServing with an error also queues its Notice",
			prep: func(t *testing.T, l *Loop, clk *webHostClock) func() {
				return func() { l.WebServing("", inUse) }
			},
			want: func(t *testing.T, v WebView, events []Event) {
				assert.Contains(t, v.Error, "address already in use")
				raised, _ := noticesAndStarts(events)
				require.Len(t, raised, 1)
				assert.ErrorContains(t, raised[0].Err, "address already in use")
			},
		},
		{
			name: "RedeemPairing",
			prep: func(t *testing.T, l *Loop, clk *webHostClock) func() {
				p, err := l.PairDevice("iPad")
				require.NoError(t, err)
				return func() {
					name, _, err := l.RedeemPairing(p.Code)
					require.NoError(t, err)
					assert.Equal(t, "iPad", name)
				}
			},
			want: func(t *testing.T, v WebView, _ []Event) { assert.Equal(t, "iPad", webHostDevice(v, "iPad").Name) },
		},
		{
			name: "TouchDevice a minute after the last time",
			prep: func(t *testing.T, l *Loop, clk *webHostClock) func() {
				return func() {
					clk.at = clk.at.Add(time.Minute)
					l.TouchDevice("iPhone")
				}
			},
			want: func(t *testing.T, v WebView, _ []Event) {
				assert.True(t, webHostDevice(v, "iPhone").LastSeen.After(webHostDevice(v, "iPhone").Paired))
			},
		},
		{
			name: "SetNotify",
			prep: func(t *testing.T, l *Loop, clk *webHostClock) func() {
				return func() {
					_, err := l.SetNotify("iPhone", NotifyChange{Waiting: webHostPtr(false)})
					require.NoError(t, err)
				}
			},
			want: func(t *testing.T, v WebView, _ []Event) { assert.False(t, webHostDevice(v, "iPhone").Notify.Waiting) },
		},
		{
			name: "SetPushSubscription",
			prep: func(t *testing.T, l *Loop, clk *webHostClock) func() {
				return func() {
					_, err := l.SetPushSubscription("iPhone", webHostSub(t, "web.push.apple.com"))
					require.NoError(t, err)
				}
			},
			want: func(t *testing.T, v WebView, _ []Event) { assert.Equal(t, PushOK, webHostDevice(v, "iPhone").Push) },
		},
		{
			name: "PushResult",
			prep: func(t *testing.T, l *Loop, clk *webHostClock) func() {
				_, err := l.SetPushSubscription("iPhone", webHostSub(t, "web.push.apple.com"))
				require.NoError(t, err)
				return func() { l.PushResult("iPhone", PushExpired) }
			},
			want: func(t *testing.T, v WebView, _ []Event) {
				assert.Equal(t, PushExpired, webHostDevice(v, "iPhone").Push)
			},
		},
	}
	for _, tc := range cases {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			l, clk, _ := webHostLoop(t)
			act := tc.prep(t, l, clk)
			l.Sync()
			webHostDrainWakes(l)

			act()

			select {
			case <-l.Wakes():
			case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
				t.Fatal("no wake after the call")
			}
			events := l.Sync()
			var view *WebView
			for _, ev := range events {
				if mc, ok := ev.(ModelChanged); ok {
					v := mc.View.Web
					view = &v
				}
			}
			require.NotNil(t, view, "the Sync after the wake carries the model view")
			tc.want(t, *view, events)
		})
	}
}

// A call that changed nothing wakes no client: a wake has the server build and
// diff every view, and TouchDevice runs on every authenticated request.
func TestWebHostLoop_ACallThatChangesNothingWakesNoClient(t *testing.T) {
	l, _, hash := webHostLoop(t)

	name, ok := l.DeviceByToken(hash)
	assert.True(t, ok)
	assert.Equal(t, "iPhone", name)
	_, ok = l.DeviceByToken([32]byte{9})
	assert.False(t, ok)
	assert.Empty(t, l.PushTargets())
	_, err := l.VAPIDKey()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	l.TouchDevice("iPhone") // no minute has passed since the pairing
	l.TouchDevice("nobody")
	l.PushResult("nobody", PushExpired)
	l.PushResult("iPhone", PushExpired) // it has no subscription
	l.WebServing("", nil)               // it was not serving
	_, _, err = l.RedeemPairing("23456789")
	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, ErrPairingCode, "a refusal reaches the caller")
	_, err = l.SetNotify("nobody", NotifyChange{})
	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, ErrNoDevice)
	_, err = l.SetPushSubscription("nobody", webHostSub(t, "web.push.apple.com"))
	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, ErrNoDevice)

	assert.Empty(t, l.Wakes(), "no wake waits")
}

// A call after Stop runs nothing, so the ones that return an error must not
// answer with a success.
func TestWebHostLoop_AfterStopNoCallSucceeds(t *testing.T) {
	l, _, hash := webHostLoop(t)
	l.Stop()

	_, _, err := l.RedeemPairing("23456789")
	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, ErrWebOff, "a stopped loop pairs no one")
	_, err = l.SetNotify("iPhone", NotifyChange{})
	assert.Error(t, err)
	_, err = l.SetPushSubscription("iPhone", webHostSub(t, "web.push.apple.com"))
	assert.Error(t, err)
	_, err = l.VAPIDKey()
	assert.Error(t, err, "and hands out no key")
	_, ok := l.DeviceByToken(hash)
	assert.False(t, ok, "it knows no device")
}
```

```go
package core

// The WebHost methods of Loop: each runs its namesake on the model, on the
// loop's goroutine. They are not Core methods: only the daemon's web layer
// calls them, in process, and nothing about them crosses the wire. A call
// that changed what a client sees (the Web view) or queued an event wakes the
// client (webCall), so the next Sync publishes it.

var _ WebHost = (*Loop)(nil)

// RedeemPairing runs Model.RedeemPairing on the loop. After Stop it answers
// ErrWebOff: the loop runs nothing, and a nil error would read as a device.
func (l *Loop) RedeemPairing(code string) (name, token string, err error) {
	err = ErrWebOff
	l.webCall(func(m *Model) bool {
		name, token, err = m.RedeemPairing(code)
		return err == nil
	})
	return name, token, err
}

// DeviceByToken runs Model.DeviceByToken on the loop.
func (l *Loop) DeviceByToken(hash [32]byte) (name string, ok bool) {
	l.do(func(m *Model) { name, ok = m.DeviceByToken(hash) })
	return name, ok
}

// TouchDevice runs Model.TouchDevice on the loop; it wakes the client only
// when it wrote, since it runs on every authenticated request.
func (l *Loop) TouchDevice(name string) {
	l.webCall(func(m *Model) bool { return m.TouchDevice(name) })
}

// SetNotify runs Model.SetNotify on the loop. After Stop it answers ErrWebOff.
func (l *Loop) SetNotify(name string, c NotifyChange) (dev WebDevice, err error) {
	err = ErrWebOff
	l.webCall(func(m *Model) bool {
		dev, err = m.SetNotify(name, c)
		return err == nil
	})
	return dev, err
}

// SetPushSubscription runs Model.SetPushSubscription on the loop. After Stop
// it answers ErrWebOff.
func (l *Loop) SetPushSubscription(name string, sub PushSubscription) (dev WebDevice, err error) {
	err = ErrWebOff
	l.webCall(func(m *Model) bool {
		dev, err = m.SetPushSubscription(name, sub)
		return err == nil
	})
	return dev, err
}

// PushTargets runs Model.PushTargets on the loop.
func (l *Loop) PushTargets() []PushTarget { return get(l, (*Model).PushTargets) }

// PushResult runs Model.PushResult on the loop.
func (l *Loop) PushResult(device string, state PushState) {
	l.webCall(func(m *Model) bool { return m.PushResult(device, state) })
}

// VAPIDKey runs Model.VAPIDKey on the loop. After Stop it answers ErrWebOff.
func (l *Loop) VAPIDKey() (key VAPIDKey, err error) {
	err = ErrWebOff
	l.do(func(m *Model) { key, err = m.VAPIDKey() })
	return key, err
}

// WebServing runs Model.WebServing on the loop.
func (l *Loop) WebServing(addr string, err error) {
	l.webCall(func(m *Model) bool { return m.WebServing(addr, err) })
}

// webCall runs f on the loop and wakes the client when f says it changed the
// model's Web view, or when the model has events queued after it (a Notice).
// A wake has the server build and diff every view, so a call that changed
// nothing raises none. The wake is raised on the loop's goroutine, inside the
// call: Stop closes Wakes only once that goroutine has exited, so a signal
// from the caller's goroutine after the call could send on a closed channel.
func (l *Loop) webCall(f func(*Model) (changed bool)) {
	l.do(func(m *Model) {
		if f(m) || len(m.out.Events) > 0 {
			l.signal()
		}
	})
}
```

## Story 5: `loom web`: enable, disable, pair, devices, revoke and status

- **Developer:** intermediate-dev
- **Files:** `internal/cli/web.go`, `internal/cli/web_test.go`, `main.go`, `internal/cli/README.md`, `internal/cli/CLAUDE.md`
- **Interfaces:** exposes `cli.WebCmd`; uses `Core.Web`, `ConfigureWeb`, `PairDevice` and `RevokeDevice` (Story 3)
- **Verify:** `CGO_ENABLED=0 go test ./internal/cli/ .`
- **Review:** deep

`loom web` is a daemon client through `cli.Join`, like `loom account`: each subcommand makes the core request of the same name and reads the replica's `Web()`. `enable --listen <addr> --origin <url>` (both required) sends `ConfigureWeb`, then waits up to 5 s (`webWait`) for the daemon's web layer to say whether it serves (`WebView.Serving`, or `WebView.Error`) and reports which; every `ConfigureWeb` bumps `WebView.Rev` and the layer rebinds whenever `Rev` changes, so running `enable` again is the retry of a failed bind (the port was taken): `enable` calls `ConfigureWeb` once, and waits only for an answer whose `Rev` is past the one it read before the call (`webSettled`), so it never reports the state from before its own call; the one exception is the stored `listen` and `origin` while `Web().Serving` is true (`servesAlready`), which changes nothing, so open streams aren't dropped, and says it serves; `disable` sends `ConfigureWeb("", <stored origin>)`, which turns the web off and keeps the origin; `pair [--name <device>]` (default `iPhone`) sends `PairDevice` and prints the code as `XXXX-XXXX` with its expiry and the origin; `devices [--json]` lists the devices; `revoke <name>` or `revoke --all` sends `RevokeDevice`; `status` prints the settings, whether it serves or why not, and the device count. Nothing here validates a setting or a name: the model does, and its refusal is the command's error. The tests serve a model in process (`serveDaemon`) and play the web layer themselves, through `*core.Loop`'s `core.WebHost` methods (Story 4: `WebServing`, `RedeemPairing`, `SetNotify` with a `core.NotifyChange`, `SetPushSubscription`, `PushResult`). The story has four tasks: the command and `enable`, `disable`, `status`; then `pair`, `devices`, `revoke`; then `main.go`; then the package's `README.md` and `CLAUDE.md`. No code here holds a landmine marker.

### Task 5.1: `WebCmd`, `enable`, `disable` and `status`

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/cli/web.go`
- Test: `internal/cli/web_test.go`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `internal/cli/web_test.go`:

```go
package cli

import (
	"bytes"
	"errors"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
)

// runWeb runs `loom web args…` on a fresh command tree (newWebCmd), so no
// flag carries over from another test.
func runWeb(t *testing.T, args ...string) (stdout, stderr string, err error) {
	t.Helper()
	cmd := newWebCmd()
	var out, errOut bytes.Buffer
	cmd.SetOut(&out)
	cmd.SetErr(&errOut)
	cmd.SetArgs(args)
	err = cmd.Execute()
	return out.String(), errOut.String(), err
}

// webDaemon serves a model in process over a global dir of the test's own,
// so the web.json it writes is the test's.
func webDaemon(t *testing.T) *testDaemon {
	t.Helper()
	isolateWorkspaces(t)
	return serveDaemon(t, "true")
}

// webView is the web state the daemon serves now, as the test's client has
// it.
func webView(t *testing.T, d *testDaemon) core.WebView {
	t.Helper()
	require.NoError(t, d.client.FlushForTest())
	d.client.Sync()
	return d.client.Web()
}

// webLayer plays the daemon's web layer for a test that has none: whenever
// WebView.Rev differs from the last one it acted on (unlike the real layer,
// which also acts on its first view, this fake treats the Rev it finds when
// it starts as already acted on), it reports addr and serveErr through
// core.WebHost.WebServing, as the layer does after it binds (or fails to). A
// ConfigureWeb of any values bumps Rev and clears what the layer reported, so
// only a command that really sends one makes it report; and WebServing does
// not bump Rev, so it reports once per ConfigureWeb. It reads Rev through a
// client of its own, and the test's cleanup waits for it.
func webLayer(t *testing.T, d *testDaemon, addr string, serveErr error) {
	t.Helper()
	c, err := Join()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	c.Sync()
	acted := c.Web().Rev
	stop, done := make(chan struct{}), make(chan struct{})
	go func() {
		defer close(done)
		defer c.Close()
		for {
			select {
			case <-stop:
				return
			case <-time.After(5 * time.Millisecond):
			}
			c.Sync()
			if w := c.Web(); w.Rev != acted {
				acted = w.Rev
				d.loop.WebServing(addr, serveErr)
			}
		}
	}()
	t.Cleanup(func() {
		close(stop)
		<-done
	})
}

// webDevice pairs a device called name, as a phone would: a code, then its
// redemption.
func webDevice(t *testing.T, d *testDaemon, name string) {
	t.Helper()
	p, err := d.loop.PairDevice(name)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	_, _, err = d.loop.RedeemPairing(p.Code)
	require.NoError(t, err)
}

func TestWebEnable_ReportsServing(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)
	webLayer(t, d, "127.0.0.1:7420", nil)

	out, _, err := runWeb(t, "enable", "--listen", "127.0.0.1:7420", "--origin", "https://loom.example.com")

	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Contains(t, out, "Serving on 127.0.0.1:7420")
	assert.Contains(t, out, "https://loom.example.com")
	w := webView(t, d)
	assert.Equal(t, "127.0.0.1:7420", w.Listen)
	assert.Equal(t, "https://loom.example.com", w.Origin)
}

func TestWebEnable_ReportsWhyItIsNotServing(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)
	webLayer(t, d, "", errors.New("listen tcp 127.0.0.1:7420: bind: address already in use"))

	_, _, err := runWeb(t, "enable", "--listen", "127.0.0.1:7420", "--origin", "https://loom.example.com")

	require.Error(t, err)
	assert.Contains(t, err.Error(), "not serving")
	assert.Contains(t, err.Error(), "address already in use")
	assert.Equal(t, "127.0.0.1:7420", webView(t, d).Listen, "the settings are kept, as the command says")
}

func TestWebEnable_SaysWhenTheDaemonDoesNotAnswer(t *testing.T) {
	old := webWait
	webWait = 150 * time.Millisecond
	t.Cleanup(func() { webWait = old })
	d := webDaemon(t) // no web layer reports

	_, _, err := runWeb(t, "enable", "--listen", "127.0.0.1:7420", "--origin", "https://loom.example.com")

	require.Error(t, err)
	assert.Contains(t, err.Error(), "did not say")
	assert.Contains(t, err.Error(), "loom web status")
	assert.Equal(t, "127.0.0.1:7420", webView(t, d).Listen)
}

func TestWebEnable_AgainRetriesAFailedBind(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)
	require.NoError(t, d.loop.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))
	d.loop.WebServing("", errors.New("listen tcp 127.0.0.1:7420: bind: address already in use"))
	// The layer reports when Rev changes, so it says "serving" only if enable
	// sends a ConfigureWeb of the stored values, which bumps Rev and clears
	// the stale Error; an enable that sent nothing would fail with it.
	webLayer(t, d, "127.0.0.1:7420", nil)
	before := webView(t, d)

	out, _, err := runWeb(t, "enable", "--listen", "127.0.0.1:7420", "--origin", "https://loom.example.com")

	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Contains(t, out, "Serving on 127.0.0.1:7420")
	w := webView(t, d)
	assert.Equal(t, before.Rev+1, w.Rev, "one ConfigureWeb, not a disable and an enable")
	assert.True(t, w.Serving)
	assert.Empty(t, w.Error, "the failed bind's error is gone")
	assert.Equal(t, "127.0.0.1:7420", w.Listen)
	assert.Equal(t, "https://loom.example.com", w.Origin)
}

func TestWebEnable_AgainRetryFailsAgainWithTheNewError(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)
	require.NoError(t, d.loop.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))
	d.loop.WebServing("", errors.New("first: address already in use"))
	webLayer(t, d, "", errors.New("second: address already in use"))

	_, _, err := runWeb(t, "enable", "--listen", "127.0.0.1:7420", "--origin", "https://loom.example.com")

	require.Error(t, err)
	assert.Contains(t, err.Error(), "not serving")
	assert.Contains(t, err.Error(), "second: address already in use", "the retry's answer, not the stale one")
	assert.NotContains(t, err.Error(), "first")
}

func TestWebEnable_WhenItAlreadyServesChangesNothing(t *testing.T) {
	old := webWait
	webWait = 150 * time.Millisecond
	t.Cleanup(func() { webWait = old })
	d := webDaemon(t)
	require.NoError(t, d.loop.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))
	d.loop.WebServing("127.0.0.1:7420", nil)
	// No layer reports: a ConfigureWeb would clear the model's serving state
	// and nothing would set it again, so the command would time out.
	before := webView(t, d)

	out, _, err := runWeb(t, "enable", "--listen", "127.0.0.1:7420", "--origin", "https://loom.example.com")

	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Contains(t, out, "Serving on 127.0.0.1:7420")
	w := webView(t, d)
	assert.Equal(t, before.Rev, w.Rev, "no ConfigureWeb was sent, so open streams stay")
	assert.True(t, w.Serving, "the listener was not taken down")
	assert.Equal(t, "127.0.0.1:7420", w.Addr)
}

func TestWebEnable_NewValuesAreAPlainEnable(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)
	require.NoError(t, d.loop.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))
	d.loop.WebServing("", errors.New("listen tcp 127.0.0.1:7420: bind: address already in use"))
	webLayer(t, d, "127.0.0.1:7421", nil)

	out, _, err := runWeb(t, "enable", "--listen", "127.0.0.1:7421", "--origin", "https://loom.example.com")

	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Contains(t, out, "Serving on 127.0.0.1:7421")
	w := webView(t, d)
	assert.Equal(t, "127.0.0.1:7421", w.Listen)
	assert.Equal(t, "https://loom.example.com", w.Origin)
}

func TestServesAlready(t *testing.T) {
	const listen, origin = "127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"
	for _, tc := range []struct {
		name string
		w    core.WebView
		want bool
	}{
		{"stored values, serving", core.WebView{Listen: listen, Origin: origin, Serving: true, Addr: listen}, true},
		{"stored values, a failed bind", core.WebView{Listen: listen, Origin: origin, Error: "address already in use"}, false},
		{"stored values, nothing reported yet", core.WebView{Listen: listen, Origin: origin}, false},
		{"another listen, serving", core.WebView{Listen: "127.0.0.1:7421", Origin: origin, Serving: true, Addr: "127.0.0.1:7421"}, false},
		{"another origin, serving", core.WebView{Listen: listen, Origin: "https://other.example.com", Serving: true, Addr: listen}, false},
		{"off, the origin kept", core.WebView{Origin: origin}, false},
	} {
		assert.Equal(t, tc.want, servesAlready(tc.w, listen, origin), tc.name)
	}
}

func TestWebEnable_NeedsBothFlags(t *testing.T) {
	for _, args := range [][]string{
		{"enable"},
		{"enable", "--listen", "127.0.0.1:7420"},
		{"enable", "--origin", "https://loom.example.com"},
	} {
		_, _, err := runWeb(t, args...)
		require.Error(t, err, "%v", args)
		assert.Contains(t, err.Error(), "required flag(s)", "%v", args)
	}
}

func TestWebEnable_PassesTheDaemonsRefusalOn(t *testing.T) {
	for _, tc := range []struct{ name, listen, origin string }{
		{"a host name", "localhost:7420", "https://loom.example.com"},
		{"a public address", "0.0.0.0:7420", "https://loom.example.com"},
		{"port zero", "127.0.0.1:0", "https://loom.example.com"},
		{"a plain http origin", "127.0.0.1:7420", "http://loom.example.com"},
		{"an origin with a path", "127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com/app"},
	} {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			d := webDaemon(t)

			_, _, err := runWeb(t, "enable", "--listen", tc.listen, "--origin", tc.origin)

			require.Error(t, err)
			assert.Empty(t, webView(t, d).Listen, "a refused setting is not stored")
		})
	}
}

func TestWebSettled(t *testing.T) {
	const listen = "127.0.0.1:7420"
	const after = 5 // the Rev enable read before its ConfigureWeb
	for _, tc := range []struct {
		name string
		w    core.WebView
		want bool
	}{
		{"off", core.WebView{Rev: after + 1}, false},
		{"stored, nothing reported yet", core.WebView{Listen: listen, Rev: after + 1}, false},
		{"serving what was asked", core.WebView{Listen: listen, Serving: true, Addr: listen, Rev: after + 1}, true},
		{"serving what was asked, before the call", core.WebView{Listen: listen, Serving: true, Addr: listen, Rev: after}, false},
		{"serving the previous address", core.WebView{Listen: listen, Serving: true, Addr: "127.0.0.1:7000", Rev: after + 1}, false},
		{"another address is stored", core.WebView{Listen: "127.0.0.1:7000", Serving: true, Addr: "127.0.0.1:7000", Rev: after + 1}, false},
		{"it failed to listen", core.WebView{Listen: listen, Error: "address already in use", Rev: after + 1}, true},
		{"it failed to listen, before the call", core.WebView{Listen: listen, Error: "address already in use", Rev: after}, false},
		{"another address failed", core.WebView{Listen: "127.0.0.1:7000", Error: "address already in use", Rev: after + 1}, false},
	} {
		assert.Equal(t, tc.want, webSettled(tc.w, listen, after), tc.name)
	}
}

func TestSameAddr(t *testing.T) {
	assert.True(t, sameAddr("127.0.0.1:7420", "127.0.0.1:7420"))
	assert.True(t, sameAddr("[::1]:7420", "[0:0:0:0:0:0:0:1]:7420"), "one address, two spellings")
	assert.False(t, sameAddr("127.0.0.1:7420", "127.0.0.1:7421"))
	assert.False(t, sameAddr("127.0.0.1:7420", "[::1]:7420"))
	assert.True(t, sameAddr("not an address", "not an address"), "unparsable text compares as text")
	assert.False(t, sameAddr("not an address", "127.0.0.1:7420"))
}

func TestWebDisable_TurnsItOffAndKeepsTheOrigin(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)
	require.NoError(t, d.loop.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))
	webDevice(t, d, "iPhone")

	out, _, err := runWeb(t, "disable")

	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Contains(t, out, "off")
	w := webView(t, d)
	assert.Empty(t, w.Listen)
	assert.Equal(t, "https://loom.example.com", w.Origin)
	assert.Len(t, w.Devices, 1, "paired devices are kept")
}

func TestWebDisable_WhenOffChangesNothing(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)

	out, _, err := runWeb(t, "disable")

	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Contains(t, out, "already off")

	assert.Empty(t, w.Origin)
}

func TestWebStatus_WhenOff(t *testing.T) {
	webDaemon(t)

	require.NoError(t, err)

}

func TestWebStatus_WhenServing(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)
	require.NoError(t, d.loop.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))

func TestWebStatus_SaysWhyItDoesNotServe(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)
	require.NoError(t, d.loop.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))
	d.loop.WebServing("", errors.New("listen tcp 127.0.0.1:7420: bind: address already in use"))

	out, _, err := runWeb(t, "status")

	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Regexp(t, `Serving:\s+no: listen tcp 127\.0\.0\.1:7420: bind: address already in use`, out)
}
```
