Create `internal/cli/web.go`:

```go
package cli

import (
	"fmt"
	"io"
	"net/netip"
	"strconv"
	"time"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
	"github.com/spf13/cobra"
)

// WebCmd is `loom web`: turn on the daemon's web app, pair and revoke the
// phones that use it, and see its state, as clients of the daemon. main
// attaches it.
var WebCmd = newWebCmd()

// webWait bounds how long `enable` waits for the daemon's web layer to say
// whether it serves: it reports once it has bound the listener, or failed
// to.
var webWait = 5 * time.Second

// newWebCmd builds the `loom web` command tree, with flags of its own:
// each call is independent (tests build one per run).
func newWebCmd() *cobra.Command {
	cmd := &cobra.Command{
		Use:   "web",
		Short: "Serve loom's fleet to a paired phone: enable, disable, pair, devices, revoke, status",
	}
	cmd.AddCommand(
		newWebEnableCmd(),
		newWebDisableCmd(),
		newWebStatusCmd(),
	)
	return cmd
}

func newWebEnableCmd() *cobra.Command {
	var listen, origin string
	cmd := &cobra.Command{
		Use:   "enable --listen <addr> --origin <url>",
		Short: "Turn the web app on, or change where it listens and its public address",
		Long: "Turn the web app on: the daemon listens on --listen (a loopback address, " +
			"127.0.0.1:<port> or [::1]:<port>, which the tunnel reaches) and answers only " +
			"requests for --origin (the https URL the phone opens). The daemon applies it " +
			"at once; this command waits a few seconds to say whether it serves. " +
			"Run it again to retry a bind that failed (the port was taken); with the " +
			"same values while it serves, it changes nothing.",
		Args: cobra.NoArgs,
		RunE: func(c *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
			loomErrors(c)
			out := c.OutOrStdout()
			return withDaemon(c, func(d *client) error {
				w := d.Web()
				if servesAlready(w, listen, origin) {
					// A ConfigureWeb would make the layer close the listener
					// and rebind, dropping the open streams for nothing.
					fmt.Fprintf(out, "Serving on %s for %s (nothing changed)\nPair a phone with: loom web pair\n", w.Addr, w.Origin)
					return nil
				}
				// Every ConfigureWeb bumps Rev and the layer rebinds when it
				// changes, so this is also the retry of a failed bind; the
				// wait wants an answer past the Rev read here.
				if err := d.ConfigureWeb(listen, origin); err != nil {
					return err
				}
				return d.awaitServing(out, listen, w.Rev)
			})
		},
	}
	cmd.Flags().StringVar(&listen, "listen", "", "Loopback address to listen on, 127.0.0.1:<port> or [::1]:<port>")
	cmd.Flags().StringVar(&origin, "origin", "", "Public address of the app, https://<host>")
	_ = cmd.MarkFlagRequired("listen")
	_ = cmd.MarkFlagRequired("origin")
	return cmd
}

// servesAlready reports whether enabling listen and origin would change
// nothing: the daemon holds exactly these settings and is serving them. The
// comparison is exact, as the model's is: a differently spelled address is a
// change on its own. A ConfigureWeb of the same values would still bump Rev
// and make the web layer close the listener and bind again, so the caller
// sends none. Settings that failed to bind, or that the layer has not
// answered yet, are not served.
func servesAlready(w core.WebView, listen, origin string) bool {
	return w.Serving && w.Listen == listen && w.Origin == origin
}

// awaitServing waits, up to webWait, for the daemon to say whether it
// serves listen, and reports which. after is the WebView.Rev the caller read
// before its ConfigureWeb: only an answer with a later Rev counts. The
// settings are stored either way.
func (c *client) awaitServing(out io.Writer, listen string, after uint64) error {
	var w core.WebView
	settled, err := c.await(webWait, func([]core.Event) bool {
		w = c.Web()
		return webSettled(w, listen, after)
	})
	switch {
	case err != nil:
		return err
	case !settled:
		return fmt.Errorf("saved, but the daemon did not say within %s whether it serves %s; see loom web status", webWait, listen)
	case w.Serving && sameAddr(w.Addr, listen):
		fmt.Fprintf(out, "Serving on %s for %s\nPair a phone with: loom web pair\n", w.Addr, w.Origin)
		return nil
	default:
		return fmt.Errorf("saved, but not serving: %s; fix it and run loom web enable again", w.Error)
	}
}

// webSettled reports whether w answers a request to listen at listen: its
// Rev is past after (the Rev read before the request, which every
// ConfigureWeb bumps, so a state from before the request never counts), the
// daemon holds that address, and it either serves it or says why it does
// not. A WebView still serving the previous address, or holding the previous
// error, is not an answer yet.
func webSettled(w core.WebView, listen string, after uint64) bool {
	if w.Rev <= after || !sameAddr(w.Listen, listen) {
		return false
	}
	return w.Error != "" || (w.Serving && sameAddr(w.Addr, listen))
}

// sameAddr reports whether a and b are the same host and port, however
// spelled (an IPv6 address has several); text that is not an address
// compares as text.
func sameAddr(a, b string) bool {
	pa, errA := netip.ParseAddrPort(a)
	pb, errB := netip.ParseAddrPort(b)
	if errA != nil || errB != nil {
		return a == b
	}
	return pa == pb
}

func newWebDisableCmd() *cobra.Command {
	return &cobra.Command{
		Use:   "disable",
		Short: "Turn the web app off; paired devices and the origin are kept",
		Args:  cobra.NoArgs,
		RunE: func(c *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
			loomErrors(c)
			out := c.OutOrStdout()
			return withDaemon(c, func(d *client) error {
				w := d.Web()
				if w.Listen == "" {
					fmt.Fprintln(out, "The web app is already off.")
					return nil
				}
				if err := d.ConfigureWeb("", w.Origin); err != nil {
					return err
				}
				fmt.Fprintln(out, "The web app is off. Paired devices are kept; loom web enable turns it on again.")
				return nil
			})
		},
	}
}

func newWebStatusCmd() *cobra.Command {
	return &cobra.Command{
		Use:   "status",
		Short: "Show the web app's settings, whether it serves, and how many devices are paired",
		Args:  cobra.NoArgs,
		RunE: func(c *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
			loomErrors(c)
			return withDaemon(c, func(d *client) error {
				writeWebStatus(c.OutOrStdout(), d.Web())
				return nil
			})
		},
	}
}

// writeWebStatus prints w: whether the web is on, where it listens and for
// which origin, whether it serves or why not, and the device count.
func writeWebStatus(out io.Writer, w core.WebView) {
	row := func(label, value string) { fmt.Fprintf(out, "%-9s %s\n", label+":", value) }
	if w.Listen == "" {
		row("Web", "off")
	} else {
		row("Web", "on")
		row("Listen", w.Listen)
	}
	if w.Origin != "" {
		row("Origin", w.Origin)
	}
	switch {
	case w.Listen == "":
		if w.Error != "" {
			row("Error", w.Error)
		}
	case w.Serving:
		row("Serving", "yes, on "+w.Addr)
	case w.Error != "":
		row("Serving", "no: "+w.Error)
	default:
		row("Serving", "no")
	}
	row("Devices", strconv.Itoa(len(w.Devices)))
}
```

```go
// pushSubscription is a subscription the model accepts: an Apple push
// endpoint, a real P-256 point and 16 bytes of auth.
func pushSubscription(t *testing.T) core.PushSubscription {
	t.Helper()
	key, err := ecdh.P256().GenerateKey(rand.Reader)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	auth := make([]byte, 16)
	_, err = rand.Read(auth)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	return core.PushSubscription{
		Endpoint: "https://web.push.apple.com/abc",
		P256DH:   base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(key.PublicKey().Bytes()),
		Auth:     base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(auth),
	}
}

// boolPtr is a pointer to b, for a core.NotifyChange field.
func boolPtr(b bool) *bool { return &b }

// lineWith is the first line of out that contains s.
func lineWith(t *testing.T, out, s string) string {
	t.Helper()
	for _, line := range strings.Split(out, "\n") {
		if strings.Contains(line, s) {
			return line
		}
	}
	t.Fatalf("no line with %q in:\n%s", s, out)
	return ""
}

const codeAlphabet = "23456789ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTUVWXYZ"

var pairingCodeLine = regexp.MustCompile(`Pairing code for (.+): ([` + codeAlphabet + `]{4}-[` + codeAlphabet + `]{4})`)

func TestWebPair_PrintsTheCodeTheDaemonRedeems(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)
	require.NoError(t, d.loop.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))

	out, _, err := runWeb(t, "pair")

	require.NoError(t, err)
	m := pairingCodeLine.FindStringSubmatch(out)
	require.NotNil(t, m, out)
	assert.Equal(t, "iPhone", m[1], "the default device name")
	assert.Contains(t, out, "https://loom.example.com", "where to enter it")
	assert.Regexp(t, `expires at \d\d:\d\d:\d\d`, out)
	name, token, err := d.loop.RedeemPairing(m[2])
	require.NoError(t, err, "the printed code, dash and all, is the daemon's")
	assert.Equal(t, "iPhone", name)
	assert.NotEmpty(t, token)
	assert.Len(t, webView(t, d).Devices, 1)
}

func TestWebPair_NamesTheDevice(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)
	require.NoError(t, d.loop.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))

	out, _, err := runWeb(t, "pair", "--name", "Work iPad")

	require.NoError(t, err)
	m := pairingCodeLine.FindStringSubmatch(out)
	require.NotNil(t, m, out)
	name, _, err := d.loop.RedeemPairing(m[2])
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, "Work iPad", name)
}

	_, _, err := runWeb(t, "pair")

func TestWebDevices_ListsEachDevice(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)
	require.NoError(t, d.loop.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))
	webDevice(t, d, "iPhone")
	webDevice(t, d, "iPad")
	_, err := d.loop.SetNotify("iPhone", core.NotifyChange{Waiting: boolPtr(true)})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	_, err = d.loop.SetPushSubscription("iPhone", pushSubscription(t))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	_, err = d.loop.SetPushSubscription("iPad", pushSubscription(t))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	d.loop.PushResult("iPad", core.PushFailing(500))

	out, _, err := runWeb(t, "devices")

	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Regexp(t, `NAME\s+PAIRED\s+LAST SEEN\s+NOTIFY\s+PUSH`, out)
	phone := lineWith(t, out, "iPhone")
	assert.Regexp(t, `\swaiting\s+ok\s*$`, phone)
	assert.NotContains(t, phone, "finished")
	assert.Contains(t, lineWith(t, out, "iPad"), "failing: 500")
}

	assert.NotContains(t, out, "NAME")
}

func TestWebDevices_JSON(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)
	require.NoError(t, d.loop.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))
	webDevice(t, d, "iPhone")
	_, err := d.loop.SetNotify("iPhone", core.NotifyChange{Finished: boolPtr(true)})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	_, err = d.loop.SetPushSubscription("iPhone", pushSubscription(t))
	require.NoError(t, err)

	out, _, err := runWeb(t, "devices", "--json")

	require.NoError(t, err)
	var rows []webDeviceJSON
	require.NoError(t, json.Unmarshal([]byte(out), &rows), out)
	require.Len(t, rows, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, "iPhone", rows[0].Name)
	assert.False(t, rows[0].Notify.Waiting)
	assert.True(t, rows[0].Notify.Finished)
	assert.Nil(t, rows[0].Notify.PausedUntil)
	assert.Equal(t, "ok", rows[0].Push)
	assert.False(t, rows[0].Paired.IsZero())
}

func TestWebDevices_JSONIsAnEmptyArrayWhenNonePaired(t *testing.T) {
	webDaemon(t)

	out, _, err := runWeb(t, "devices", "--json")

	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.JSONEq(t, `[]`, out)
}

func TestWebNotifyText(t *testing.T) {
	now := time.Date(2026, 10, 21, 12, 0, 0, 0, time.Local)
	later := now.Add(time.Hour)
	for _, tc := range []struct {
		name string
		n    core.NotifySettings
		want string
	}{
		{"none", core.NotifySettings{}, "off"},
		{"waiting", core.NotifySettings{Waiting: true}, "waiting"},
		{"both", core.NotifySettings{Waiting: true, Finished: true}, "waiting,finished"},
		{"paused", core.NotifySettings{Finished: true, PausedUntil: later}, "finished (paused until " + later.Format("15:04") + ")"},
		{"a pause that ended", core.NotifySettings{Waiting: true, PausedUntil: now.Add(-time.Minute)}, "waiting"},
		{"paused with nothing on", core.NotifySettings{PausedUntil: later}, "off"},
	} {
		assert.Equal(t, tc.want, notifyText(tc.n, now), tc.name)
	}
}

func TestWebSeenText(t *testing.T) {
	assert.Equal(t, "never", seenText(time.Time{}))
	at := time.Date(2026, 10, 21, 9, 5, 0, 0, time.Local)
	assert.Equal(t, "2026-10-21 09:05", seenText(at))
}

func TestWebRevoke_RemovesTheNamedDevice(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)
	require.NoError(t, d.loop.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))
	webDevice(t, d, "iPhone")
	webDevice(t, d, "iPad")

	out, _, err := runWeb(t, "revoke", "iPhone")

	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Contains(t, out, "Revoked iPhone")
	devs := webView(t, d).Devices
	require.Len(t, devs, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, "iPad", devs[0].Name)
}

func TestWebRevoke_AnUnknownNameIsAnError(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)
	require.NoError(t, d.loop.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))
	webDevice(t, d, "iPhone")

	_, _, err := runWeb(t, "revoke", "iphone")

	require.Error(t, err)
	assert.Contains(t, err.Error(), "no device has that name")
	assert.Len(t, webView(t, d).Devices, 1, "a name matches exactly, not by case")
}

func TestWebRevoke_AllRemovesEveryDevice(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)
	require.NoError(t, d.loop.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))
	webDevice(t, d, "iPhone")
	webDevice(t, d, "iPad")

	out, _, err := runWeb(t, "revoke", "--all")

	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Contains(t, out, "Revoked iPhone")
	assert.Contains(t, out, "Revoked iPad")
	assert.Empty(t, webView(t, d).Devices)

	out, _, err = runWeb(t, "revoke", "--all")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Contains(t, out, "No paired devices.")
}

func TestWebRevoke_NeedsOneNameOrAll(t *testing.T) {
	d := webDaemon(t)
	require.NoError(t, d.loop.ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", "https://loom.example.com"))
	webDevice(t, d, "iPhone")

	_, _, err := runWeb(t, "revoke")
	require.Error(t, err, "neither a name nor --all")
	_, _, err = runWeb(t, "revoke", "iPhone", "--all")
	require.Error(t, err, "both a name and --all")
	_, _, err = runWeb(t, "revoke", "iPhone", "iPad")
	require.Error(t, err, "two names")

	assert.Len(t, webView(t, d).Devices, 1, "none of them revoked anything")
}
```

```go
// defaultDeviceName is what `pair` calls a phone when --name is not given.
const defaultDeviceName = "iPhone"

// webTimeFormat is how the device list shows a moment, in local time.
const webTimeFormat = "2006-01-02 15:04"

func newWebPairCmd() *cobra.Command {
	var name string
	cmd := &cobra.Command{
		Use:   "pair [--name <device>]",
		Short: "Make a one-time code that pairs a phone",
		Long: "Make a code the phone trades for its own revocable token. It works once, for ten " +
			"minutes; a second pair for the same name replaces the first code. The web app " +
			"must be on (loom web enable).",
		Args: cobra.NoArgs,
		RunE: func(c *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
			loomErrors(c)
			out := c.OutOrStdout()
			return withDaemon(c, func(d *client) error {
				p, err := d.PairDevice(name)
				if err != nil {
					return err
				}
				fmt.Fprintf(out, "Pairing code for %s: %s\n", strings.TrimSpace(name), formatPairingCode(p.Code))
				fmt.Fprintf(out, "Open %s on the phone and enter it. It works once and expires at %s.\n",
					d.Web().Origin, p.Expires.Local().Format("15:04:05"))
				return nil
			})
		},
	}
	cmd.Flags().StringVar(&name, "name", defaultDeviceName, "Name for the device")
	return cmd
}

// formatPairingCode shows an 8-character code as XXXX-XXXX, the way the app
// accepts it (it ignores the dash).
func formatPairingCode(code string) string {
	if len(code) != 8 {
		return code
	}
	return code[:4] + "-" + code[4:]
}

func newWebDevicesCmd() *cobra.Command {
	var asJSON bool
	cmd := &cobra.Command{
		Use:   "devices",
		Short: "List the paired devices: when paired and last seen, their notifications and push state",
		Args:  cobra.NoArgs,
		RunE: func(c *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
			loomErrors(c)
			return withDaemon(c, func(d *client) error {
				devs := d.Web().Devices
				if asJSON {
					return writeDevicesJSON(c.OutOrStdout(), devs)
				}
				return writeDevicesTable(c.OutOrStdout(), devs, time.Now())
			})
		},
	}
	cmd.Flags().BoolVar(&asJSON, "json", false, "Print the devices as a JSON array")
	return cmd
}

// webDeviceJSON is a device as `devices --json` prints it: the settings and
// state, never the token.
type webDeviceJSON struct {
	Name     string        `json:"name"`
	Paired   time.Time     `json:"paired"`
	LastSeen *time.Time    `json:"last_seen,omitempty"`
	Notify   webNotifyJSON `json:"notify"`
	// Push is the subscription's state: "" (none), "ok", "expired" or
	// "failing: <status>".
	Push string `json:"push"`
}

type webNotifyJSON struct {
	Waiting     bool       `json:"waiting"`
	Finished    bool       `json:"finished"`
	PausedUntil *time.Time `json:"paused_until,omitempty"`
}

func writeDevicesJSON(out io.Writer, devs []core.WebDevice) error {
	rows := make([]webDeviceJSON, 0, len(devs))
	for _, dev := range devs {
		row := webDeviceJSON{
			Name:   dev.Name,
			Paired: dev.Paired,
			Notify: webNotifyJSON{Waiting: dev.Notify.Waiting, Finished: dev.Notify.Finished},
			Push:   string(dev.Push),
		}
		if !dev.LastSeen.IsZero() {
			t := dev.LastSeen
			row.LastSeen = &t
		}
		if !dev.Notify.PausedUntil.IsZero() {
			t := dev.Notify.PausedUntil
			row.Notify.PausedUntil = &t
		}
		rows = append(rows, row)
	}
	enc := json.NewEncoder(out)
	enc.SetIndent("", "  ")
	return enc.Encode(rows)
}

func writeDevicesTable(out io.Writer, devs []core.WebDevice, now time.Time) error {
	if len(devs) == 0 {
		_, err := fmt.Fprintln(out, "No paired devices.")
		return err
	}
	w := tabwriter.NewWriter(out, 0, 4, 2, ' ', 0)
	fmt.Fprintln(w, "NAME\tPAIRED\tLAST SEEN\tNOTIFY\tPUSH")
	for _, dev := range devs {
		fmt.Fprintf(w, "%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\n", dev.Name, dev.Paired.Local().Format(webTimeFormat),
			seenText(dev.LastSeen), notifyText(dev.Notify, now), pushText(dev.Push))
	}
	return w.Flush()
}

// seenText is when a device was last seen, or "never".
func seenText(t time.Time) string {
	if t.IsZero() {
		return "never"
	}
	return t.Local().Format(webTimeFormat)
}

// notifyText lists the kinds of notification a device gets, with "off" for
// none, and until when they are paused while that is still ahead of now.
func notifyText(n core.NotifySettings, now time.Time) string {
	var kinds []string
	if n.Waiting {
		kinds = append(kinds, "waiting")
	}
	if n.Finished {
		kinds = append(kinds, "finished")
	}
	if len(kinds) == 0 {
		return "off"
	}
	s := strings.Join(kinds, ",")
	if n.PausedUntil.After(now) {
		s += " (paused until " + n.PausedUntil.Local().Format("15:04") + ")"
	}
	return s
}

// pushText is a subscription's state as the table shows it.
func pushText(s core.PushState) string {
	if s == core.PushNone {
		return "none"
	}
	return string(s)
}

func newWebRevokeCmd() *cobra.Command {
	var all bool
	cmd := &cobra.Command{
		Use:   "revoke <name> | --all",
		Short: "Unpair a device, or every device: its app is signed out and its push subscription dropped",
		Args:  cobra.MaximumNArgs(1),
		RunE: func(c *cobra.Command, args []string) error {
			loomErrors(c)
			if all == (len(args) == 1) {
				return errors.New("name one device to revoke, or pass --all")
			}
			out := c.OutOrStdout()
			return withDaemon(c, func(d *client) error {
				if !all {
					if err := d.RevokeDevice(args[0]); err != nil {
						return err
					}
					fmt.Fprintf(out, "Revoked %s\n", args[0])
					return nil
				}
				devs := d.Web().Devices
				if len(devs) == 0 {
					fmt.Fprintln(out, "No paired devices.")
					return nil
				}
				for i, dev := range devs {
					if err := d.RevokeDevice(dev.Name); err != nil {
						return fmt.Errorf("revoked %d of %d devices, then %s failed: %w", i, len(devs), dev.Name, err)
					}
					fmt.Fprintf(out, "Revoked %s\n", dev.Name)
				}
				return nil
			})
		},
	}
	cmd.Flags().BoolVar(&all, "all", false, "Revoke every paired device")
	return cmd
}
```


## Story 6: The web layer: its client, its listener, the daemon's wiring, the guard, pairing and auth

- **Developer:** intermediate-dev
- **Files:** `internal/web/web.go`, `internal/web/server.go`, `internal/web/auth.go`, `internal/web/jsonio.go`, `internal/web/web_test.go`, `internal/web/auth_test.go`, `internal/web/testmain_test.go`, `internal/web/CLAUDE.md`, `internal/web/README.md`, `internal/daemon/serve.go`, `internal/daemon/serve_test.go`
- **Interfaces:** exposes `web.Options`, `web.Start`, `(*web.Layer).Close` and the package seams of the contract (`sink`, `addSink`, `mux`, `guard`, `deviceHandler`, `authed`, the JSON helpers, the cookie helpers, the `Layer` fields); uses `rpc.DialAs` and `rpc.RoleWeb` (Story 2), `Core.Web` (Story 3) and `core.WebHost` (Story 4)
- **Verify:** `CGO_ENABLED=0 go test ./internal/web/... ./internal/daemon/ && CGO_ENABLED=0 go test -tags e2e ./e2e/...`
- **Review:** deep

This story creates the package `internal/web` and starts it from the daemon. `web.Start` joins the daemon's own `rpc.Server` over `net.Pipe` as a `web` client (so it never counts toward the notice backlog), builds the layer's parts, and drains its client as the TUI does (`Wakes`, then `Sync`): every batch goes to each sink in turn, and each published `WebView` is applied by closing the listener and binding a loopback one for its `listen` and `origin` whenever its `Rev` (bumped by every `ConfigureWeb`, never by a report) differs from the last the layer acted on (no listener while `listen` is empty; the outcome reported through `WebHost.WebServing`, which leaves `Rev` alone; a bind that fails leaves the web off, with the reason, until the next `ConfigureWeb`). Every request passes `guard` (the Host and Origin checks, the security headers, the 1 MiB cap); `POST /api/v1/pair` trades a code for the `__Host-loom` device cookie, and `authed` is the check every later route wraps itself in. A panic in the drain or the listener stops the layer and keeps a notice, never the daemon: each goroutine the layer starts defers `recoverPanic` first, which calls `fail`, and `fail` returns at once and stops the layer on a goroutine of its own, through the same one-time stop `Close` runs, so a fail racing a Close neither hangs nor stops twice. The daemon starts the layer after the mod server and closes it first on a stop, before the bye. Five tasks: the JSON helpers with the package's `CLAUDE.md` (the coverage lint fails a Go package without one), pairing and the cookie, the guard, the lifecycle, and the daemon's wiring with the README. No code here holds a landmine marker. Two assumptions cross stories: the device cookie holds the token text `RedeemPairing` returns (unpadded base64url of 32 random bytes), and `authed` asks `DeviceByToken` for `sha256.Sum256(raw)` of the 32 bytes that text decodes to, which is what Story 4's model stores (`TestPair_ACodeFromTheModelPairsAPhoneOnce` fails if the two sides disagree); and Story 3's `ConfigureWeb` and `PairDevice` are called on an ordinary `rpc.Client` in the tests.

### Task 6.1: The JSON helpers, the test main and the package's CLAUDE.md

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/web/jsonio.go`
- Create: `internal/web/web_test.go`
- Create: `internal/web/testmain_test.go`
- Create: `internal/web/CLAUDE.md`

The coverage lint (`tools/claudemd`) fails a Go package with no `CLAUDE.md`, and `TestEveryConfigReachingPackageIsolatesLoomDirs` fails one whose tests can reach `config` without a `TestMain` that isolates the loom dirs, so both land with the first file.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `internal/web/testmain_test.go`:

```go
package web

import (
	"context"
	"fmt"
	"os"
	"testing"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/internal/testenv"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/session/tmux"
)

// TestMain points LOOM_HOME and LOOM_GLOBAL_DIR at throwaway directories, so
// no test here can resolve the developer's real ~/.loom, and every tmux
// command at a private server: the tests boot a model, whose background jobs
// outlive a test's own t.Setenv and must not reach the developer's default
// server, where loom's live sessions are.
func TestMain(m *testing.M) {
	cleanup := testenv.MustIsolateLoomDirs()
	sock := fmt.Sprintf("lt-web-%d", os.Getpid())
	if err := os.Setenv(tmux.EnvTmuxSocket, sock); err != nil {
		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "set %s: %v\n", tmux.EnvTmuxSocket, err)
		os.Exit(1)
	}
	code := m.Run()
	_ = tmux.CommandOnSocket(context.Background(), sock, "kill-server").Run()
	cleanup()
	os.Exit(code)
}
```

```go
package web

import (
	"net/http"
	"net/http/httptest"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
)

func TestWriteJSON_AnswersTheStatusAndTheBody(t *testing.T) {
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	writeJSON(rec, http.StatusCreated, map[string]any{"id": 7, "notices": []string{}})
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusCreated, rec.Code)
	assert.Equal(t, "application/json", rec.Header().Get("Content-Type"))
	assert.JSONEq(t, `{"id": 7, "notices": []}`, rec.Body.String())
}

func TestWriteJSON_AValueThatWillNotEncodeIsA500(t *testing.T) {
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	writeJSON(rec, http.StatusOK, map[string]any{"c": make(chan int)})
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusInternalServerError, rec.Code, "never a 200 with half a body")
	assert.JSONEq(t, `{"error": "the answer could not be encoded"}`, rec.Body.String())
}

func TestWriteError_IsAnErrorObject(t *testing.T) {
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	writeError(rec, http.StatusConflict, "the session is already paused")
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusConflict, rec.Code)
	assert.JSONEq(t, `{"error": "the session is already paused"}`, rec.Body.String())
}

func TestReadJSON(t *testing.T) {
	type body struct {
		Code string `json:"code"`
	}
	for _, tc := range []struct {
		name  string
		body  string
		limit int64  // a cap on the body, as guard sets; 0 for none
		want  string // part of the error, or "" for none
	}{
		{"one object", `{"code": "K7QM"}`, 0, ""},
		{"white space after it", "{\"code\": \"K7QM\"}\n  \n", 0, ""},
		{"a field the route does not take", `{"code": "K7QM", "extra": 1}`, 0, `unknown field "extra"`},
		{"nothing", ``, 0, "the body is empty"},
		{"only white space", " \n", 0, "the body is empty"},
		{"two objects", `{"code": "A"} {"code": "B"}`, 0, "more than one JSON value"},
		{"junk after the object", `{"code": "A"} x`, 0, "more than one JSON value"},
		{"not JSON", `not json`, 0, "the body is not the JSON this route takes"},
		{"truncated", `{"code": `, 0, "the body is not the JSON this route takes"},
		{"the wrong type", `{"code": 7}`, 0, "the body is not the JSON this route takes"},
		{"over the cap", `{"code": "K7QM-2XPA-and-more"}`, 16, "the body is over 16 bytes"},
	} {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, "/x", strings.NewReader(tc.body))
			if tc.limit > 0 {
				req.Body = http.MaxBytesReader(httptest.NewRecorder(), req.Body, tc.limit)
			}
			var got body
			err := readJSON(req, &got)
			if tc.want == "" {
				require.NoError(t, err)
				assert.Equal(t, "K7QM", got.Code)
				return
			}
			require.Error(t, err)
			assert.Contains(t, err.Error(), tc.want)
		})
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `internal/web/jsonio.go`:

```go
package web

import (
	"bytes"
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"io"
	"net/http"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/log"
)

// writeJSON answers status with v as a JSON body. A value that will not
// encode is logged and answered as a 500 with a fixed body: the encoding
// happens before the header, so a handler's bug never sends half a response.
func writeJSON(w http.ResponseWriter, status int, v any) {
	body, err := json.Marshal(v)
	if err != nil {
		log.For("web").Error("web.encode_failed", "err", err)
		status, body = http.StatusInternalServerError, []byte(`{"error":"the answer could not be encoded"}`)
	}
	w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
	w.WriteHeader(status)
	_, _ = w.Write(append(body, '\n'))
}

// writeError answers status with {"error": msg}.
func writeError(w http.ResponseWriter, status int, msg string) {
	writeJSON(w, status, map[string]string{"error": msg})
}

// readJSON decodes r's body, which must hold exactly one JSON value, into v.
// An empty body, a field v does not have, a second value and a body past the
// cap (guard's http.MaxBytesReader) are errors whose text can go back to the
// client in a 400.
func readJSON(r *http.Request, v any) error {
	raw, err := io.ReadAll(r.Body)
	if err != nil {
		var tooBig *http.MaxBytesError
		if errors.As(err, &tooBig) {
			return fmt.Errorf("the body is over %d bytes", tooBig.Limit)
		}
		return fmt.Errorf("the body could not be read: %w", err)
	}
	if len(bytes.TrimSpace(raw)) == 0 {
		return errors.New("the body is empty")
	}
	dec := json.NewDecoder(bytes.NewReader(raw))
	dec.DisallowUnknownFields()
	if err := dec.Decode(v); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("the body is not the JSON this route takes: %w", err)
	}
	if _, err := dec.Token(); !errors.Is(err, io.EOF) {
		return errors.New("the body holds more than one JSON value")
	}
	return nil
}
```

Create `internal/web/CLAUDE.md` (the first line is the dir's name, as the lint requires; later tasks append rules at the end of the file, and Story 13 appends after them):

```markdown
# internal/web

The daemon's web layer, started by `loom serve`: a client of the daemon's own `rpc.Server` plus `core.WebHost`, serving the loom PWA on a loopback port that a Cloudflare tunnel reaches (pairing, a JSON API, a live stream, Web Push). It holds no session state: the model owns every session and every device record.

## Rules when modifying this package

- **Decode a request body only with `readJSON`, and answer only with `writeJSON` and `writeError`.** `readJSON` refuses an empty body, a field the route doesn't take, a second value and a body past the cap, so a misspelt field is a 400 rather than a silently ignored value; the writers encode before the header, so a value that won't encode is a 500 with a fixed body, never a 200 with half of one. **Enforced** by `TestReadJSON` and `TestWriteJSON_AValueThatWillNotEncodeIsA500`.
```

```go
package web

import (
	"bytes"
	"crypto/sha256"
	"encoding/base64"
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"net/http"
	"net/http/httptest"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
)

// scriptedHost is a core.WebHost scripted by a test. It embeds the
// interface, so a method it does not override panics: a test that reaches
// one has found a call it did not expect.
type scriptedHost struct {
	core.WebHost

	// RedeemPairing answers these, and remembers the codes it was asked about.
	name, token string
	err         error
	redeemed    []string

	// DeviceByToken looks a hash up in devices, which maps the SHA-256 of a
	// device's 32 token bytes to its name (hold fills it), and remembers
	// each hash it was asked.
	devices map[[32]byte]string
	asked   [][32]byte

	// TouchDevice remembers the devices it was told of.
	touched []string
}

// hold makes the host know the device called name by the token
// deviceToken(seed), as the model does: by the SHA-256 of the token's 32
// bytes, not of its text.
func (h *scriptedHost) hold(name string, seed byte) {
	if h.devices == nil {
		h.devices = map[[32]byte]string{}
	}
	h.devices[deviceTokenHash(seed)] = name
}

func (h *scriptedHost) RedeemPairing(code string) (string, string, error) {
	h.redeemed = append(h.redeemed, code)
	return h.name, h.token, h.err
}

func (h *scriptedHost) DeviceByToken(hash [32]byte) (string, bool) {
	h.asked = append(h.asked, hash)
	name, ok := h.devices[hash]
	return name, ok
}

func (h *scriptedHost) TouchDevice(name string) { h.touched = append(h.touched, name) }

// deviceToken is a device token as the model makes one: 32 bytes (all seed)
// as unpadded base64url, which is the text the cookie holds.
func deviceToken(seed byte) string {
	return base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(bytes.Repeat([]byte{seed}, tokenBytes))
}

// deviceTokenHash is the hash the model stores for deviceToken(seed): the
// SHA-256 of the 32 bytes the text decodes to, not of the text.
func deviceTokenHash(seed byte) [32]byte {
	return sha256.Sum256(bytes.Repeat([]byte{seed}, tokenBytes))
}

func TestTokenHash(t *testing.T) {
	good := deviceToken(7)
	hash, ok := tokenHash(good)
	require.True(t, ok)
	assert.Equal(t, deviceTokenHash(7), hash, "the hash of the 32 bytes the text decodes to, as the model stores it")
	assert.NotEqual(t, sha256.Sum256([]byte(good)), hash, "not the hash of the cookie's text")
	other, ok := tokenHash(deviceToken(8))
	require.True(t, ok)
	assert.NotEqual(t, hash, other, "another token, another hash")

	for name, bad := range map[string]string{
		"empty":                 "",
		"a word of 8 bytes":     "not-a-token",
		"31 bytes":              base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(bytes.Repeat([]byte{7}, tokenBytes-1)),
		"33 bytes":              base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(bytes.Repeat([]byte{7}, tokenBytes+1)),
		"64 bytes":              base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(bytes.Repeat([]byte{7}, 2*tokenBytes)),
		"padded":                good + "=",
		"the standard alphabet": good[:42] + "+",
		"a space":               good[:42] + " ",
		"not base64 at all":     "!!!!",
	} {
		_, ok := tokenHash(bad)
		assert.False(t, ok, name)
	}
}

func TestDeviceCookie(t *testing.T) {
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	setDeviceCookie(rec, deviceToken(1))
	cookies := rec.Result().Cookies()
	require.Len(t, cookies, 1)
	c := cookies[0]
	assert.Equal(t, "__Host-loom", c.Name)
	assert.Equal(t, deviceToken(1), c.Value)
	assert.Equal(t, "/", c.Path)
	assert.Empty(t, c.Domain, "a __Host- cookie names no domain")
	assert.True(t, c.Secure)
	assert.True(t, c.HttpOnly)
	assert.Equal(t, http.SameSiteStrictMode, c.SameSite)
	assert.Equal(t, 400*24*60*60, c.MaxAge, "400 days, in seconds")

	rec = httptest.NewRecorder()
	clearDeviceCookie(rec)
	cookies = rec.Result().Cookies()
	require.Len(t, cookies, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, "__Host-loom", cookies[0].Name)
	assert.Empty(t, cookies[0].Value)
	assert.Negative(t, cookies[0].MaxAge, "the browser drops it")
	assert.Equal(t, "/", cookies[0].Path)
	assert.True(t, cookies[0].Secure, "a __Host- cookie is refused unless Secure, a clearing one too")
}

// serveAuthed runs one GET carrying cookies through authed on a layer whose
// host is host, and returns the answer and the devices the handler served.
func serveAuthed(host *scriptedHost, cookies ...*http.Cookie) (*httptest.ResponseRecorder, []string) {
	l := &Layer{host: host}
	var served []string
	h := l.authed(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, device string) {
		served = append(served, device)
		writeJSON(w, http.StatusOK, map[string]string{"device": device})
	})
	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/api/v1/x", nil)
	for _, c := range cookies {
		req.AddCookie(c)
	}
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	h.ServeHTTP(rec, req)
	return rec, served
}

// requireRefusedUnasked fails unless rec is a 401 with an error object, the
// handler never ran, and the host was neither asked about a token nor told of
// a device.
func requireRefusedUnasked(t *testing.T, rec *httptest.ResponseRecorder, served []string, host *scriptedHost) {
	t.Helper()
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusUnauthorized, rec.Code)
	assert.Contains(t, rec.Body.String(), `"error"`)
	assert.Empty(t, served, "the handler never runs for an unpaired request")
	assert.Empty(t, host.asked, "a cookie that is not a token never reaches the host")
	assert.Empty(t, host.touched, "an unpaired request touches no device")
}

func TestAuthed_AGoodCookieNamesItsDevice(t *testing.T) {
	host := &scriptedHost{}
	host.hold("iPhone", 7)
	host.hold("iPad", 8)

	rec, served := serveAuthed(host, &http.Cookie{Name: cookieName, Value: deviceToken(7)})

	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusOK, rec.Code)
	assert.Equal(t, []string{"iPhone"}, served)
	require.Equal(t, [][32]byte{deviceTokenHash(7)}, host.asked, "asked once, by the SHA-256 of the token's 32 bytes")
	assert.NotEqual(t, sha256.Sum256([]byte(deviceToken(7))), host.asked[0], "not by the hash of the cookie's text")
	assert.Equal(t, []string{"iPhone"}, host.touched, "the device was seen")
}

func TestAuthed_TextThatDoesNotDecodeIsRefusedWithoutAskingTheHost(t *testing.T) {
	good := deviceToken(7)
	for name, text := range map[string]string{
		"the standard alphabet": good[:42] + "+",
		"padding":               good + "=",
		"a character in it":     good[:20] + "*" + good[21:],
		"not base64 at all":     "!!!!",
	} {
		t.Run(name, func(t *testing.T) {
			host := &scriptedHost{}
			host.hold("iPhone", 7)
			rec, served := serveAuthed(host, &http.Cookie{Name: cookieName, Value: text})
			requireRefusedUnasked(t, rec, served, host)
		})
	}
}

func TestAuthed_TextOfTheWrongLengthIsRefusedWithoutAskingTheHost(t *testing.T) {
	for name, n := range map[string]int{
		"empty":    0,
		"8 bytes":  8,
		"31 bytes": tokenBytes - 1,
		"33 bytes": tokenBytes + 1,
		"64 bytes": 2 * tokenBytes,
	} {
		t.Run(name, func(t *testing.T) {
			text := base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(bytes.Repeat([]byte{7}, n))
			host := &scriptedHost{}
			host.hold("iPhone", 7)
			rec, served := serveAuthed(host, &http.Cookie{Name: cookieName, Value: text})
			requireRefusedUnasked(t, rec, served, host)
		})
	}
}

func TestAuthed_AWellFormedTokenNoDeviceHoldsIsRefused(t *testing.T) {
	host := &scriptedHost{}
	host.hold("iPhone", 7)

	rec, served := serveAuthed(host, &http.Cookie{Name: cookieName, Value: deviceToken(8)})

	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusUnauthorized, rec.Code)
	assert.Contains(t, rec.Body.String(), `"error"`)
	assert.Empty(t, served, "the handler never runs for an unpaired request")
	assert.Equal(t, [][32]byte{deviceTokenHash(8)}, host.asked, "a well-formed token is asked about, once")
	assert.Empty(t, host.touched, "an unpaired request touches no device")
}

func TestAuthed_NoDeviceCookieIsRefusedWithoutAskingTheHost(t *testing.T) {
	for name, cookies := range map[string][]*http.Cookie{
		"no cookie":           nil,
		"another cookie name": {{Name: "loom", Value: deviceToken(7)}},
	} {
		t.Run(name, func(t *testing.T) {
			host := &scriptedHost{}
			host.hold("iPhone", 7)
			rec, served := serveAuthed(host, cookies...)
			requireRefusedUnasked(t, rec, served, host)
		})
	}
}

func TestPair_AnswersWhatTheHostSays(t *testing.T) {
	good := deviceToken(7)
	const code = `{"code": "AAAA-AAAA"}`
	for _, tc := range []struct {
		name     string
		body     string
		err      error // what the host answers
		status   int
		redeems  []string // the codes the host was asked about
		sentinel error    // the refusal whose own text is the whole answer, or nil
		text     string   // part of the answer
	}{
		{"a code that pairs", `{"code": "k7qm-2xpa"}`, nil, http.StatusOK, []string{"k7qm-2xpa"}, nil, `"device":"iPhone"`},
		{"a wrong code is a 400, not a 401 (which the app reads as unpaired)", code, core.ErrPairingCode, http.StatusBadRequest, []string{"AAAA-AAAA"}, core.ErrPairingCode, "wrong, used or expired"},
		{"a wrapped wrong code is the same refusal", code, fmt.Errorf("redeem: %w", core.ErrPairingCode), http.StatusBadRequest, []string{"AAAA-AAAA"}, core.ErrPairingCode, "wrong, used or expired"},
		{"too many wrong codes", code, core.ErrPairingLocked, http.StatusTooManyRequests, []string{"AAAA-AAAA"}, core.ErrPairingLocked, "run loom web pair again"},
		{"a name taken", code, core.ErrDeviceExists, http.StatusConflict, []string{"AAAA-AAAA"}, core.ErrDeviceExists, "has that name already"},
		{"a name taken, wrapped with the name", code, fmt.Errorf("%w: %q", core.ErrDeviceExists, "iPhone"), http.StatusConflict, []string{"AAAA-AAAA"}, core.ErrDeviceExists, "has that name already"},
		{"16 devices paired", code, core.ErrTooManyDevices, http.StatusConflict, []string{"AAAA-AAAA"}, core.ErrTooManyDevices, "16 devices are paired"},
		{"the web off", code, core.ErrWebOff, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, []string{"AAAA-AAAA"}, core.ErrWebOff, "loom web enable"},
		{"a failure it does not name", code, errors.New("disk on fire"), http.StatusInternalServerError, []string{"AAAA-AAAA"}, nil, "pairing failed"},
		{"a field the route does not take", `{"code": "AAAA-AAAA", "name": "x"}`, nil, http.StatusBadRequest, nil, nil, "unknown field"},
		{"a code of the wrong type", `{"code": 7}`, nil, http.StatusBadRequest, nil, nil, "not the JSON"},
		{"no body", ``, nil, http.StatusBadRequest, nil, nil, "empty"},
		{"a code far too long", `{"code": "` + strings.Repeat("A", maxCodeLen+1) + `"}`, nil, http.StatusBadRequest, nil, nil, "too long"},
	} {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			host := &scriptedHost{name: "iPhone", token: good, err: tc.err}
			l := &Layer{host: host}
			req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, "/api/v1/pair", strings.NewReader(tc.body))
			rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
			l.mux().ServeHTTP(rec, req)

			assert.Equal(t, tc.status, rec.Code)
			assert.Contains(t, rec.Body.String(), tc.text)
			assert.Equal(t, tc.redeems, host.redeemed)
			if tc.sentinel != nil {
				want, err := json.Marshal(map[string]string{"error": tc.sentinel.Error()})
				require.NoError(t, err)
				assert.JSONEq(t, string(want), rec.Body.String(), "the sentinel's own text, nothing the host wrapped around it")
			}
			cookies := rec.Result().Cookies()
			if tc.status != http.StatusOK {
				assert.Empty(t, cookies, "no cookie unless the code paired")
				assert.NotContains(t, rec.Body.String(), "disk on fire", "an error the layer does not name is not echoed")
				return
			}
			require.Len(t, cookies, 1)
			assert.Equal(t, cookieName, cookies[0].Name)
			assert.Equal(t, good, cookies[0].Value, "the token the host made, unchanged")
		})
	}
}

func TestPair_TakesPostOnly(t *testing.T) {
	host := &scriptedHost{}
	l := &Layer{host: host}
	for _, method := range []string{http.MethodGet, http.MethodHead, http.MethodPut, http.MethodDelete} {
		rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
		l.mux().ServeHTTP(rec, httptest.NewRequest(method, "/api/v1/pair", nil))
		assert.Equal(t, http.StatusMethodNotAllowed, rec.Code, method)
	}
	assert.Empty(t, host.redeemed)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `internal/web/web.go`. The bodies of `mux`, `startParts` and `stopParts` are the contract's pinned text: later stories add their lines to them, in story order:

```go
// Package web is the daemon's web layer: a client of the daemon's own
// rpc.Server (joined over net.Pipe as the web role) plus core.WebHost for
// device records, serving the loom PWA, a JSON API and a live stream on a
// loopback port that a Cloudflare tunnel reaches, and sending Web Push. It
// holds no session state: the model owns every session and every device. It
// logs under subsystem=web.
package web

import (
	"net/http"
	"sync"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
)

// sink is a part of the layer that follows the daemon's events. The drain
// hands every batch Sync returns to each sink in turn, on its own
// goroutine: a sink never blocks and never does network I/O.
type sink interface{ apply(evs []core.Event) }

// Layer is the web layer inside a running daemon: Start builds it and Close
// stops it. Its parts (the stream hub, the request tracker, the push sender)
// each follow the daemon's events as a sink and register their routes in mux.
type Layer struct {
	host core.WebHost

	// sinkMu guards sinks.
	sinkMu sync.Mutex

	sinks []sink
}

// addSink adds s to the sinks the drain feeds. The layer's parts add theirs
// in startParts, before the drain begins; a sink added later sees the
// batches from then on.
func (l *Layer) addSink(s sink) {
	l.sinkMu.Lock()
	defer l.sinkMu.Unlock()
	l.sinks = append(l.sinks, s)
}

// mux is the layer's routes, one registrar per part.
func (l *Layer) mux() *http.ServeMux {
	m := http.NewServeMux()
	l.routePair(m)
	return m
}

// startParts builds the layer's parts and adds their sinks, before the drain starts.
func (l *Layer) startParts() {
}

// stopParts stops the layer's parts, first in Close.
func (l *Layer) stopParts() {
}
```

Create `internal/web/auth.go`:

```go
package web

import (
	"crypto/sha256"
	"encoding/base64"
	"errors"
	"net/http"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/log"
)

const (
	// cookieName is the device cookie. Browsers accept a __Host- cookie only
	// when it is Secure, has Path=/ and names no Domain, so no sibling host
	// can plant one.
	cookieName = "__Host-loom"
	// cookieMaxAge is 400 days in seconds, the longest a browser keeps one.
	cookieMaxAge = 400 * 24 * 60 * 60
	// tokenBytes is the size of a device token before it is encoded.
	tokenBytes = 32
	// maxCodeLen bounds the pairing code a request may carry. A code is eight
	// characters and the model drops dashes and spaces, so this is ample.
	maxCodeLen = 64
)

// deviceHandler serves a request from the paired device it names.
type deviceHandler func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, device string)

// setDeviceCookie gives the browser the device token, as the one cookie the
// layer trusts.
func setDeviceCookie(w http.ResponseWriter, token string) {
	http.SetCookie(w, deviceCookie(token, cookieMaxAge))
}

// clearDeviceCookie makes the browser drop the device cookie.
func clearDeviceCookie(w http.ResponseWriter) {
	http.SetCookie(w, deviceCookie("", -1))
}

// deviceCookie is the cookie with the attributes that make it safe to hold a
// credential: Secure and Path=/ for the __Host- prefix, HttpOnly so no
// script reads it, SameSite=Strict so no cross-site request carries it.
func deviceCookie(value string, maxAge int) *http.Cookie {
	return &http.Cookie{
		Name:     cookieName,
		Value:    value,
		Path:     "/",
		MaxAge:   maxAge,
		Secure:   true,
		HttpOnly: true,
		SameSite: http.SameSiteStrictMode,
	}
}

// tokenHash is the hash the host knows a device by: the SHA-256 of the 32
// bytes the cookie's unpadded base64url text decodes to, which is what the
// model stored when it made the token. Hashing the text instead would match
// no device. A value that does not decode to exactly 32 bytes is no token,
// and has no hash.
func tokenHash(value string) ([32]byte, bool) {
	raw, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(value)
	if err != nil || len(raw) != tokenBytes {
		return [32]byte{}, false
	}
	return sha256.Sum256(raw), true
}

// authed is h for a paired device only: a request whose cookie names no
// device is a 401, on which the app clears its snapshot and shows pairing.
// A cookie that is not a device token is refused without asking the host.
// The device is touched (last seen) before h runs.
func (l *Layer) authed(h deviceHandler) http.Handler {
	return http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		device, ok := l.deviceByCookie(r)
		if !ok {
			writeError(w, http.StatusUnauthorized, "this phone is not paired")
			return
		}
		l.host.TouchDevice(device)
		h(w, r, device)
	})
}

// deviceByCookie is the device r's cookie names, asked of the host by the
// hash of the token's 32 bytes (tokenHash). A cookie that is not a token is
// never looked up.
func (l *Layer) deviceByCookie(r *http.Request) (string, bool) {
	c, err := r.Cookie(cookieName)
	if err != nil {
		return "", false
	}
	hash, ok := tokenHash(c.Value)
	if !ok {
		return "", false
	}
	return l.host.DeviceByToken(hash)
}

// routePair registers the one route a phone can call before it is paired.
func (l *Layer) routePair(m *http.ServeMux) {
	m.Handle("POST /api/v1/pair", http.HandlerFunc(l.pair))
}

// pairRefusals are the host's refusals of a pairing code, with the status
// the app keys on, tried in order with errors.Is. A wrong code is a 400 and
// never a 401: the app reads every 401 as "this phone is not paired" and
// clears what it holds.
var pairRefusals = []struct {
	err    error
	status int
}{
	{core.ErrPairingLocked, http.StatusTooManyRequests},
	{core.ErrPairingCode, http.StatusBadRequest},
	{core.ErrDeviceExists, http.StatusConflict},
	{core.ErrTooManyDevices, http.StatusConflict},
	{core.ErrWebOff, http.StatusServiceUnavailable},
}

// pair trades a pairing code for the device cookie. The model decides
// whether the code is right (it consumes it, counts the misses and makes the
// device); this maps its answer: a refusal in pairRefusals answers
// {"error": <the sentinel's own text>}, never the text of what the host
// wrapped around it, and any other error is a fixed 500, since an error can
// carry paths or names.
func (l *Layer) pair(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
	var body struct {
		Code string `json:"code"`
	}
	if err := readJSON(r, &body); err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, err.Error())
		return
	}
	if len(body.Code) > maxCodeLen {
		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "the code is too long")
		return
	}
	name, token, err := l.host.RedeemPairing(body.Code)
	if err == nil {
		setDeviceCookie(w, token)
		log.For("web").Info("web.paired", "device", name)
		writeJSON(w, http.StatusOK, map[string]string{"device": name})
		return
	}
	for _, refusal := range pairRefusals {
		if errors.Is(err, refusal.err) {
			log.For("web").Warn("web.pair_refused", "reason", refusal.err.Error())
			writeError(w, refusal.status, refusal.err.Error())
			return
		}
	}
	log.For("web").Error("web.pair_failed", "err", err)
	writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "pairing failed; the daemon's log says why")
}
```

Append to `internal/web/CLAUDE.md`, directly after its last line:

```markdown
- **Authenticate a request only through `authed`, which decodes the cookie's unpadded base64url token, refuses one that is not exactly 32 bytes, and asks the host by the SHA-256 of those bytes.** The model stores the hash of the 32 bytes, so the layer never holds, logs or compares a token, and hashing the cookie's text instead matches no device; a cookie that does not decode to 32 bytes never reaches the host. A cookie that names no device is a 401 before the route runs; a route registered without `authed` is open to the internet. **Enforced** for the check by `TestTokenHash`, `TestAuthed_AGoodCookieNamesItsDevice`, `TestAuthed_TextThatDoesNotDecodeIsRefusedWithoutAskingTheHost`, `TestAuthed_TextOfTheWrongLengthIsRefusedWithoutAskingTheHost`, `TestAuthed_AWellFormedTokenNoDeviceHoldsIsRefused` and `TestAuthed_NoDeviceCookieIsRefusedWithoutAskingTheHost`; that every route uses `authed` is **Convention** — an API route anyone can call.
- **Set and clear the device cookie only through `setDeviceCookie` and `clearDeviceCookie`.** They give the `__Host-loom` cookie its attributes (Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, Path=/, the lifetime `cookieMaxAge`); a browser drops a `__Host-` cookie without Secure or Path=/, and without HttpOnly or SameSite=Strict a script or a cross-site request could carry it. **Enforced** by `TestDeviceCookie`.
- **Map the host's pairing refusals by sentinel with `errors.Is` (a wrong code 400, too many wrong codes 429, a name taken or 16 devices 409, the web off 503), answer the sentinel's own text, and give any other error a fixed 500.** The model's errors can carry paths and device names, and the app tells a wrong code from a locked one by status; a wrong code answered 401 reads as "not paired" and clears the app's snapshot. **Enforced** by `TestPair_AnswersWhatTheHostSays` and `TestPair_TakesPostOnly`.
```

```go
// guardOrigin is the origin the guard tests enforce.
const guardOrigin = "https://loom.example.com"

// guardedBy is guard around a handler that answers 200 with the number of
// body bytes it read, or 413 with the reader's error. The layer enforces
// origin, as a bound listener's does.
func guardedBy(t *testing.T, origin string) http.Handler {
	t.Helper()
	rule, err := parseOrigin(origin)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	l := &Layer{}
	l.rule.Store(&rule)
	return l.guard(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		n, err := io.Copy(io.Discard, r.Body)
		if err != nil {
			writeError(w, http.StatusRequestEntityTooLarge, err.Error())
			return
		}
		writeJSON(w, http.StatusOK, map[string]int64{"read": n})
	}))
}

// sendGuarded runs one request through h with host as its Host header and
// origins as its Origin headers (none when nil).
func sendGuarded(h http.Handler, method, host string, origins []string, body io.Reader) *httptest.ResponseRecorder {
	req := httptest.NewRequest(method, "/api/v1/x", body)
	req.Host = host
	if origins != nil {
		req.Header["Origin"] = origins
	}
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	h.ServeHTTP(rec, req)
	return rec
}

func TestParseOrigin(t *testing.T) {
	for origin, host := range map[string]string{
		"https://loom.example.com":      "loom.example.com",
		"https://LOOM.example.com:443":  "loom.example.com",
		"https://loom.example.com:8443": "loom.example.com:8443",
		"http://127.0.0.1:7420":         "127.0.0.1:7420",
		"http://localhost:80":           "localhost",
	} {
		rule, err := parseOrigin(origin)
		require.NoError(t, err, origin)
		assert.Equal(t, host, rule.host, origin)
	}
	for _, bad := range []string{
		"", "loom.example.com", "ftp://loom.example.com", "https://", "https://user@loom.example.com",
		"https://loom.example.com/path", "https://loom.example.com/", "https://loom.example.com?x=1",
		"https://loom.example.com?", "https://loom.example.com#f",
	} {
		_, err := parseOrigin(bad)
		assert.Error(t, err, bad)
	}
}

func TestGuard_TheHostMustBeTheOrigins(t *testing.T) {
	for _, tc := range []struct {
		name   string
		origin string
		host   string
		status int
	}{
		{"the origin's host", guardOrigin, "loom.example.com", http.StatusOK},
		{"in any case", guardOrigin, "LOOM.Example.COM", http.StatusOK},
		{"with the scheme's default port", guardOrigin, "loom.example.com:443", http.StatusOK},
		{"another port", guardOrigin, "loom.example.com:8443", http.StatusMisdirectedRequest},
		{"another host", guardOrigin, "evil.example", http.StatusMisdirectedRequest},
		{"the host as a prefix of another", guardOrigin, "loom.example.com.evil.example", http.StatusMisdirectedRequest},
		{"the host as a suffix of another", guardOrigin, "evil-loom.example.com", http.StatusMisdirectedRequest},
		{"no host", guardOrigin, "", http.StatusMisdirectedRequest},
		{"an origin with a port", "https://loom.example.com:8443", "loom.example.com:8443", http.StatusOK},
		{"an origin with a port, asked for by the bare host", "https://loom.example.com:8443", "loom.example.com", http.StatusMisdirectedRequest},
		{"an origin naming its default port", "https://loom.example.com:443", "loom.example.com", http.StatusOK},
		{"a development origin", "http://127.0.0.1:7420", "127.0.0.1:7420", http.StatusOK},
		{"a development origin on another port", "http://127.0.0.1:7420", "127.0.0.1:7421", http.StatusMisdirectedRequest},
		{"localhost is not 127.0.0.1", "http://127.0.0.1:7420", "localhost:7420", http.StatusMisdirectedRequest},
	} {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			rec := sendGuarded(guardedBy(t, tc.origin), http.MethodGet, tc.host, nil, nil)
			assert.Equal(t, tc.status, rec.Code)
		})
	}
}

func TestGuard_AnUnsafeMethodMustCarryTheOrigin(t *testing.T) {
	for _, tc := range []struct {
		name    string
		method  string
		origins []string
		status  int
	}{
		{"a POST from the origin", http.MethodPost, []string{guardOrigin}, http.StatusOK},
		{"a POST without an Origin", http.MethodPost, nil, http.StatusForbidden},
		{"a POST from another site", http.MethodPost, []string{"https://evil.example"}, http.StatusForbidden},
		{"a POST from the http scheme", http.MethodPost, []string{"http://loom.example.com"}, http.StatusForbidden},
		{"a POST from another port", http.MethodPost, []string{"https://loom.example.com:8443"}, http.StatusForbidden},
		{"a POST from a subdomain", http.MethodPost, []string{"https://app.loom.example.com"}, http.StatusForbidden},
		{"an opaque origin", http.MethodPost, []string{"null"}, http.StatusForbidden},
		{"an Origin with a path", http.MethodPost, []string{guardOrigin + "/x"}, http.StatusForbidden},
		{"two Origin headers", http.MethodPost, []string{guardOrigin, guardOrigin}, http.StatusForbidden},
		{"the origin in another case", http.MethodPost, []string{"HTTPS://LOOM.EXAMPLE.COM"}, http.StatusOK},
		{"the origin naming its default port", http.MethodPost, []string{"https://loom.example.com:443"}, http.StatusOK},
		{"a PUT without an Origin", http.MethodPut, nil, http.StatusForbidden},
		{"a DELETE from another site", http.MethodDelete, []string{"https://evil.example"}, http.StatusForbidden},
		{"a GET from another site is not checked", http.MethodGet, []string{"https://evil.example"}, http.StatusOK},
		{"a GET without one", http.MethodGet, nil, http.StatusOK},
		{"a HEAD from another site is not checked", http.MethodHead, []string{"https://evil.example"}, http.StatusOK},
	} {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			rec := sendGuarded(guardedBy(t, guardOrigin), tc.method, "loom.example.com", tc.origins, nil)
			assert.Equal(t, tc.status, rec.Code)
		})
	}
}

func TestGuard_EveryAnswerCarriesTheSecurityHeaders(t *testing.T) {
	h := guardedBy(t, guardOrigin)
	for _, tc := range []struct {
		name   string
		rec    *httptest.ResponseRecorder
		status int
	}{
		{"served", sendGuarded(h, http.MethodGet, "loom.example.com", nil, nil), http.StatusOK},
		{"a wrong host", sendGuarded(h, http.MethodGet, "evil.example", nil, nil), http.StatusMisdirectedRequest},
		{"a wrong origin", sendGuarded(h, http.MethodPost, "loom.example.com", []string{"https://evil.example"}, nil), http.StatusForbidden},
	} {
		assert.Equal(t, tc.status, tc.rec.Code, tc.name)
		header := tc.rec.Header()
		assert.Equal(t, "default-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'self'",
			header.Get("Content-Security-Policy"), tc.name)
		assert.Equal(t, "nosniff", header.Get("X-Content-Type-Options"), tc.name)
		assert.Equal(t, "no-referrer", header.Get("Referrer-Policy"), tc.name)
	}
}

func TestGuard_NoRuleAnswersNothing(t *testing.T) {
	called := false
	l := &Layer{}
	h := l.guard(http.HandlerFunc(func(http.ResponseWriter, *http.Request) { called = true }))
	rec := sendGuarded(h, http.MethodGet, "loom.example.com", nil, nil)
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusMisdirectedRequest, rec.Code, "a layer with no listener refuses every request")
	assert.False(t, called)
	assert.NotEmpty(t, rec.Header().Get("Content-Security-Policy"), "the refusal carries the headers too")
}

func TestGuard_TheBodyIsCapped(t *testing.T) {
	h := guardedBy(t, guardOrigin)

	rec := sendGuarded(h, http.MethodPost, "loom.example.com", []string{guardOrigin}, strings.NewReader(strings.Repeat("a", maxBody)))
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusOK, rec.Code, "a body of exactly the cap")
	assert.JSONEq(t, `{"read": 1048576}`, rec.Body.String())

	rec = sendGuarded(h, http.MethodPost, "loom.example.com", []string{guardOrigin}, strings.NewReader(strings.Repeat("a", maxBody+1)))
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusRequestEntityTooLarge, rec.Code, "one byte more")
	assert.Contains(t, rec.Body.String(), "too large")
}

// The stream (Story 7) flushes each event through http.ResponseController,
// which reaches the real Flusher only if guard hands the handler the
// ResponseWriter it was given, or one with an Unwrap() http.ResponseWriter.
// The handler stays open after its flush, so the client has the first event
// only if the flush went through to the connection.
func TestGuard_AHandlerBehindItCanFlushToTheClient(t *testing.T) {
	unblock := make(chan struct{})
	flushed := make(chan error, 1)
	l := &Layer{}
	srv := httptest.NewServer(l.guard(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "text/event-stream")
		_, _ = io.WriteString(w, "event: ping\n\n")
		flushed <- http.NewResponseController(w).Flush()
		<-unblock
	})))
	defer srv.Close()
	defer close(unblock) // runs first, so Close does not wait on the handler
	rule, err := parseOrigin(srv.URL)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	l.rule.Store(&rule)

	client := &http.Client{Timeout: 5 * time.Second}
	resp, err := client.Get(srv.URL)
	require.NoError(t, err, "the response head reached the client before the handler returned")
	defer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()

	require.NoError(t, <-flushed, "Flush through the ResponseController failed behind guard")
	line, err := bufio.NewReader(resp.Body).ReadString('\n')
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, "event: ping\n", line)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `internal/web/server.go`:

```go
package web

import (
	"fmt"
	"net"
	"net/http"
	"net/url"
	"strings"
)

// maxBody caps a request's body, as the mod socket's own cap does.
const maxBody = 1 << 20

// contentSecurityPolicy lets the page load only its own files and images
// from itself or data: URLs, and forbids framing, a base tag and any form
// post elsewhere. The markup has no inline script and no style attribute,
// so nothing needs 'unsafe-inline'.
const contentSecurityPolicy = "default-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'self'"

// originRule is the origin a listener serves, as guard enforces it: the
// scheme and the host with the scheme's default port dropped, in lower case,
// so https://loom.example.com and https://loom.example.com:443 are one.
type originRule struct {
	scheme string
	host   string
}

// parseOrigin reads the configured origin: scheme://host[:port] with no
// user, path, query or fragment. The model validates the same shape before it
// stores one; a value that fails here serves nothing rather than anything.
func parseOrigin(raw string) (originRule, error) {
	u, err := url.Parse(raw)
	if err != nil || (u.Scheme != "http" && u.Scheme != "https") || u.Host == "" ||
		u.User != nil || u.Opaque != "" || u.Path != "" || u.RawQuery != "" || u.ForceQuery || u.Fragment != "" {
		return originRule{}, fmt.Errorf("the origin %q is not scheme://host[:port]", raw)
	}
	return originRule{scheme: u.Scheme, host: hostKey(u.Scheme, u.Host)}, nil
}

// hostKey is hostport in lower case with scheme's default port dropped.
func hostKey(scheme, hostport string) string {
	hostport = strings.ToLower(hostport)
	host, port, err := net.SplitHostPort(hostport)
	if err != nil || port != defaultPort(scheme) {
		return hostport
	}
	if strings.Contains(host, ":") {
		return "[" + host + "]"
	}
	return host
}

// defaultPort is the port a URL of scheme names when it names none.
func defaultPort(scheme string) string {
	switch scheme {
	case "http":
		return "80"
	case "https":
		return "443"
	}
	return ""
}

// allowsHost reports whether a request's Host header names the origin's
// host. The tunnel passes the Host the phone asked for, and a rebound DNS
// name arrives with its own.
func (o originRule) allowsHost(host string) bool {
	return host != "" && hostKey(o.scheme, host) == o.host
}

// allowsOrigin reports whether an Origin header is the origin itself:
// browsers send the scheme, host and port alone, so a path, a user or a
// query means it is not one.
func (o originRule) allowsOrigin(header string) bool {
	u, err := url.Parse(header)
	if err != nil || u.Scheme != o.scheme || u.Host == "" ||
		u.User != nil || u.Opaque != "" || u.Path != "" || u.RawQuery != "" || u.Fragment != "" {
		return false
	}
	return hostKey(u.Scheme, u.Host) == o.host
}

// guard is the door every route stands behind. It sets the security headers
// on every answer, refuses a Host that is not the origin's (421: DNS
// rebinding), refuses any request but a GET or HEAD without the origin's own
// Origin header (403: a cross-site post), and caps the body at maxBody. With
// no origin rule (no listener runs) it refuses everything. It hands next the
// ResponseWriter it was given, unwrapped, so a stream flushes through
// http.NewResponseController.
func (l *Layer) guard(next http.Handler) http.Handler {
	return http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		h := w.Header()
		h.Set("Content-Security-Policy", contentSecurityPolicy)
		h.Set("X-Content-Type-Options", "nosniff")
		h.Set("Referrer-Policy", "no-referrer")
		rule := l.rule.Load()
		if rule == nil || !rule.allowsHost(r.Host) {
			writeError(w, http.StatusMisdirectedRequest, "this is not the host loom serves")
			return
		}
		if r.Method != http.MethodGet && r.Method != http.MethodHead {
			if origins := r.Header.Values("Origin"); len(origins) != 1 || !rule.allowsOrigin(origins[0]) {
				writeError(w, http.StatusForbidden, "the request does not come from loom's origin")
				return
			}
		}
		r.Body = http.MaxBytesReader(w, r.Body, maxBody)
		next.ServeHTTP(w, r)
	})
}
```

```go
type Layer struct {
	host core.WebHost

	// rule is the origin rule of the listener now running, which guard
	// enforces; nil while none runs, and then guard refuses every request.
	rule atomic.Pointer[originRule]

	// sinkMu guards sinks.
	sinkMu sync.Mutex

	sinks []sink
}
```

Append to `internal/web/CLAUDE.md`, directly after its last line:

```markdown
- **Wrap every route in `guard`, let a layer with no origin rule refuse everything, and keep `guard` handing the handler the `ResponseWriter` it was given (or a wrapper with `Unwrap() http.ResponseWriter`).** `guard` answers 421 unless the Host header is the origin's host (DNS rebinding), 403 for any method but GET and HEAD without the origin's own `Origin` header (a cross-site post), puts the security headers on every answer and caps bodies at `maxBody`; a listener bound without a rule would answer any Host, and a wrapper without `Unwrap` makes `http.NewResponseController(w).Flush()` fail, so the live stream would buffer until it closes. **Enforced** by `TestGuard_TheHostMustBeTheOrigins`, `TestGuard_AnUnsafeMethodMustCarryTheOrigin`, `TestGuard_EveryAnswerCarriesTheSecurityHeaders`, `TestGuard_NoRuleAnswersNothing`, `TestGuard_TheBodyIsCapped`, `TestGuard_AHandlerBehindItCanFlushToTheClient` and `TestParseOrigin`.
```
