```go
// deadMachine is a machine with no live tmux session and no claude: every
// command fails, every output is empty.
func deadMachine() cmd_test.MockCmdExec {
	return cmd_test.MockCmdExec{
		RunFunc:    func(*exec.Cmd) error { return &exec.ExitError{} },
		OutputFunc: func(*exec.Cmd) ([]byte, error) { return nil, &exec.ExitError{} },
	}
}

// webStack is the daemon's stack in one process, built as internal/daemon
// builds it: a booted model on its loop, served by the real rpc.Server.
// layer is the web layer under test, once start has run; tui is an ordinary
// client of the same server, which is how `loom web` reaches it.
type webStack struct {
	loop  *core.Loop
	srv   *rpc.Server
	layer *Layer
	tui   *rpc.Client
}

// newWebStack boots a model over a fresh global dir.
func newWebStack(t *testing.T) *webStack { return newWebStackIn(t, t.TempDir()) }

// newWebStackIn boots a model over dir, which a test may seed first.
func newWebStackIn(t *testing.T, dir string) *webStack {
	t.Helper()
	t.Setenv(config.EnvGlobalDir, dir)
	model := core.New(core.Options{CmdExec: deadMachine()})
	model.Boot()
	s := &webStack{loop: core.Start(model)}
	s.srv = rpc.NewServer(s.loop)
	s.loop.Begin()
	t.Cleanup(func() {
		s.layer.Close()
		if s.tui != nil {
			s.tui.Close()
		}
		s.srv.Close()
		s.loop.Stop()
	})
	return s
}

// start starts the web layer on the stack's server, with the loop as host.
func (s *webStack) start(t *testing.T) {
	t.Helper()
	layer, err := Start(Options{Server: s.srv, Host: s.loop, Hostname: "testhost", Build: "test build"})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	s.layer = layer
}

// connect joins the stack's server as an ordinary client, once.
func (s *webStack) connect(t *testing.T) *rpc.Client {
	t.Helper()
	if s.tui != nil {
		return s.tui
	}
	a, b := net.Pipe()
	s.srv.Serve(a)
	c, err := rpc.Dial(b)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	s.tui = c
	return c
}

// enable has the model enable the web on a free loopback port, with the
// origin a development phone would use, and waits until the layer serves it.
func (s *webStack) enable(t *testing.T) (addr, origin string) {
	t.Helper()
	addr = freeLoopbackAddr(t)
	origin = "http://" + addr
	require.NoError(t, s.connect(t).ConfigureWeb(addr, origin))
	s.waitWeb(t, func(v core.WebView) bool { return v.Serving && v.Addr == addr })
	return addr, origin
}

// waitWeb waits until the published WebView satisfies ok, and returns it.
func (s *webStack) waitWeb(t *testing.T, ok func(core.WebView) bool) core.WebView {
	t.Helper()
	var view core.WebView
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool {
		view = s.connect(t).Web()
		return ok(view)
	}, 10*time.Second, 10*time.Millisecond, "the published web settings never became what the test waits for")
	return view
}

// waitNotice waits until the ordinary client has been sent a notice whose
// text holds want. It reads the client's events, so each notice is seen once.
func (s *webStack) waitNotice(t *testing.T, want string) {
	t.Helper()
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool {
		for _, ev := range s.connect(t).Sync() {
			n, ok := ev.(core.Notice)
			if !ok {
				continue
			}
			text := n.Info
			if n.Err != nil {
				text = n.Err.Error()
			}
			if strings.Contains(text, want) {
				return true
			}
		}
		return false
	}, 10*time.Second, 10*time.Millisecond, "no notice said %q", want)
}

// pair pairs a device called name through the pairing route, as the phone
// does, and returns the cookie it was given. The web must be serving at addr.
func (s *webStack) pair(t *testing.T, addr, name string) *http.Cookie {
	t.Helper()
	pairing, err := s.connect(t).PairDevice(name)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	resp := httpDo(t, addr, http.MethodPost, "/api/v1/pair", `{"code": "`+pairing.Code+`"}`)
	require.Equal(t, http.StatusOK, resp.StatusCode, bodyText(t, resp))
	cookies := resp.Cookies()
	require.Len(t, cookies, 1)
	return cookies[0]
}

// whoAmI runs a request carrying cookie through authed and returns the
// status and the device the handler saw ("" when it did not run).
func (s *webStack) whoAmI(cookie *http.Cookie) (int, string) {
	var device string
	h := s.layer.authed(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, name string) {
		device = name
		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusNoContent)
	})
	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/api/v1/who", nil)
	if cookie != nil {
		req.AddCookie(cookie)
	}
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	h.ServeHTTP(rec, req)
	return rec.Code, device
}

// funcSink is a sink that calls a function.
type funcSink func(evs []core.Event)

func (f funcSink) apply(evs []core.Event) { f(evs) }

// gateDrain returns a sink that stops the layer's drain, before it applies
// the batch, at the first published model change whose web view satisfies
// stop; held is closed once the drain stands there, and release lets it go
// (any number of times). Model changes pile up behind the stopped drain, and
// the client the layer drains coalesces them into one.
func gateDrain(stop func(core.WebView) bool) (sink funcSink, held <-chan struct{}, release func()) {
	heldCh, gate := make(chan struct{}), make(chan struct{})
	var holdOnce, releaseOnce sync.Once
	sink = func(evs []core.Event) {
		for _, ev := range evs {
			if m, ok := ev.(core.ModelChanged); ok && stop(m.View.Web) {
				holdOnce.Do(func() {
					close(heldCh)
					<-gate
				})
			}
		}
	}
	return sink, heldCh, func() { releaseOnce.Do(func() { close(gate) }) }
}

// boundServer is the http.Server the layer listens with now, nil while none
// does. Every bind makes a new one, which is how a test sees the layer bind
// again.
func boundServer(l *Layer) *http.Server {
	l.bindMu.Lock()
	defer l.bindMu.Unlock()
	return l.srv
}

// onOriginChange is a sink that calls f for each published model change that
// carries origin, which a test makes happen by configuring the web with that
// origin: a stray batch before it does not set f off.
func onOriginChange(origin string, f func()) funcSink {
	return func(evs []core.Event) {
		for _, ev := range evs {
			if m, ok := ev.(core.ModelChanged); ok && m.View.Web.Origin == origin {
				f()
			}
		}
	}
}

// freeLoopbackAddr is a loopback address nothing listens on yet.
func freeLoopbackAddr(t *testing.T) string {
	t.Helper()
	ln, err := net.Listen("tcp", "127.0.0.1:0")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	addr := ln.Addr().String()
	require.NoError(t, ln.Close())
	return addr
}

// requireNoListener waits until nothing accepts connections at addr.
func requireNoListener(t *testing.T, addr string) {
	t.Helper()
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool {
		c, err := net.DialTimeout("tcp", addr, time.Second)
		if err != nil {
			return true
		}
		_ = c.Close()
		return false
	}, 10*time.Second, 10*time.Millisecond, "%s still accepts connections", addr)
}

// portOf is the port of a host:port address.
func portOf(t *testing.T, addr string) string {
	t.Helper()
	_, port, err := net.SplitHostPort(addr)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	return port
}

func httpClient() *http.Client {
	return &http.Client{Timeout: 10 * time.Second, Transport: &http.Transport{DisableKeepAlives: true}}
}

// httpDo sends method path to the layer at addr as the phone does: the Host
// is addr and a request that changes things carries the origin the stack's
// enable uses. mutate edits the request first.
func httpDo(t *testing.T, addr, method, path, body string, mutate ...func(*http.Request)) *http.Response {
	t.Helper()
	req, err := http.NewRequest(method, "http://"+addr+path, strings.NewReader(body))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	if method != http.MethodGet && method != http.MethodHead {
		req.Header.Set("Origin", "http://"+addr)
	}
	for _, m := range mutate {
		m(req)
	}
	resp, err := httpClient().Do(req)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	t.Cleanup(func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() })
	return resp
}

// httpProbe is the status of a GET of path at addr with host as the Host
// header (addr's own when empty), or 0 when nothing answers.
func httpProbe(addr, path, host string) int {
	req, err := http.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "http://"+addr+path, nil)
	if err != nil {
		return 0
	}
	if host != "" {
		req.Host = host
	}
	resp, err := httpClient().Do(req)
	if err != nil {
		return 0
	}
	_ = resp.Body.Close()
	return resp.StatusCode
}

func bodyText(t *testing.T, resp *http.Response) string {
	t.Helper()
	data, err := io.ReadAll(resp.Body)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	return string(data)
}

func TestStart_NeedsAServerAndAHost(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	_, err := Start(Options{Host: s.loop})
	assert.Error(t, err, "no server")
	_, err = Start(Options{Server: s.srv})
	assert.Error(t, err, "no host")
}

func TestStart_RefusesAStoppingServer(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.srv.Bye()
	layer, err := Start(Options{Server: s.srv, Host: s.loop})
	require.Error(t, err)
	assert.Nil(t, layer)
	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, core.ErrUnavailable, "a server that is stopping cannot be joined")
}

// The layer's own connection is the web role, which the server does not
// count as a client: a notice raised while no TUI is connected waits for the
// next one, rather than going to a layer that has no use for it.
func TestStart_ANoticeRaisedWithOnlyTheLayerConnectedWaitsForTheNextTUI(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	s.loop.WebServing("", errors.New("the port is taken"))

	s.connect(t) // the next TUI joins after the notice was raised
	s.waitNotice(t, "the port is taken")
}

func TestApplyWeb_ListensOnceTheModelEnablesTheWeb(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	tui := s.connect(t)
	assert.False(t, tui.Web().Serving, "the web is off until web.json enables it")
	assert.Empty(t, tui.Web().Error)

	addr, _ := s.enable(t)
	assert.Equal(t, addr, tui.Web().Addr)
	assert.Empty(t, tui.Web().Error)
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusNotFound, httpDo(t, addr, http.MethodGet, "/nowhere", "").StatusCode, "the mux answers behind the guard")
}

func TestListener_GuardsEveryRequestAtTheDoor(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	addr, _ := s.enable(t)
	const wrongCode = `{"code": "AAAA-AAAA"}`

	resp := httpDo(t, addr, http.MethodPost, "/api/v1/pair", wrongCode)
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusBadRequest, resp.StatusCode, "the right Host and Origin reach the pairing route, which refuses a code nobody made")
	assert.Contains(t, bodyText(t, resp), core.ErrPairingCode.Error(), "with the model's refusal, which a truncated body (below) does not have")
	assert.Equal(t, "nosniff", resp.Header.Get("X-Content-Type-Options"))

	resp = httpDo(t, addr, http.MethodPost, "/api/v1/pair", wrongCode, func(r *http.Request) { r.Host = "evil.example" })
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusMisdirectedRequest, resp.StatusCode, "a wrong Host")
	assert.Equal(t, contentSecurityPolicy, resp.Header.Get("Content-Security-Policy"), "the refusal carries the headers")

	resp = httpDo(t, addr, http.MethodPost, "/api/v1/pair", wrongCode, func(r *http.Request) { r.Header.Set("Origin", "https://evil.example") })
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusForbidden, resp.StatusCode, "a cross-site post")

	resp = httpDo(t, addr, http.MethodPost, "/api/v1/pair", `{"code":`)
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusBadRequest, resp.StatusCode, "a truncated body")
}

func TestApplyWeb_ChangingTheListenAddressMovesTheListener(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	first, _ := s.enable(t)

	second := freeLoopbackAddr(t)
	require.NoError(t, s.tui.ConfigureWeb(second, "http://"+second))
	s.waitWeb(t, func(v core.WebView) bool { return v.Serving && v.Addr == second })

	requireNoListener(t, first)
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusNotFound, httpDo(t, second, http.MethodGet, "/nowhere", "").StatusCode)
}

func TestApplyWeb_ChangingOnlyTheOriginChangesTheGuard(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	addr, _ := s.enable(t)
	port := portOf(t, addr)

	require.Equal(t, http.StatusNotFound, httpProbe(addr, "/nowhere", ""), "the first origin's host is served")
	require.Equal(t, http.StatusMisdirectedRequest, httpProbe(addr, "/nowhere", "localhost:"+port), "and no other")

	require.NoError(t, s.tui.ConfigureWeb(addr, "http://localhost:"+port))
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool {
		return httpProbe(addr, "/nowhere", "localhost:"+port) == http.StatusNotFound
	}, 10*time.Second, 10*time.Millisecond, "the new origin's host is never served")
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusMisdirectedRequest, httpProbe(addr, "/nowhere", ""), "the old origin's host no longer is")
}

func TestApplyWeb_DisablingStopsServing(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	addr, origin := s.enable(t)

	require.NoError(t, s.tui.ConfigureWeb("", origin))
	s.waitWeb(t, func(v core.WebView) bool { return !v.Serving && v.Addr == "" })

	requireNoListener(t, addr)
	assert.Empty(t, s.tui.Web().Error)
}

func TestApplyWeb_ATakenPortLeavesTheWebOffAndSaysWhy(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	tui := s.connect(t)
	taken, err := net.Listen("tcp", "127.0.0.1:0")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	t.Cleanup(func() { _ = taken.Close() })
	addr := taken.Addr().String()
	origin := "http://" + addr

	require.NoError(t, tui.ConfigureWeb(addr, origin), "the settings are valid; the port is the problem")
	view := s.waitWeb(t, func(v core.WebView) bool { return v.Error != "" })
	assert.False(t, view.Serving)
	assert.Empty(t, view.Addr)
	assert.Contains(t, view.Error, addr)
	s.waitNotice(t, addr)

	// Turning the web off clears the error the layer set, and turning it on
	// once the port is free listens. (The next tests retry with the very values
	// that failed, and show that nothing but a ConfigureWeb retries.)
	require.NoError(t, tui.ConfigureWeb("", origin))
	s.waitWeb(t, func(v core.WebView) bool { return v.Error == "" })
	require.NoError(t, taken.Close())
	require.NoError(t, tui.ConfigureWeb(addr, origin))
	s.waitWeb(t, func(v core.WebView) bool { return v.Serving && v.Addr == addr })
}

// The layer acts on the revision every ConfigureWeb bumps, not on whether
// listen and origin differ: the very settings that failed bind again once the
// port is free, and nothing but a ConfigureWeb makes that happen.
func TestApplyWeb_TheSameSettingsAgainRetryAFailedBind(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	tui := s.connect(t)
	taken, err := net.Listen("tcp", "127.0.0.1:0")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	t.Cleanup(func() { _ = taken.Close() })
	addr := taken.Addr().String()
	origin := "http://" + addr

	require.NoError(t, tui.ConfigureWeb(addr, origin))
	s.waitWeb(t, func(v core.WebView) bool { return v.Error != "" })
	require.NoError(t, taken.Close())
	require.Never(t, func() bool { return tui.Web().Serving }, 300*time.Millisecond, 10*time.Millisecond,
		"the port is free, but nothing but a ConfigureWeb retries a bind")

	require.NoError(t, tui.ConfigureWeb(addr, origin), "the very settings that failed")
	view := s.waitWeb(t, func(v core.WebView) bool { return v.Serving && v.Addr == addr })
	assert.Empty(t, view.Error)
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusNotFound, httpDo(t, addr, http.MethodGet, "/nowhere", "").StatusCode)
}

// A disable and an enable made while the drain is busy reach it as one
// ModelChanged, which carries the settings the listener already has. Only the
// revision tells it to close the listener and bind again.
func TestApplyWeb_TwoConfigurationsCoalescedIntoOneSyncStillRebind(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	tui := s.connect(t)
	addr, origin := s.enable(t)
	bound := boundServer(s.layer)
	require.NotNil(t, bound)
	rev := tui.Web().Rev

	// Stop the drain on the next change that leaves the settings alone (a
	// device pairing), so the two configurations below pile up behind it.
	sink, held, release := gateDrain(func(v core.WebView) bool { return v.Rev == rev && len(v.Devices) == 1 })
	t.Cleanup(release)
	s.layer.addSink(sink)
	s.pair(t, addr, "iPhone")
	select {
	case <-held:
	case <-time.After(10 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("the drain never reached the change it was to stop at")
	}

	require.NoError(t, tui.ConfigureWeb("", origin))
	require.NoError(t, tui.ConfigureWeb(addr, origin))
	final := tui.Web()
	require.NotEqual(t, rev, final.Rev, "each ConfigureWeb bumps the revision")
	require.Equal(t, addr, final.Listen, "the pair ends where it began")
	require.Equal(t, origin, final.Origin)
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool { return s.layer.client.Web().Rev == final.Rev }, 10*time.Second, 10*time.Millisecond,
		"the layer's client never received both configurations")
	require.Same(t, bound, boundServer(s.layer), "the stopped drain has not touched the listener")

	release()
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool {
		now := boundServer(s.layer)
		return now != nil && now != bound
	}, 10*time.Second, 10*time.Millisecond, "the layer never bound again")
	s.waitWeb(t, func(v core.WebView) bool { return v.Serving && v.Addr == addr })
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusNotFound, httpDo(t, addr, http.MethodGet, "/nowhere", "").StatusCode)
}

// The model publishes again for a report to WebServing, the layer's own or
// anyone's. A report does not bump the revision, so it never makes the layer
// bind, however free the port is.
func TestApplyWeb_AWebServingReportNeverBinds(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	tui := s.connect(t)
	taken, err := net.Listen("tcp", "127.0.0.1:0")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	t.Cleanup(func() { _ = taken.Close() })
	addr := taken.Addr().String()

	require.NoError(t, tui.ConfigureWeb(addr, "http://"+addr))
	s.waitWeb(t, func(v core.WebView) bool { return v.Error != "" })
	require.NoError(t, taken.Close())

	s.loop.WebServing("", errors.New("a report from elsewhere"))
	s.waitWeb(t, func(v core.WebView) bool { return strings.Contains(v.Error, "a report from elsewhere") })
	require.Never(t, func() bool { return boundServer(s.layer) != nil }, 300*time.Millisecond, 10*time.Millisecond,
		"listen is set and the port is free, but only a ConfigureWeb binds")
	assert.False(t, tui.Web().Serving)
}

func TestApplyWeb_AWebJSONTheModelLeftAloneKeepsItsReason(t *testing.T) {
	dir := t.TempDir()
	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(dir, core.WebFile), []byte("{not json"), 0o600))
	s := newWebStackIn(t, dir)
	tui := s.connect(t)
	before := tui.Web().Error
	require.NotEmpty(t, before, "the model says why it left web.json alone")

	s.start(t)
	require.Never(t, func() bool { return tui.Web().Error != before }, 300*time.Millisecond, 10*time.Millisecond,
		"the layer must not clear or replace a reason it did not set")
	assert.False(t, tui.Web().Serving)
}

func TestBind_ListensOnLoopbackOnly(t *testing.T) {
	for _, listen := range []string{
		"0.0.0.0:7420", "[::]:7420", "192.168.1.5:7420", ":7420", "localhost:7420", "example.com:7420", "127.0.0.1", "7420",
	} {
		t.Run(listen, func(t *testing.T) {
			l := &Layer{}
			addr, err := l.bind(webKey{listen: listen, origin: "http://127.0.0.1:7420"})
			require.Error(t, err)
			assert.Empty(t, addr)
			assert.Nil(t, l.srv, "nothing listens")
			assert.Nil(t, l.rule.Load())
		})
	}

	l := &Layer{}
	addr, err := l.bind(webKey{listen: "127.0.0.1:0", origin: "http://127.0.0.1:7420"})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	t.Cleanup(l.shutListener)
	assert.True(t, strings.HasPrefix(addr, "127.0.0.1:"), addr)
	assert.NotNil(t, l.rule.Load())

	// An origin it cannot use leaves nothing listening, not the old listener.
	_, err = l.bind(webKey{listen: "127.0.0.1:0", origin: "loom.example.com"})
	require.Error(t, err)
	assert.Nil(t, l.srv)
	assert.Nil(t, l.rule.Load())
	requireNoListener(t, addr)

	// Once the layer is shut it binds nothing.
	l.shutListener()
	_, err = l.bind(webKey{listen: "127.0.0.1:0", origin: "http://127.0.0.1:7420"})
	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, errLayerClosed)
}

func TestDrain_HandsEveryBatchToEverySinkInTurn(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	addr, _ := s.enable(t)

	var mu sync.Mutex
	var order []string
	var secondSawTheModelChange bool
	record := func(name string) funcSink {
		return func(evs []core.Event) {
			mu.Lock()
			defer mu.Unlock()
			order = append(order, name)
			for _, ev := range evs {
				if _, ok := ev.(core.ModelChanged); ok && name == "second" {
					secondSawTheModelChange = true
				}
			}
		}
	}
	s.layer.addSink(record("first"))
	s.layer.addSink(record("second"))

	require.NoError(t, s.tui.ConfigureWeb(addr, "http://localhost:"+portOf(t, addr)))
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool {
		mu.Lock()
		defer mu.Unlock()
		return secondSawTheModelChange
	}, 10*time.Second, 10*time.Millisecond, "the second sink never saw the model change")

	mu.Lock()
	defer mu.Unlock()
	for i, name := range order {
		assert.Equal(t, []string{"first", "second"}[i%2], name, "every batch goes to the first sink, then the second")
	}
}

func TestDrain_APanickingSinkStopsTheLayerNotTheDaemon(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	addr, _ := s.enable(t)

	origin := "http://localhost:" + portOf(t, addr)
	s.layer.addSink(onOriginChange(origin, func() { panic("boom") }))
	require.NoError(t, s.tui.ConfigureWeb(addr, origin), "a changed origin makes a batch")

	// The layer stops, and the host is told why.
	view := s.waitWeb(t, func(v core.WebView) bool { return !v.Serving && strings.Contains(v.Error, "boom") })
	assert.Contains(t, view.Error, "drain panicked")
	s.waitNotice(t, "boom")
	requireNoListener(t, addr)

	// The daemon side carries on: the model still answers an ordinary client's
	// request and publishes what it changed.
	assert.NotEmpty(t, s.tui.Workspaces(), "the daemon goes on")
	require.NoError(t, s.tui.ConfigureWeb("", origin))
	s.waitWeb(t, func(v core.WebView) bool { return v.Listen == "" })
	assert.NotPanics(t, s.layer.Close, "a layer that failed still closes")
}

// recoverPanic is what each goroutine the layer starts defers first (Stories
// 7 and 12 defer it in their own): it must swallow the panic, stop the layer
// and say where it happened.
func TestRecoverPanic_StopsTheLayerAndNamesWhere(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	addr, _ := s.enable(t)

	returned := make(chan struct{})
	go func() {
		defer close(returned) // runs after the recovery
		defer s.layer.recoverPanic("the test part")
		panic("kaboom")
	}()
	select {
	case <-returned:
	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("the goroutine never returned")
	}

	view := s.waitWeb(t, func(v core.WebView) bool { return !v.Serving && strings.Contains(v.Error, "kaboom") })
	assert.Contains(t, view.Error, "the test part panicked", "the error names the goroutine")
	s.waitNotice(t, "kaboom")
	requireNoListener(t, addr)
	assert.NotEmpty(t, s.tui.Workspaces(), "the daemon goes on")
}

// A part calls fail from the goroutine that panicked, which may be the drain
// that Close waits for: fail must not wait for the stop it starts.
func TestFail_ReturnsAtOnceFromTheDrainItself(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	addr, _ := s.enable(t)

	origin := "http://localhost:" + portOf(t, addr)
	took := make(chan time.Duration, 1)
	var once sync.Once
	s.layer.addSink(onOriginChange(origin, func() {
		once.Do(func() {
			began := time.Now()
			s.layer.fail(errors.New("a part gave up"))
			took <- time.Since(began)
		})
	}))
	require.NoError(t, s.tui.ConfigureWeb(addr, origin), "a changed origin makes a batch")

	select {
	case d := <-took:
		assert.Less(t, d, drainGrace/2, "fail waited for the layer to stop, on the goroutine the stop waits for")
	case <-time.After(2 * drainGrace):
		t.Fatal("fail never returned")
	}
	view := s.waitWeb(t, func(v core.WebView) bool { return !v.Serving && strings.Contains(v.Error, "a part gave up") })
	assert.False(t, view.Serving)
	requireNoListener(t, addr)
}

// fail and Close run one stop between them. Whichever comes first stops the
// layer, the other waits for it, and neither hangs nor closes anything twice
// (a second close of the drain's channel panics).
func TestFail_RacingAClose_StopsOnceAndHangsNowhere(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	addr, _ := s.enable(t)

	finished := make(chan struct{})
	go func() {
		defer close(finished)
		var wg sync.WaitGroup
		for i := 0; i < 4; i++ {
			wg.Add(2)
			go func() { defer wg.Done(); s.layer.fail(errors.New("a part failed")) }()
			go func() { defer wg.Done(); s.layer.Close() }()
		}
		wg.Wait()
	}()
	select {
	case <-finished:
	case <-time.After(2 * drainGrace):
		t.Fatal("a fail racing a Close hung")
	}

	// A Close returns only once the stop it shares is complete, so nothing
	// listens now, without waiting.
	c, err := net.DialTimeout("tcp", addr, time.Second)
	if err == nil {
		_ = c.Close()
	}
	assert.Error(t, err, "the listener was open after Close returned")
	assert.NotPanics(t, s.layer.Close)
}

func TestClose_ClosesTheListenerAndMayBeRepeated(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	addr, _ := s.enable(t)

	s.layer.Close()
	requireNoListener(t, addr)
	assert.NotPanics(t, s.layer.Close, "a second close is harmless")
	assert.NotPanics(t, (*Layer)(nil).Close, "so is one on a layer that never started")
}
```

```go
// A code the model made pairs a phone once, and the cookie it gets names the
// device to the layer through the model: the hash the layer computes from the
// 32 bytes the token decodes to is the one the model stored.
func TestPair_ACodeFromTheModelPairsAPhoneOnce(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	addr, _ := s.enable(t)
	pairing, err := s.tui.PairDevice("iPhone")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	typed := strings.ToLower(pairing.Code[:4] + "-" + pairing.Code[4:])
	payload := `{"code": "` + typed + `"}`

	resp := httpDo(t, addr, http.MethodPost, "/api/v1/pair", payload)
	require.Equal(t, http.StatusOK, resp.StatusCode, bodyText(t, resp))
	cookies := resp.Cookies()
	require.Len(t, cookies, 1)
	cookie := cookies[0]
	assert.Equal(t, cookieName, cookie.Name)
	raw, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(cookie.Value)
	require.NoError(t, err, "the model's token is unpadded base64url")
	assert.Len(t, raw, tokenBytes, "of 32 bytes")

	again := httpDo(t, addr, http.MethodPost, "/api/v1/pair", payload)
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusBadRequest, again.StatusCode, "a code works once; a used one is a wrong one, not an unpaired phone")
	assert.Empty(t, again.Cookies())

	status, device := s.whoAmI(cookie)
	require.Equal(t, http.StatusNoContent, status, "the hash of the token's 32 bytes, computed here, is the one the model stored")
	assert.Equal(t, "iPhone", device)
	status, device = s.whoAmI(&http.Cookie{Name: cookieName, Value: deviceToken(9)})
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusUnauthorized, status, "a well-formed token the model never made")
	assert.Empty(t, device)
}

func TestAuthed_RevokingADeviceRefusesOnlyItsCookie(t *testing.T) {
	s := newWebStack(t)
	s.start(t)
	addr, _ := s.enable(t)
	phone := s.pair(t, addr, "iPhone")
	tablet := s.pair(t, addr, "iPad")

	status, device := s.whoAmI(phone)
	require.Equal(t, http.StatusNoContent, status)
	require.Equal(t, "iPhone", device)

	require.NoError(t, s.tui.RevokeDevice("iPhone"))
	status, device = s.whoAmI(phone)
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusUnauthorized, status, "a revoked device's cookie")
	assert.Empty(t, device)
	status, device = s.whoAmI(tablet)
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusNoContent, status, "another device's cookie still works")
	assert.Equal(t, "iPad", device)
}
```

Replace with:

```go
type Layer struct {
	client   *rpc.Client
	host     core.WebHost
	hostname string
	build    string
	now      func() time.Time

	// rule is the origin rule of the listener now running, which guard
	// enforces; nil while none runs, and then guard refuses every request.
	rule atomic.Pointer[originRule]

	// bindMu guards the listener (srv) and closed, which bind, shutListener
	// and the unbinding take it for.
	bindMu sync.Mutex
	srv    *http.Server
	closed bool

	// applied, rev and reported are the drain's own: whether it has acted on
	// a published WebView yet, the Rev of the last one it acted on, and
	// whether it has told the host anything that turning the web off must
	// undo. Only the drain's goroutine touches them.
	applied  bool
	rev      uint64
	reported bool

	// done ends the drain, and drained is closed when the drain returns.
	// stopOnce runs the layer's stop once, whether Close or fail comes first.
	done     chan struct{}
	drained  chan struct{}
	stopOnce sync.Once

	// sinkMu guards sinks.
	sinkMu sync.Mutex

	sinks []sink
}
```

Append to the end of `internal/web/web.go` (after `stopParts`):

```go
// Options configure Start.
type Options struct {
	Server   *rpc.Server  // the daemon's server, dialled over net.Pipe with rpc.DialAs(…, rpc.RoleWeb)
	Host     core.WebHost // the loop
	Hostname string       // shown in the app's header
	Build    string       // the daemon's build ID: in the snapshot and in sw.js
}

// drainGrace is how long Close waits for the drain to return before it
// closes the client under it.
const drainGrace = 5 * time.Second

// Start joins the daemon's server as the web layer's client, builds the
// layer's parts and starts draining the client. The drain applies the
// published settings, so the layer listens once web.json enables the web,
// and binds afresh whenever a ConfigureWeb has changed the view's Rev. A
// server that cannot be joined (it is stopping, say) is an error, and the
// daemon runs on without the web.
func Start(o Options) (*Layer, error) {
	if o.Server == nil || o.Host == nil {
		return nil, errors.New("web: Start needs the daemon's server and its host")
	}
	a, b := net.Pipe()
	o.Server.Serve(a)
	client, err := rpc.DialAs(b, rpc.RoleWeb)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("web: join the daemon's server: %w", err)
	}
	l := &Layer{
		client:   client,
		host:     o.Host,
		hostname: o.Hostname,
		build:    o.Build,
		now:      time.Now,
		done:     make(chan struct{}),
		drained:  make(chan struct{}),
	}
	l.startParts()
	go l.drain()
	log.For("web").Info("web.started", "host", l.hostname, "build", l.build)
	return l, nil
}

// Close stops the layer: its parts first (stopParts: the streams hear their
// bye), then the listener, then the drain, then the layer's client. It is
// safe to call twice, concurrently, on a layer that failed or is failing
// (then it returns once that stop is complete), and on nil.
func (l *Layer) Close() {
	if l == nil {
		return
	}
	l.stop(nil)
}

// stop is the layer's one stop, shared by Close and fail: whichever comes
// first runs it, and any other caller waits until it is complete (sync.Once),
// so a fail racing a Close neither hangs nor closes anything twice. A non-nil
// cause is told to the host last, once the drain has returned: a binding the
// drain was making cannot report "serving" over it.
func (l *Layer) stop(cause error) {
	l.stopOnce.Do(func() {
		l.stopPartsSafely()
		close(l.done)
		l.shutListener()
		select {
		case <-l.drained:
		case <-time.After(drainGrace):
			log.For("web").Warn("web.drain_wedged")
		}
		l.client.Close()
		if cause != nil {
			l.report("", cause)
		}
	})
}

// stopPartsSafely is stopParts that logs a panic instead of raising it into
// the daemon's stop.
func (l *Layer) stopPartsSafely() {
	defer func() {
		if r := recover(); r != nil {
			log.For("web").Error("web.stop_parts_panicked", "panic", r, "stack", string(debug.Stack()))
		}
	}()
	l.stopParts()
}

// drain follows the daemon as the TUI does: a batch of events on each wake
// (Sync). Every batch goes to each sink, and each published WebView is
// applied. A lost client ends the layer, since the model it follows is gone.
func (l *Layer) drain() {
	defer l.recoverPanic("drain")
	defer close(l.drained)
	l.applyWeb(l.client.Web())
	for {
		l.deliver(l.client.Sync())
		if err := l.client.Err(); err != nil {
			l.fail(fmt.Errorf("lost the daemon's model: %w", err))
			return
		}
		select {
		case _, ok := <-l.client.Wakes():
			if !ok {
				return
			}
		case <-l.done:
			return
		}
	}
}

// deliver hands a batch to every sink in turn, then applies the settings a
// ModelChanged in it carries.
func (l *Layer) deliver(evs []core.Event) {
	if len(evs) == 0 {
		return
	}
	l.sinkMu.Lock()
	sinks := slices.Clone(l.sinks)
	l.sinkMu.Unlock()
	for _, s := range sinks {
		s.apply(evs)
	}
	for _, ev := range evs {
		if m, ok := ev.(core.ModelChanged); ok {
			l.applyWeb(m.View.Web)
		}
	}
}

// applyWeb makes the listener match the published settings whenever v.Rev
// differs from the Rev it last acted on (the first view it sees always
// counts): it closes the listener, which ends every open stream, and binds
// v.Listen when that is not empty. Every ConfigureWeb bumps Rev and nothing
// else does, so a view that carries only a report (WebServing), a device or
// a pairing leaves the listener alone, and a disable and an enable that one
// Sync coalesced into a single ModelChanged still rebind, though the settings
// they end on equal the ones already bound. A listen that fails leaves the
// web off, and the host is told why (a notice for the next TUI); it is tried
// again only by the next ConfigureWeb, whatever values that carries. The host
// hears only what the layer did: an error the model set itself (a web.json it
// left alone) is not the layer's to clear.
func (l *Layer) applyWeb(v core.WebView) {
	if l.applied && l.rev == v.Rev {
		return
	}
	l.applied, l.rev = true, v.Rev
	key := webKey{listen: v.Listen, origin: v.Origin}
	addr, err := l.bind(key)
	switch {
	case errors.Is(err, errLayerClosed):
		return
	case err != nil:
		log.For("web").Warn("web.listen_failed", "listen", key.listen, "origin", key.origin, "err", err)
		l.reported = true
		l.host.WebServing("", fmt.Errorf("the web layer cannot listen: %w", err))
	case addr != "":
		log.For("web").Info("web.serving", "addr", addr, "origin", key.origin)
		l.reported = true
		l.host.WebServing(addr, nil)
	case l.reported:
		log.For("web").Info("web.off")
		l.reported = false
		l.host.WebServing("", nil)
	}
}

// recoverPanic ends a goroutine of the layer that panicked, and is deferred
// first in each one: it recovers the panic, logs it with its stack under
// subsystem=web and stops the layer (fail) with an error naming where, so the
// daemon goes on without the web. It must be deferred directly (defer
// l.recoverPanic("drain")), since recover only works in the deferred call.
func (l *Layer) recoverPanic(where string) {
	r := recover()
	if r == nil {
		return
	}
	log.For("web").Error("web.panic", "where", where, "panic", r, "stack", string(debug.Stack()))
	l.fail(fmt.Errorf("the web layer stopped: %s panicked: %v", where, r))
}

// fail stops the layer because of err, and returns at once: the stop (stop)
// runs on a goroutine of its own, so a part may call fail from the goroutine
// that panicked, which the stop may be waiting for (the drain). The stop ends
// every stream, closes the listener and the client, and tells the host err
// through WebServing("", err), whose notice waits for the next TUI. Close
// runs the same stop: whichever comes first runs it, once.
func (l *Layer) fail(err error) {
	log.For("web").Error("web.stopped", "err", err)
	go func() {
		defer l.recoverPanic("stop")
		l.stop(err)
	}()
}

// report tells the host the listener's state, and logs a panic from the host
// (a failed model re-raises its own) rather than raising it.
func (l *Layer) report(addr string, err error) {
	defer func() {
		if r := recover(); r != nil {
			log.For("web").Error("web.report_failed", "panic", r)
		}
	}()
	l.host.WebServing(addr, err)
}
```

Append to the end of `internal/web/server.go`:

```go
const (
	// readHeaderTimeout and idleTimeout bound a connection that is slow to
	// ask or idle after an answer. There is no read or write timeout: a
	// stream stays open for hours and pings instead.
	readHeaderTimeout = 10 * time.Second
	idleTimeout       = 120 * time.Second
)

// webKey is the settings a listener is bound for: bind's argument. When to
// bind is the view's Rev (applyWeb), not a comparison of keys.
type webKey struct{ listen, origin string }

// errLayerClosed is bind's answer once the layer has closed or failed: it
// binds nothing after that.
var errLayerClosed = errors.New("the web layer is closed")

// bind makes the listener match key and returns the address it listens on,
// "" when key turns the web off. The old listener closes first, which ends
// every stream. An address that is not a loopback IP literal and port, an
// origin guard cannot enforce, and a listen that fails all leave nothing
// listening and are errors: the layer falls back to no address.
func (l *Layer) bind(key webKey) (string, error) {
	l.bindMu.Lock()
	defer l.bindMu.Unlock()
	if l.closed {
		return "", errLayerClosed
	}
	l.unbindLocked()
	if key.listen == "" {
		return "", nil
	}
	if ap, err := netip.ParseAddrPort(key.listen); err != nil || !ap.Addr().IsLoopback() {
		return "", fmt.Errorf("%q is not a loopback IP address and port", key.listen)
	}
	rule, err := parseOrigin(key.origin)
	if err != nil {
		return "", err
	}
	ln, err := net.Listen("tcp", key.listen)
	if err != nil {
		return "", err
	}
	l.srv = &http.Server{
		Handler:           l.guard(l.mux()),
		ReadHeaderTimeout: readHeaderTimeout,
		IdleTimeout:       idleTimeout,
		ErrorLog:          slog.NewLogLogger(log.For("web").Handler(), slog.LevelWarn),
	}
	l.rule.Store(&rule)
	go l.serve(l.srv, ln)
	return ln.Addr().String(), nil
}

// unbindLocked closes the running listener and every connection on it. The
// rule goes first, so a request on a connection about to close is refused.
// l.bindMu is held.
func (l *Layer) unbindLocked() {
	l.rule.Store(nil)
	if l.srv != nil {
		_ = l.srv.Close()
		l.srv = nil
	}
}

// shutListener closes the listener for good: bind binds nothing after it.
func (l *Layer) shutListener() {
	l.bindMu.Lock()
	defer l.bindMu.Unlock()
	l.closed = true
	l.unbindLocked()
}

// serve serves ln until it closes. A listener that stops on its own is a
// failure of the layer.
func (l *Layer) serve(srv *http.Server, ln net.Listener) {
	defer l.recoverPanic("listener")
	if err := srv.Serve(ln); err != nil && !errors.Is(err, http.ErrServerClosed) {
		l.fail(fmt.Errorf("the listener stopped: %w", err))
	}
}
```

Append to `internal/web/CLAUDE.md`, directly after its last line:

```markdown
- **Join the daemon's server only as `rpc.RoleWeb`.** The server keeps a notice for the next TUI only while no other connection exists, and the always-connected web client would swallow every notice raised while no TUI is open. **Enforced** by `TestStart_ANoticeRaisedWithOnlyTheLayerConnectedWaitsForTheNextTUI`.
- **Change the listener only from the drain, only on a loopback IP literal, and leave the web off when a bind fails.** A failed or refused address reported as serving, or a fallback to another address, exposes a port nobody chose. **Enforced** by `TestApplyWeb_ListensOnceTheModelEnablesTheWeb`, `TestApplyWeb_ChangingTheListenAddressMovesTheListener`, `TestApplyWeb_ChangingOnlyTheOriginChangesTheGuard`, `TestApplyWeb_DisablingStopsServing`, `TestApplyWeb_ATakenPortLeavesTheWebOffAndSaysWhy` and `TestBind_ListensOnLoopbackOnly`.
- **Rebind exactly when `WebView.Rev` differs from the last one the layer acted on: never by comparing `listen` and `origin`, and never on a `WebServing` report.** Every `ConfigureWeb` bumps `Rev` and nothing else does, so a disable and an enable that one `Sync` coalesces into a single `ModelChanged` (the settings already bound) still rebind, a retry with the very values that failed still binds, and the layer's own report does not end every open stream by rebinding. Compared by key, a coalesced pair or a retry leaves no listener while the model's `listen` is set, and nothing says why. **Enforced** by `TestApplyWeb_TwoConfigurationsCoalescedIntoOneSyncStillRebind`, `TestApplyWeb_TheSameSettingsAgainRetryAFailedBind` and `TestApplyWeb_AWebServingReportNeverBinds`.
- **Tell the host (`WebServing`) only what the layer did, and clear only an error the layer set.** A `web.json` the model left alone keeps its reason in `WebView.Error` with `listen` empty; reporting "off" over it would wipe the only explanation of why the web is dead. **Enforced** by `TestApplyWeb_AWebJSONTheModelLeftAloneKeepsItsReason`.
- **Defer `l.recoverPanic(where)` first in every goroutine the layer starts, and stop the layer only through `fail` or `Close`, never by waiting for the stop `fail` starts.** A panic stops the layer, reports it through `WebServing` (a notice for the next TUI) and leaves the daemon running; `fail` returns at once and runs the one stop `Close` also runs, on a goroutine of its own, so a part may call it from the goroutine the stop waits for. **Enforced** by `TestDrain_APanickingSinkStopsTheLayerNotTheDaemon`, `TestRecoverPanic_StopsTheLayerAndNamesWhere`, `TestFail_ReturnsAtOnceFromTheDrainItself` and `TestFail_RacingAClose_StopsOnceAndHangsNowhere`; the goroutines later parts start are **Convention** — a panic that takes the daemon down.
- **Run sinks only on the drain, and never block or do network I/O in one.** A sink that blocks stalls the drain, so the client's queues grow and every part stops following the model. **Enforced** by `TestDrain_HandsEveryBatchToEverySinkInTurn`; a sink that blocks is **Convention** — a layer that silently stops following the daemon.
- **Set the cookie to exactly the token text the host returns, and hash the 32 bytes that text decodes to, as the model does.** Hashing the cookie's text on one side and the bytes on the other makes every paired phone a stranger, every route answering 401 with nothing logged. **Enforced** by `TestPair_ACodeFromTheModelPairsAPhoneOnce` and `TestAuthed_RevokingADeviceRefusesOnlyItsCookie`.
```

```go
// freeLoopbackAddr is a loopback address nothing listens on yet.
func freeLoopbackAddr(t *testing.T) string {
	t.Helper()
	ln, err := net.Listen("tcp", "127.0.0.1:0")
	require.NoError(t, err)
	addr := ln.Addr().String()
	require.NoError(t, ln.Close())
	return addr
}

// refused reports whether nothing accepts connections at addr.
func refused(addr string) bool {
	c, err := net.DialTimeout("tcp", addr, time.Second)
	if err != nil {
		return true
	}
	_ = c.Close()
	return false
}

// postPairing posts body to the web layer's pairing route at addr as the
// phone does (with the layer's own origin), and returns the status and the
// body. A non-empty host replaces the Host header.
func postPairing(t *testing.T, addr, body, host string) (int, string) {
	t.Helper()
	req, err := http.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, "http://"+addr+"/api/v1/pair", strings.NewReader(body))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	req.Header.Set("Origin", "http://"+addr)
	if host != "" {
		req.Host = host
	}
	client := &http.Client{Timeout: 10 * time.Second, Transport: &http.Transport{DisableKeepAlives: true}}
	resp, err := client.Do(req)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	defer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()
	data, err := io.ReadAll(resp.Body)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	return resp.StatusCode, string(data)
}

// The daemon runs the web layer beside the mod server. It listens only once
// web.json enables the web, on the model's say, and its guard and pairing
// route answer behind the daemon's own listener.
func TestServe_ServesTheWebLayerOnceWebJSONEnablesIt(t *testing.T) {
	dir := globalDir(t)
	r := serve(t, dir, Options{})
	c := dial(t, r.socket)
	assert.False(t, c.Web().Serving, "the web is off until web.json enables it")

	addr := freeLoopbackAddr(t)
	require.NoError(t, c.ConfigureWeb(addr, "http://"+addr))
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool { return c.Web().Serving }, 10*time.Second, 10*time.Millisecond, "the layer never listened")
	assert.Equal(t, addr, c.Web().Addr)
	assert.FileExists(t, filepath.Join(dir, core.WebFile), "the settings are the model's file")

	status, body := postPairing(t, addr, `{"code": "AAAA-AAAA"}`, "")
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusBadRequest, status, "the guard let the right Host and Origin through to a pairing route that refuses a code nobody made")
	assert.Contains(t, body, "pairing")
	status, _ = postPairing(t, addr, `{"code": "AAAA-AAAA"}`, "evil.example")
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusMisdirectedRequest, status, "and refused a wrong Host")
}

// The web layer stops first: its listener is closed before any client hears
// the daemon's bye, so a phone's streams end with the layer's own bye and a
// request of the phone's cannot reach a loop that is quiescing. A run in
// which the order is wrong can pass by timing, since the window is short.
func TestServe_AStopClosesTheWebListenerBeforeAnyClientHearsTheBye(t *testing.T) {
	dir := globalDir(t)
	r := serve(t, dir, Options{})
	c := dial(t, r.socket)
	addr := freeLoopbackAddr(t)
	require.NoError(t, c.ConfigureWeb(addr, "http://"+addr))
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool { return c.Web().Serving }, 10*time.Second, 10*time.Millisecond, "the layer never listened")
	require.False(t, refused(addr))

	close(r.stop)
	require.Eventually(t, c.Stopping, 10*time.Second, 5*time.Millisecond, "the client heard the bye")
	assert.True(t, refused(addr), "the web listener was open when a client heard the bye")
	select {
	case err := <-r.done:
		require.NoError(t, err)
	case <-time.After(20 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("the daemon never stopped")
	}
}

// The settings are the model's, in web.json: the next daemon serves the web
// again without being told.
func TestServe_TheWebLayerComesBackWithTheNextDaemon(t *testing.T) {
	dir := globalDir(t)
	first := serve(t, dir, Options{})
	c := dial(t, first.socket)
	addr := freeLoopbackAddr(t)
	require.NoError(t, c.ConfigureWeb(addr, "http://"+addr))
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool { return c.Web().Serving }, 10*time.Second, 10*time.Millisecond, "the layer never listened")
	require.NoError(t, first.shutdown(t))
	require.True(t, refused(addr), "a stopped daemon serves no web")

	second := serve(t, dir, Options{})
	c2 := dial(t, second.socket)
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool { return c2.Web().Serving }, 10*time.Second, 10*time.Millisecond, "the next daemon never served the stored settings")
	assert.Equal(t, addr, c2.Web().Addr)
	status, _ := postPairing(t, addr, `{"code": "AAAA-AAAA"}`, "")
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusBadRequest, status)
}
```

Replace with:

```go
// startWeb starts the web layer (internal/web) as a client of the daemon's
// own server, with the loop as its host. The layer listens only when web.json
// enables the web, so a daemon with the web off pays one idle connection. One
// that can't start is logged and costs only the web: it returns nil, which
// Close accepts.
func startWeb(srv *rpc.Server, loop *core.Loop, rec Record) *web.Layer {
	layer, err := web.Start(web.Options{Server: srv, Host: loop, Hostname: rec.Host, Build: rec.Build})
	if err != nil {
		log.For("serve").Warn("serve.web_start_failed", "err", err)
		return nil
	}
	return layer
}
```

Create `internal/web/README.md`:

```markdown
# internal/web

The daemon's web layer: what `loom serve` starts so a phone can see the fleet and act on it. It is a client of the daemon's own `rpc.Server`, joined over `net.Pipe` as the `web` role, plus `core.WebHost` for the device records the model keeps in `web.json`. It serves an embedded app, a JSON API and a live stream on a loopback port that a Cloudflare tunnel reaches, and sends Web Push. It holds no session state, and logs under `subsystem=web`. Rules for changing it: [`CLAUDE.md`](CLAUDE.md).

## Files

| File | Holds |
|---|---|
| `web.go` | `Options`, `Start`, `Layer` and `Close`; the drain; `fail` and `recoverPanic`; the `sink` and `mux` seams each part registers through |
| `server.go` | the listener (`bind`), `guard` and the origin rule |
| `auth.go` | pairing (`routePair`), `authed`, the device cookie |
| `jsonio.go` | `readJSON`, `writeJSON`, `writeError` |
| `testmain_test.go` | isolates the loom dirs and tmux for the package's tests |

## How it runs

`daemon.Serve` calls `web.Start` after the mod server listens and `Layer.Close` first when it stops, before the bye. `Start` joins the server as the `web` role, which the server does not count as a client, so a notice raised while only the layer is connected waits for the next TUI. It builds its parts (`startParts`) and then drains the client as the TUI does: on each wake it takes `Sync`'s events, hands them to each sink in turn, and applies the `WebView` a `ModelChanged` carries.

The layer listens only while `WebView.Listen` is set. It acts on `WebView.Rev`, which the model bumps on every `ConfigureWeb` and on nothing else: whenever `Rev` differs from the last one the layer acted on, it closes the listener, which ends every stream, and binds `Listen` unless it is empty (then it stays closed). Two `ConfigureWeb`s that one `Sync` coalesces still differ from that `Rev`, so a disable and an enable are never missed. What happened goes back to the model through `WebHost.WebServing`, which leaves `Rev` alone, so a report never binds. A listen that fails (the port is taken) leaves the web off with the reason in `WebView.Error` and a notice for the next TUI, and is tried again only by the next `ConfigureWeb`, the same values included: `loom web enable` again. The listener takes loopback IP literals only, whatever `web.json` says.

A panic in the drain or the listener stops the layer, tells the model (the same notice) and leaves the daemon running. Each goroutine the layer starts defers `recoverPanic` first, which calls `fail`; `fail` returns at once and stops the layer on a goroutine of its own, so a part can call it from the goroutine that panicked. `Close` runs the same stop, once between them: whichever comes first stops the layer, and the other waits until it is complete. `Close` still has to run at the daemon's stop.

## Every request

`guard` stands in front of every route:

1. The security headers go on the answer, errors included.
2. The `Host` must be the origin's host (default port dropped, any case), else 421.
3. A method other than GET or HEAD must carry exactly one `Origin` header equal to the origin, else 403.
4. The body is capped at `maxBody` (1 MiB).

With no listener bound there is no origin rule, and `guard` refuses everything.

## Pairing and the device cookie

`POST /api/v1/pair` takes `{"code": "K7QM-2XPA"}` and asks the host to redeem it. The model checks the code, consumes it and makes the device; the layer sets the token it returns as the cookie `__Host-loom` (Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, Path=/) and answers `{"device": "<name>"}`. The refusals are `{"error": <text>}` with a status the app keys on: a wrong, used or expired code is 400 (never 401, which the app reads as "not paired"), too many wrong codes 429, a name already paired or 16 devices paired 409, and a web that is off 503; any other failure is a 500 with a fixed text.

`authed` wraps every route after pairing: it reads the cookie, decodes it as unpadded base64url, refuses (401, without asking the host) a value that is not exactly 32 bytes, and asks the host which device holds the SHA-256 of those 32 bytes. No device is a 401; otherwise the device is touched (last seen) and the handler runs with its name.

## Tests

The tests boot a model on its loop, serve it with the real `rpc.Server`, start the layer on it and talk to it over HTTP on a loopback port (`webStack` in `web_test.go`). `TestMain` points the loom dirs and tmux at throwaway ones.
```

Append to `internal/web/CLAUDE.md`, directly after its last line:

```markdown
- **Start the layer after the mod server and close it first when the daemon stops, before the server's bye (`daemon.Serve`).** The streams hear their own bye and the listener closes before any client is told the daemon is stopping, so a phone shows "loom is restarting" rather than a dropped connection, and no request of a phone reaches a loop that is quiescing. A layer that can't start costs only the web. **Enforced** by `TestServe_ServesTheWebLayerOnceWebJSONEnablesIt`, `TestServe_AStopClosesTheWebListenerBeforeAnyClientHearsTheBye` and `TestServe_TheWebLayerComesBackWithTheNextDaemon` (`internal/daemon/serve_test.go`); that a failed start leaves the daemon running is **Convention** — a daemon that exits because the web could not start.
```

## Story 7: The fleet projection and the live stream

- **Developer:** intermediate-dev
- **Files:** `internal/web/fleet.go`, `internal/web/fleet_test.go`, `internal/web/stream.go`, `internal/web/stream_test.go`, `internal/web/web.go`
- **Interfaces:** exposes the JSON shapes of the contract, `groupOf`, `noticeOf`, `noticeJSON` and `l.hub` (`publish`, `sendTo`, `closeDevice`); uses the Story 6 seams, `core.InstanceView.RemoteSession` and `WaitDetail` (Story 1), `ModelView.Web`, `WebDevice`, `NotifySettings` and `PushState` (Story 3), and `core.WebHost` (Story 4)
- **Verify:** `CGO_ENABLED=0 go test ./internal/web/...`
- **Review:** deep

A sink turns `ViewsChanged` and `WorkspacesChanged` into display-ready sessions, grouped and labelled, in a projection the app can read without mapping anything (`fleet.go`). The hub (`stream.go`) is that sink: it keeps the projection, the device records from `ModelChanged` and the last 20 notices; `GET /api/v1/stream` gives each connection a snapshot (the build, the host, the workspaces with their sessions, the device, the badge, which is the number of sessions in `needs_you` across the workspaces, and the recent notices), then each changed workspace's list at most four times a second (a flusher goroutine, `publishEvery`), the notices as they come, a `device` event when the device's own record changes, a ping every 20 s, and `bye` when the layer closes. It enforces 4 streams per device (a fifth ends that device's oldest) and 32 in all (a 33rd is refused with 503), sets the cookie again on connect, ends a device's streams, with no `bye`, when a published device list drops the device or names it with another pairing time, and refuses with 503 a device that list does not name (a connection that raced the hub reading a pairing or a revocation: the model vouched for the cookie an instant before, and the app tries again). A stream that falls 64 frames behind is ended, and its app reconnects for a fresh snapshot. Two tasks keep the tree green: Task 7.1 is the projection alone, Task 7.2 the hub, the route and the three pinned functions of `web.go` that wire the hub into the layer, together, since the stream tests run the whole layer on Story 6's `webStack`. None of the code holds a landmine marker.

Where the contract is silent this story chooses as follows, and Stories 8, 10 and 12 are to be read against it. It declares the constants it uses (`maxStreamsPerDevice`, `maxStreams`, `pingEvery`, `publishEvery`, `recentNotices`) in `stream.go`; Story 6 declares `maxBody` and Story 8 `replyWait`. A session's `agents` is a list of `{"name", "description", "idle"}`, `since` is an RFC 3339 string (`""` when the status was not entered in this process), and `device.notify.paused_until` is an RFC 3339 string (`""` when not paused). The ring of recent notices holds every notice the hub sends to a stream: those the sink takes from the model (`Req` 0), and those sent through `publish` or `sendTo`, which is how Story 8 sends a request's late notice to the requesting device, so a phone that missed it (it was not connected) finds it in its next snapshot. The ring is shared by all devices, and a notice returned in an HTTP response is not in it. A panic in the hub's flusher is the layer's: the flusher defers `l.recoverPanic("hub")` first, which calls Story 6's `fail`, so the whole layer stops (that call returns at once and the stop runs on a goroutine of its own, which ends every stream through `stopParts`, that is `hub.close`). `close` cannot deadlock with a flusher that is itself panicking: the flusher closes `exited` before `recoverPanic` runs (it is deferred first, so it runs last), and `close` waits for the flusher and the streams for `byeGrace` at most. The tests run the layer on Story 6's `webStack` (a booted model, the real `rpc.Server`, the layer on a loopback port, a device paired through the pairing route), over a model with a workspace and sessions on a loop that holds its jobs and never ticks, so only a test changes a session; pure functions (`groupOf`, `sessionOf`, `deviceOf`, `noticeOf`, the projection) are tested on view literals. Where a test cannot get what it needs from the model (a notice tied to a request, a flusher that panics, a stream nothing reads, a hub that lags the model), it uses the hub's own seams (`apply` with a literal event, the knobs `every`, `ping` and `flushFn`, `subscribe`) or holds the drain with a gate sink (`holdDrain`), and says so.

```go
// fleetView is a started instance in a status; a test sets what else it needs.
func fleetView(id core.InstanceID, title string, status session.Status) core.InstanceView {
	return core.InstanceView{ID: id, Title: title, Status: status, Started: true}
}

func TestGroupOf_EachStatusLandsInItsGroup(t *testing.T) {
	cases := []struct {
		name    string
		status  session.Status
		started bool
		group   string
		label   string
	}{
		{"prompting needs you", session.Prompting, true, "needs_you", "Needs you"},
		{"prompting needs you before it is started too", session.Prompting, false, "needs_you", "Needs you"},
		{"a started ready is your turn", session.Ready, true, "your_turn", "Your turn"},
		{"an unstarted ready is paused", session.Ready, false, "paused", "Not started"},
		{"running is working", session.Running, true, "working", "Working"},
		{"running is working before it is started too", session.Running, false, "working", "Working"},
		{"loading is working", session.Loading, false, "working", "Loading"},
		{"deleting is working", session.Deleting, true, "working", "Deleting"},
		{"paused", session.Paused, true, "paused", "Paused"},
		{"recoverable is paused", session.Recoverable, false, "paused", "Recoverable"},
		{"a status this build does not know is shown as itself", session.Status(99), true, "paused", "Status(99)"},
	}
	for _, tc := range cases {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			v := fleetView(1, "t", tc.status)
			v.Started = tc.started
			group, label := groupOf(v)
			assert.Equal(t, tc.group, group)
			assert.Equal(t, tc.label, label)
		})
	}
}

func TestSessionOf_ProjectsEveryFactTheAppShows(t *testing.T) {
	v := core.InstanceView{
		ID:             1234567,
		Title:          "fix login",
		Branch:         "loom/fix-login",
		RepoPath:       "/home/me/src/app",
		WorktreePath:   "/home/me/src/app/.loom/worktrees/fix-login",
		TmuxSession:    "loom_fix-login",
		Program:        "claude",
		Status:         session.Prompting,
		StatusSince:    time.Date(2026, 10, 21, 9, 30, 0, 0, time.UTC),
		WaitReason:     "permission",
		WaitDetail:     "rm -rf build",
		LastMessage:    "Should I delete the build dir?",
		HasLastMessage: true,
		Subagents:      []subagent.View{{Name: "explorer", Description: "reads the tree", Idle: true}},
		Diff:           git.DiffStats{Added: 12, Removed: 3, Content: "a long diff body"},
		HasDiff:        true,
		GitHub: github.State{
			Known: true, HasPR: true, PRNumber: 42, PRState: github.PROpen, Checks: github.ChecksFailing,
		},
		Ahead:         2,
		Behind:        5,
		ParityKnown:   true,
		Account:       "work",
		RemoteSession: "session_01AbC",
		Started:       true,
		Bell:          true,
	}

	got, err := json.Marshal(sessionOf(7, v))
	require.NoError(t, err)

	// The paths, the program and the diff body are not in it: the app shows
	// none of them.
	assert.JSONEq(t, `{
		"id": 1234567, "workspace": 7, "title": "fix login", "branch": "loom/fix-login",
		"group": "needs_you", "label": "Needs you", "status": "Prompting",
		"since": "2026-10-21T09:30:00Z",
		"wait": {"reason": "permission", "detail": "rm -rf build"},
		"last_message": "Should I delete the build dir?",
		"diff": {"added": 12, "removed": 3},
		"ahead": 2, "behind": 5, "parity_known": true,
		"pr": {"number": 42, "state": "open", "checks": "failing"},
		"account": "work",
		"agents": [{"name": "explorer", "description": "reads the tree", "idle": true}],
		"remote_url": "https://claude.ai/code/session_01AbC"
	}`, string(got))
}

func TestSessionOf_WithholdsWhatIsNotKnown(t *testing.T) {
	v := fleetView(2, "idle", session.Ready)
	v.LastMessage = "stale words"                                             // HasLastMessage is false
	v.Diff = git.DiffStats{Added: 9, Removed: 9}                              // HasDiff is false
	v.Ahead, v.Behind = 4, 4                                                  // ParityKnown is false
	v.GitHub = github.State{HasPR: true, PRNumber: 8, PRState: github.PROpen} // Known is false

	s := sessionOf(1, v)

	assert.Empty(t, s.LastMessage)
	assert.Equal(t, diffJSON{}, s.Diff)
	assert.Zero(t, s.Ahead)
	assert.Zero(t, s.Behind)
	assert.False(t, s.ParityKnown)
	assert.Nil(t, s.PR)
	assert.Empty(t, s.Since)
	assert.Empty(t, s.RemoteURL)

	// Empty lists and no PR are `[]` and `null` on the wire, never absent.
	b, err := json.Marshal(s)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Contains(t, string(b), `"pr":null`)
	assert.Contains(t, string(b), `"agents":[]`)
	assert.Contains(t, string(b), `"since":""`)
}

func TestSessionOf_ThePRNeedsAKnownPRInAStateWeName(t *testing.T) {
	cases := []struct {
		name  string
		state github.State
		want  bool
	}{
		{"a known PR", github.State{Known: true, HasPR: true, PRNumber: 1, PRState: github.PROpen}, true},
		{"known, with no PR", github.State{Known: true}, false},
		{"a PR in a repo that was not queried", github.State{HasPR: true, PRNumber: 1, PRState: github.PROpen}, false},
		{"a PR in no state", github.State{Known: true, HasPR: true, PRNumber: 1}, false},
		{"a PR in a state this build does not know", github.State{Known: true, HasPR: true, PRNumber: 1, PRState: github.PRState(99)}, false},
		{"checks this build does not know", github.State{Known: true, HasPR: true, PRNumber: 1, PRState: github.PROpen, Checks: github.Checks(99)}, false},
	}
	for _, tc := range cases {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			pr := prOf(tc.state)
			if tc.want {
				require.NotNil(t, pr)
				assert.Equal(t, 1, pr.Number)
			} else {
				assert.Nil(t, pr)
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestPRWords(t *testing.T) {
	for state, word := range map[github.PRState]string{
		github.PRDraft:  "draft",
		github.PROpen:   "open",
		github.PRMerged: "merged",
		github.PRClosed: "closed",
	} {
		pr := prOf(github.State{Known: true, HasPR: true, PRNumber: 7, PRState: state})
		require.NotNil(t, pr, word)
		assert.Equal(t, word, pr.State)
		assert.Equal(t, "none", pr.Checks)
	}
	for checks, word := range map[github.Checks]string{
		github.ChecksNone:    "none",
		github.ChecksPending: "pending",
		github.ChecksPassing: "passing",
		github.ChecksFailing: "failing",
	} {
		pr := prOf(github.State{Known: true, HasPR: true, PRNumber: 7, PRState: github.PROpen, Checks: checks})
		require.NotNil(t, pr, word)
		assert.Equal(t, word, pr.Checks)
		assert.Equal(t, "open", pr.State)
	}
}

func TestSessionOf_TheRemoteLinkStaysOnClaudesPath(t *testing.T) {
	v := fleetView(1, "t", session.Running)
	v.RemoteSession = "a/b?c#d"
	assert.Equal(t, "https://claude.ai/code/a%2Fb%3Fc%23d", sessionOf(1, v).RemoteURL)

	v.RemoteSession = ""
	assert.Empty(t, sessionOf(1, v).RemoteURL)
}

func TestDeviceOf(t *testing.T) {
	until := time.Date(2026, 10, 21, 11, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
	d := core.WebDevice{
		Name:     "iPhone",
		Paired:   time.Date(2026, 10, 1, 8, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC),
		LastSeen: time.Date(2026, 10, 21, 10, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC),
		Notify:   core.NotifySettings{Waiting: true, PausedUntil: until},
		Push:     core.PushOK,
	}

	got := deviceOf(d)
	assert.Equal(t, deviceJSON{
		Name:   "iPhone",
		Notify: notifyJSON{Waiting: true, Finished: false, PausedUntil: "2026-10-21T11:00:00Z"},
		Push:   "ok",
	}, got)

	// The app's record has no token, no pairing time and no last-seen time.
	b, err := json.Marshal(got)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.JSONEq(t, `{"name": "iPhone", "notify": {"waiting": true, "finished": false, "paused_until": "2026-10-21T11:00:00Z"}, "push": "ok"}`, string(b))

	d.Notify.PausedUntil = time.Time{}
	d.Push = core.PushFailing(500)
	got = deviceOf(d)
	assert.Equal(t, "", got.Notify.PausedUntil)
	assert.Equal(t, "failing: 500", got.Push)
}

func TestNoticeOf(t *testing.T) {
	at := time.Date(2026, 10, 21, 12, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)

	failed := noticeOf(core.Notice{Err: errors.New("push failed")}, at)
	assert.Equal(t, "push failed", failed.Text)
	assert.True(t, failed.Error)
	assert.True(t, failed.At.Equal(at))

	info := noticeOf(core.Notice{Info: "stash kept"}, at)
	assert.Equal(t, "stash kept", info.Text)
	assert.False(t, info.Error)

	b, err := json.Marshal(info)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.JSONEq(t, `{"text": "stash kept", "error": false, "at": "2026-10-21T12:00:00Z"}`, string(b))
}

func TestWorkspaceOf(t *testing.T) {
	b, err := json.Marshal(workspaceOf(core.WorkspaceView{ID: 3, Name: "loom", Label: "loom", RepoPath: "/home/me/loom", ConfigDir: "/home/me/loom/.loom"}))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.JSONEq(t, `{"id": 3, "name": "loom", "label": "loom"}`, string(b))

	global := workspaceOf(core.WorkspaceView{ID: 4, Label: "global"})
	assert.Equal(t, "", global.Name)
	assert.Equal(t, "global", global.Label)
}

func TestSnapshotJSON_HasTheContractsKeys(t *testing.T) {
	b, err := json.Marshal(snapshotJSON{
		Workspaces: []workspaceJSON{},
		Sessions:   map[core.WorkspaceID][]sessionJSON{},
		Notices:    []noticeJSON{},
	})
	require.NoError(t, err)

	var keys map[string]json.RawMessage
	require.NoError(t, json.Unmarshal(b, &keys))
	var got []string
	for k := range keys {
		got = append(got, k)
	}
	assert.ElementsMatch(t, []string{"build", "host", "workspaces", "sessions", "device", "badge", "notices"}, got)
}

func TestProjection_KeepsTheModelsOrderAndLeavesWorkspaceTerminalsOut(t *testing.T) {
	p := newProjection()
	p.setWorkspaces([]core.WorkspaceView{{ID: 1, Name: "a", Label: "a"}})
	terminal := fleetView(12, "terminal", session.Ready)
	terminal.IsWorkspaceTerminal = true

	ok := p.setViews(1, []core.InstanceView{
		fleetView(11, "listed first", session.Running),
		terminal,
		fleetView(10, "listed second, with the lower ID", session.Prompting),
	})

	require.True(t, ok)
	require.Len(t, p.sessions[1], 2)
	assert.Equal(t, core.InstanceID(11), p.sessions[1][0].ID)
	assert.Equal(t, core.InstanceID(10), p.sessions[1][1].ID)
	assert.Equal(t, core.WorkspaceID(1), p.sessions[1][0].Workspace)
}

func TestProjection_IgnoresViewsOfAWorkspaceItDoesNotServe(t *testing.T) {
	p := newProjection()
	p.setWorkspaces([]core.WorkspaceView{{ID: 1, Label: "a"}})

	ok := p.setViews(9, []core.InstanceView{fleetView(1, "x", session.Running)})

	assert.False(t, ok)
	assert.NotContains(t, p.sessions, core.WorkspaceID(9))
}

func TestProjection_ANewWorkspaceStartsEmptyAndAGoneOneLosesItsSessions(t *testing.T) {
	p := newProjection()
	p.setWorkspaces([]core.WorkspaceView{{ID: 1, Label: "a"}, {ID: 2, Label: "b"}})
	require.True(t, p.setViews(1, []core.InstanceView{fleetView(10, "kept", session.Running)}))
	require.True(t, p.setViews(2, []core.InstanceView{fleetView(20, "lost", session.Running)}))

	p.setWorkspaces([]core.WorkspaceView{{ID: 1, Label: "a"}, {ID: 3, Label: "c"}})

	require.Len(t, p.workspaces, 2)
	assert.Equal(t, core.WorkspaceID(3), p.workspaces[1].ID)
	require.Len(t, p.sessions[1], 1, "a workspace that stays keeps its sessions")
	assert.Equal(t, "kept", p.sessions[1][0].Title)
	assert.NotNil(t, p.sessions[3], "a new workspace has an empty list, not none")
	assert.Empty(t, p.sessions[3])
	assert.NotContains(t, p.sessions, core.WorkspaceID(2))
	assert.True(t, p.served(1))
	assert.True(t, p.served(3))
	assert.False(t, p.served(2))
	assert.False(t, p.served(9))
}

func TestProjection_TheBadgeCountsWhatNeedsYouAcrossTheWorkspaces(t *testing.T) {
	p := newProjection()
	assert.Zero(t, p.badge(), "an empty fleet needs no one")
	p.setWorkspaces([]core.WorkspaceView{{ID: 1, Label: "a"}, {ID: 2, Label: "b"}})
	terminal := fleetView(14, "terminal", session.Prompting)
	terminal.IsWorkspaceTerminal = true
	require.True(t, p.setViews(1, []core.InstanceView{
		fleetView(10, "asks", session.Prompting),
		fleetView(11, "works", session.Running),
		fleetView(12, "idle", session.Ready),
		terminal,
	}))
	require.True(t, p.setViews(2, []core.InstanceView{
		fleetView(20, "also asks", session.Prompting),
		fleetView(21, "paused", session.Paused),
	}))

	assert.Equal(t, 2, p.badge(), "the terminal is not a session of the fleet")

	require.True(t, p.setViews(1, []core.InstanceView{fleetView(10, "answered", session.Running)}))
	assert.Equal(t, 1, p.badge())
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it to see it fail**

Run: `CGO_ENABLED=0 go test ./internal/web/ -run 'TestGroupOf|TestSessionOf|TestPRWords|TestDeviceOf|TestNoticeOf|TestWorkspaceOf|TestSnapshotJSON|TestProjection'`

Expected: the package fails to build, `./fleet_test.go:NN:NN: undefined: groupOf` (and `sessionOf`, `prOf`, `deviceOf`, `noticeOf`, `workspaceOf`, `snapshotJSON`, `newProjection`), then `FAIL github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/internal/web [build failed]`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `internal/web/fleet.go`:

```go
package web

import (
	"net/url"
	"time"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/session"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/session/github"
)

// The projection turns the replica's views into what the app reads. The app
// maps nothing itself: the group, the label, the PR words and the Claude
// link are decided here, once, from the model's own facts, so the phone shows
// the status the TUI shows.

// remoteBase is where a session's bridge ID opens in Claude.
const remoteBase = "https://claude.ai/code/"

// The groups the app lists, as the wire spells them.
const (
	groupNeedsYou = "needs_you"
	groupYourTurn = "your_turn"
	groupWorking  = "working"
	groupPaused   = "paused"
)

// workspaceJSON is a served workspace. It carries no path.
type workspaceJSON struct {
	ID    core.WorkspaceID `json:"id"`
	Name  string           `json:"name"`
	Label string           `json:"label"`
}

type waitJSON struct {
	Reason string `json:"reason"`
	Detail string `json:"detail"`
}

type diffJSON struct {
	Added   int `json:"added"`
	Removed int `json:"removed"`
}

type prJSON struct {
	Number int    `json:"number"`
	State  string `json:"state"`
	Checks string `json:"checks"`
}

// agentJSON is one running subagent, as subagent.View has it.
type agentJSON struct {
	Name        string `json:"name"`
	Description string `json:"description"`
	Idle        bool   `json:"idle"`
}

// sessionJSON is one session as the app reads it. IDs are numbers: they fit
// in 53 bits, exact in JavaScript. Since is an RFC 3339 time, "" when the
// status was not entered in this process; PR is null without a known PR.
type sessionJSON struct {
	ID          core.InstanceID  `json:"id"`
	Workspace   core.WorkspaceID `json:"workspace"`
	Title       string           `json:"title"`
	Branch      string           `json:"branch"`
	Group       string           `json:"group"`
	Label       string           `json:"label"`
	Status      string           `json:"status"`
	Since       string           `json:"since"`
	Wait        waitJSON         `json:"wait"`
	LastMessage string           `json:"last_message"`
	Diff        diffJSON         `json:"diff"`
	Ahead       int              `json:"ahead"`
	Behind      int              `json:"behind"`
	ParityKnown bool             `json:"parity_known"`
	PR          *prJSON          `json:"pr"`
	Account     string           `json:"account"`
	Agents      []agentJSON      `json:"agents"`
	RemoteURL   string           `json:"remote_url"`
}

// notifyJSON is a device's notification settings. PausedUntil is an RFC 3339
// time, "" when they are not paused.
type notifyJSON struct {
	Waiting     bool   `json:"waiting"`
	Finished    bool   `json:"finished"`
	PausedUntil string `json:"paused_until"`
}

// deviceJSON is a device as its own app reads it: no token, no pairing or
// last-seen time. It is comparable, so a stream can tell when it changed.
type deviceJSON struct {
	Name   string     `json:"name"`
	Notify notifyJSON `json:"notify"`
	Push   string     `json:"push"`
}

// noticeJSON is a line for the app's toast and its list of recent notices.
type noticeJSON struct {
	Text  string    `json:"text"`
	Error bool      `json:"error"`
	At    time.Time `json:"at"`
}

// snapshotJSON is the first event of every stream: everything the app shows,
// so nothing is replayed. Sessions is keyed by workspace ID, and Badge is the
// number of sessions in needs_you across the workspaces.
type snapshotJSON struct {
	Build      string                             `json:"build"`
	Host       string                             `json:"host"`
	Workspaces []workspaceJSON                    `json:"workspaces"`
	Sessions   map[core.WorkspaceID][]sessionJSON `json:"sessions"`
	Device     deviceJSON                         `json:"device"`
	Badge      int                                `json:"badge"`
	Notices    []noticeJSON                       `json:"notices"`
}

// workspacesJSON is the `workspaces` event.
type workspacesJSON struct {
	Workspaces []workspaceJSON `json:"workspaces"`
}

// sessionsJSON is the `sessions` event: one workspace's whole list.
type sessionsJSON struct {
	Workspace core.WorkspaceID `json:"workspace"`
	Sessions  []sessionJSON    `json:"sessions"`
}

// groupOf says which of the app's four groups a session is listed under and
// what it is called there. Prompting needs the person; a started Ready is
// their turn; Running, Loading and Deleting are the agent's; the rest wait.
// A status this build does not know is listed last under its own name, never
// as one it might be.
func groupOf(v core.InstanceView) (group, label string) {
	switch v.Status {
	case session.Prompting:
		return groupNeedsYou, "Needs you"
	case session.Ready:
		if v.Started {
			return groupYourTurn, "Your turn"
		}
		return groupPaused, "Not started"
	case session.Running:
		return groupWorking, "Working"
	case session.Loading:
		return groupWorking, "Loading"
	case session.Deleting:
		return groupWorking, "Deleting"
	case session.Paused:
		return groupPaused, "Paused"
	case session.Recoverable:
		return groupPaused, "Recoverable"
	}
	return groupPaused, v.Status.String()
}

// sessionOf is the view of one instance of workspace ws as the app reads it.
// A fact the view does not vouch for (a last message without HasLastMessage,
// a diff without HasDiff, ahead and behind without ParityKnown) is left at
// its zero value rather than shown stale.
func sessionOf(ws core.WorkspaceID, v core.InstanceView) sessionJSON {
	group, label := groupOf(v)
	s := sessionJSON{
		ID:          v.ID,
		Workspace:   ws,
		Title:       v.Title,
		Branch:      v.Branch,
		Group:       group,
		Label:       label,
		Status:      v.Status.String(),
		Wait:        waitJSON{Reason: v.WaitReason, Detail: v.WaitDetail},
		PR:          prOf(v.GitHub),
		Account:     v.Account,
		Agents:      make([]agentJSON, 0, len(v.Subagents)),
		ParityKnown: v.ParityKnown,
	}
	if !v.StatusSince.IsZero() {
		s.Since = v.StatusSince.UTC().Format(time.RFC3339)
	}
	if v.HasLastMessage {
		s.LastMessage = v.LastMessage
	}
	if v.HasDiff {
		s.Diff = diffJSON{Added: v.Diff.Added, Removed: v.Diff.Removed}
	}
	if v.ParityKnown {
		s.Ahead, s.Behind = v.Ahead, v.Behind
	}
	for _, a := range v.Subagents {
		s.Agents = append(s.Agents, agentJSON{Name: a.Name, Description: a.Description, Idle: a.Idle})
	}
	if v.RemoteSession != "" {
		s.RemoteURL = remoteBase + url.PathEscape(v.RemoteSession)
	}
	return s
}

// prOf is the session's pull request, nil unless GitHub's answer for its
// repository is known, names a PR, and has a state and checks this build
// knows. The app shows no PR rather than a guessed one.
func prOf(s github.State) *prJSON {
	if !s.Known || !s.HasPR {
		return nil
	}
	var state, checks string
	switch s.PRState {
	case github.PRDraft:
		state = "draft"
	case github.PROpen:
		state = "open"
	case github.PRMerged:
		state = "merged"
	case github.PRClosed:
		state = "closed"
	default:
		return nil
	}
	switch s.Checks {
	case github.ChecksNone:
		checks = "none"
	case github.ChecksPending:
		checks = "pending"
	case github.ChecksPassing:
		checks = "passing"
	case github.ChecksFailing:
		checks = "failing"
	default:
		return nil
	}
	return &prJSON{Number: s.PRNumber, State: state, Checks: checks}
}

// workspaceOf is a served workspace as the app reads it.
func workspaceOf(w core.WorkspaceView) workspaceJSON {
	return workspaceJSON{ID: w.ID, Name: w.Name, Label: w.Label}
}

// deviceOf is a device record as its own app reads it.
func deviceOf(d core.WebDevice) deviceJSON {
	n := notifyJSON{Waiting: d.Notify.Waiting, Finished: d.Notify.Finished}
	if !d.Notify.PausedUntil.IsZero() {
		n.PausedUntil = d.Notify.PausedUntil.UTC().Format(time.RFC3339)
	}
	return deviceJSON{Name: d.Name, Notify: n, Push: string(d.Push)}
}

// noticeOf is a model notice as the app reads it, stamped with the time the
// web layer saw it: Err is shown as an error, else Info as a line.
func noticeOf(n core.Notice, at time.Time) noticeJSON {
	if n.Err != nil {
		return noticeJSON{Text: n.Err.Error(), Error: true, At: at}
	}
	return noticeJSON{Text: n.Info, At: at}
}

// projection is the fleet as the app reads it: the served workspaces in
// serve order, and each one's sessions in the model's display order. It takes
// no lock; the hub's mutex guards it. Its slices are replaced, never edited,
// so a copy of one stays valid.
type projection struct {
	workspaces []workspaceJSON
	sessions   map[core.WorkspaceID][]sessionJSON
}

func newProjection() *projection {
	return &projection{
		workspaces: []workspaceJSON{},
		sessions:   map[core.WorkspaceID][]sessionJSON{},
	}
}

// setWorkspaces replaces the served set. A workspace that stays keeps its
// sessions, a new one starts with none until its views arrive, and one that
// went loses them.
func (p *projection) setWorkspaces(views []core.WorkspaceView) {
	workspaces := make([]workspaceJSON, 0, len(views))
	sessions := make(map[core.WorkspaceID][]sessionJSON, len(views))
	for _, v := range views {
		workspaces = append(workspaces, workspaceOf(v))
		if kept, ok := p.sessions[v.ID]; ok {
			sessions[v.ID] = kept
		} else {
			sessions[v.ID] = []sessionJSON{}
		}
	}
	p.workspaces, p.sessions = workspaces, sessions
}

// setViews replaces one workspace's sessions, leaving workspace terminals
// out. It reports false, and changes nothing, for a workspace that is not
// served: the replica drops such views too.
func (p *projection) setViews(ws core.WorkspaceID, views []core.InstanceView) bool {
	if _, ok := p.sessions[ws]; !ok {
		return false
	}
	out := make([]sessionJSON, 0, len(views))
	for _, v := range views {
		if v.IsWorkspaceTerminal {
			continue
		}
		out = append(out, sessionOf(ws, v))
	}
	p.sessions[ws] = out
	return true
}

// served reports whether ws is in the served set.
func (p *projection) served(ws core.WorkspaceID) bool {
	_, ok := p.sessions[ws]
	return ok
}

// badge is the number of sessions that need the person, across the served
// workspaces: the number the app puts on its icon.
func (p *projection) badge() int {
	n := 0
	for _, list := range p.sessions {
		for _, s := range list {
			if s.Group == groupNeedsYou {
				n++
			}
		}
	}
	return n
}
```

### Task 7.2: The hub, the stream route, and the hub in the layer

**Files:**
