- Test: `internal/web/stream_test.go`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `internal/web/stream_test.go`. Every stream test runs the layer on Story 6's `webStack`: a booted model that serves the workspace "toy" and the sessions a test gives it (never started, on a loop that holds its jobs and never ticks, so nothing but the test moves a session), the real `rpc.Server`, the layer listening on a loopback port, and the device "iPhone" paired through the pairing route. A test changes a session on the model, then `FlushForTest` has the server publish it, and the stream is read over a real HTTP connection with the device's cookie. The fixture is `streamEnv`; `hubSaw` and `holdDrain` use Story 6's `funcSink`, added after the hub, to know when the drain has handed the hub a change and to hold the drain where a test needs the hub to lag the model.

```go
package web

import (
	"bufio"
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"net/http"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"reflect"
	"slices"
	"strings"
	"sync"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/config"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core/rpc"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/session"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
)

// streamWait bounds how long a stream test waits for something to happen.
const streamWait = 3 * time.Second

// streamSession is a session the fixture's workspace holds from the start: the
// status it is in, and the wait fields it carries. None is ever started.
type streamSession struct {
	title          string
	status         session.Status
	reason, detail string
}

// streamEnv is Story 6's webStack over a model that serves the workspace "toy"
// with the given sessions, on a loop that holds its jobs and never ticks. The
// layer listens at addr, and cookie is the paired device "iPhone"'s. The hub's
// knobs are tuned to 20 ms between lists.
type streamEnv struct {
	*webStack

	addr     string
	cookie   *http.Cookie
	ws       core.WorkspaceID
	insts    map[string]*session.Instance
	ids      map[string]core.InstanceID
	sessions int
}

func newStreamEnv(t *testing.T, seeds ...streamSession) *streamEnv {
	t.Helper()
	t.Setenv(config.EnvGlobalDir, t.TempDir())
	repo := t.TempDir()
	def := config.Workspace{Name: "toy", Path: repo}
	cfgDir := config.WorkspaceConfigDir(&def)
	require.NoError(t, os.MkdirAll(cfgDir, 0o755))
	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(cfgDir, config.StateFileName), []byte(`{"instances":[]}`), 0o644))
	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(cfgDir, config.ConfigFileName), []byte(`{"default_program":"claude"}`), 0o644))
	reg := &config.WorkspaceRegistry{}
	require.NoError(t, reg.Add("toy", repo))

	m := core.New(core.Options{Registry: reg, CmdExec: deadMachine()})
	m.Boot()
	var toy core.WorkspaceView
	for _, v := range m.Workspaces() {
		if v.Name == "toy" {
			toy = v
		}
	}
	require.NotZero(t, toy.ID, "the model serves the workspace toy")
	e := &streamEnv{
		ws:       toy.ID,
		insts:    map[string]*session.Instance{},
		ids:      map[string]core.InstanceID{},
		sessions: len(seeds),
	}
	for _, seed := range seeds {
		inst, err := session.NewInstance(session.InstanceOptions{Title: seed.title, Path: t.TempDir(), Program: "claude"})
		require.NoError(t, err)
		require.NoError(t, inst.TransitionTo(seed.status))
		inst.SetWaitReason(seed.reason)
		inst.SetWaitDetail(seed.detail)
		m.WorkspaceForTest(toy.ID).AddForTest(inst)
		e.insts[seed.title] = inst
		e.ids[seed.title] = m.IDOfForTest(inst)
	}

	// newWebStack's stack, but on a loop that holds its jobs and has not
	// begun, so nothing ticks.
	e.webStack = &webStack{loop: core.StartForTest(m)}
	e.srv = rpc.NewServer(e.loop)
	t.Cleanup(func() {
		e.layer.Close()
		if e.tui != nil {
			e.tui.Close()
		}
		e.srv.Close()
		e.loop.Stop()
	})
	e.start(t)
	e.addr, _ = e.enable(t)
	e.cookie = e.pair(t, e.addr, "iPhone")
	e.tune(func(h *hub) { h.every = 20 * time.Millisecond })
	e.settle(t)
	return e
}

// waitUntil polls cond, on the test's goroutine, until it holds.
func waitUntil(t *testing.T, what string, cond func() bool) {
	t.Helper()
	deadline := time.Now().Add(2 * streamWait)
	for !cond() {
		if time.Now().After(deadline) {
			t.Fatalf("timed out: %s", what)
		}
		time.Sleep(10 * time.Millisecond)
	}
}

// tune changes the hub's knobs under its lock, as its goroutines read them.
func (e *streamEnv) tune(fn func(h *hub)) {
	h := e.layer.hub
	h.mu.Lock()
	defer h.mu.Unlock()
	fn(h)
}

// waitHub polls ok, which runs under the hub's lock, until it holds.
func (e *streamEnv) waitHub(t *testing.T, what string, ok func(h *hub) bool) {
	t.Helper()
	h := e.layer.hub
	waitUntil(t, what, func() bool {
		h.mu.Lock()
		defer h.mu.Unlock()
		return ok(h)
	})
}

// settle waits until the hub holds the fixture's sessions and the device, and
// has published all it holds, so a stream opened next is level with every
// other and sees only what the test does after.
func (e *streamEnv) settle(t *testing.T) {
	t.Helper()
	e.waitHub(t, "the hub never read the model and published it", func(h *hub) bool {
		if len(h.devices) == 0 || len(h.fleet.workspaces) == 0 {
			return false
		}
		total := 0
		for _, list := range h.fleet.sessions {
			total += len(list)
		}
		if total != e.sessions || !reflect.DeepEqual(h.fleet.workspaces, h.pubWorkspaces) {
			return false
		}
		for _, w := range h.fleet.workspaces {
			if !reflect.DeepEqual(h.fleet.sessions[w.ID], h.pubSessions[w.ID]) {
				return false
			}
		}
		return true
	})
}

// change edits a session on the model, then has the server publish it.
func (e *streamEnv) change(t *testing.T, edit func()) {
	t.Helper()
	edit()
	require.NoError(t, e.tui.FlushForTest())
}

// pairAs pairs a device through the pairing route and waits until the hub has
// read its record, so a stream opened with the cookie is not refused for being
// ahead of the hub.
func (e *streamEnv) pairAs(t *testing.T, name string) *http.Cookie {
	t.Helper()
	cookie := e.pair(t, e.addr, name)
	e.waitHub(t, "the hub never read the device "+name, func(h *hub) bool {
		_, ok := h.devices[name]
		return ok
	})
	return cookie
}

// open connects a stream with cookie, which must be accepted.
func (e *streamEnv) open(t *testing.T, cookie *http.Cookie) *streamReader {
	t.Helper()
	resp := httpDo(t, e.addr, http.MethodGet, "/api/v1/stream", "", func(r *http.Request) { r.AddCookie(cookie) })
	if resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
		t.Fatalf("the stream answered %d: %s", resp.StatusCode, bodyText(t, resp))
	}
	return readFrames(t, resp)
}

// attempt connects a stream with cookie and returns the status, and the body
// of a refusal. A stream it was given is closed again at once.
func (e *streamEnv) attempt(t *testing.T, cookie *http.Cookie) (int, string) {
	t.Helper()
	resp := httpDo(t, e.addr, http.MethodGet, "/api/v1/stream", "", func(r *http.Request) { r.AddCookie(cookie) })
	if resp.StatusCode == http.StatusOK {
		_ = resp.Body.Close()
		return resp.StatusCode, ""
	}
	return resp.StatusCode, bodyText(t, resp)
}

// hubSaw runs trigger, then waits until the drain has handed the sinks an
// event that match accepts. The probe sink is added after the hub, so the hub
// has applied that batch by the time the probe sees it.
func (e *streamEnv) hubSaw(t *testing.T, match func(core.Event) bool, trigger func()) {
	t.Helper()
	saw := make(chan struct{})
	var once sync.Once
	e.layer.addSink(funcSink(func(evs []core.Event) {
		for _, ev := range evs {
			if match(ev) {
				once.Do(func() { close(saw) })
			}
		}
	}))
	trigger()
	select {
	case <-saw:
	case <-time.After(streamWait):
		t.Fatal("the drain never handed the sinks the change")
	}
}

// holdDrain stops the layer's drain inside a sink: it changes inst's program,
// which the app does not show, and waits until a batch has reached the gate.
// From then on the hub reads nothing more from the model, until the returned
// function lets the drain go on. The test's cleanup lets it go too, before the
// layer closes.
func (e *streamEnv) holdDrain(t *testing.T, inst *session.Instance) (release func()) {
	t.Helper()
	held, gate := make(chan struct{}), make(chan struct{})
	var once sync.Once
	e.layer.addSink(funcSink(func([]core.Event) {
		once.Do(func() {
			close(held)
			<-gate
		})
	}))
	release = sync.OnceFunc(func() { close(gate) })
	t.Cleanup(release)
	e.change(t, func() { inst.SetProgram("held") })
	select {
	case <-held:
	case <-time.After(streamWait):
		t.Fatal("the drain never reached the gate")
	}
	return release
}

// viewsWhere matches a ViewsChanged of workspace ws that holds a view ok accepts.
func viewsWhere(ws core.WorkspaceID, ok func(core.InstanceView) bool) func(core.Event) bool {
	return func(ev core.Event) bool {
		vc, isViews := ev.(core.ViewsChanged)
		return isViews && vc.WS == ws && slices.ContainsFunc(vc.Views, ok)
	}
}

// streamFrame is one server-sent event, with the time it was read.
type streamFrame struct {
	Event string
	Data  json.RawMessage
	At    time.Time
}

// streamReader reads a response as server-sent events.
type streamReader struct {
	t      *testing.T
	frames chan streamFrame
	quit   chan struct{}
}

func readFrames(t *testing.T, resp *http.Response) *streamReader {
	t.Helper()
	r := &streamReader{t: t, frames: make(chan streamFrame, 256), quit: make(chan struct{})}
	t.Cleanup(func() { close(r.quit) })
	go func() {
		defer close(r.frames)
		sc := bufio.NewScanner(resp.Body)
		sc.Buffer(make([]byte, 0, 64<<10), 1<<20)
		var event string
		var data []string
		for sc.Scan() {
			line := sc.Text()
			switch {
			case line == "":
				if event != "" {
					f := streamFrame{Event: event, Data: json.RawMessage(strings.Join(data, "\n")), At: time.Now()}
					select {
					case r.frames <- f:
					case <-r.quit:
						return
					}
				}
				event, data = "", nil
			case strings.HasPrefix(line, "event: "):
				event = strings.TrimPrefix(line, "event: ")
			case strings.HasPrefix(line, "data: "):
				data = append(data, strings.TrimPrefix(line, "data: "))
			}
		}
	}()
	return r
}

func (r *streamReader) next() streamFrame {
	r.t.Helper()
	select {
	case f, ok := <-r.frames:
		require.True(r.t, ok, "the stream ended")
		return f
	case <-time.After(streamWait):
		r.t.Fatal("no event within the wait")
		return streamFrame{}
	}
}

// expect reads the next event and requires its name.
func (r *streamReader) expect(event string) streamFrame {
	r.t.Helper()
	f := r.next()
	require.Equal(r.t, event, f.Event, "data: %s", f.Data)
	return f
}

// none requires that nothing arrives, and the stream stays open, for d.
func (r *streamReader) none(d time.Duration) {
	r.t.Helper()
	select {
	case f, ok := <-r.frames:
		if !ok {
			r.t.Fatal("the stream ended")
		}
		r.t.Fatalf("unexpected %s event: %s", f.Event, f.Data)
	case <-time.After(d):
	}
}

// ended reads until the stream ends and returns what came before.
func (r *streamReader) ended() []streamFrame {
	r.t.Helper()
	var got []streamFrame
	timeout := time.After(streamWait)
	for {
		select {
		case f, ok := <-r.frames:
			if !ok {
				return got
			}
			got = append(got, f)
		case <-timeout:
			r.t.Fatalf("the stream did not end; read %d events", len(got))
		}
	}
}

func unframe[T any](t *testing.T, f streamFrame) T {
	t.Helper()
	var v T
	require.NoError(t, json.Unmarshal(f.Data, &v), "data: %s", f.Data)
	return v
}

// sessionNamed is the session titled title in list.
func sessionNamed(t *testing.T, list []sessionJSON, title string) sessionJSON {
	t.Helper()
	for _, s := range list {
		if s.Title == title {
			return s
		}
	}
	t.Fatalf("no session titled %q in %v", title, list)
	return sessionJSON{}
}

func TestStream_StartsWithASnapshot(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t,
		streamSession{title: "fix login", status: session.Prompting, reason: "permission", detail: "rm -rf build"},
		streamSession{title: "add api", status: session.Running},
		streamSession{title: "old work", status: session.Paused},
	)

	s := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	snap := unframe[snapshotJSON](t, s.expect("snapshot"))

	assert.Equal(t, "test build", snap.Build)
	assert.Equal(t, "testhost", snap.Host)
	var labels []string
	for _, w := range snap.Workspaces {
		labels = append(labels, w.Label)
	}
	assert.ElementsMatch(t, []string{"global", "toy"}, labels)
	assert.Len(t, snap.Sessions, 2, "one list for each workspace")

	list := snap.Sessions[e.ws]
	require.Len(t, list, 3)
	fix := sessionNamed(t, list, "fix login")
	assert.Equal(t, e.ids["fix login"], fix.ID)
	assert.Equal(t, e.ws, fix.Workspace)
	assert.Equal(t, "needs_you", fix.Group)
	assert.Equal(t, "Needs you", fix.Label)
	assert.Equal(t, "Prompting", fix.Status)
	assert.Equal(t, waitJSON{Reason: "permission", Detail: "rm -rf build"}, fix.Wait)
	_, err := time.Parse(time.RFC3339, fix.Since)
	assert.NoError(t, err, "the status was entered in this process")
	assert.Equal(t, "working", sessionNamed(t, list, "add api").Group)
	assert.Equal(t, "paused", sessionNamed(t, list, "old work").Group)
	assert.Equal(t, 1, snap.Badge, "one session needs you")

	assert.Equal(t, deviceJSON{Name: "iPhone", Notify: notifyJSON{Waiting: true, Finished: true}}, snap.Device)
	assert.Empty(t, snap.Notices)
	s.none(100 * time.Millisecond)
}

func TestStream_AnEmptyFleetIsEmptyListsNotNulls(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t)

	s := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	var raw map[string]json.RawMessage
	require.NoError(t, json.Unmarshal(s.expect("snapshot").Data, &raw))

	assert.JSONEq(t, `[]`, string(raw["notices"]))
	assert.JSONEq(t, `0`, string(raw["badge"]))
	assert.NotEqual(t, "null", string(raw["workspaces"]))
	var lists map[string]json.RawMessage
	require.NoError(t, json.Unmarshal(raw["sessions"], &lists))
	require.Len(t, lists, 2, "the global workspace and toy")
	for id, list := range lists {
		assert.JSONEq(t, `[]`, string(list), "workspace %s", id)
	}
}

func TestStream_SendsTheWholeListWhenASessionChanges(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t,
		streamSession{title: "fix login", status: session.Running, reason: "permission", detail: "go test ./..."},
		streamSession{title: "add api", status: session.Running},
	)
	s := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	s.expect("snapshot")

	e.change(t, func() { require.NoError(t, e.insts["fix login"].TransitionTo(session.Prompting)) })

	list := unframe[sessionsJSON](t, s.expect("sessions"))
	assert.Equal(t, e.ws, list.Workspace)
	require.Len(t, list.Sessions, 2, "the whole list, not the one that changed")
	fix := sessionNamed(t, list.Sessions, "fix login")
	assert.Equal(t, "needs_you", fix.Group)
	assert.Equal(t, waitJSON{Reason: "permission", Detail: "go test ./..."}, fix.Wait)
	assert.Equal(t, "working", sessionNamed(t, list.Sessions, "add api").Group)
	s.none(100 * time.Millisecond)
}

func TestStream_SendsTheWorkspacesWhenTheyChange(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t, streamSession{title: "one", status: session.Running})
	s := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	s.expect("snapshot")

	require.NoError(t, e.tui.RenameWorkspace("toy", "renamed"))

	ws := unframe[workspacesJSON](t, s.expect("workspaces"))
	var toy workspaceJSON
	for _, w := range ws.Workspaces {
		if w.ID == e.ws {
			toy = w
		}
	}
	assert.Equal(t, workspaceJSON{ID: e.ws, Name: "renamed", Label: "renamed"}, toy, "the ID stays")
	s.none(100 * time.Millisecond) // its sessions did not change
}

func TestStream_BatchesAWorkspacesChangesIntoFewLists(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t, streamSession{title: "busy", status: session.Running})
	e.tune(func(h *hub) { h.every = 150 * time.Millisecond })
	s := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	s.expect("snapshot")

	const changes = 10
	for i := range changes {
		e.change(t, func() { e.insts["busy"].SetWaitReason(fmt.Sprintf("reason %d", i)) })
	}

	lists := 0
	for {
		list := unframe[sessionsJSON](t, s.expect("sessions"))
		lists++
		if sessionNamed(t, list.Sessions, "busy").Wait.Reason == fmt.Sprintf("reason %d", changes-1) {
			break
		}
	}
	assert.Less(t, lists, changes, "the changes were batched, not sent one by one")
}

func TestStream_PublishesAtMostFourListsASecond(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t, streamSession{title: "busy", status: session.Running})
	e.tune(func(h *hub) { h.every = publishEvery })
	s := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	s.expect("snapshot")

	// A change every 10 ms for a second and a half.
	var last string
	for end := time.Now().Add(1500 * time.Millisecond); time.Now().Before(end); {
		last = fmt.Sprintf("reason at %d", time.Now().UnixNano())
		e.change(t, func() { e.insts["busy"].SetWaitReason(last) })
		time.Sleep(10 * time.Millisecond)
	}

	var arrivals []time.Time
	var final sessionsJSON
collect:
	for {
		select {
		case f, ok := <-s.frames:
			require.True(t, ok, "the stream ended")
			if f.Event == "sessions" {
				arrivals = append(arrivals, f.At)
				final = unframe[sessionsJSON](t, f)
			}
		case <-time.After(2 * publishEvery):
			break collect
		}
	}

	require.GreaterOrEqual(t, len(arrivals), 3, "the lists keep coming while the workspace changes")
	for i := 1; i < len(arrivals); i++ {
		assert.GreaterOrEqual(t, arrivals[i].Sub(arrivals[i-1]), publishEvery-100*time.Millisecond)
	}
	assert.Equal(t, last, sessionNamed(t, final.Sessions, "busy").Wait.Reason, "the last list holds the last change")
}

func TestStream_AChangeTheAppCannotSeeIsNotPublished(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t, streamSession{title: "fix", status: session.Running})
	s := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	s.expect("snapshot")

	// The program is in the model's view of the session and not in the app's.
	e.hubSaw(t, viewsWhere(e.ws, func(v core.InstanceView) bool { return v.Program == "aider" }), func() {
		e.change(t, func() { e.insts["fix"].SetProgram("aider") })
	})
	s.none(200 * time.Millisecond)

	e.change(t, func() { e.insts["fix"].SetWaitReason("because") })
	list := unframe[sessionsJSON](t, s.expect("sessions"))
	assert.Equal(t, "because", sessionNamed(t, list.Sessions, "fix").Wait.Reason)
}

func TestStream_NoticesNotTiedToARequestReachEveryStreamAndTheNextSnapshot(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t)
	padCookie := e.pairAs(t, "iPad")
	a := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	a.expect("snapshot")
	b := e.open(t, padCookie)
	b.expect("snapshot")

	// Story 8's tracker takes a notice tied to a request of the web client, and
	// the sink leaves it alone; an empty notice says nothing. (A literal batch:
	// the model ties a notice to a request only inside a request's job.)
	e.layer.hub.apply([]core.Event{core.Notice{Info: "belongs to a request", Req: 5}, core.Notice{}})
	// A notice the model raises on its own reaches every connection.
	e.loop.WebServing("", errors.New("push failed"))

	for _, s := range []*streamReader{a, b} {
		n := unframe[noticeJSON](t, s.expect("notice"))
		assert.Contains(t, n.Text, "push failed", "the notice tied to a request was not published")
		assert.True(t, n.Error)
		assert.WithinDuration(t, time.Now(), n.At, 10*time.Second)
	}
	snap := unframe[snapshotJSON](t, e.open(t, e.cookie).expect("snapshot"))
	require.Len(t, snap.Notices, 1)
	assert.Contains(t, snap.Notices[0].Text, "push failed")
}

func TestHub_KeepsTheLastTwentyNotices(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t)
	for i := range recentNotices + 5 {
		e.layer.hub.publish("notice", noticeJSON{Text: fmt.Sprintf("n%d", i), At: time.Now()})
	}

	snap := unframe[snapshotJSON](t, e.open(t, e.cookie).expect("snapshot"))

	require.Len(t, snap.Notices, recentNotices)
	assert.Equal(t, "n5", snap.Notices[0].Text)
	assert.Equal(t, "n24", snap.Notices[recentNotices-1].Text)
}

func TestHub_PublishAndSendToReachTheRightStreamsAndAreKept(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t)
	padCookie := e.pairAs(t, "iPad")
	a := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	a.expect("snapshot")
	b := e.open(t, padCookie)
	b.expect("snapshot")

	e.layer.hub.sendTo("iPhone", "notice", noticeJSON{Text: "only you", At: time.Now()})
	e.layer.hub.publish("notice", noticeJSON{Text: "everyone", At: time.Now()})
	e.layer.hub.sendTo("iPhone", "device", deviceJSON{Name: "iPhone"})

	assert.Equal(t, "only you", unframe[noticeJSON](t, a.expect("notice")).Text)
	assert.Equal(t, "everyone", unframe[noticeJSON](t, a.expect("notice")).Text)
	a.expect("device")
	assert.Equal(t, "everyone", unframe[noticeJSON](t, b.expect("notice")).Text)
	b.none(100 * time.Millisecond)

	// Both notices are in the next snapshot, the one sent to a single device too;
	// the device event is not a notice and is not kept.
	snap := unframe[snapshotJSON](t, e.open(t, padCookie).expect("snapshot"))
	require.Len(t, snap.Notices, 2)
	assert.Equal(t, "only you", snap.Notices[0].Text)
	assert.Equal(t, "everyone", snap.Notices[1].Text)
}

func TestHub_ANoticeSentToADeviceWithNoStreamIsInItsNextSnapshot(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t)

	// Story 8 sends a request's late notice to its device, which may have
	// left: the phone finds it when it comes back.
	e.layer.hub.sendTo("iPhone", "notice", noticeJSON{Text: "while you were away", Error: true, At: time.Now()})

	snap := unframe[snapshotJSON](t, e.open(t, e.cookie).expect("snapshot"))
	require.Len(t, snap.Notices, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, "while you were away", snap.Notices[0].Text)
	assert.True(t, snap.Notices[0].Error)
}

func TestStream_ADeviceEventGoesToThatDevicesStreamsOnly(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t)
	padCookie := e.pairAs(t, "iPad")
	a := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	a.expect("snapshot")
	b := e.open(t, padCookie)
	b.expect("snapshot")

	off := false
	got, err := e.loop.SetNotify("iPhone", core.NotifyChange{Finished: &off})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.False(t, got.Notify.Finished)

	dev := unframe[deviceJSON](t, a.expect("device"))
	assert.Equal(t, deviceJSON{Name: "iPhone", Notify: notifyJSON{Waiting: true}}, dev)
	// The app's record has no last-seen time: touching a device says nothing.
	a.none(100 * time.Millisecond)
	b.none(100 * time.Millisecond)
}

func TestStream_RevokingADeviceEndsItsStreamsWithNoBye(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t)
	padCookie := e.pairAs(t, "iPad")
	a1 := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	a1.expect("snapshot")
	a2 := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	a2.expect("snapshot")
	b := e.open(t, padCookie)
	b.expect("snapshot")

	require.NoError(t, e.tui.RevokeDevice("iPhone"))

	for _, s := range []*streamReader{a1, a2} {
		for _, f := range s.ended() {
			assert.NotEqual(t, "bye", f.Event, "the app would call a revoked stream a restart")
		}
	}
	status, _ := e.attempt(t, e.cookie)
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusUnauthorized, status, "the revoked cookie")
	e.layer.hub.publish("notice", noticeJSON{Text: "still here", At: time.Now()})
	assert.Equal(t, "still here", unframe[noticeJSON](t, b.expect("notice")).Text)
}

func TestStream_ADeviceTheHubHasNotReadYetIsRefusedUntilItHas(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t, streamSession{title: "one", status: session.Running})
	release := e.holdDrain(t, e.insts["one"])

	// The model pairs the iPad while the drain is held: it vouches for the
	// cookie, and the list the hub has read does not name the device.
	padCookie := e.pair(t, e.addr, "iPad")
	status, body := e.attempt(t, padCookie)
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, status)
	assert.Contains(t, body, "try again")

	release()
	waitUntil(t, "the hub never read the iPad's record", func() bool {
		status, _ := e.attempt(t, padCookie)
		return status == http.StatusOK
	})
}

func TestStream_ADeviceOfTheSameNamePairedAgainEndsTheOldStreams(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t, streamSession{title: "one", status: session.Running})
	old := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	old.expect("snapshot")
	release := e.holdDrain(t, e.insts["one"])

	// The iPhone is revoked and paired again while the hub looks away, so the
	// list it reads next names the iPhone with another pairing time.
	require.NoError(t, e.tui.RevokeDevice("iPhone"))
	fresh := e.pair(t, e.addr, "iPhone")
	release()

	for _, f := range old.ended() {
		assert.NotEqual(t, "bye", f.Event)
	}
	e.open(t, fresh).expect("snapshot")
}

func TestHub_CloseDeviceEndsOnlyThatDevicesStreams(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t)
	padCookie := e.pairAs(t, "iPad")
	a := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	a.expect("snapshot")
	b := e.open(t, padCookie)
	b.expect("snapshot")

	e.layer.hub.closeDevice("iPhone")

	for _, f := range a.ended() {
		assert.NotEqual(t, "bye", f.Event)
	}
	b.none(100 * time.Millisecond)
}

func TestStream_ATextInASessionCannotForgeAFrame(t *testing.T) {
	hostile := "x\n\nevent: bye\ndata: {}\n\n"
	e := newStreamEnv(t, streamSession{title: hostile, status: session.Running})
	s := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	snap := unframe[snapshotJSON](t, s.expect("snapshot"))
	require.Len(t, snap.Sessions[e.ws], 1)
	assert.Equal(t, hostile, snap.Sessions[e.ws][0].Title)

	e.change(t, func() { e.insts[hostile].SetWaitDetail(hostile) })

	list := unframe[sessionsJSON](t, s.expect("sessions"))
	assert.Equal(t, hostile, list.Sessions[0].Wait.Detail)
	s.none(100 * time.Millisecond) // no bye, and no event of its own
}

func TestStream_PingsKeepItOpen(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t)
	e.tune(func(h *hub) { h.ping = 30 * time.Millisecond })
	s := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	s.expect("snapshot")

	s.expect("ping")
	s.expect("ping")
}

func TestStream_SetsTheDeviceCookieAgain(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t)

	resp := httpDo(t, e.addr, http.MethodGet, "/api/v1/stream", "", func(r *http.Request) { r.AddCookie(e.cookie) })
	require.Equal(t, http.StatusOK, resp.StatusCode)
	readFrames(t, resp).expect("snapshot")

	assert.Equal(t, "text/event-stream", resp.Header.Get("Content-Type"))
	var got *http.Cookie
	for _, c := range resp.Cookies() {
		if c.Name == cookieName {
			got = c
		}
	}
	require.NotNil(t, got, "the cookie is set again so a device in use never expires")
	assert.Equal(t, e.cookie.Value, got.Value)
	assert.Positive(t, got.MaxAge)
}

func TestStream_AFifthStreamEndsTheDevicesOldest(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t)
	padCookie := e.pairAs(t, "iPad")
	var held []*streamReader
	for range maxStreamsPerDevice {
		s := e.open(t, e.cookie)
		s.expect("snapshot")
		held = append(held, s)
	}
	other := e.open(t, padCookie)
	other.expect("snapshot")

	fifth := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	fifth.expect("snapshot")

	for _, f := range held[0].ended() {
		assert.NotEqual(t, "bye", f.Event)
	}
	e.layer.hub.publish("notice", noticeJSON{Text: "alive", At: time.Now()})
	alive := append([]*streamReader{fifth, other}, held[1:]...)
	for _, s := range alive {
		assert.Equal(t, "alive", unframe[noticeJSON](t, s.expect("notice")).Text)
	}
}

func TestStream_ThirtyTwoStreamsAreTheMost(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t)
	perDevice := maxStreams / maxStreamsPerDevice
	cookies := []*http.Cookie{e.cookie}
	for i := 1; i <= perDevice; i++ {
		cookies = append(cookies, e.pairAs(t, fmt.Sprintf("device-%d", i)))
	}
	var all []*streamReader
	for _, cookie := range cookies[:perDevice] {
		for range maxStreamsPerDevice {
			s := e.open(t, cookie)
			s.expect("snapshot")
			all = append(all, s)
		}
	}
	require.Len(t, all, maxStreams)

	// A 33rd, from a device with no stream, is refused.
	extra := cookies[perDevice]
	status, body := e.attempt(t, extra)
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, status)
	assert.Contains(t, body, "too many open streams")

	// A fifth from a device at its own limit takes that device's oldest
	// stream's place, so the total stays at 32.
	e.open(t, cookies[0]).expect("snapshot")
	for _, f := range all[0].ended() {
		assert.NotEqual(t, "bye", f.Event)
	}
	status, _ = e.attempt(t, extra)
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, status)
}

func TestStream_AStreamThatCannotKeepUpIsEnded(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t)
	slow, err := e.layer.hub.subscribe("iPhone") // nothing reads it
	require.NoError(t, err)
	t.Cleanup(func() { e.layer.hub.release(slow) })

	for range streamQueue + 5 {
		e.layer.hub.publish("notice", noticeJSON{Text: "x", At: time.Now()})
	}

	select {
	case <-slow.done:
	default:
		t.Fatal("the stream that fell behind was not ended")
	}
	e.layer.hub.mu.Lock()
	_, held := e.layer.hub.subs[slow]
	e.layer.hub.mu.Unlock()
	assert.False(t, held)

	// The next connection starts clean, with a snapshot.
	e.open(t, e.cookie).expect("snapshot")
}

func TestStream_TheLayersCloseSendsByeThenEndsEveryStreamAndRefusesMore(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t)
	padCookie := e.pairAs(t, "iPad")
	a := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	a.expect("snapshot")
	b := e.open(t, padCookie)
	b.expect("snapshot")

	e.layer.Close()

	for _, s := range []*streamReader{a, b} {
		s.expect("bye")
		s.ended()
	}
	requireNoListener(t, e.addr)
	_, err := e.layer.hub.subscribe("iPhone")
	assert.ErrorIs(t, err, errHubClosed)
}

func TestHub_CloseOfNoHubIsANoop(t *testing.T) {
	var h *hub
	assert.NotPanics(t, h.close)
}

func TestHub_APanicInTheFlusherStopsTheWholeLayer(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t, streamSession{title: "one", status: session.Running})
	e.tune(func(h *hub) {
		h.every = 5 * time.Millisecond
		h.flushFn = func() { panic("boom") }
	})
	s := e.open(t, e.cookie)
	s.expect("snapshot")

	e.change(t, func() { e.insts["one"].SetWaitReason("because") })

	// The layer stops as it does for a panic in the drain: the streams end (the
	// stop says bye to them first), the listener closes, and the model is told
	// why, which keeps a notice for the next TUI. The daemon goes on.
	s.ended()
	requireNoListener(t, e.addr)
	view := e.waitWeb(t, func(v core.WebView) bool { return !v.Serving && v.Error != "" })
	assert.Contains(t, view.Error, "hub")
	assert.NotEmpty(t, e.tui.Workspaces())
}

func TestStream_TheRouteNeedsADeviceAndTakesGetOnly(t *testing.T) {
	e := newStreamEnv(t)
	require.NotNil(t, e.layer.hub, "Start builds the hub with the layer's parts")
	assert.Contains(t, e.layer.sinks, sink(e.layer.hub), "the drain hands the hub every Sync's events")

	for name, cookie := range map[string]*http.Cookie{
		"no cookie":               nil,
		"a token no device holds": {Name: cookieName, Value: deviceToken(9)},
	} {
		resp := httpDo(t, e.addr, http.MethodGet, "/api/v1/stream", "", func(r *http.Request) {
			if cookie != nil {
				r.AddCookie(cookie)
			}
		})
		assert.Equal(t, http.StatusUnauthorized, resp.StatusCode, name)
	}

	post := httpDo(t, e.addr, http.MethodPost, "/api/v1/stream", "", func(r *http.Request) { r.AddCookie(e.cookie) })
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusMethodNotAllowed, post.StatusCode)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `internal/web/stream.go`:

```go
package web

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"log/slog"
	"net/http"
	"reflect"
	"slices"
	"sync"
	"time"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/log"
)

// The stream's bounds (spec, "Limits" and "The stream").
const (
	maxStreamsPerDevice = 4
	maxStreams          = 32
	pingEvery           = 20 * time.Second
	publishEvery        = 250 * time.Millisecond
	recentNotices       = 20
)

const (
	// streamQueue is how many events a stream may hold unwritten. A stream
	// that falls further behind is ended: its app reconnects, and the snapshot
	// it gets rebuilds everything the queue held.
	streamQueue = 64
	// byeGrace bounds how long close waits for the flusher to return and for
	// the streams to write their bye.
	byeGrace = 2 * time.Second
)

var (
	errStreamsFull   = errors.New("too many open streams")
	errHubClosed     = errors.New("loom is stopping")
	errDeviceUnknown = errors.New("loom has not read this device's record yet; try again")

	pingFrame = sseFrame{event: "ping", data: []byte("{}")}
	byeFrame  = sseFrame{event: "bye", data: []byte("{}")}
)

var _ sink = (*hub)(nil)

// sseFrame is one server-sent event with its data already encoded. The data
// is compact JSON, which escapes every newline, so no text a session carries
// can end the frame or start another.
type sseFrame struct {
	event string
	data  []byte
}

func frameOf(event string, data any) (sseFrame, error) {
	b, err := json.Marshal(data)
	if err != nil {
		return sseFrame{}, fmt.Errorf("encode %s event: %w", event, err)
	}
	return sseFrame{event: event, data: b}, nil
}

// streamSub is one open stream. The hub's mutex guards paired and dev; the
// rest is set before the stream is shared, or is a channel.
type streamSub struct {
	device string
	seq    uint64 // connection order: the lowest is the device's oldest
	ping   time.Duration
	out    chan sseFrame
	done   chan struct{} // closed to end the stream
	once   sync.Once
	// bye is set before done closes: the stream says goodbye as it ends
	// (the layer is closing) instead of just stopping (a revocation).
	bye bool
	// paired is the pairing time of the device record the stream began with,
	// and dev the record last sent.
	paired time.Time
	dev    deviceJSON
}

func (s *streamSub) end() { s.once.Do(func() { close(s.done) }) }

// offer queues a frame without blocking, and reports whether it fit.
func (s *streamSub) offer(f sseFrame) bool {
	select {
	case s.out <- f:
		return true
	default:
		return false
	}
}

// hubDevice is a device record as the hub last saw it published.
type hubDevice struct {
	rec    deviceJSON
	paired time.Time
}

// hub is the web layer's stream part and its sink: it keeps the fleet
// projection, the device records and the recent notices from the events the
// drain hands it, and fans them out to the open streams. apply, publish,
// sendTo and closeDevice never block and never do network I/O; each stream's
// own handler goroutine writes to its connection.
type hub struct {
	l  *Layer
	lg *slog.Logger

	mu      sync.Mutex
	closed  bool
	subs    map[*streamSub]struct{}
	counter uint64
	fleet   *projection
	// devices are the records the last published device list held.
	devices map[string]hubDevice
	// notices are the last recentNotices the hub sent to a stream, oldest
	// first: the model's own, and what publish and sendTo were given.
	notices []noticeJSON
	// pubWorkspaces and pubSessions are what the streams were last told, so
	// a flush sends only a list that differs from it.
	pubWorkspaces []workspaceJSON
	pubSessions   map[core.WorkspaceID][]sessionJSON
	// ping, every and flushFn are the knobs the tests turn, read under mu.
	ping    time.Duration
	every   time.Duration
	flushFn func()

	wake     chan struct{}
	stop     chan struct{}
	exited   chan struct{}
	handlers sync.WaitGroup
}

// newHub builds the hub and starts its flusher.
func newHub(l *Layer) *hub {
	h := &hub{
		l:             l,
		lg:            log.For("web", "part", "stream"),
		subs:          map[*streamSub]struct{}{},
		fleet:         newProjection(),
		devices:       map[string]hubDevice{},
		notices:       []noticeJSON{},
		pubWorkspaces: []workspaceJSON{},
		pubSessions:   map[core.WorkspaceID][]sessionJSON{},
		ping:          pingEvery,
		every:         publishEvery,
		wake:          make(chan struct{}, 1),
		stop:          make(chan struct{}),
		exited:        make(chan struct{}),
	}
	h.flushFn = h.flush
	go h.run()
	return h
}

// apply takes the events one Sync returned. A workspace or its sessions
// changing wakes the flusher, which publishes at most every publishEvery;
// a notice not tied to a request, and a device record changing, go out at once.
func (h *hub) apply(evs []core.Event) {
	h.mu.Lock()
	defer h.mu.Unlock()
	if h.closed {
		return
	}
	moved := false
	for _, ev := range evs {
		switch ev := ev.(type) {
		case core.WorkspacesChanged:
			h.fleet.setWorkspaces(ev.Views)
			moved = true
		case core.ViewsChanged:
			if h.fleet.setViews(ev.WS, ev.Views) {
				moved = true
			}
		case core.ModelChanged:
			h.applyDevicesLocked(ev.View.Web.Devices)
		case core.Notice:
			// A notice tied to a request of the web client is its reply's.
			if ev.Req == 0 {
				h.noticeLocked(ev)
			}
		}
	}
	if moved {
		select {
		case h.wake <- struct{}{}:
		default:
		}
	}
}

// applyDevicesLocked takes the published device list. A stream whose device
// is gone, or is there with another pairing time (revoked, then paired again
// under the same name), is ended with no bye; one whose device record changed
// is sent the new record.
func (h *hub) applyDevicesLocked(devices []core.WebDevice) {
	known := make(map[string]hubDevice, len(devices))
	for _, d := range devices {
		known[d.Name] = hubDevice{rec: deviceOf(d), paired: d.Paired}
	}
	h.devices = known
	for sub := range h.subs {
		d, ok := known[sub.device]
		if !ok || !sub.paired.Equal(d.paired) {
			h.dropLocked(sub)
			continue
		}
		if d.rec == sub.dev {
			continue
		}
		sub.dev = d.rec
		f, err := frameOf("device", d.rec)
		if err != nil {
			h.lg.Error("encode the device record", "device", sub.device, "err", err)
			continue
		}
		h.offerLocked(sub, f)
	}
}

// noticeLocked sends a model notice to every stream and keeps it. A notice
// with no text says nothing and is dropped.
func (h *hub) noticeLocked(n core.Notice) {
	nj := noticeOf(n, h.l.now())
	if nj.Text == "" {
		return
	}
	h.publishLocked("notice", nj)
}

// publish sends an event to every open stream. A notice is also kept among
// the recent ones.
func (h *hub) publish(event string, data any) {
	h.mu.Lock()
	defer h.mu.Unlock()
	h.publishLocked(event, data)
}

func (h *hub) publishLocked(event string, data any) {
	h.keepLocked(event, data)
	h.broadcastLocked(event, data)
}

// sendTo sends an event to the open streams of one device. A notice is kept
// among the recent ones even when the device has no stream open, so a phone
// that missed it finds it in its next snapshot.
func (h *hub) sendTo(device, event string, data any) {
	f, err := frameOf(event, data)
	if err != nil {
		h.lg.Error("encode an event", "event", event, "err", err)
		return
	}
	h.mu.Lock()
	defer h.mu.Unlock()
	h.keepLocked(event, data)
	for sub := range h.subs {
		if sub.device == device {
			h.offerLocked(sub, f)
		}
	}
}

// keepLocked keeps a notice among the last recentNotices, which every
// snapshot carries. An event that is not a notice, and a notice with no text,
// is not kept.
func (h *hub) keepLocked(event string, data any) {
	n, ok := data.(noticeJSON)
	if event != "notice" || !ok || n.Text == "" {
		return
	}
	h.notices = append(h.notices, n)
	if len(h.notices) > recentNotices {
		h.notices = slices.Clone(h.notices[len(h.notices)-recentNotices:])
	}
}

// closeDevice ends the open streams of one device, with no bye, so the app
// reconnects, is refused and shows pairing.
func (h *hub) closeDevice(device string) {
	h.mu.Lock()
	defer h.mu.Unlock()
	for sub := range h.subs {
		if sub.device == device {
			h.dropLocked(sub)
		}
	}
}

func (h *hub) broadcastLocked(event string, data any) {
	f, err := frameOf(event, data)
	if err != nil {
		h.lg.Error("encode an event", "event", event, "err", err)
		return
	}
	for sub := range h.subs {
		h.offerLocked(sub, f)
	}
}

// offerLocked queues a frame for a stream, and ends the stream if it is full.
func (h *hub) offerLocked(sub *streamSub, f sseFrame) {
	if !sub.offer(f) {
		h.lg.Warn("a stream fell too far behind; ending it", "device", sub.device)
		h.dropLocked(sub)
	}
}

// dropLocked takes a stream out of the set and ends it. Its handler stops
// writing at once, whatever is still queued.
func (h *hub) dropLocked(sub *streamSub) {
	delete(h.subs, sub)
	sub.end()
}

// flush publishes the workspace list and each workspace's session list that
// differs from what the streams were last told.
func (h *hub) flush() {
	h.mu.Lock()
	defer h.mu.Unlock()
	if h.closed {
		return
	}
	if !reflect.DeepEqual(h.fleet.workspaces, h.pubWorkspaces) {
		h.pubWorkspaces = h.fleet.workspaces
		h.broadcastLocked("workspaces", workspacesJSON{Workspaces: h.fleet.workspaces})
	}
	for _, w := range h.fleet.workspaces {
		cur := h.fleet.sessions[w.ID]
		if reflect.DeepEqual(cur, h.pubSessions[w.ID]) {
			continue
		}
		h.pubSessions[w.ID] = cur
		h.broadcastLocked("sessions", sessionsJSON{Workspace: w.ID, Sessions: cur})
	}
	for id := range h.pubSessions {
		if !h.fleet.served(id) {
			delete(h.pubSessions, id)
		}
	}
}

// run is the flusher: each wake waits one publishEvery, then flushes, so a
// workspace's list goes out at most four times a second however fast its
// views change. A panic stops the whole layer: recoverPanic, deferred first,
// calls the layer's fail. It runs last, after exited is closed, so the stop
// that fail starts (which ends in close, waiting on exited) never waits on a
// flusher that is still unwinding.
func (h *hub) run() {
	defer h.l.recoverPanic("hub")
	defer close(h.exited)
	for {
		select {
		case <-h.wake:
		case <-h.stop:
			return
		}
		h.mu.Lock()
		every, flush := h.every, h.flushFn
		h.mu.Unlock()
		t := time.NewTimer(every)
		select {
		case <-t.C:
		case <-h.stop:
			t.Stop()
			return
		}
		flush()
	}
}

// end marks the hub closed and ends every stream, with a bye or without, and
// stops the flusher. It reports whether this call was the one that closed it.
func (h *hub) end(bye bool) bool {
	h.mu.Lock()
	defer h.mu.Unlock()
	if h.closed {
		return false
	}
	h.closed = true
	for sub := range h.subs {
		sub.bye = bye
		h.dropLocked(sub)
	}
	close(h.stop)
	return true
}

// close sends bye on every stream and ends them, refuses new ones, and waits
// up to byeGrace for the flusher to return and the handlers to write the bye,
// so the listener can close behind it. A nil hub, and a hub already closed,
// do nothing. The layer's stop calls it from Close, and from the goroutine
// fail starts when a part panicked, the hub's flusher among them.
func (h *hub) close() {
	if h == nil || !h.end(true) {
		return
	}
	finished := make(chan struct{})
	go func() {
		defer h.l.recoverPanic("hub")
		defer close(finished)
		<-h.exited
		h.handlers.Wait()
	}()
	select {
	case <-finished:
	case <-time.After(byeGrace):
		h.lg.Warn("the flusher or a stream was still running after the grace; closing the listener ends the streams")
	}
}

// subscribe adds a stream for device, ending the device's oldest first when
// it already has maxStreamsPerDevice, and queues the snapshot as its first
// event. It refuses with errHubClosed once the hub is closed, with
// errDeviceUnknown when the device list the hub has read does not name the
// device (the host answered for it an instant before: a connection that raced
// the hub reading a pairing or a revocation, which the app retries), and with
// errStreamsFull when maxStreams streams are open. On success the caller must
// call release.
func (h *hub) subscribe(device string) (*streamSub, error) {
	h.mu.Lock()
	defer h.mu.Unlock()
	if h.closed {
		return nil, errHubClosed
	}
	dev, ok := h.devices[device]
	if !ok {
		return nil, errDeviceUnknown
	}
	mine := 0
	var oldest *streamSub
	for s := range h.subs {
		if s.device != device {
			continue
		}
		mine++
		if oldest == nil || s.seq < oldest.seq {
			oldest = s
		}
	}
	if mine >= maxStreamsPerDevice {
		h.dropLocked(oldest)
	}
	if len(h.subs) >= maxStreams {
		return nil, errStreamsFull
	}
	snap, err := frameOf("snapshot", h.snapshotLocked(dev))
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("snapshot: %w", err)
	}
	h.counter++
	sub := &streamSub{
		device: device,
		seq:    h.counter,
		ping:   h.ping,
		out:    make(chan sseFrame, streamQueue),
		done:   make(chan struct{}),
		paired: dev.paired,
		dev:    dev.rec,
	}
	sub.out <- snap
	h.subs[sub] = struct{}{}
	h.handlers.Add(1)
	return sub, nil
}

// release takes a stream out of the set when its handler returns.
func (h *hub) release(sub *streamSub) {
	h.mu.Lock()
	delete(h.subs, sub)
	h.mu.Unlock()
	sub.end()
	h.handlers.Done()
}

// snapshotLocked is everything the app shows, for a device.
func (h *hub) snapshotLocked(dev hubDevice) snapshotJSON {
	return snapshotJSON{
		Build:      h.l.build,
		Host:       h.l.hostname,
		Workspaces: h.fleet.workspaces,
		Sessions:   h.fleet.sessions,
		Device:     dev.rec,
		Badge:      h.fleet.badge(),
		Notices:    h.notices,
	}
}

// routeStream serves GET /api/v1/stream to a paired device.
func (l *Layer) routeStream(m *http.ServeMux) {
	m.Handle("GET /api/v1/stream", l.authed(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, device string) {
		l.hub.serveStream(w, r, device)
	}))
}

// serveStream writes device's stream until the client leaves, the hub ends
// it, or a write fails. It sets the device cookie again first, so a device in
// use never expires, and answers 503 when the limits refuse it.
func (h *hub) serveStream(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, device string) {
	sub, err := h.subscribe(device)
	if err != nil {
		if !errors.Is(err, errStreamsFull) && !errors.Is(err, errHubClosed) && !errors.Is(err, errDeviceUnknown) {
			h.lg.Error("start a stream", "device", device, "err", err)
			err = errors.New("could not start the stream")
		}
		writeError(w, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, err.Error())
		return
	}
	defer h.release(sub)

	if c, err := r.Cookie(cookieName); err == nil {
		setDeviceCookie(w, c.Value)
	}
	hd := w.Header()
	hd.Set("Content-Type", "text/event-stream")
	hd.Set("Cache-Control", "no-store")
	w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
	rc := http.NewResponseController(w)
	if rc.Flush() != nil {
		return
	}
	send := func(f sseFrame) bool {
		if _, err := fmt.Fprintf(w, "event: %s\ndata: %s\n\n", f.event, f.data); err != nil {
			return false
		}
		return rc.Flush() == nil
	}

	tick := time.NewTicker(sub.ping)
	defer tick.Stop()
	for {
		// An ended stream gets nothing more, whatever is queued: a revoked
		// device must not read another list.
		select {
		case <-sub.done:
			if sub.bye {
				send(byeFrame)
			}
			return
		default:
		}
		select {
		case f := <-sub.out:
			if !send(f) {
				return
			}
		case <-tick.C:
			if !send(pingFrame) {
				return
			}
		case <-sub.done:
		case <-r.Context().Done():
			return
		}
	}
}
```

## Story 8: The API: sessions and their actions, launch defaults, the device, and the requests' replies and notices

- **Developer:** intermediate-dev
- **Files:** `internal/web/api.go`, `internal/web/api_test.go`, `internal/web/reqs.go`, `internal/web/reqs_test.go`, `internal/web/web.go`, `e2e/web_test.go`, `e2e/CLAUDE.md`
- **Interfaces:** exposes the API routes and bodies of the contract and `l.reqs` (`next`, `wait`); uses the Story 6 and 7 seams, the `Core` lifecycle requests and `RevokeDevice` (Story 3), `core.WebHost` and `core.OriginWeb` (Story 4)
- **Verify:** `CGO_ENABLED=0 go test ./internal/web/... && CGO_ENABLED=0 go test -tags e2e ./e2e/...`
- **Review:** deep

Every route under `/api/v1` that Stories 6, 7 and 12 do not serve lives here. A handler takes the web client's next request ID (`l.reqs.next`), makes the `Core` request on `l.client`, and answers from the `Reply` the drain routes back to it (`l.reqs.wait`): 200 with the request's notices, 409 with the model's own text for a refusal or a failed job, 404 for a session the model does not hold, 503 once the daemon is stopping or lost (the client's own refusals, `TakeRefused`, which only `reqs` drains), and 202 `{"pending": true}` when 30 s (`replyWait`) pass with no `Reply`. From then on that request's outcome and its notices go to the device's stream, as do the notices that follow a response (a failed start's, a `Started` whose first prompt went unsent). `POST /sessions` checks and resolves as `loom session new` does, and refuses a blocked remote control as it does, naming the workspace's setting since the app has no flag; the device routes change the notification settings (`core.NotifyChange`), store the push subscription (padding stripped from its keys) or drop it (for the app's launch check: Apple answers 201 to a subscription the phone has dropped, so the sender would never learn of it), and unpair the phone. The request tracker (`reqs`) is a sink beside Story 7's: that one takes the notices tied to no request, this one those tied to the web client's. The Go tests run on Story 6's `webStack`, built over a model with a workspace and sessions and a loop that holds its jobs; Task 8.5 drives the whole API over HTTP against a sandbox daemon, and names that scenario in `e2e/CLAUDE.md`. None of this story's code holds a landmine marker, so no task needs `effort: xhigh`.

```go
package web

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"sync"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
)

// reqsFixture is a tracker over a bare layer (no client, no hub) on a clock
// the test holds, with the notices it sends to the stream recorded.
type reqsFixture struct {
	r   *reqs
	now time.Time

	mu   sync.Mutex
	sent []lateNotice
}

func newReqsFixture(t *testing.T) *reqsFixture {
	t.Helper()
	f := &reqsFixture{now: time.Date(2026, 10, 21, 12, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)}
	f.r = newReqs(&Layer{now: func() time.Time { return f.now }})
	f.r.timeout = time.Second
	f.r.poll = 5 * time.Millisecond
	f.r.setLate(func(device string, n noticeJSON) {
		f.mu.Lock()
		defer f.mu.Unlock()
		f.sent = append(f.sent, lateNotice{device: device, n: n})
	})
	return f
}

// late is what went to the stream so far.
func (f *reqsFixture) late() []lateNotice {
	f.mu.Lock()
	defer f.mu.Unlock()
	return append([]lateNotice(nil), f.sent...)
}

// answered files a Reply for req and takes it, as a handler would.
func (f *reqsFixture) answered(t *testing.T, req core.ReqID, device string) {
	t.Helper()
	f.r.apply([]core.Event{core.Reply{Req: req}})
	_, _, ok := f.r.wait(context.Background(), req, device)
	require.True(t, ok)
}

func TestReqs_NextNumbersRequestsFromOne(t *testing.T) {
	f := newReqsFixture(t)
	assert.Equal(t, core.ReqID(1), f.r.next(), "0 asks for no Reply")
	assert.Equal(t, core.ReqID(2), f.r.next())
}

func TestReqs_WaitReturnsTheReplyAndTheNoticesBeforeIt(t *testing.T) {
	f := newReqsFixture(t)
	req, other := f.r.next(), f.r.next()
	f.r.apply([]core.Event{
		core.Notice{Req: req, Info: "stash kept"},
		core.Notice{Req: other, Info: "another request's"},
		core.Notice{Info: "tied to no request"},
		core.Reply{Req: req, ID: 7},
	})

	reply, notes, ok := f.r.wait(context.Background(), req, "phone")

	require.True(t, ok)
	require.NoError(t, reply.Err)
	assert.Equal(t, core.InstanceID(7), reply.ID)
	require.Len(t, notes, 1, "only this request's notice")
	assert.Equal(t, "stash kept", notes[0].Text)
	assert.False(t, notes[0].Error)
	assert.Equal(t, f.now, notes[0].At)
	assert.Empty(t, f.late(), "nothing went to the stream")
}

func TestReqs_ARefusalIsTheRepliesError(t *testing.T) {
	f := newReqsFixture(t)
	req := f.r.next()
	f.r.apply([]core.Event{core.Reply{Req: req, Err: errors.New("kill x: the session is busy (Loading)")}})

	reply, notes, ok := f.r.wait(context.Background(), req, "phone")

	require.True(t, ok)
	assert.EqualError(t, reply.Err, "kill x: the session is busy (Loading)")
	assert.NotNil(t, notes, "an answer's notices are a list, even an empty one")
	assert.Empty(t, notes)
}

func TestReqs_ARepliesNoticeRidesTheAnswerOnce(t *testing.T) {
	f := newReqsFixture(t)
	stash := errors.New("kept the stash abc123")

	// A kill raises its success notice twice: as a Notice and in the Reply.
	req := f.r.next()
	f.r.apply([]core.Event{core.Notice{Req: req, Err: stash}, core.Reply{Req: req, Notice: stash}})
	_, notes, ok := f.r.wait(context.Background(), req, "phone")
	require.True(t, ok)
	require.Len(t, notes, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, "kept the stash abc123", notes[0].Text)
	assert.True(t, notes[0].Error)

	// A Reply's notice that no Notice repeated still reaches the answer.
	req = f.r.next()
	f.r.apply([]core.Event{core.Reply{Req: req, Notice: stash}})
	_, notes, ok = f.r.wait(context.Background(), req, "phone")
	require.True(t, ok)
	require.Len(t, notes, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, "kept the stash abc123", notes[0].Text)
}

func TestReqs_TheTimeoutLeavesTheOutcomeToTheStream(t *testing.T) {
	f := newReqsFixture(t)
	f.r.timeout = 20 * time.Millisecond
	req := f.r.next()
	f.r.apply([]core.Event{core.Notice{Req: req, Info: "heard before giving up"}})

	_, notes, ok := f.r.wait(context.Background(), req, "phone")

	assert.False(t, ok, "no Reply within the wait")
	assert.Empty(t, notes)
	failure := errors.New("push x: rejected")
	f.r.apply([]core.Event{core.Notice{Req: req, Err: failure}, core.Reply{Req: req, Err: failure}})
	late := f.late()
	require.Len(t, late, 2, "what was held back, then the failure once")
	assert.Equal(t, "phone", late[0].device)
	assert.Equal(t, "heard before giving up", late[0].n.Text)
	assert.Equal(t, "phone", late[1].device)
	assert.Equal(t, "push x: rejected", late[1].n.Text)
	assert.True(t, late[1].n.Error)
}

func TestReqs_ASuccessThatCameAfterTheTimeoutSaysNothing(t *testing.T) {
	f := newReqsFixture(t)
	f.r.timeout = 20 * time.Millisecond
	req := f.r.next()
	_, _, ok := f.r.wait(context.Background(), req, "phone")
	require.False(t, ok)

	f.r.apply([]core.Event{core.Reply{Req: req}})

	assert.Empty(t, f.late(), "the session's own update shows it")
}

func TestReqs_ACancelledRequestGivesUpToo(t *testing.T) {
	f := newReqsFixture(t)
	req := f.r.next()
	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
	cancel()

	_, _, ok := f.r.wait(ctx, req, "phone")

	assert.False(t, ok, "the phone went away")
	f.r.apply([]core.Event{core.Notice{Req: req, Info: "after it left"}})
	late := f.late()
	require.Len(t, late, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, "phone", late[0].device)
}

func TestReqs_NoticesAfterTheAnswerGoToTheStream(t *testing.T) {
	f := newReqsFixture(t)
	req := f.r.next()
	f.answered(t, req, "phone")

	f.r.apply([]core.Event{
		core.Started{Req: req, ID: 3, Title: "fresh"},
		core.Started{Req: req, ID: 3, Title: "fresh", PromptNotSent: "say hello"},
		core.Notice{Req: req, Err: errors.New("start fresh: no worktree")},
	})

	late := f.late()
	require.Len(t, late, 2, "a start that sent its prompt says nothing")
	assert.Equal(t, "phone", late[0].device)
	assert.Contains(t, late[0].n.Text, "say hello")
	assert.False(t, late[0].n.Error)
	assert.Equal(t, "start fresh: no worktree", late[1].n.Text)
	assert.True(t, late[1].n.Error)
}

func TestReqs_ANoticeForAnUntrackedRequestGoesToEveryStream(t *testing.T) {
	f := newReqsFixture(t)

	f.r.apply([]core.Event{core.Notice{Req: 99, Info: "nobody waits for this"}})

	late := f.late()
	require.Len(t, late, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, "", late[0].device, "no device: every stream")
	assert.Equal(t, "nobody waits for this", late[0].n.Text)
}

func TestReqs_ARefusedRequestIsUnavailable(t *testing.T) {
	f := newReqsFixture(t)
	req, answeredFirst := f.r.next(), f.r.next()
	f.r.apply([]core.Event{core.Reply{Req: answeredFirst}})
	once := true
	f.r.refused = func() []core.ReqID {
		if !once {
			return nil
		}
		once = false
		return []core.ReqID{req, answeredFirst}
	}

	reply, _, ok := f.r.wait(context.Background(), req, "phone")

	require.True(t, ok)
	assert.ErrorIs(t, reply.Err, core.ErrUnavailable, "the client refused it itself; no Reply will come")
	reply, _, ok = f.r.wait(context.Background(), answeredFirst, "phone")
	require.True(t, ok)
	assert.NoError(t, reply.Err, "a Reply that came first wins over the refusal list")
}

func TestReqs_ALostClientFailsEveryWaiter(t *testing.T) {
	f := newReqsFixture(t)
	first, second := f.r.next(), f.r.next()
	f.r.lost = func() error { return errors.New("connection to the model lost") }

	for _, req := range []core.ReqID{first, second} {
		reply, _, ok := f.r.wait(context.Background(), req, "phone")
		require.True(t, ok)
		assert.ErrorIs(t, reply.Err, core.ErrUnavailable)
		assert.ErrorContains(t, reply.Err, "connection to the model lost")
	}
}

func TestReqs_ForgetsAnAnsweredRequestAfterAWhile(t *testing.T) {
	f := newReqsFixture(t)
	req := f.r.next()
	f.answered(t, req, "phone")
	f.r.apply([]core.Event{core.Notice{Req: req, Info: "while kept"}})

	f.now = f.now.Add(reqKeep + time.Minute)
	f.r.next() // forgets what is older than reqKeep
	f.r.apply([]core.Event{core.Notice{Req: req, Info: "after forgotten"}})

	late := f.late()
	require.Len(t, late, 2)
	assert.Equal(t, "phone", late[0].device, "kept: its device's stream")
	assert.Equal(t, "", late[1].device, "forgotten: every stream")
}

func TestReqs_BoundsWhatItTracksAndKeepsWhatIsAwaited(t *testing.T) {
	f := newReqsFixture(t)
	first := f.r.next()
	f.answered(t, first, "phone")
	for i := 1; i < maxReqs; i++ {
		f.answered(t, f.r.next(), "phone")
	}
	pending := f.r.next() // over the bound: the oldest answered one goes

	f.r.apply([]core.Event{
		core.Notice{Req: first, Info: "the oldest, forgotten"},
		core.Notice{Req: first + 1, Info: "the next, kept"},
	})
	late := f.late()
	require.Len(t, late, 2)
	assert.Equal(t, "", late[0].device)
	assert.Equal(t, "phone", late[1].device)

	f.r.next() // over the bound again: another answered one goes, never the awaited
	f.r.apply([]core.Event{core.Notice{Req: pending, Info: "still awaited"}, core.Reply{Req: pending}})
	_, notes, ok := f.r.wait(context.Background(), pending, "phone")
	require.True(t, ok)
	require.Len(t, notes, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, "still awaited", notes[0].Text)
	assert.Len(t, f.late(), 2, "it went into the answer, not to the stream")
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `internal/web/reqs.go`:

```go
package web

import (
	"context"
	"fmt"
	"sync"
	"time"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/log"
)

const (
	// replyWait is how long a handler waits for its request's Reply before it
	// answers 202 and leaves the outcome to the device's stream.
	replyWait = 30 * time.Second
	// reqPoll is how often a handler that waits looks for a refusal the
	// client made itself, which no Reply follows.
	reqPoll = 100 * time.Millisecond
	// reqKeep is how long a request stays tracked, to catch the notices that
	// follow its Reply (a start's).
	reqKeep = 15 * time.Minute
	// maxReqs bounds the tracked requests. Answered ones go oldest first; a
	// request a handler still waits for never does, and the handlers bound
	// those.
	maxReqs = 256
)

// reqs tracks the web client's requests. It hands out their IDs (next),
// keeps the Reply and the notices the drain routes back for each (apply, as
// a sink) and lets the handler that made one wait for its answer (wait). A
// notice tied to no request is the stream's, which Story 7's sink takes.
//
// It is the only caller of rpc.Client.TakeRefused in this package: that list
// is drained by the caller, so a second caller would take the other's.
type reqs struct {
	l *Layer

	// timeout, poll, refused and lost are fields for tests to replace:
	// newReqs sets them from the layer's client.
	timeout time.Duration
	poll    time.Duration
	refused func() []core.ReqID
	lost    func() error

	mu      sync.Mutex
	last    core.ReqID
	entries map[core.ReqID]*reqEntry
	late    func(device string, n noticeJSON)
}

// reqEntry is one request's record.
type reqEntry struct {
	at      time.Time
	device  string
	done    chan struct{}
	seen    map[string]bool
	notices []noticeJSON
	reply   core.Reply
	replied bool
	// detached is set when the handler gave up waiting (the wait ran out, or
	// the phone went away) and answered when it took the Reply: from then on
	// the request's notices go to the device's stream.
	detached bool
	answered bool
}

// lateNotice is a notice for a device's stream, or for every stream when
// device is "".
type lateNotice struct {
	device string
	n      noticeJSON
}

func newReqs(l *Layer) *reqs {
	r := &reqs{
		l:       l,
		timeout: replyWait,
		poll:    reqPoll,
		entries: map[core.ReqID]*reqEntry{},
	}
	r.late = r.toHub
	if l.client != nil {
		r.refused = l.client.TakeRefused
		r.lost = l.client.Err
	}
	return r
}

// toHub puts n on device's streams, or on every stream for "".
func (r *reqs) toHub(device string, n noticeJSON) {
	if r.l.hub == nil {
		return
	}
	if device == "" {
		r.l.hub.publish("notice", n)
		return
	}
	r.l.hub.sendTo(device, "notice", n)
}

// setLate replaces where late notices go: a test seam.
func (r *reqs) setLate(f func(device string, n noticeJSON)) {
	r.mu.Lock()
	defer r.mu.Unlock()
	r.late = f
}

// stream sends n where late notices go. It takes no lock but its own, for
// the instant it reads the function.
func (r *reqs) stream(device string, n noticeJSON) {
	r.mu.Lock()
	f := r.late
	r.mu.Unlock()
	f(device, n)
}

// next makes the ID of a new request and tracks it, so a Reply and notices
// that arrive before wait is called are kept.
func (r *reqs) next() core.ReqID {
	r.mu.Lock()
	defer r.mu.Unlock()
	r.forget()
	r.last++
	r.entries[r.last] = &reqEntry{
		at:   r.l.now(),
		done: make(chan struct{}),
		seen: map[string]bool{},
	}
	return r.last
}

// forget drops the requests kept past reqKeep, then the oldest answered ones
// while there are too many. r.mu is held.
func (r *reqs) forget() {
	now := r.l.now()
	for req, e := range r.entries {
		if now.Sub(e.at) > reqKeep {
			delete(r.entries, req)
		}
	}
	for len(r.entries) >= maxReqs {
		var oldest core.ReqID
		for req, e := range r.entries {
			if e.settled() && (oldest == 0 || req < oldest) {
				oldest = req
			}
		}
		if oldest == 0 {
			return
		}
		delete(r.entries, oldest)
	}
}

// settled reports whether no handler waits for e any more.
func (e *reqEntry) settled() bool { return e.answered || e.detached }

// apply is the sink: it files the Replies and the notices that name the web
// client's requests. The notices it files for a request nobody waits for
// any more are sent on, after it has let go of its lock.
func (r *reqs) apply(evs []core.Event) {
	var out []lateNotice
	r.mu.Lock()
	for _, ev := range evs {
		switch e := ev.(type) {
		case core.Reply:
			out = r.fileReply(out, e)
		case core.Notice:
			if e.Req != 0 {
				out = r.fileNotice(out, e.Req, noticeOf(e, r.l.now()))
			}
		case core.Started:
			if text := e.PromptNotSentNotice(); e.Req != 0 && text != "" {
				out = r.fileNotice(out, e.Req, noticeOf(core.Notice{Info: text}, r.l.now()))
			}
		}
	}
	r.mu.Unlock()
	for _, n := range out {
		r.stream(n.device, n.n)
	}
}

// fileNotice puts n with request req: in its answer while a handler waits
// for it, on its device's stream after, and on every stream for a request
// no longer tracked. r.mu is held.
func (r *reqs) fileNotice(out []lateNotice, req core.ReqID, n noticeJSON) []lateNotice {
	e := r.entries[req]
	if e == nil {
		log.For("web").Warn("web.notice_unclaimed", "req", uint64(req), "text", n.Text)
		return append(out, lateNotice{n: n})
	}
	return e.file(out, n)
}

// file puts n with e, once per text: the model raises a success notice both
// as a Notice and in the Reply.
func (e *reqEntry) file(out []lateNotice, n noticeJSON) []lateNotice {
	if e.seen[n.Text] {
		return out
	}
	e.seen[n.Text] = true
	if e.detached || e.answered {
		return append(out, lateNotice{device: e.device, n: n})
	}
	e.notices = append(e.notices, n)
	return out
}

// fileReply keeps rep for the handler waiting for it. A Reply that comes
// after the handler gave up is the request's outcome, which goes to the
// device's stream when it is a failure. r.mu is held.
func (r *reqs) fileReply(out []lateNotice, rep core.Reply) []lateNotice {
	e := r.entries[rep.Req]
	if e == nil || e.replied {
		return out
	}
	e.reply, e.replied = rep, true
	if rep.Notice != nil {
		out = e.file(out, noticeOf(core.Notice{Err: rep.Notice}, r.l.now()))
	}
	if e.detached && rep.Err != nil {
		out = e.file(out, noticeOf(core.Notice{Err: rep.Err}, r.l.now()))
	}
	close(e.done)
	return out
}

// fail ends request req with err, unless its Reply came first. r.mu is held.
func (r *reqs) fail(req core.ReqID, err error) {
	e := r.entries[req]
	if e == nil || e.replied {
		return
	}
	e.reply, e.replied = core.Reply{Req: req, Err: err}, true
	close(e.done)
}

// sweep fails the requests the client refused itself (the daemon is
// stopping), which no Reply will answer, and every one still waiting once
// the client has lost the model.
func (r *reqs) sweep() {
	var gone []core.ReqID
	if r.refused != nil {
		gone = r.refused()
	}
	var lost error
	if r.lost != nil {
		lost = r.lost()
	}
	if len(gone) == 0 && lost == nil {
		return
	}
	r.mu.Lock()
	defer r.mu.Unlock()
	for _, req := range gone {
		r.fail(req, core.ErrUnavailable)
	}
	if lost != nil {
		err := fmt.Errorf("%w: %v", core.ErrUnavailable, lost)
		for req := range r.entries {
			r.fail(req, err)
		}
	}
}

// wait returns request req's Reply and the notices that came before it, with
// true. It returns false when replyWait passes, or ctx ends (the phone went
// away), with no Reply: from then on the request's notices and its outcome
// go to device's stream. A request the client refused as unavailable is
// answered with that error.
func (r *reqs) wait(ctx context.Context, req core.ReqID, device string) (core.Reply, []noticeJSON, bool) {
	r.mu.Lock()
	e := r.entries[req]
	if e != nil {
		e.device = device
	}
	r.mu.Unlock()
	if e == nil {
		return core.Reply{Req: req, Err: fmt.Errorf("request %d is not tracked", req)}, nil, true
	}
	timer := time.NewTimer(r.timeout)
	defer timer.Stop()
	tick := time.NewTicker(r.poll)
	defer tick.Stop()
	for {
		r.sweep()
		select {
		case <-e.done:
			return r.take(e)
		case <-timer.C:
			return r.giveUp(e)
		case <-ctx.Done():
			return r.giveUp(e)
		case <-tick.C:
		}
	}
}

// take hands out e's Reply and notices; later notices go to the stream.
func (r *reqs) take(e *reqEntry) (core.Reply, []noticeJSON, bool) {
	r.mu.Lock()
	defer r.mu.Unlock()
	e.answered = true
	return e.reply, append([]noticeJSON{}, e.notices...), true
}

// giveUp ends the wait with no Reply: what was held goes to the stream now,
// and the rest as it comes. A Reply that arrived meanwhile wins.
func (r *reqs) giveUp(e *reqEntry) (core.Reply, []noticeJSON, bool) {
	r.mu.Lock()
	if e.replied {
		r.mu.Unlock()
		return r.take(e)
	}
	e.detached = true
	held, device := e.notices, e.device
	e.notices = nil
	r.mu.Unlock()
	for _, n := range held {
		r.stream(device, n)
	}
	return core.Reply{}, nil, false
}
```

**Files:**

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `internal/web/api_test.go`. Its fixture is Story 6's `webStack` (a booted model, the real `rpc.Server`, the layer started with `Start` and listening on a loopback port, the pairing route), built over a model that has a workspace and sessions and runs on a loop that holds its jobs: nothing starts or kills a real session, and a test decides when a job finishes. The helpers `httpDo`, `bodyText` and `webStack`'s `start`, `enable` and `pair` are Story 6's. Requests go over HTTP, so the guard, `authed` and the routes are all in the path:

```go
package web

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"net/http"
	"os"
	"os/exec"
	"path/filepath"
	"strings"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/cmd/cmd_test"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/config"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core/rpc"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/session"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
)

// apiFixture is what an API test's daemon holds.
type apiFixture struct {
	// insts are added to the workspace "toy", never started.
	insts []*session.Instance
	// auth, when set, is the default account's remote-control auth.
	auth *session.RemoteControlAuth
	// workspaceConfig is the workspace's config.json; "" is the Claude
	// program with every other setting at its default.
	workspaceConfig string
}

// apiEnv is a webStack over a model with the workspace "toy" and the
// fixture's sessions, on a loop that holds its jobs. The layer listens at
// addr, and cookie is the paired device "phone".
type apiEnv struct {
	*webStack
	t      *testing.T
	addr   string
	cookie *http.Cookie
	ws     core.WorkspaceID
	ids    map[string]core.InstanceID
}

func newAPIEnv(t *testing.T, f apiFixture) *apiEnv {
	t.Helper()
	t.Setenv(config.EnvGlobalDir, t.TempDir())
	def := config.Workspace{Name: "toy", Path: t.TempDir()}
	cfgDir := config.WorkspaceConfigDir(&def)
	require.NoError(t, os.MkdirAll(cfgDir, 0o755))
	wsConfig := f.workspaceConfig
	if wsConfig == "" {
		wsConfig = `{"default_program":"claude"}`
	}
	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(cfgDir, config.StateFileName), []byte(`{"instances":[]}`), 0o644))
	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(cfgDir, config.ConfigFileName), []byte(wsConfig), 0o644))
	dead := cmd_test.MockCmdExec{
		RunFunc:    func(*exec.Cmd) error { return &exec.ExitError{} },
		OutputFunc: func(*exec.Cmd) ([]byte, error) { return nil, nil },
	}
	m := core.New(core.Options{Registry: &config.WorkspaceRegistry{Workspaces: []config.Workspace{def}}, CmdExec: dead})
	m.Boot()
	if f.auth != nil {
		m.SetRCAuth(*f.auth)
	}
	var ws core.WorkspaceView
	for _, v := range m.Workspaces() {
		if v.Name == "toy" {
			ws = v
		}
	}
	require.NotZero(t, ws.ID, "the model serves the workspace")
	ids := map[string]core.InstanceID{}
	for _, inst := range f.insts {
		m.WorkspaceForTest(ws.ID).AddForTest(inst)
		ids[inst.Title] = m.IDOfForTest(inst)
	}

	// newWebStack's stack, but on a loop that holds its jobs and has not
	// begun (no tick).
	s := &webStack{loop: core.StartForTest(m)}
	s.srv = rpc.NewServer(s.loop)
	t.Cleanup(func() {
		s.layer.Close()
		if s.tui != nil {
			s.tui.Close()
		}
		s.srv.Close()
		s.loop.Stop()
	})
	_ = s.loop.JobsForTest() // what the boot queued is never run
	s.start(t)
	addr, _ := s.enable(t)
	cookie := s.pair(t, addr, "phone")
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool {
		if _, ok := s.layer.client.Workspace(ws.ID); !ok {
			return false
		}
		for _, id := range ids {
			if _, ok := s.layer.client.View(id); !ok {
				return false
			}
		}
		return true
	}, 5*time.Second, 10*time.Millisecond, "the web client never saw the model's state")
	return &apiEnv{webStack: s, t: t, addr: addr, cookie: cookie, ws: ws.ID, ids: ids}
}

// apiInstance is a fixture session in status st, never started.
func apiInstance(t *testing.T, title string, st session.Status) *session.Instance {
	t.Helper()
	inst, err := session.NewInstance(session.InstanceOptions{Title: title, Path: t.TempDir(), Program: "claude"})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.NoError(t, inst.TransitionTo(st))
	return inst
}

// send makes one request to the layer's listener (httpDo), carrying the
// paired device's cookie when asDevice is set, and returns the status, the
// body and the headers. A string body goes out as it is, any other as JSON.
func (e *apiEnv) send(method, path string, body any, asDevice bool) (int, []byte, http.Header) {
	e.t.Helper()
	var raw string
	switch b := body.(type) {
	case nil:
	case string:
		raw = b
	default:
		enc, err := json.Marshal(b)
		require.NoError(e.t, err)
		raw = string(enc)
	}
	var mutate []func(*http.Request)
	if asDevice {
		mutate = append(mutate, func(r *http.Request) { r.AddCookie(e.cookie) })
	}
	resp := httpDo(e.t, e.addr, method, path, raw, mutate...)
	return resp.StatusCode, []byte(bodyText(e.t, resp)), resp.Header
}

// do is send as the paired device.
func (e *apiEnv) do(method, path string, body any) (int, []byte) {
	e.t.Helper()
	status, out, _ := e.send(method, path, body, true)
	return status, out
}

// view is the session id as the web client's replica holds it.
func (e *apiEnv) view(id core.InstanceID) core.InstanceView {
	e.t.Helper()
	v, ok := e.layer.client.View(id)
	require.True(e.t, ok, "the replica holds session %d", id)
	return v
}

// apiErrorOf is the "error" of a response body.
func apiErrorOf(t *testing.T, body []byte) string {
	t.Helper()
	var v struct {
		Error string `json:"error"`
	}
	require.NoError(t, json.Unmarshal(body, &v), "body: %s", body)
	return v.Error
}

func TestAPI_SessionRoutesNeedADevice(t *testing.T) {
	env := newAPIEnv(t, apiFixture{insts: []*session.Instance{apiInstance(t, "one", session.Running)}})
	id := env.ids["one"]

	for _, action := range []string{"send", "merge", "pause", "resume", "kill", "push"} {
		path := fmt.Sprintf("/api/v1/sessions/%d/%s", id, action)
		status, out, _ := env.send(http.MethodPost, path, map[string]any{"text": "hi", "source": 1}, false)
		assert.Equal(t, http.StatusUnauthorized, status, action)
		assert.NotEmpty(t, apiErrorOf(t, out), action)
	}
	assert.Equal(t, session.Running, env.view(id).Status, "no request reached the model")
}

func TestAPI_AnUnknownSessionIs404(t *testing.T) {
	env := newAPIEnv(t, apiFixture{insts: []*session.Instance{apiInstance(t, "one", session.Running)}})

	for _, path := range []string{
		"/api/v1/sessions/999999/pause",
		"/api/v1/sessions/abc/kill",
		"/api/v1/sessions/0/resume",
		"/api/v1/sessions/-4/push",
		"/api/v1/sessions/9007199254740993/send", // 2^53 + 1: no JSON number holds it
	} {
		status, out := env.do(http.MethodPost, path, map[string]any{"text": "hi"})
		assert.Equal(t, http.StatusNotFound, status, path)
		assert.Equal(t, "no such session", apiErrorOf(t, out), path)
	}

	merge := fmt.Sprintf("/api/v1/sessions/%d/merge", env.ids["one"])
	status, out := env.do(http.MethodPost, merge, map[string]any{"source": 999999})
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusNotFound, status)
	assert.Equal(t, "source: no such session", apiErrorOf(t, out))
	for _, body := range []any{map[string]any{}, map[string]any{"source": 0}, "not json"} {
		status, out = env.do(http.MethodPost, merge, body)
		assert.Equal(t, http.StatusBadRequest, status, "%v", body)
		assert.NotEmpty(t, apiErrorOf(t, out))
	}
}

func TestAPI_ARefusalIs409WithTheModelsText(t *testing.T) {
	env := newAPIEnv(t, apiFixture{insts: []*session.Instance{
		apiInstance(t, "busy", session.Loading),
		apiInstance(t, "idle", session.Running),
	}})
	busy, idle := env.ids["busy"], env.ids["idle"]

	for _, c := range []struct {
		name string
		path string
		body any
		want string
	}{
		{"kill a busy session", fmt.Sprintf("/api/v1/sessions/%d/kill", busy), nil, "kill busy: the session is busy (Loading)"},
		{"pause a busy session", fmt.Sprintf("/api/v1/sessions/%d/pause", busy), nil, "pause busy: the session is busy (Loading)"},
		{"push a busy session", fmt.Sprintf("/api/v1/sessions/%d/push", busy), nil, "push busy: the session is busy (Loading)"},
		{"resume a session that is not paused", fmt.Sprintf("/api/v1/sessions/%d/resume", idle), nil, "resume idle: the session is not paused (Running)"},
		{"reply to a session that has not started", fmt.Sprintf("/api/v1/sessions/%d/send", idle), map[string]any{"text": "hi"}, "send a prompt to idle: the session has not started"},
		{"merge into a session that has not started", fmt.Sprintf("/api/v1/sessions/%d/merge", idle), map[string]any{"source": busy}, "merge into idle: the session has not started"},
	} {
		status, out := env.do(http.MethodPost, c.path, c.body)
		assert.Equal(t, http.StatusConflict, status, c.name)
		assert.Contains(t, apiErrorOf(t, out), c.want, c.name)
	}
	assert.Equal(t, session.Loading, env.view(busy).Status, "a refusal changes nothing")
	assert.Equal(t, session.Running, env.view(idle).Status)
}

func TestAPI_SendChecksTheReply(t *testing.T) {
	env := newAPIEnv(t, apiFixture{insts: []*session.Instance{apiInstance(t, "idle", session.Running)}})
	path := fmt.Sprintf("/api/v1/sessions/%d/send", env.ids["idle"])

	for _, c := range []struct {
		name string
		body any
	}{
		{"an empty reply", map[string]any{"text": ""}},
		{"a blank reply", map[string]any{"text": " \n\t "}},
		{"no text", map[string]any{}},
		{"a reply over 64 KiB", map[string]any{"text": strings.Repeat("a", 64<<10+1)}},
		{"a field it does not know", map[string]any{"text": "hi", "from": "me"}},
		{"a body that is not JSON", "not json"},
	} {
		status, out := env.do(http.MethodPost, path, c.body)
		assert.Equal(t, http.StatusBadRequest, status, c.name)
		assert.NotEmpty(t, apiErrorOf(t, out), c.name)
	}

	// 64 KiB exactly is the longest the model is asked to take: it refuses
	// this session in its own words, since it never started.
	status, out := env.do(http.MethodPost, path, map[string]any{"text": strings.Repeat("a", 64<<10)})
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusConflict, status)
	assert.Contains(t, apiErrorOf(t, out), "the session has not started")
}

func TestAPI_AStoppingDaemonIs503(t *testing.T) {
	env := newAPIEnv(t, apiFixture{insts: []*session.Instance{apiInstance(t, "one", session.Running)}})
	id := env.ids["one"]
	env.srv.Bye()
	require.Eventually(t, env.layer.client.Stopping, 5*time.Second, 10*time.Millisecond, "the client never heard the bye")

	status, out := env.do(http.MethodPost, fmt.Sprintf("/api/v1/sessions/%d/pause", id), nil)

	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, status)
	assert.Contains(t, apiErrorOf(t, out), "unavailable")
	assert.Equal(t, session.Running, env.view(id).Status, "the model never saw it")
}

func TestAPI_APendingRequestAnswers202AndItsOutcomeGoesToTheStream(t *testing.T) {
	env := newAPIEnv(t, apiFixture{insts: []*session.Instance{apiInstance(t, "slow", session.Running)}})
	env.layer.reqs.timeout = 100 * time.Millisecond
	late := make(chan lateNotice, 4)
	env.layer.reqs.setLate(func(device string, n noticeJSON) { late <- lateNotice{device: device, n: n} })

	// The loop holds the pause's job, so no Reply comes within the wait.
	status, out := env.do(http.MethodPost, fmt.Sprintf("/api/v1/sessions/%d/pause", env.ids["slow"]), nil)
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusAccepted, status)
	assert.JSONEq(t, `{"pending": true}`, string(out))

	// The job finishes after the answer: an unstarted session can't be
	// paused, so the model raises the failure as a Notice and a Reply.
	jobs := env.loop.JobsForTest()
	require.Len(t, jobs, 1)
	env.loop.DeliverForTest(jobs[0]())
	env.layer.client.ReloadAccounts() // makes the server publish what the delivery produced
	select {
	case got := <-late:
		assert.Equal(t, "phone", got.device)
		assert.True(t, got.n.Error)
		assert.Contains(t, got.n.Text, "cannot pause instance that has not been started")
	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("the outcome never reached the device's stream")
	}
	select {
	case again := <-late:
		t.Fatalf("the failure came twice: %+v", again)
	case <-time.After(200 * time.Millisecond):
	}
}

func TestAPI_ModelStatus(t *testing.T) {
	for _, c := range []struct {
		err  error
		want int
	}{
		{core.ErrNoSession, http.StatusNotFound},
		{fmt.Errorf("pause x: %w", core.ErrNoSession), http.StatusNotFound},
		{core.ErrUnavailable, http.StatusServiceUnavailable},
		{fmt.Errorf("%w: connection lost", core.ErrUnavailable), http.StatusServiceUnavailable},
		{core.ErrWebOff, http.StatusServiceUnavailable},
		{core.ErrNoDevice, http.StatusUnauthorized},
		{fmt.Errorf("%w: %q", core.ErrNoDevice, "phone"), http.StatusUnauthorized},
		{errors.New("kill x: the session is busy (Loading)"), http.StatusConflict},
		{errors.New("push x: rejected"), http.StatusConflict},
	} {
		assert.Equal(t, c.want, modelStatus(c.err), c.err.Error())
	}
}
```
