- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `internal/web/webpush/vapid.go`:

```go
package webpush

import (
	"bytes"
	"crypto/ecdsa"
	"crypto/elliptic"
	"crypto/rand"
	"crypto/sha256"
	"encoding/asn1"
	"encoding/base64"
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"math/big"
	"net/url"
	"strings"
	"time"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
)

const (
	// vapidLifetime is how long a token is valid. RFC 8292 caps it at 24
	// hours; half of that leaves room for a push service's clock to differ.
	vapidLifetime = 12 * time.Hour
	// jwtHeader is RFC 8292 section 2's JOSE header: ES256 is ECDSA on P-256
	// with SHA-256.
	jwtHeader = `{"typ":"JWT","alg":"ES256"}`
)

// vapidClaims are the JWT's claims, in the order RFC 8292's example has them.
type vapidClaims struct {
	Aud string `json:"aud"`
	Exp int64  `json:"exp"`
	Sub string `json:"sub"`
}

// VAPIDAuth returns the Authorization header value for a push to endpoint:
// "vapid t=<JWT>, k=<public key>" (RFC 8292 section 3). The JWT is ES256 over
// the claims aud (the endpoint's origin, so one token serves every push to
// that service), exp (now plus 12 hours) and sub (subject, an https: or
// mailto: URI the push service can contact). key.Public must be key.Private's
// own point: a mismatch is refused, since the service would reject the token.
func VAPIDAuth(key core.VAPIDKey, endpoint, subject string, now time.Time) (string, error) {
	if !strings.HasPrefix(subject, "https://") && !strings.HasPrefix(subject, "mailto:") {
		return "", fmt.Errorf("webpush: VAPID subject %q is not an https: or mailto: URI", subject)
	}
	aud, err := audience(endpoint)
	if err != nil {
		return "", err
	}
	priv, err := ecdsa.ParseRawPrivateKey(elliptic.P256(), key.Private)
	if err != nil {
		return "", fmt.Errorf("webpush: VAPID private key: %w", err)
	}
	pub, err := priv.PublicKey.Bytes()
	if err != nil {
		return "", fmt.Errorf("webpush: VAPID public key: %w", err)
	}
	if !bytes.Equal(pub, key.Public) {
		return "", errors.New("webpush: the VAPID public key is not the private key's")
	}

	input, err := vapidSigningInput(aud, subject, now.Add(vapidLifetime).Unix())
	if err != nil {
		return "", err
	}
	digest := sha256.Sum256([]byte(input))
	der, err := ecdsa.SignASN1(rand.Reader, priv, digest[:])
	if err != nil {
		return "", fmt.Errorf("webpush: signing: %w", err)
	}
	// SignASN1 answers a DER SEQUENCE of r and s; a JWS wants both as
	// 32-byte big-endian integers, one after the other.
	var rs struct{ R, S *big.Int }
	if rest, err := asn1.Unmarshal(der, &rs); err != nil || len(rest) != 0 {
		return "", errors.New("webpush: signing: malformed ECDSA signature")
	}
	sig := make([]byte, 64)
	rs.R.FillBytes(sig[:32])
	rs.S.FillBytes(sig[32:])

	return "vapid t=" + input + "." + base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(sig) +
		", k=" + base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(key.Public), nil
}

// vapidSigningInput is the JWT's "header.claims" text, which the signature covers.
func vapidSigningInput(aud, sub string, exp int64) (string, error) {
	claims, err := json.Marshal(vapidClaims{Aud: aud, Exp: exp, Sub: sub})
	if err != nil {
		return "", fmt.Errorf("webpush: claims: %w", err)
	}
	enc := base64.RawURLEncoding
	return enc.EncodeToString([]byte(jwtHeader)) + "." + enc.EncodeToString(claims), nil
}

// audience is the endpoint's origin: scheme, host and any port, no path. An
// endpoint with no host, or a scheme that is not http or https, is no push
// resource.
func audience(endpoint string) (string, error) {
	u, err := url.Parse(endpoint)
	if err != nil {
		return "", fmt.Errorf("webpush: endpoint: %w", err)
	}
	if (u.Scheme != "https" && u.Scheme != "http") || u.Host == "" {
		return "", errors.New("webpush: the endpoint is not an http(s) URL with a host")
	}
	return u.Scheme + "://" + u.Host, nil
}
```

```go
type ctxKey struct{}

func TestNewRequest(t *testing.T) {
	sub, receiver := newSub(t)
	sub.Endpoint = "https://web.push.apple.com/wpush/v2/abc?x=1"
	key := newVAPIDKey(t)
	now := time.Unix(1_700_000_000, 0)
	ctx := context.WithValue(context.Background(), ctxKey{}, "marker")

	req, err := NewRequest(ctx, sub, key, "https://loom.example.com", Message{
		Payload: []byte(`{"kind":"waiting"}`),
		TTL:     time.Hour,
		Urgency: "high",
		Topic:   "AAAAAAAAACo",
	}, now)
	require.NoError(t, err)

	assert.Equal(t, http.MethodPost, req.Method)
	assert.Equal(t, sub.Endpoint, req.URL.String())
	assert.Equal(t, "marker", req.Context().Value(ctxKey{}), "the caller's context rides the request")
	assert.Equal(t, "aes128gcm", req.Header.Get("Content-Encoding"))
	assert.Equal(t, "application/octet-stream", req.Header.Get("Content-Type"))
	assert.Equal(t, "3600", req.Header.Get("TTL"))
	assert.Equal(t, "high", req.Header.Get("Urgency"))
	assert.Equal(t, "AAAAAAAAACo", req.Header.Get("Topic"))

	body, err := io.ReadAll(req.Body)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.EqualValues(t, len(body), req.ContentLength)
	got, err := open(receiver, sub.Auth, body)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Equal(t, `{"kind":"waiting"}`, string(got), "the body is the payload, encrypted for the subscription")

	jwt, k := splitAuth(t, req.Header.Get("Authorization"))
	assert.Equal(t, b64s(key.Public), k)
	claims, ok := verifyJWT(t, jwt, key.Public)
	assert.True(t, ok)
	assert.Equal(t, "https://web.push.apple.com", claims.Aud, "aud is the endpoint's origin")
	assert.Equal(t, now.Add(12*time.Hour).Unix(), claims.Exp)
}

func TestNewRequest_OmitsWhatIsNotSet(t *testing.T) {

	assert.Equal(t, "0", req.Header.Get("TTL"), "a TTL of zero is sent: the service delivers now or never")

}

func TestNewRequest_AcceptsEveryUrgencyOfRFC8030(t *testing.T) {

	for _, urgency := range []string{"very-low", "low", "normal", "high"} {
		req, err := NewRequest(context.Background(), sub, newVAPIDKey(t), "https://loom.example.com",
			Message{Payload: []byte("x"), Urgency: urgency}, time.Unix(1_700_000_000, 0))

func TestNewRequest_RefusesWhatItCannotSend(t *testing.T) {
	sub, _ := newSub(t)
	key := newVAPIDKey(t)
	now := time.Unix(1_700_000_000, 0)
	ok := Message{Payload: []byte("x"), TTL: time.Hour, Urgency: "normal", Topic: "AAAAAAAAACo"}

	_, err := NewRequest(context.Background(), sub, key, "https://loom.example.com", ok, now)
	require.NoError(t, err, "control: the unmodified message sends")
	_, err = NewRequest(context.Background(), sub, key, "https://loom.example.com",
		Message{Payload: []byte("x"), Topic: strings.Repeat("a", 32)}, now)
	require.NoError(t, err, "control: a 32-character topic is the longest allowed")

	badSub := sub
	badSub.Auth = nil
	badEndpoint := sub
	badEndpoint.Endpoint = "ftp://web.push.apple.com/x"
	for name, tc := range map[string]struct {
		sub     Subscription
		key     core.VAPIDKey
		subject string
		msg     Message
	}{
		"unknown urgency":  {sub, key, "https://loom.example.com", Message{Payload: ok.Payload, Urgency: "urgent"}},
		"topic too long":   {sub, key, "https://loom.example.com", Message{Payload: ok.Payload, Topic: strings.Repeat("a", 34)}},
		"topic of 5 chars": {sub, key, "https://loom.example.com", Message{Payload: ok.Payload, Topic: "abcde"}},
		"topic with slash": {sub, key, "https://loom.example.com", Message{Payload: ok.Payload, Topic: "a/b"}},
		"topic with space": {sub, key, "https://loom.example.com", Message{Payload: ok.Payload, Topic: "a b"}},
		"negative TTL":     {sub, key, "https://loom.example.com", Message{Payload: ok.Payload, TTL: -time.Second}},
		"payload too big":  {sub, key, "https://loom.example.com", Message{Payload: bytes.Repeat([]byte("x"), maxPayload+1)}},
		"bad subscription": {badSub, key, "https://loom.example.com", ok},
		"bad endpoint":     {badEndpoint, key, "https://loom.example.com", ok},
		"bad subject":      {sub, key, "loom.example.com", ok},
		"bad VAPID key":    {sub, core.VAPIDKey{}, "https://loom.example.com", ok},
	} {
		t.Run(name, func(t *testing.T) {
			req, err := NewRequest(context.Background(), tc.sub, tc.key, tc.subject, tc.msg, now)
			assert.Error(t, err)
			assert.Nil(t, req)
		})
	}
}

// Apple answers a Topic whose length is 1 mod 4 with 400 BadWebPushTopic,
// though RFC 8030 allows it: unpadded base64url never has such a length (the
// spike, 2026-10-22). Every other length up to maxTopic is sent as it is.
func TestNewRequest_TopicLengths(t *testing.T) {
	sub, _ := newSub(t)
	key := newVAPIDKey(t)
	now := time.Unix(1_700_000_000, 0)
	for n := 1; n <= maxTopic; n++ {
		topic := strings.Repeat("a", n)
		req, err := NewRequest(context.Background(), sub, key, "https://loom.example.com",
			Message{Payload: []byte("x"), Topic: topic}, now)
		if n%4 == 1 {
			assert.Error(t, err, "a topic of %d characters", n)
			assert.Nil(t, req)
			continue
		}
		require.NoError(t, err, "a topic of %d characters", n)
		assert.Equal(t, topic, req.Header.Get("Topic"))
	}
}
```

```go
package webpush

import (
	"bytes"
	"context"
	"fmt"
	"net/http"
	"strconv"
	"time"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
)

// maxTopic is RFC 8030 section 5.4's cap on a Topic header's length.
const maxTopic = 32

// Message is one push: its plaintext payload and the delivery headers of RFC 8030.
type Message struct {
	Payload []byte
	// TTL is how long the push service keeps the message for an offline
	// device, sent in whole seconds.
	TTL time.Duration
	// Urgency is "very-low", "low", "normal" or "high" (RFC 8030 section 5.3);
	// empty sends no Urgency header, which means normal.
	Urgency string
	// Topic replaces any waiting message with the same topic (section 5.4):
	// up to 32 characters of the URL-safe base64 alphabet, of a length
	// unpadded base64url can have (never 1 mod 4, which Apple refuses);
	// empty sends none.
	Topic string
}

// NewRequest builds the POST that delivers msg to sub: the payload encrypted
// by Encrypt, the VAPID Authorization from VAPIDAuth, and the TTL, Urgency and
// Topic headers. It sends nothing; the caller sets the timeout, through ctx or
// its client, and reads the push service's status.
func NewRequest(ctx context.Context, sub Subscription, key core.VAPIDKey, subject string, msg Message, now time.Time) (*http.Request, error) {
	switch msg.Urgency {
	case "", "very-low", "low", "normal", "high":
	default:
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("webpush: unknown urgency %q", msg.Urgency)
	}
	if !validTopic(msg.Topic) {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("webpush: topic %q is over %d characters, outside the URL-safe base64 alphabet, or of a length that is 1 mod 4", msg.Topic, maxTopic)
	}
	if msg.TTL < 0 {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("webpush: negative TTL %v", msg.TTL)
	}
	body, err := Encrypt(sub, msg.Payload)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	auth, err := VAPIDAuth(key, sub.Endpoint, subject, now)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, sub.Endpoint, bytes.NewReader(body))
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("webpush: request: %w", err)
	}
	req.Header.Set("Authorization", auth)
	req.Header.Set("Content-Encoding", "aes128gcm")
	req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/octet-stream")
	req.Header.Set("TTL", strconv.FormatInt(int64(msg.TTL/time.Second), 10))
	if msg.Urgency != "" {
		req.Header.Set("Urgency", msg.Urgency)
	}
	if msg.Topic != "" {
		req.Header.Set("Topic", msg.Topic)
	}
	return req, nil
}

// validTopic accepts an empty topic, or up to 32 characters of A-Z, a-z, 0-9,
// "-" and "_" whose length is not 1 mod 4: unpadded base64url never has such
// a length, and Apple answers one with 400 BadWebPushTopic although RFC 8030
// allows it (the spike, 2026-10-22).
func validTopic(topic string) bool {
	if len(topic) > maxTopic || len(topic)%4 == 1 {
		return false
	}
	for _, c := range topic {
		switch {
		case c >= 'a' && c <= 'z', c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z', c >= '0' && c <= '9', c == '-', c == '_':
		default:
			return false
		}
	}
	return true
}
```

## Story 12: Push: the triggers, the sender, the push key, and notifications on the phone

- **Developer:** intermediate-dev
- **Files:** `internal/web/push.go`, `internal/web/push_test.go`, `internal/web/triggers.go`, `internal/web/triggers_test.go`, `internal/web/web.go`, `internal/web/static/sw.js`, `internal/web/static/js/push.mjs`, `internal/web/static/js/app.mjs`
- **Interfaces:** exposes `routePush`, the push payload, and `PushButton`, `enablePush`, `pushState` and `checkPush` in `js/push.mjs`; uses the webpush package (Story 11), `core.WebHost` (Story 4), the Story 6 seams, `groupOf` (Story 7), `DELETE /api/v1/device/push` (Story 8), Story 9's `launched` hook and Story 10's `PushButton` stub
- **Verify:** `CGO_ENABLED=0 go test ./internal/web/... && bash -c 'for f in internal/web/static/js/*.mjs; do node --check "$f" || exit 1; done; node --check internal/web/static/sw.js'`
- **Review:** deep

A sink compares each workspace's projection with the last one it saw (`triggers.go`: a workspace's first projection is a baseline and pushes nothing, workspace terminals are left out) and works out a waiting push when a session enters Prompting or stays in it with a new reason or tool summary, and a finished push when a started session goes from Running to Ready, each carrying the count of `needs_you` sessions across the workspaces as its badge. A sender goroutine of its own (`push.go`), never the drain, takes each push off a queue of at most 100, addresses it to the devices whose settings allow its kind and are not paused, encrypts and signs it through the `webpush` package and posts it with a 10 s timeout, at most 30 a minute per device. It retries a 429, a 5xx or a transport error three times, honouring `Retry-After`, and records the outcome through `PushResult` (a 2xx is ok, a 404 or 410 expired, any other status failing). `GET /api/v1/push-key` serves the VAPID public key to a paired device. The service worker shows each push as one notification per session, sets the Home Screen badge and opens the session on a tap, and `js/push.mjs` replaces Story 10's stub with the button that asks for the permission inside the tap, subscribes with the key and posts the subscription, and shows that it is waiting until the phone settles (after an unsubscribe iOS took over two minutes, in the spike). Once per launch, `app.mjs`'s `launched` hook calls `checkPush`: Apple answers 201 to a subscription the phone has dropped, so the phone posts its subscription again while loom's state is ok or none, and, holding none or no permission to notify while loom holds one, has loom drop its copy (`DELETE /api/v1/device/push`, Story 8) and says so. Where the spec leaves the sender's edges open this story chooses: a `Retry-After` over 30 s is not waited out, the push is given up on and recorded failing, since one goroutine serves every device and a push is stale within the hour; a transport error that outlasts its retries is logged and records nothing, being the network's trouble and not the subscription's; the 100-push queue counts pushes before they are addressed to devices; a panic in the sender stops the whole web layer, as the spec's failure modes say: the sender defers Story 6's `recoverPanic("push")`, which logs the stack and calls `Layer.fail`, and `fail` (which returns at once and stops the layer on a goroutine of its own) closes the pusher with the other parts, ends the streams, closes the listener and raises the notice through `WebServing("", err)`, so the pusher has no stop or notice of its own, and the sender marks itself done before its panic is recovered so that the stop's `close` never waits on a sender still inside `recoverPanic`; the push client follows no redirect; a finished push says nothing of the session's last message, which a lock screen would show. The button uses Story 10's classes (`sc-btn`, `sc-primary`, `sc-block`, `sc-small`, `sc-dim`) and leaves "Notifications stopped; turn them on again." to Story 10's settings screen, which already prints it below the button. The sender, the key route and the layer's wiring are tested on Story 6's `webStack` (the model as the pusher's host, the real `rpc.Server`, the layer on a loopback port), with an `httptest` TLS server standing in for Apple's push service; the trigger functions are tested on view literals. No landmine marker appears in the story's code.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `internal/web/triggers_test.go`. It pins each transition on both sides (the baseline, a republished state, each term of the waiting and finished guards, terminals, workspaces served again), the badge across workspaces, the texts, and the payload and headers a push carries, the `Topic` of every ID's size included:

```go
package web

import (
	"encoding/base64"
	"encoding/binary"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/session"
)

// pushTestView is a started session in a status.
func pushTestView(id core.InstanceID, status session.Status) core.InstanceView {
	return core.InstanceView{ID: id, Title: "fix the build", Status: status, Started: true}
}

func TestPushWatch_TheFirstProjectionIsABaseline(t *testing.T) {
	w := newPushWatch()
	views := []core.InstanceView{pushTestView(1, session.Prompting), pushTestView(2, session.Running)}

	assert.Empty(t, w.observe(10, views), "a session already waiting when the layer starts pushes nothing")
	assert.Empty(t, w.observe(10, views), "the same state published again is no transition either")
}

func TestPushWatch_WaitingFiresOnEnteringPrompting(t *testing.T) {
	w := newPushWatch()
	w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{pushTestView(1, session.Running)})

	prompting := pushTestView(1, session.Prompting)
	prompting.WaitReason = "permission: Bash"
	prompting.WaitDetail = "git push origin main"
	jobs := w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{prompting})

	require.Len(t, jobs, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, pushJob{
		Kind:    pushWaiting,
		Session: 1,
		Title:   "fix the build",
		Body:    "permission: Bash · git push origin main",
		Badge:   1,
	}, jobs[0])
	assert.Empty(t, w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{prompting}), "republished unchanged, it pushes nothing")
}

func TestPushWatch_WaitingFiresAgainOnANewReasonOrToolSummary(t *testing.T) {
	w := newPushWatch()
	first := pushTestView(1, session.Prompting)
	first.WaitReason, first.WaitDetail = "permission: Bash", "ls"
	w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{first})

	assert.Empty(t, w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{first}))

	newReason := first
	newReason.WaitReason = "sandbox request"
	require.Len(t, w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{newReason}), 1, "a new reason")
	assert.Empty(t, w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{newReason}))

	newDetail := newReason
	newDetail.WaitDetail = "rm -rf build"
	require.Len(t, w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{newDetail}), 1, "a new tool summary")
	assert.Empty(t, w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{newDetail}))
}

func TestPushWatch_FinishedNeedsAStartedSessionGoingFromRunningToReady(t *testing.T) {
	cases := []struct {
		name     string
		from, to session.Status
		started  bool
		want     int
	}{
		{"running to ready", session.Running, session.Ready, true, 1},
		{"running to ready, never started", session.Running, session.Ready, false, 0},
		{"loading to ready", session.Loading, session.Ready, true, 0},
		{"prompting to ready", session.Prompting, session.Ready, true, 0},
		{"paused to ready", session.Paused, session.Ready, true, 0},
		{"ready to running", session.Ready, session.Running, true, 0},
		{"running to paused", session.Running, session.Paused, true, 0},
		{"running to running", session.Running, session.Running, true, 0},
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		t.Run(c.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			w := newPushWatch()
			before := pushTestView(1, c.from)
			before.Started = c.started
			w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{before})

			after := pushTestView(1, c.to)
			after.Started = c.started
			jobs := w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{after})

			require.Len(t, jobs, c.want)
			if c.want == 1 {
				assert.Equal(t, pushFinished, jobs[0].Kind)
				assert.Equal(t, pushFinishedBody, jobs[0].Body)
				assert.Equal(t, "fix the build", jobs[0].Title)
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestPushWatch_ASessionFirstSeenInAKnownWorkspace(t *testing.T) {
	w := newPushWatch()
	w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{pushTestView(1, session.Running)})

	jobs := w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{
		pushTestView(1, session.Running),
		pushTestView(2, session.Ready),
		pushTestView(3, session.Running),
	})
	assert.Empty(t, jobs, "a new session that is Ready or Running is nothing to push")

	jobs = w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{
		pushTestView(1, session.Running),
		pushTestView(2, session.Ready),
		pushTestView(3, session.Running),
		pushTestView(4, session.Prompting),
	})
	require.Len(t, jobs, 1, "a new session that already waits for you is a waiting push")
	assert.Equal(t, core.InstanceID(4), jobs[0].Session)
	assert.Equal(t, pushWaiting, jobs[0].Kind)
}

func TestPushWatch_WorkspaceTerminalsNeverPushOrCount(t *testing.T) {
	w := newPushWatch()
	terminal := pushTestView(1, session.Running)
	terminal.IsWorkspaceTerminal = true
	w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{terminal, pushTestView(2, session.Running)})

	terminal.Status = session.Prompting
	assert.Empty(t, w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{terminal, pushTestView(2, session.Running)}))

	terminal.Status = session.Running
	w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{terminal, pushTestView(2, session.Running)})
	terminal.Status = session.Ready
	assert.Empty(t, w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{terminal, pushTestView(2, session.Running)}))

	terminal.Status = session.Prompting
	jobs := w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{terminal, pushTestView(2, session.Prompting)})
	require.Len(t, jobs, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, core.InstanceID(2), jobs[0].Session)
	assert.Equal(t, 1, jobs[0].Badge, "the terminal waiting is not a session that needs you")
}

func TestPushWatch_EachWorkspaceHasItsOwnBaseline(t *testing.T) {
	w := newPushWatch()
	w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{pushTestView(1, session.Running)})

	assert.Empty(t, w.observe(20, []core.InstanceView{pushTestView(2, session.Prompting)}),
		"a workspace first seen later is a baseline of its own, even with a session already waiting")
	require.Len(t, w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{pushTestView(1, session.Prompting)}), 1)
}

func TestPushWatch_AWorkspaceNoLongerServedStartsOverAsABaseline(t *testing.T) {
	w := newPushWatch()
	w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{pushTestView(1, session.Running)})
	w.observe(20, []core.InstanceView{pushTestView(2, session.Running)})

	w.forget([]core.WorkspaceView{{ID: 20}})

	assert.Empty(t, w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{pushTestView(1, session.Prompting)}),
		"served again, workspace 10 is a baseline, not a transition")
	require.Len(t, w.observe(20, []core.InstanceView{pushTestView(2, session.Prompting)}), 1,
		"workspace 20 was kept")
}

func TestPushWatch_TheBadgeCountsEverySessionThatNeedsYou(t *testing.T) {
	w := newPushWatch()
	w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{pushTestView(1, session.Running), pushTestView(2, session.Prompting)})
	w.observe(20, []core.InstanceView{pushTestView(3, session.Running)})

	jobs := w.observe(20, []core.InstanceView{pushTestView(3, session.Prompting)})
	require.Len(t, jobs, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, 2, jobs[0].Badge, "one waiting in workspace 10 and this one")

	// Workspace 10's waiting session moves on: the count falls with it.
	w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{pushTestView(1, session.Running), pushTestView(2, session.Running)})
	jobs = w.observe(20, []core.InstanceView{pushTestView(3, session.Prompting), pushTestView(4, session.Prompting)})
	require.Len(t, jobs, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, 2, jobs[0].Badge, "sessions 3 and 4 in workspace 20")
}

func TestPushWatch_Texts(t *testing.T) {
	waiting := func(title, reason, detail string) pushJob {
		w := newPushWatch()
		w.observe(10, nil)
		v := pushTestView(1, session.Prompting)
		v.Title, v.WaitReason, v.WaitDetail = title, reason, detail
		jobs := w.observe(10, []core.InstanceView{v})
		require.Len(t, jobs, 1)
		return jobs[0]
	}

	assert.Equal(t, "Waiting for you", waiting("t", "", "").Body, "no reason and no summary")
	assert.Equal(t, "sandbox request", waiting("t", "sandbox request", "").Body)
	assert.Equal(t, "rm -rf build", waiting("t", "", "rm -rf build").Body)
	assert.Equal(t, "loom", waiting("  \n ", "", "").Title, "a title of white space falls back")
	assert.Equal(t, "a b c", waiting("a\tb\x1b\n c", "", "").Title, "control characters and runs of white space flatten")

	long := waiting(strings.Repeat("t", 200), "", strings.Repeat("é", 500))
	assert.Equal(t, strings.Repeat("t", 79)+"…", long.Title)
	assert.Equal(t, strings.Repeat("é", 199)+"…", long.Body)
}

func TestPushJob_PayloadIsTheContractsJSON(t *testing.T) {
	raw, err := pushJob{Kind: pushWaiting, Session: 42, Title: "fix", Body: "permission: Bash", Badge: 3}.payload()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.JSONEq(t, `{"kind":"waiting","session":42,"title":"fix","body":"permission: Bash","badge":3,"url":"/#s/42"}`, string(raw))
}

func TestPushJob_HeadersFollowTheKind(t *testing.T) {
	waiting := pushJob{Kind: pushWaiting, Session: 42}
	finished := pushJob{Kind: pushFinished, Session: 9007199254740991}

	assert.Equal(t, "high", waiting.urgency())
	assert.Equal(t, "normal", finished.urgency())
	assert.Equal(t, "AAAAAAAAACo", waiting.topic())
	assert.Equal(t, "AB________8", finished.topic())
}

// Apple refuses a Topic whose length is 1 mod 4 (spec, "Push"), which "s<id>"
// has for every 4- and 8-digit ID. The topic is the ID's 8 bytes as unpadded
// base64url: 11 characters for IDs of every size, and one per session.
func TestPushJob_TopicHasALengthAppleTakes(t *testing.T) {
	seen := map[string]core.InstanceID{}
	for _, id := range []core.InstanceID{0, 1, 9, 10, 999, 1000, 9999, 12345678, 99999999, 9007199254740991} {
		topic := pushJob{Session: id}.topic()
		assert.Len(t, topic, 11, "id %d", id)
		raw, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(topic)
		require.NoError(t, err, "id %d", id)
		require.Len(t, raw, 8, "id %d", id)
		assert.Equal(t, uint64(id), binary.BigEndian.Uint64(raw), "id %d", id)
		assert.NotContains(t, seen, topic, "id %d shares a topic", id)
		seen[topic] = id
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `internal/web/triggers.go`:

```go
package web

import (
	"encoding/base64"
	"encoding/binary"
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"strings"
	"unicode"
	"unicode/utf8"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/session"
)

// The decisions behind a push: which transition of which session is one, and
// what it says. push.go owns the queue, the sender and the route.

const (
	pushWaiting  = "waiting"
	pushFinished = "finished"
)

// pushFinishedBody is what a finished push says: the session's last message
// stays out of it, since a lock screen shows the body to whoever holds the
// phone.
const pushFinishedBody = "Finished; ready for your next message"

// pushJob is one push worked out from a transition, before it is addressed to
// any device. Badge counts the sessions that need you across every workspace
// once the transition is applied.
type pushJob struct {
	Kind    string
	Session core.InstanceID
	Title   string
	Body    string
	Badge   int
}

// pushPayload is the JSON the service worker reads (sw.js: data.<key>).
type pushPayload struct {
	Kind    string          `json:"kind"`
	Session core.InstanceID `json:"session"`
	Title   string          `json:"title"`
	Body    string          `json:"body"`
	Badge   int             `json:"badge"`
	URL     string          `json:"url"`
}

// payload is the job as the service worker receives it, before encryption.
func (j pushJob) payload() ([]byte, error) {
	return json.Marshal(pushPayload{
		Kind:    j.Kind,
		Session: j.Session,
		Title:   j.Title,
		Body:    j.Body,
		Badge:   j.Badge,
		URL:     fmt.Sprintf("/#s/%d", j.Session),
	})
}

// urgency is the push's Urgency header: a session waiting for you is worth
// waking the phone, a finished one can wait for its next wake.
func (j pushJob) urgency() string {
	if j.Kind == pushWaiting {
		return "high"
	}
	return "normal"
}

// topic is the push's Topic header, one per session, so a phone that was
// offline gets only each session's latest push: the session ID's 8 bytes,
// big-endian, as unpadded base64url, always 11 characters. Apple refuses a
// Topic whose length is 1 mod 4 (spec, "Push"), so "s<id>" would fail for
// every 4- and 8-digit ID.
func (j pushJob) topic() string {
	return base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(binary.BigEndian.AppendUint64(nil, uint64(j.Session)))
}

// pushSeen is what the last projection showed of one session.
type pushSeen struct {
	status session.Status
	reason string
	detail string
}

// pushWatch works out the pushes a projection implies by comparing each
// session with the last one it saw. The first projection of a workspace is a
// baseline and implies nothing, so a daemon restart sends no flood. It is
// touched by one goroutine, the drain's.
type pushWatch struct {
	seen  map[core.WorkspaceID]map[core.InstanceID]pushSeen
	needs map[core.WorkspaceID]int
}

func newPushWatch() *pushWatch {
	return &pushWatch{
		seen:  map[core.WorkspaceID]map[core.InstanceID]pushSeen{},
		needs: map[core.WorkspaceID]int{},
	}
}

// forget drops what it knows of every workspace the model no longer serves,
// so one served again starts over with a baseline.
func (w *pushWatch) forget(served []core.WorkspaceView) {
	keep := make(map[core.WorkspaceID]bool, len(served))
	for _, v := range served {
		keep[v.ID] = true
	}
	for ws := range w.seen {
		if !keep[ws] {
			delete(w.seen, ws)
			delete(w.needs, ws)
		}
	}
}

// observe takes a workspace's whole projection and returns the pushes it
// implies, in the projection's order:
//   - waiting: a session enters Prompting, or stays in it with a new reason
//     or a new tool summary, or appears already Prompting after the
//     workspace's baseline;
//   - finished: a started session goes from Running to Ready.
//
// Workspace terminals never push and are not counted.
func (w *pushWatch) observe(ws core.WorkspaceID, views []core.InstanceView) []pushJob {
	prev, baselined := w.seen[ws]
	next := make(map[core.InstanceID]pushSeen, len(views))
	needs := 0
	var jobs []pushJob
	for _, v := range views {
		if v.IsWorkspaceTerminal {
			continue
		}
		now := pushSeen{status: v.Status, reason: v.WaitReason, detail: v.WaitDetail}
		next[v.ID] = now
		if group, _ := groupOf(v); group == "needs_you" {
			needs++
		}
		if !baselined {
			continue
		}
		before, known := prev[v.ID]
		switch {
		case now.status == session.Prompting && (!known || before != now):
			jobs = append(jobs, pushJob{Kind: pushWaiting, Session: v.ID, Title: pushTitle(v), Body: pushWaitBody(v)})
		case known && before.status == session.Running && now.status == session.Ready && v.Started:
			jobs = append(jobs, pushJob{Kind: pushFinished, Session: v.ID, Title: pushTitle(v), Body: pushFinishedBody})
		}
	}
	w.seen[ws], w.needs[ws] = next, needs
	badge := 0
	for _, n := range w.needs {
		badge += n
	}
	for i := range jobs {
		jobs[i].Badge = badge
	}
	return jobs
}

// pushTitle is the notification's title: the session's.
func pushTitle(v core.InstanceView) string {
	if title := pushPlain(v.Title, 80); title != "" {
		return title
	}
	return "loom"
}

// pushWaitBody says why the session waits: Claude's reason, then the tool
// summary of a permission request.
func pushWaitBody(v core.InstanceView) string {
	reason := pushPlain(v.WaitReason, 80)
	detail := pushPlain(v.WaitDetail, 200)
	switch {
	case reason != "" && detail != "":
		return reason + " · " + detail
	case reason != "":
		return reason
	case detail != "":
		return detail
	}
	return "Waiting for you"
}

// pushPlain makes s one line of at most limit runes: control characters and
// runs of white space become one space, and a longer text is cut with "…".
// It keeps a payload far under the 4096-byte record a push may carry.
func pushPlain(s string, limit int) string {
	flat := strings.Join(strings.FieldsFunc(s, func(r rune) bool {
		return unicode.IsSpace(r) || unicode.IsControl(r)
	}), " ")
	if utf8.RuneCountInString(flat) <= limit {
		return flat
	}
	return string([]rune(flat)[:limit-1]) + "…"
}
```

**Files:**

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `internal/web/push_test.go`. The sender is tested over the production path: the pusher's host is the model itself (a `webStack`'s loop, booted and served by the real `rpc.Server`), so the targets, the settings, the VAPID key and every recorded result are the model's own. The fakes are Apple's push service (an `httptest` TLS server that records each request and answers from a script), the clock, and the retry waits (recorded, not slept). The tests cover the request and its headers, every outcome (2xx, 404, 410, other statuses, 429 and 5xx with and without `Retry-After`, around the 30 s cap, transport errors), each device's settings and pause, the 30-a-minute limit on both sides of the minute, what it cannot sign, the 100-push queue, the sink feeding the sender (a baseline queues nothing), `close` cutting a retry wait short, a panic in the sender that must not leave `close` waiting on it, and the client refusing a redirect. The tests that need the layer itself (its route, its parts, a panic stopping it) follow in Task 12.3.

```go
package web

import (
	"context"
	"crypto/ecdh"
	"crypto/rand"
	"crypto/tls"
	"crypto/x509"
	"encoding/base64"
	"io"
	"net"
	"net/http"
	"net/http/httptest"
	"strings"
	"sync"
	"sync/atomic"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/session"
)

// pushTestStart is the fake clock's first reading, on a whole second so an
// HTTP date built from it reads back exactly.
var pushTestStart = time.Date(2026, 10, 21, 9, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)

const (
	// pushTestOrigin is the origin the rig's model is configured with: an
	// https one, which can be a VAPID subject.
	pushTestOrigin = "https://loom.example.com"
	// pushTestBaseline is the status every rig device starts out failing
	// with, so that a push that records nothing is told from one that
	// records ok.
	pushTestBaseline = 599
)

func pushPtr[T any](v T) *T { return &v }

// pushReply is how the fake push service answers one request: a status, with
// a Retry-After header when retryAfter is set, or (drop) the connection cut
// before any answer, which the sender sees as a transport error.
type pushReply struct {
	status     int
	retryAfter string
	drop       bool
}

// pushRequest is what the fake push service saw of one request.
type pushRequest struct {
	path   string
	header http.Header
	body   []byte
}

// pushService stands in for Apple's push service: an httptest TLS server
// that records every request and answers from a script, the last reply for
// every request after the script's end. transport sends every request to it,
// whatever host the endpoint names.
type pushService struct {
	srv *httptest.Server

	mu      sync.Mutex
	replies []pushReply
	reqs    []pushRequest
	served  int
}

// newPushService starts the service; it answers 201 until a test scripts it.
func newPushService(t *testing.T) *pushService {
	t.Helper()
	svc := &pushService{replies: []pushReply{{status: http.StatusCreated}}}
	svc.srv = httptest.NewTLSServer(http.HandlerFunc(svc.serve))
	t.Cleanup(svc.srv.Close)
	return svc
}

func (svc *pushService) serve(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
	body, _ := io.ReadAll(r.Body)
	svc.mu.Lock()
	reply := svc.replies[min(svc.served, len(svc.replies)-1)]
	svc.served++
	svc.reqs = append(svc.reqs, pushRequest{path: r.URL.Path, header: r.Header.Clone(), body: body})
	svc.mu.Unlock()
	if reply.drop {
		panic(http.ErrAbortHandler)
	}
	if reply.retryAfter != "" {
		w.Header().Set("Retry-After", reply.retryAfter)
	}
	w.WriteHeader(reply.status)
}

// transport dials the service for any address, and trusts its certificate,
// which httptest makes for example.com and 127.0.0.1. Keep-alives are off, so
// every request is a connection of its own and a cut one is never replayed.
func (svc *pushService) transport() *http.Transport {
	pool := x509.NewCertPool()
	pool.AddCert(svc.srv.Certificate())
	return &http.Transport{
		TLSClientConfig: &tls.Config{RootCAs: pool, ServerName: "example.com", MinVersion: tls.VersionTLS12},
		DialContext: func(ctx context.Context, network, _ string) (net.Conn, error) {
			return (&net.Dialer{}).DialContext(ctx, network, svc.srv.Listener.Addr().String())
		},
		DisableKeepAlives: true,
	}
}

// script replaces the replies and starts them over.
func (svc *pushService) script(replies ...pushReply) {
	svc.mu.Lock()
	defer svc.mu.Unlock()
	svc.replies, svc.served = replies, 0
}

func (svc *pushService) seen() []pushRequest {
	svc.mu.Lock()
	defer svc.mu.Unlock()
	return append([]pushRequest(nil), svc.reqs...)
}

// requested is the device each request was for (the endpoints end in the
// device's name), in order.
func (svc *pushService) requested() []string {
	var out []string
	for _, req := range svc.seen() {
		out = append(out, strings.TrimPrefix(req.path, "/"))
	}
	return out
}

// forget clears the record of requests, not the script's place.
func (svc *pushService) forget() {
	svc.mu.Lock()
	defer svc.mu.Unlock()
	svc.reqs = nil
}

// pushClock is the clock a test moves by hand.
type pushClock struct {
	mu  sync.Mutex
	now time.Time
}

func (c *pushClock) Now() time.Time {
	c.mu.Lock()
	defer c.mu.Unlock()
	return c.now
}

func (c *pushClock) Advance(d time.Duration) {
	c.mu.Lock()
	defer c.mu.Unlock()
	c.now = c.now.Add(d)
}

// pushTestSub is a subscription with a real P-256 key and a 16-byte secret,
// as a browser makes one, at an endpoint on Apple's host.
func pushTestSub(t *testing.T, device string) core.PushSubscription {
	t.Helper()
	k, err := ecdh.P256().GenerateKey(rand.Reader)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	auth := make([]byte, 16)
	_, err = rand.Read(auth)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	return core.PushSubscription{
		Endpoint: "https://web.push.apple.com/" + device,
		P256DH:   base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(k.PublicKey().Bytes()),
		Auth:     base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(auth),
	}
}

// The wrappers below are the model with one thing it would never do itself,
// so the sender's handling of it can be tested; every other WebHost method
// is the model's.

// keylessHost cannot give a VAPID key, as the model cannot before its first
// enable.
type keylessHost struct{ core.WebHost }

func (keylessHost) VAPIDKey() (core.VAPIDKey, error) { return core.VAPIDKey{}, core.ErrWebOff }

// extraTargetHost has one more push target than the model holds, ahead of
// the model's.
type extraTargetHost struct {
	core.WebHost
	extra core.PushTarget
}

func (h extraTargetHost) PushTargets() []core.PushTarget {
	return append([]core.PushTarget{h.extra}, h.WebHost.PushTargets()...)
}

// panicHost's PushTargets panics, as a defect on the sender's path would.
type panicHost struct{ core.WebHost }

func (panicHost) PushTargets() []core.PushTarget { panic("boom") }

// pushRig is a pusher over the model of a webStack, a fake push service and
// a fake clock. Its sender is not started and its waits are recorded, not
// slept. No layer runs: the tests that need one start it in Task 12.3's
// newPushStack.
type pushRig struct {
	s     *webStack
	p     *pusher
	svc   *pushService
	clock *pushClock

	mu    sync.Mutex
	waits []time.Duration
}

// newPushRig boots the model with the web enabled (so it has a VAPID key and
// pairs devices) and pairs a device per name, each subscribed at Apple's
// host, taking both kinds of push, and failing with pushTestBaseline. The
// push service answers 201 until a test scripts it.
func newPushRig(t *testing.T, devices ...string) *pushRig {
	t.Helper()
	s := newWebStack(t)
	require.NoError(t, s.connect(t).ConfigureWeb("127.0.0.1:7420", pushTestOrigin))
	rig := &pushRig{s: s, svc: newPushService(t), clock: &pushClock{now: pushTestStart}}
	// A sender that panics in a test should fail the test, so nothing here
	// recovers.
	rig.p = newPusherWith(pushEnv{
		host:         s.loop,
		now:          rig.clock.Now,
		origin:       func() string { return pushTestOrigin },
		recoverPanic: func(string) {},
	})
	rig.p.httpc.Transport = rig.svc.transport()
	rig.p.wait = func(_ context.Context, d time.Duration) bool {
		rig.mu.Lock()
		defer rig.mu.Unlock()
		rig.waits = append(rig.waits, d)
		return true
	}
	t.Cleanup(rig.p.close)
	for _, name := range devices {
		rig.addDevice(t, name, core.NotifyChange{})
	}
	return rig
}

// addDevice pairs a device on the model as the pairing route does, gives it a
// push subscription at Apple's host and the settings notify sets, and marks
// it failing with pushTestBaseline.
func (r *pushRig) addDevice(t *testing.T, name string, notify core.NotifyChange) {
	t.Helper()
	pairing, err := r.s.connect(t).PairDevice(name)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	_, _, err = r.s.loop.RedeemPairing(pairing.Code)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	_, err = r.s.loop.SetPushSubscription(name, pushTestSub(t, name))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	_, err = r.s.loop.SetNotify(name, notify)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	r.s.loop.PushResult(name, core.PushFailing(pushTestBaseline))
	require.Equal(t, core.PushFailing(pushTestBaseline), r.state(t, name))
}

// stateOf is the device's push state in the model. A SetNotify that changes
// nothing answers with the device as stored, from the model's own loop, so
// the answer is never behind a PushResult that has returned.
func (r *pushRig) stateOf(device string) (core.PushState, error) {
	d, err := r.s.loop.SetNotify(device, core.NotifyChange{})
	return d.Push, err
}

func (r *pushRig) state(t *testing.T, device string) core.PushState {
	t.Helper()
	state, err := r.stateOf(device)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	return state
}

// waited is the retry waits asked for so far; nil when there are none.
func (r *pushRig) waited() []time.Duration {
	r.mu.Lock()
	defer r.mu.Unlock()
	return append([]time.Duration(nil), r.waits...)
}

var (
	pushTestWaiting  = pushJob{Kind: pushWaiting, Session: 42, Title: "fix the build", Body: "permission: Bash", Badge: 1}
	pushTestFinished = pushJob{Kind: pushFinished, Session: 42, Title: "fix the build", Body: pushFinishedBody, Badge: 0}
)

func TestPusher_PostsAnEncryptedPushWithItsHeaders(t *testing.T) {
	rig := newPushRig(t, "iPhone")

	rig.p.deliver(pushTestWaiting)

	reqs := rig.svc.seen()
	require.Len(t, reqs, 1)
	req := reqs[0]
	assert.Equal(t, "/iPhone", req.path)
	assert.Equal(t, "aes128gcm", req.header.Get("Content-Encoding"))
	assert.Equal(t, "3600", req.header.Get("TTL"))
	assert.Equal(t, "high", req.header.Get("Urgency"))
	assert.Equal(t, "AAAAAAAAACo", req.header.Get("Topic"))
	assert.True(t, strings.HasPrefix(req.header.Get("Authorization"), "vapid t="), req.header.Get("Authorization"))
	assert.NotEmpty(t, req.body)
	assert.NotContains(t, string(req.body), "permission", "the push service sees ciphertext only")
	assert.Equal(t, core.PushOK, rig.state(t, "iPhone"), "the model heard the result")

	rig.svc.forget()
	rig.p.deliver(pushTestFinished)
	require.Len(t, rig.svc.seen(), 1)
	assert.Equal(t, "normal", rig.svc.seen()[0].header.Get("Urgency"))
}

func TestPusher_RecordsEachOutcome(t *testing.T) {
	date := pushTestStart.Add(10 * time.Second).UTC().Format(http.TimeFormat)
	created := pushReply{status: http.StatusCreated}
	baseline := core.PushFailing(pushTestBaseline)
	cases := []struct {
		name     string
		replies  []pushReply
		want     core.PushState
		requests int
		waits    []time.Duration
	}{
		{"created", []pushReply{created}, core.PushOK, 1, nil},
		{"accepted", []pushReply{{status: http.StatusAccepted}}, core.PushOK, 1, nil},
		{"not found", []pushReply{{status: http.StatusNotFound}}, core.PushExpired, 1, nil},
		{"gone", []pushReply{{status: http.StatusGone}}, core.PushExpired, 1, nil},
		{"bad request", []pushReply{{status: http.StatusBadRequest}}, core.PushFailing(400), 1, nil},
		{"forbidden: the VAPID token was refused", []pushReply{{status: http.StatusForbidden}}, core.PushFailing(403), 1, nil},
		{"payload too large", []pushReply{{status: http.StatusRequestEntityTooLarge}}, core.PushFailing(413), 1, nil},
		{"redirect", []pushReply{{status: http.StatusTemporaryRedirect}}, core.PushFailing(307), 1, nil},
		{
			"429 with Retry-After, then created",
			[]pushReply{{status: http.StatusTooManyRequests, retryAfter: "7"}, created},
			core.PushOK, 2, []time.Duration{7 * time.Second},
		},
		{
			"429 with a Retry-After date, then created",
			[]pushReply{{status: http.StatusTooManyRequests, retryAfter: date}, created},
			core.PushOK, 2, []time.Duration{10 * time.Second},
		},
		{
			"Retry-After at the longest the sender waits",
			[]pushReply{{status: http.StatusTooManyRequests, retryAfter: "30"}, created},
			core.PushOK, 2, []time.Duration{30 * time.Second},
		},
		{
			"Retry-After past it is given up on",
			[]pushReply{{status: http.StatusTooManyRequests, retryAfter: "31"}},
			core.PushFailing(429), 1, nil,
		},
		{
			"an hour's Retry-After is given up on",
			[]pushReply{{status: http.StatusServiceUnavailable, retryAfter: "3600"}},
			core.PushFailing(503), 1, nil,
		},
		{
			"429 for good, three retries at its Retry-After",
			[]pushReply{{status: http.StatusTooManyRequests, retryAfter: "2"}},
			core.PushFailing(429), 4, []time.Duration{2 * time.Second, 2 * time.Second, 2 * time.Second},
		},
		{
			"503 twice, then created, backing off",
			[]pushReply{{status: http.StatusServiceUnavailable}, {status: http.StatusServiceUnavailable}, created},
			core.PushOK, 3, []time.Duration{time.Second, 2 * time.Second},
		},
		{
			"503 for good, three retries backing off",
			[]pushReply{{status: http.StatusServiceUnavailable}},
			core.PushFailing(503), 4, []time.Duration{time.Second, 2 * time.Second, 4 * time.Second},
		},
		{
			"429, then gone",
			[]pushReply{{status: http.StatusTooManyRequests}, {status: http.StatusGone}},
			core.PushExpired, 2, []time.Duration{time.Second},
		},
		{
			"a transport error, then created",
			[]pushReply{{drop: true}, created},
			core.PushOK, 2, []time.Duration{time.Second},
		},
		{
			"transport errors for good record nothing",
			[]pushReply{{drop: true}},
			baseline, 4, []time.Duration{time.Second, 2 * time.Second, 4 * time.Second},
		},
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		t.Run(c.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			rig := newPushRig(t, "iPhone")
			rig.svc.script(c.replies...)

			rig.p.deliver(pushTestWaiting)

			assert.Equal(t, c.want, rig.state(t, "iPhone"))
			assert.Len(t, rig.svc.seen(), c.requests)
			assert.Equal(t, c.waits, rig.waited())
			if c.want == core.PushExpired {
				assert.Empty(t, rig.s.loop.PushTargets(), "the model dropped the subscription")
				rig.svc.forget()
				rig.p.deliver(pushTestWaiting)
				assert.Empty(t, rig.svc.seen(), "and the next push goes nowhere")
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestPusher_HonoursEachDevicesSettings(t *testing.T) {
	rig := newPushRig(t)
	at := rig.clock.Now()
	rig.addDevice(t, "both", core.NotifyChange{})
	rig.addDevice(t, "waitingOnly", core.NotifyChange{Finished: pushPtr(false)})
	rig.addDevice(t, "finishedOnly", core.NotifyChange{Waiting: pushPtr(false)})
	rig.addDevice(t, "off", core.NotifyChange{Waiting: pushPtr(false), Finished: pushPtr(false)})
	rig.addDevice(t, "paused", core.NotifyChange{PausedUntil: pushPtr(at.Add(time.Hour))})
	rig.addDevice(t, "pauseEndsNow", core.NotifyChange{PausedUntil: pushPtr(at)})
	rig.addDevice(t, "pauseOver", core.NotifyChange{PausedUntil: pushPtr(at.Add(-time.Minute))})

	rig.p.deliver(pushTestWaiting)
	assert.ElementsMatch(t, []string{"both", "waitingOnly", "pauseEndsNow", "pauseOver"}, rig.svc.requested())

	rig.svc.forget()
	rig.p.deliver(pushTestFinished)
	assert.ElementsMatch(t, []string{"both", "finishedOnly", "pauseEndsNow", "pauseOver"}, rig.svc.requested())
}

func TestPusher_AtMostThirtyPushesAMinutePerDevice(t *testing.T) {
	rig := newPushRig(t, "iPhone")
	for i := 0; i < pushPerMinute; i++ {
		rig.p.deliver(pushTestWaiting)
	}
	require.Len(t, rig.svc.seen(), pushPerMinute)

	rig.p.deliver(pushTestWaiting)
	assert.Len(t, rig.svc.seen(), pushPerMinute, "the 31st push of the minute is dropped")

	// The model orders its devices by name, so iPad comes before iPhone.
	rig.addDevice(t, "iPad", core.NotifyChange{})
	rig.svc.forget()
	rig.p.deliver(pushTestWaiting)
	assert.Equal(t, []string{"iPad"}, rig.svc.requested(), "the limit is the device's own")

	rig.svc.forget()
	rig.clock.Advance(59 * time.Second)
	rig.p.deliver(pushTestWaiting)
	assert.Equal(t, []string{"iPad"}, rig.svc.requested(), "59 seconds on, the first thirty still count")

	rig.svc.forget()
	rig.clock.Advance(2 * time.Second)
	rig.p.deliver(pushTestWaiting)
	assert.Equal(t, []string{"iPad", "iPhone"}, rig.svc.requested(), "a minute on, they have left the window")
}

func TestPusher_SendsNothingItCannotSign(t *testing.T) {
	baseline := core.PushFailing(pushTestBaseline)

	t.Run("no VAPID key", func(t *testing.T) {
		rig := newPushRig(t, "iPhone")
		rig.p.env.host = keylessHost{rig.s.loop}
		rig.p.deliver(pushTestWaiting)
		assert.Empty(t, rig.svc.seen())
		assert.Equal(t, baseline, rig.state(t, "iPhone"), "a push the daemon could not sign says nothing about the subscription")
	})
	t.Run("a development origin cannot be a VAPID subject", func(t *testing.T) {
		rig := newPushRig(t, "iPhone")
		rig.p.env.origin = func() string { return "http://127.0.0.1:7420" }
		rig.p.deliver(pushTestWaiting)
		assert.Empty(t, rig.svc.seen())
		assert.Equal(t, baseline, rig.state(t, "iPhone"), "a push the daemon could not build says nothing about the subscription")
	})
	t.Run("one unusable subscription leaves the others", func(t *testing.T) {
		rig := newPushRig(t, "good")
		bad := core.PushTarget{
			Device:       "bad",
			Notify:       core.NotifySettings{Waiting: true, Finished: true},
			Subscription: pushTestSub(t, "bad"),
		}
		bad.Subscription.P256DH = "AAAA"
		rig.p.env.host = extraTargetHost{WebHost: rig.s.loop, extra: bad}
		rig.p.deliver(pushTestWaiting)
		assert.Equal(t, []string{"good"}, rig.svc.requested())
		assert.Equal(t, core.PushOK, rig.state(t, "good"))
	})
}

func TestPusher_QueueHoldsAtMostOneHundredPushes(t *testing.T) {
	p := newPusherWith(pushEnv{now: time.Now})
	t.Cleanup(p.close)
	for i := 0; i < pushQueueMax; i++ {
		require.True(t, p.enqueue(pushTestWaiting), "push %d", i)
	}

	assert.False(t, p.enqueue(pushTestWaiting), "the 101st is dropped")
	assert.Len(t, p.queue, pushQueueMax)
}

func TestPusher_TheSenderPostsWhatApplyQueues(t *testing.T) {
	rig := newPushRig(t, "iPhone")
	views := func(status session.Status) []core.Event {
		return []core.Event{core.ViewsChanged{WS: 10, Views: []core.InstanceView{pushTestView(7, status)}}}
	}

	rig.p.apply(views(session.Running))
	assert.Empty(t, rig.p.queue, "the first projection is a baseline")

	rig.p.apply(views(session.Prompting))
	require.Len(t, rig.p.queue, 1)
	rig.p.apply(views(session.Prompting))
	assert.Len(t, rig.p.queue, 1, "the same state published again queues nothing")

	rig.p.start()
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool {
		state, err := rig.stateOf("iPhone")
		return err == nil && state == core.PushOK
	}, 5*time.Second, 5*time.Millisecond, "the sender never recorded the push")
	reqs := rig.svc.seen()
	require.Len(t, reqs, 1)
	assert.Equal(t, "AAAAAAAAAAc", reqs[0].header.Get("Topic"))
	assert.Equal(t, "high", reqs[0].header.Get("Urgency"))
}

func TestPusher_CloseEndsARetryWait(t *testing.T) {
	rig := newPushRig(t, "iPhone")
	rig.svc.script(pushReply{status: http.StatusServiceUnavailable})
	rig.p.wait = pushSleep
	rig.p.start()
	require.True(t, rig.p.enqueue(pushTestWaiting))
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool { return len(rig.svc.seen()) == 1 }, 5*time.Second, 5*time.Millisecond)

	done := make(chan struct{})
	go func() {
		rig.p.close()
		close(done)
	}()
	select {
	case <-done:
	case <-time.After(3 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("close waited out the retries")
	}

	assert.False(t, rig.p.enqueue(pushTestWaiting), "a closed pusher takes nothing")
	assert.Equal(t, core.PushFailing(pushTestBaseline), rig.state(t, "iPhone"), "a push cut short records nothing")
}

// The layer's recoverPanic stops the layer (Layer.fail) on a goroutine of its
// own, and that stop closes the pusher, which waits for the sender. If a stop
// ever ran on the panicking goroutine itself, close would wait on a sender
// that is waiting on it: the sender marks itself done before its panic is
// recovered, so it never does.
func TestPusher_ASenderThatPanicsIsDoneBeforeItIsRecovered(t *testing.T) {
	rig := newPushRig(t, "iPhone")
	rig.p.env.host = panicHost{rig.s.loop}
	outcome := make(chan string, 1)
	rig.p.env.recoverPanic = func(where string) {
		if recover() == nil {
			return
		}
		closed := make(chan struct{})
		go func() {
			rig.p.close()
			close(closed)
		}()
		select {
		case <-closed:
			outcome <- where + ": closed"
		case <-time.After(2 * time.Second):
			outcome <- where + ": close waited on the panicking sender"
		}
	}
	rig.p.start()
	require.True(t, rig.p.enqueue(pushTestWaiting))

	select {
	case got := <-outcome:
		assert.Equal(t, "push: closed", got)
	case <-time.After(10 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("the sender never panicked")
	}
}

func TestPusher_NeverFollowsARedirect(t *testing.T) {
	var elsewhereHits atomic.Int32
	elsewhere := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(http.ResponseWriter, *http.Request) {
		elsewhereHits.Add(1)
	}))
	t.Cleanup(elsewhere.Close)
	service := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		http.Redirect(w, r, elsewhere.URL, http.StatusTemporaryRedirect)
	}))
	t.Cleanup(service.Close)

	p := newPusherWith(pushEnv{now: time.Now})
	t.Cleanup(p.close)
	resp, err := p.httpc.Post(service.URL, "application/octet-stream", strings.NewReader("x"))
	require.NoError(t, err)
	defer resp.Body.Close()

	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusTemporaryRedirect, resp.StatusCode)
	assert.Zero(t, elsewhereHits.Load(), "the redirect's target was never contacted")
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it to see it fail**

Run: `CGO_ENABLED=0 go test ./internal/web/ -run 'TestPusher' -count=1`

Expected: the package fails to build, the errors including `undefined: pusher`, `undefined: pushEnv` and `undefined: newPusherWith`, and ends `FAIL	github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/internal/web [build failed]`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `internal/web/push.go`:

```go
package web

import (
	"context"
	"io"
	"log/slog"
	"net/http"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
	"sync"
	"time"

	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/core"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/internal/web/webpush"
	"github.com/aidan-bailey/loom/log"
)

// The push sender's limits (spec, "Push" and "Limits").
const (
	// pushTimeout bounds one exchange with a push service.
	pushTimeout = 10 * time.Second
	// pushQueueMax is how many pushes may wait for the sender; a push that
	// finds the queue full is dropped and logged.
	pushQueueMax = 100
	// pushPerMinute is the most pushes one device takes in a minute.
	pushPerMinute = 30
	// pushRetries is how many times a 429, a 5xx or a transport error is
	// retried, after the first attempt.
	pushRetries = 3
	// pushBackoff is the first retry's wait when the service gave no
	// Retry-After; it doubles each time.
	pushBackoff = time.Second
	// pushRetryAfterMax is the longest Retry-After the sender sits through.
	// The sender is one goroutine for every device, and a push is stale
	// within the hour anyway, so a service that asks for longer is given up
	// on, recorded as failing.
	pushRetryAfterMax = 30 * time.Second
	// pushTTL is how long a push service keeps a push for an offline phone.
	pushTTL = time.Hour
)

// pushEnv is what a pusher needs of its layer, so a test can build one
// without a layer.
type pushEnv struct {
	host core.WebHost
	now  func() time.Time
	// origin is the configured origin, which signs the VAPID token as its
	// subject.
	origin func() string
	// recoverPanic is the layer's: the sender defers it, so a panic in the
	// sender stops the whole layer (Layer.fail), as one in any of the layer's
	// goroutines does.
	recoverPanic func(where string)
}

// pusher is the layer's push sender and the sink that feeds it. apply runs on
// the drain goroutine: it works out the pushes a projection implies and
// queues them without blocking. One sender goroutine takes them off the queue,
// addresses each to the devices whose settings allow it, and posts it, so the
// drain never waits on a push service.
type pusher struct {
	env   pushEnv
	httpc *http.Client
	wait  func(ctx context.Context, d time.Duration) bool
	log   *slog.Logger

	watch *pushWatch
	queue chan pushJob

	ctx    context.Context
	cancel context.CancelFunc
	wg     sync.WaitGroup

	// sent holds each device's push times of the last minute; the sender
	// goroutine alone touches it.
	sent map[string][]time.Time
}

var _ sink = (*pusher)(nil)

// newPusher builds the layer's pusher and starts its sender.
func newPusher(l *Layer) *pusher {
	p := newPusherWith(pushEnv{
		host:         l.host,
		now:          func() time.Time { return l.now() },
		origin:       func() string { return l.client.Web().Origin },
		recoverPanic: l.recoverPanic,
	})
	p.start()
	return p
}

// newPusherWith builds a pusher that has not started its sender. Its client
// never follows a redirect: a push service answers where it means to, and a
// redirect would carry the daemon's request to a host the subscription check
// never admitted.
func newPusherWith(env pushEnv) *pusher {
	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
	return &pusher{
		env: env,
		httpc: &http.Client{
			Timeout:       pushTimeout,
			CheckRedirect: func(*http.Request, []*http.Request) error { return http.ErrUseLastResponse },
		},
		wait:   pushSleep,
		log:    log.For("web", "part", "push"),
		watch:  newPushWatch(),
		queue:  make(chan pushJob, pushQueueMax),
		ctx:    ctx,
		cancel: cancel,
		sent:   map[string][]time.Time{},
	}
}

// start runs the sender.
func (p *pusher) start() {
	p.wg.Add(1)
	go p.run()
}

// close stops the sender: a push in flight is cancelled, a wait for a retry
// ends, and close returns once the sender has. It may be called again, and it
// may be called by the stop that Layer.fail starts after the sender panicked:
// a sender that panicked has already marked itself done (see run).
func (p *pusher) close() {
	p.cancel()
	p.wg.Wait()
}

// apply is the sink: the drain hands it every Sync's events.
func (p *pusher) apply(evs []core.Event) {
	for _, ev := range evs {
		switch ev := ev.(type) {
		case core.WorkspacesChanged:
			p.watch.forget(ev.Views)
		case core.ViewsChanged:
			for _, job := range p.watch.observe(ev.WS, ev.Views) {
				p.enqueue(job)
			}
		}
	}
}

// enqueue queues a push for the sender without waiting. It reports false,
// after logging, for one it dropped because the queue is full, and false for
// any push once the pusher is closed.
func (p *pusher) enqueue(job pushJob) bool {
	if p.ctx.Err() != nil {
		return false
	}
	select {
	case p.queue <- job:
		return true
	default:
		p.log.Warn("push dropped: the queue is full", "kind", job.Kind, "session", job.Session, "limit", pushQueueMax)
		return false
	}
}

// run is the sender goroutine. A panic in it is the layer's to handle: the
// layer's recoverPanic logs the stack and calls Layer.fail, which stops the
// whole layer on a goroutine of its own and so closes this pusher, and which
// raises the notice. The two defers are in this order on purpose: wg.Done
// runs first, before the panic is recovered, so a close that waits for the
// sender (the stop fail starts, or any stop that ran on this goroutine)
// never waits on a sender that is itself inside recoverPanic.
func (p *pusher) run() {
	defer p.env.recoverPanic("push")
	defer p.wg.Done()
	for {
		select {
		case <-p.ctx.Done():
			return
		case job := <-p.queue:
			p.deliver(job)
		}
	}
}

// deliver posts one push to every device whose settings allow its kind.
func (p *pusher) deliver(job pushJob) {
	key, err := p.env.host.VAPIDKey()
	if err != nil {
		p.log.Warn("push dropped: no VAPID key", "kind", job.Kind, "session", job.Session, "err", err)
		return
	}
	payload, err := job.payload()
	if err != nil {
		p.log.Error("push dropped: payload", "kind", job.Kind, "session", job.Session, "err", err)
		return
	}
	subject := p.env.origin()
	for _, target := range p.env.host.PushTargets() {
		if p.ctx.Err() != nil {
			return
		}
		if !pushAllows(target.Notify, job.Kind, p.env.now()) {
			continue
		}
		if !p.withinRate(target.Device) {
			p.log.Warn("push dropped: the device is at its rate limit", "device", target.Device, "kind", job.Kind, "session", job.Session, "limit", pushPerMinute)
			continue
		}
		p.send(target, key, subject, job, payload)
	}
}

// pushAllows reports whether a device's settings take a push of this kind now.
func pushAllows(n core.NotifySettings, kind string, now time.Time) bool {
	if n.PausedUntil.After(now) {
		return false
	}
	switch kind {
	case pushWaiting:
		return n.Waiting
	case pushFinished:
		return n.Finished
	}
	return false
}

// withinRate counts a push against the device's minute and reports whether it
// fits; one that doesn't is not counted.
func (p *pusher) withinRate(device string) bool {
	now := p.env.now()
	cutoff := now.Add(-time.Minute)
	for name, times := range p.sent {
		keep := times[:0]
		for _, at := range times {
			if at.After(cutoff) {
				keep = append(keep, at)
			}
		}
		if len(keep) == 0 {
			delete(p.sent, name)
		} else {
			p.sent[name] = keep
		}
	}
	if len(p.sent[device]) >= pushPerMinute {
		return false
	}
	p.sent[device] = append(p.sent[device], now)
	return true
}

// send posts the payload to one device and records the outcome through
// PushResult: a 2xx is ok, a 404 or 410 is an expired subscription, and any
// other status is failing with it. A 429, a 5xx or a transport error is
// retried up to pushRetries times, after the service's Retry-After or else a
// doubling backoff. A transport error that outlasts its retries is the
// network's trouble, not the subscription's: it is logged and the recorded
// state stays.
func (p *pusher) send(target core.PushTarget, key core.VAPIDKey, subject string, job pushJob, payload []byte) {
	sub, err := webpush.ParseSubscription(target.Subscription)
	if err != nil {
		p.log.Warn("push skipped: unusable subscription", "device", target.Device, "err", err)
		return
	}
	msg := webpush.Message{Payload: payload, TTL: pushTTL, Urgency: job.urgency(), Topic: job.topic()}
	for attempt := 0; ; attempt++ {
		req, err := webpush.NewRequest(p.ctx, sub, key, subject, msg, p.env.now())
		if err != nil {
			p.log.Warn("push skipped: the request could not be built", "device", target.Device, "err", err)
			return
		}
		status, retryAfter, err := p.post(req)
		if p.ctx.Err() != nil {
			return
		}
		if err == nil {
			switch {
			case status >= 200 && status < 300:
				p.env.host.PushResult(target.Device, core.PushOK)
				return
			case status == http.StatusNotFound || status == http.StatusGone:
				p.env.host.PushResult(target.Device, core.PushExpired)
				return
			case status != http.StatusTooManyRequests && status < 500:
				p.env.host.PushResult(target.Device, core.PushFailing(status))
				return
			}
		}
		pause := pushBackoff << attempt
		if retryAfter > 0 {
			pause = retryAfter
		}
		if attempt == pushRetries || pause > pushRetryAfterMax {
			p.giveUp(target.Device, status, err)
			return
		}
		if !p.wait(p.ctx, pause) {
			return
		}
	}
}

// giveUp records a push that failed for good: an HTTP status is recorded on
// the device, and a transport error is only logged.
func (p *pusher) giveUp(device string, status int, err error) {
	if err != nil {
		p.log.Warn("push not delivered", "device", device, "err", err)
		return
	}
	p.log.Warn("push not delivered", "device", device, "status", status)
	p.env.host.PushResult(device, core.PushFailing(status))
}

// post makes one exchange and reads the status and any Retry-After, leaving
// the body unread beyond what lets the connection be reused.
func (p *pusher) post(req *http.Request) (status int, retryAfter time.Duration, err error) {
	resp, err := p.httpc.Do(req)
	if err != nil {
		return 0, 0, err
	}
	defer resp.Body.Close()
	_, _ = io.Copy(io.Discard, io.LimitReader(resp.Body, 4<<10))
	return resp.StatusCode, parseRetryAfter(resp.Header.Get("Retry-After"), p.env.now()), nil
}

// parseRetryAfter reads a Retry-After header, whole seconds or an HTTP date,
// as a wait; 0 when there is none or it can't be read. A day is the most it
// returns, which is already over what the sender sits through.
func parseRetryAfter(header string, now time.Time) time.Duration {
	header = strings.TrimSpace(header)
	if header == "" {
		return 0
	}
	if secs, err := strconv.Atoi(header); err == nil {
		return time.Duration(min(max(secs, 0), 24*60*60)) * time.Second
	}
	if at, err := http.ParseTime(header); err == nil {
		return min(max(at.Sub(now), 0), 24*time.Hour)
	}
	return 0
}

// pushSleep waits d, and reports false if ctx ended first.
func pushSleep(ctx context.Context, d time.Duration) bool {
	timer := time.NewTimer(d)
	defer timer.Stop()
	select {
	case <-timer.C:
		return true
	case <-ctx.Done():
		return false
	}
}
```

```go
// pushStack is a webStack whose layer listens on a loopback port, with the
// device "phone" paired through the pairing route.
type pushStack struct {
	*webStack
	addr   string
	cookie *http.Cookie
}

// newPushStack boots the stack and starts the layer, then enables the web and
// pairs the phone. When wrap is set the layer's host is wrap(the model), which
// is how a test gives the layer a host that fails where the model would not.
func newPushStack(t *testing.T, wrap func(core.WebHost) core.WebHost) *pushStack {
	t.Helper()
	s := newWebStack(t)
	if wrap == nil {
		s.start(t)
	} else {
		layer, err := Start(Options{Server: s.srv, Host: wrap(s.loop), Hostname: "testhost", Build: "test build"})
		require.NoError(t, err)
		s.layer = layer
	}
	addr, _ := s.enable(t)
	return &pushStack{webStack: s, addr: addr, cookie: s.pair(t, addr, "phone")}
}

// asPhone adds the paired phone's cookie to a request.
func (ps *pushStack) asPhone(r *http.Request) { r.AddCookie(ps.cookie) }

func TestStart_BuildsThePusherAndAddsItAsASink(t *testing.T) {
	ps := newPushStack(t, nil)
	p := ps.layer.push

	require.NotNil(t, p, "startParts builds the pusher")
	assert.Contains(t, ps.layer.sinks, sink(p), "the drain hands the pusher every Sync's events")
	assert.Equal(t, pushTimeout, p.httpc.Timeout, "a push gets 10 seconds")
	assert.Same(t, ps.loop, p.env.host, "the pusher reads and records through the layer's host")
	configured := ps.connect(t).Web().Origin
	require.NotEmpty(t, configured, "the stack enabled the web with an origin")
	assert.Equal(t, configured, p.env.origin(), "the VAPID subject is the origin the model is configured with")
	assert.WithinDuration(t, time.Now(), p.env.now(), time.Minute, "the clock is the layer's")
}

func TestClose_StopsThePusher(t *testing.T) {
	ps := newPushStack(t, nil)
	p := ps.layer.push
	// No device has a subscription, so the sender finds nobody to post to.
	require.True(t, p.enqueue(pushTestWaiting), "a running layer's pusher takes a push")

	ps.layer.Close()

	assert.False(t, p.enqueue(pushTestWaiting), "closing the layer stops the pusher")
}

func TestRoutePush_ServesTheKeyToAPairedDevice(t *testing.T) {
	ps := newPushStack(t, nil)
	key, err := ps.loop.VAPIDKey()
	require.NoError(t, err)

	resp := httpDo(t, ps.addr, http.MethodGet, "/api/v1/push-key", "", ps.asPhone)
	body := bodyText(t, resp)

	require.Equal(t, http.StatusOK, resp.StatusCode, body)
	assert.Contains(t, resp.Header.Get("Content-Type"), "application/json")
	var got struct {
		Key string `json:"key"`
	}
	require.NoError(t, json.Unmarshal([]byte(body), &got))
	assert.Equal(t, base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(key.Public), got.Key)
	raw, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(got.Key)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	assert.Len(t, raw, 65, "an uncompressed P-256 point")
	assert.Equal(t, byte(4), raw[0])
}

func TestRoutePush_RefusesWhoIsNotPaired(t *testing.T) {
	ps := newPushStack(t, nil)
	key, err := ps.loop.VAPIDKey()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	encoded := base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(key.Public)

	for _, c := range []struct {
		name   string
		mutate func(*http.Request)
	}{
		{"no cookie", func(*http.Request) {}},
		{"a cookie no device holds", func(r *http.Request) {
			r.AddCookie(&http.Cookie{Name: cookieName, Value: deviceToken(9)})
		}},
	} {
		resp := httpDo(t, ps.addr, http.MethodGet, "/api/v1/push-key", "", c.mutate)
		body := bodyText(t, resp)
		assert.Equal(t, http.StatusUnauthorized, resp.StatusCode, c.name)
		assert.NotContains(t, body, encoded, c.name)
	}

	require.NoError(t, ps.connect(t).RevokeDevice("phone"))
	resp := httpDo(t, ps.addr, http.MethodGet, "/api/v1/push-key", "", ps.asPhone)
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusUnauthorized, resp.StatusCode, "the phone's own cookie, once its device is revoked")
	assert.NotContains(t, bodyText(t, resp), encoded)
}

func TestRoutePush_WithoutAKeyIs503(t *testing.T) {
	ps := newPushStack(t, func(h core.WebHost) core.WebHost { return keylessHost{h} })

	resp := httpDo(t, ps.addr, http.MethodGet, "/api/v1/push-key", "", ps.asPhone)

	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, resp.StatusCode)
	assert.JSONEq(t, `{"error":"push is not available"}`, bodyText(t, resp))
}

func TestRoutePush_IsGETOnly(t *testing.T) {
	ps := newPushStack(t, nil)

	resp := httpDo(t, ps.addr, http.MethodPost, "/api/v1/push-key", "{}", ps.asPhone)

	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusMethodNotAllowed, resp.StatusCode)
	assert.Equal(t, http.StatusOK, httpDo(t, ps.addr, http.MethodGet, "/api/v1/push-key", "", ps.asPhone).StatusCode,
		"the same device and path, by GET")
}

// A panic in the sender stops the whole layer, not just the sender: the
// sender's recoverPanic is Layer.fail's way in, and fail's stop closes the
// pusher, ends the streams, closes the listener and raises a notice for the
// next TUI.
func TestPusher_APanicInTheSenderStopsTheWholeLayer(t *testing.T) {
	ps := newPushStack(t, func(h core.WebHost) core.WebHost { return panicHost{h} })
	p := ps.layer.push

	require.True(t, p.enqueue(pushTestWaiting))

	ps.waitNotice(t, "push")
	requireNoListener(t, ps.addr)
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool { return p.ctx.Err() != nil }, 5*time.Second, 5*time.Millisecond,
		"the layer's stop closed the pusher")

	closed := make(chan struct{})
	go func() {
		ps.layer.Close()
		close(closed)
	}()
	select {
	case <-closed:
	case <-time.After(10 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("Close hung after the sender panicked")
	}
}
```

```go
// routePush adds the push key route to the layer's mux.
func (l *Layer) routePush(m *http.ServeMux) {
	m.Handle("GET /api/v1/push-key", l.authed(l.pushKey))
}

// pushKey answers the VAPID public key the app subscribes with
// (pushManager.subscribe's applicationServerKey), as unpadded base64url.
func (l *Layer) pushKey(w http.ResponseWriter, _ *http.Request, _ string) {
	key, err := l.host.VAPIDKey()
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, "push is not available")
		return
	}
	writeJSON(w, http.StatusOK, map[string]string{"key": base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(key.Public)})
}
```

```go
func TestPush_ServiceWorkerReadsEveryPayloadKeyAndHandlesBothEvents(t *testing.T) {
```

The second runs `sw.js` as a worker would, in Node's `vm` with a fake `self`, and plays push and tap events into it (nothing is written to the tree; run it from the repo root):

```bash
node --input-type=module <<'JS'
import vm from "node:vm";
import fs from "node:fs";
import assert from "node:assert/strict";

// Runs internal/web/static/sw.js as a worker would, with a fake self, and
// plays the push and notificationclick events into it.
function boot(opts = {}) {
  const handlers = {};
  const shown = [];
  const badge = [];
  const opened = [];
  const focused = [];
  const navigated = [];
  const self = {
    location: { origin: "https://loom.example.com" },
    addEventListener: (name, fn) => {
      handlers[name] = fn;
    },
    registration: {
      showNotification: async (title, options) => {
        shown.push([title, options]);
      },
    },
    navigator: opts.noBadge
      ? {}
      : {
          setAppBadge: async (n) => {
            badge.push(n);
          },
          clearAppBadge: async () => {
            badge.push("clear");
          },
        },
    clients: {
      matchAll: async () =>
        (opts.windows || []).map((w) => ({
          focus: async () => {
            if (w.focusFails) throw new Error("no focus");
            focused.push(w.name);
          },
          navigate: async (url) => {
            navigated.push([w.name, url]);
          },
        })),
      openWindow: async (url) => {
        opened.push(url);
      },
    },
  };
  const ctx = vm.createContext({ self, URL, console });
  vm.runInContext(fs.readFileSync("internal/web/static/sw.js", "utf8"), ctx);
  return { handlers, shown, badge, opened, focused, navigated };
}

async function push(env, data) {
  let pending;
  env.handlers.push({ data, waitUntil: (p) => (pending = p) });
  await pending;
}

async function click(env, url) {
  let pending;
  let closed = false;
  env.handlers.notificationclick({
    notification: { close: () => (closed = true), data: url ? { url } : null },
    waitUntil: (p) => (pending = p),
  });
  await pending;
  assert.ok(closed, "the notification is closed on a tap");
}

// A push shows one notification tagged by its session, and sets the badge.
{
  const env = boot();
  await push(env, { json: () => ({ kind: "waiting", session: 42, title: "fix", body: "permission: Bash", badge: 2, url: "/#s/42" }) });
  assert.equal(env.shown.length, 1);
  assert.equal(env.shown[0][0], "fix");
  assert.equal(env.shown[0][1].body, "permission: Bash");
  assert.equal(env.shown[0][1].tag, "session-42");
  assert.equal(env.shown[0][1].renotify, true);
  assert.equal(env.shown[0][1].data.url, "https://loom.example.com/#s/42");
  assert.deepEqual(env.badge, [2]);
}
// A count of 0 clears the badge; a url off this origin opens the front page.
{
  const env = boot();
  await push(env, { json: () => ({ kind: "finished", session: 5, title: "t", body: "b", badge: 0, url: "https://elsewhere.example/#s/5" }) });
  assert.equal(env.shown[0][1].data.url, "https://loom.example.com/");
  assert.deepEqual(env.badge, ["clear"]);
}
// iOS needs every push to show something, whatever arrives.
{
  const env = boot();
  await push(env, { json: () => { throw new SyntaxError("not json"); } });
  await push(env, null);
  await push(env, { json: () => null });
  assert.equal(env.shown.length, 3);
  assert.equal(env.shown[0][0], "loom");
  assert.deepEqual(env.badge, [], "a push without a count leaves the badge alone");
}
// A worker without the Badging API still shows the notification.
{
  const env = boot({ noBadge: true });
  await push(env, { json: () => ({ title: "x", badge: 3 }) });
  assert.equal(env.shown.length, 1);
}
// A tap focuses an open window and takes it to the session.
{
  const env = boot({ windows: [{ name: "w1" }] });
  await click(env, "https://loom.example.com/#s/9");
  assert.deepEqual(env.focused, ["w1"]);
  assert.deepEqual(env.navigated, [["w1", "https://loom.example.com/#s/9"]]);
  assert.deepEqual(env.opened, []);
}
// A window that cannot be focused is skipped for the next.
{
  const env = boot({ windows: [{ name: "w1", focusFails: true }, { name: "w2" }] });
  await click(env, "https://loom.example.com/#s/9");
  assert.deepEqual(env.focused, ["w2"]);
}
// With no window open, a tap opens one.
{
  const env = boot({ windows: [] });
  await click(env, "https://loom.example.com/#s/9");
  assert.deepEqual(env.opened, ["https://loom.example.com/#s/9"]);
  await click(env, null);
  assert.equal(env.opened[1], "https://loom.example.com/");
}
console.log("ok");
JS
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Append to the end of `internal/web/static/sw.js` (after a blank line; change nothing above it):

```js

// ---- Push ----
// A push is encrypted for this worker alone: the push service carries
// ciphertext. Its JSON is {kind, session, title, body, badge, url}
// (internal/web/triggers.go). iOS drops the subscription of a worker whose
// push shows nothing, so every push shows a notification, even one this code
// cannot read.
self.addEventListener("push", (event) => {
  event.waitUntil(showPush(event));
});

async function showPush(event) {
  let data = {};
  try {
    data = event.data ? event.data.json() : {};
  } catch (err) {
    data = {};
  }
  if (data === null || typeof data !== "object") data = {};

  const title =
    typeof data.title === "string" && data.title
      ? data.title
      : data.kind === "waiting"
        ? "A session needs you"
        : "loom";
  const body =
    typeof data.body === "string" && data.body
      ? data.body
      : "Open loom to see what changed.";
  // One notification per session: a newer push for it replaces the older
  // one, and renotify still alerts.
  const tag = data.session ? "session-" + data.session : "loom";

  await self.registration.showNotification(title, {
    body,
    tag,
    renotify: true,
    icon: "/icons/icon-192.png",
    data: { url: pushTarget(data.url) },
  });
  await setBadge(data.badge);
}

// setBadge puts the count of sessions that need you on the Home Screen icon.
// A push without a count leaves the badge alone.
async function setBadge(count) {
  if (!Number.isInteger(count) || count < 0) return;
  if (!self.navigator || !self.navigator.setAppBadge) return;
  try {
    if (count > 0) await self.navigator.setAppBadge(count);
    else await self.navigator.clearAppBadge();
  } catch (err) {
    // The badge is a courtesy; the notification is already shown.
  }
}

// pushTarget is the page a notification opens: the push's url when it
// stays on this origin, else the app's front page.
function pushTarget(url) {
  try {
    const target = new URL(url, self.location.origin);
    if (target.origin === self.location.origin) return target.href;
  } catch (err) {
    // fall through to the front page
  }
  return new URL("/", self.location.origin).href;
}

self.addEventListener("notificationclick", (event) => {
  event.notification.close();
  const url =
    event.notification.data && event.notification.data.url
      ? event.notification.data.url
      : pushTarget("/");
  event.waitUntil(openApp(url));
});

// openApp brings an open window of the app to the front on the page, or
// opens one.
async function openApp(url) {
  const windows = await self.clients.matchAll({
    type: "window",
    includeUncontrolled: true,
  });
  for (const win of windows) {
    try {
      await win.focus();
      if (win.navigate) await win.navigate(url);
      return;
    } catch (err) {
      // Try the next window, then a new one.
    }
  }
  await self.clients.openWindow(url);
}
```

The check imports the module under Node and plays the browser's side with fakes (the module takes its browser objects as a last argument, so no global is touched): the base64url key, what the button area shows for each state, and `enablePush` through unsupported, a refused permission, no active worker, the whole way through over an old subscription, the daemon refusing the key, the phone refusing to subscribe and the daemon refusing the subscription, and `checkPush` on both sides of each of its terms (loom's state, the phone holding a subscription or not, notifications allowed or not, the daemon refusing).
