// The browser's side, faked: permission answers, one old subscription.
function fakes(o = {}) {
  const log = { asked: 0, subscribed: [], dropped: [] };
  const make = (name) => ({
    toJSON: () => ({ endpoint: "https://web.push.apple.com/" + name, keys: { p256dh: "P", auth: "A" } }),
    unsubscribe: async () => {
      log.dropped.push(name);
      return true;
    },
  });
  let current = o.old ? make("old") : null;
  const registration = {
    pushManager: {
      getSubscription: async () => current,
      subscribe: async (options) => {
        if (o.subscribeFails) throw new Error("push service unavailable");
        log.subscribed.push(options);
        current = make("new");
        return current;
      },
    },
  };
  const env = {
    serviceWorker: o.noWorker ? undefined : { ready: o.neverReady ? new Promise(() => {}) : Promise.resolve(registration) },
    Notification: o.noNotification
      ? undefined
      : {
          permission: o.permission || "default",
          requestPermission: async () => {
            log.asked++;
            return o.answer || "granted";
          },
        },
    hasPush: !o.noPush,
    readyMs: 20,
  };
  return { env, log };
}

// act answers from a table keyed "METHOD path" and records its calls.
function acts(table = {}) {
  const calls = [];
  const act = async (method, path, body) => {
    calls.push([method, path, body]);
    return table[method + " " + path] || { ok: true, status: 200, data: {} };
  };
  return { act, calls };
}
const keyOK = { "GET /api/v1/push-key": { ok: true, status: 200, data: { key: "AQID" } } };

assert.deepEqual(await p.pushState(fakes({ noNotification: true }).env), { supported: false, permission: "unsupported", subscribed: false });
assert.deepEqual(await p.pushState(fakes().env), { supported: true, permission: "default", subscribed: false });
assert.deepEqual(await p.pushState(fakes({ old: true, permission: "granted" }).env), { supported: true, permission: "granted", subscribed: true });

// checkPush: the phone's subscription against loom's state, once a launch.
function posts(status = 200) {
  const calls = [];
  const post = async (method, path, body) => {
    calls.push([method, path, body]);
    return { status, data: null };
  };
  return { post, calls };
}

  const shown = [];

}
// The drop refused: nothing is said, since nothing changed.
{

}
// None on either side: nothing to do.
for (const device of [{ push: "" }, { push: "expired" }, undefined]) {

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Replace the whole of `internal/web/static/js/push.mjs` with:

```js
// Notifications: the "Turn on notifications" button, the browser work behind
// it, and the launch's check of the phone's subscription. iOS gives web push
// only to an app installed to the Home Screen, and asks for the permission
// only inside a tap, so nothing here asks for it until the person presses the
// button. The settings screen renders PushButton with the device the stream
// sent and the app's act and toast; app.mjs's launched calls checkPush.
import { html, useEffect, useState } from "../lib/preact-htm.mjs";

// browser gathers the objects this module needs, so a check can hand in
// fakes. readyMs is how long to wait for an active service worker.
function browser() {
  const nav = globalThis.navigator;
  return {
    serviceWorker: nav ? nav.serviceWorker : undefined,
    Notification: globalThis.Notification,
    hasPush: "PushManager" in globalThis,
    readyMs: 5000,
  };
}

function pushSupported(env) {
  return Boolean(env.serviceWorker && env.Notification && env.hasPush);
}

// activeRegistration is the page's service worker registration once the
// worker is active, or null if none becomes active in time.
function activeRegistration(env) {
  let timer;
  const late = new Promise((resolve) => {
    timer = setTimeout(() => resolve(null), env.readyMs);
  });
  return Promise.race([env.serviceWorker.ready, late]).finally(() => clearTimeout(timer));
}

// keyBytes turns the unpadded base64url the daemon sends (the VAPID public
// key) into the bytes pushManager.subscribe takes.
export function keyBytes(text) {
  const b64 = text.replace(/-/g, "+").replace(/_/g, "/");
  const raw = atob(b64 + "=".repeat((4 - (b64.length % 4)) % 4));
  return Uint8Array.from(raw, (c) => c.charCodeAt(0));
}

// pushState is what this phone knows: whether it can take push at all, the
// notification permission, and whether it holds a subscription.
export async function pushState(env = browser()) {
  if (!pushSupported(env)) {
    return { supported: false, permission: "unsupported", subscribed: false };
  }
  let subscribed = false;
  try {
    const registration = await activeRegistration(env);
    subscribed = registration ? (await registration.pushManager.getSubscription()) !== null : false;
  } catch (err) {
    subscribed = false;
  }
  return { supported: true, permission: env.Notification.permission, subscribed };
}

// enablePush asks for the permission, subscribes with the daemon's VAPID key
// and hands the subscription to the daemon through act (the app's act, which
// has already shown the reason when an API call fails). It resolves
// {ok, error}: error is a sentence for the person, or "" when act showed why.
// The permission request comes before any other await, inside the tap that
// called this.
export async function enablePush(act, env = browser()) {
  if (!pushSupported(env)) {
    return {
      ok: false,
      error: "Notifications need loom on the Home Screen. Tap Share, then Add to Home Screen, and open loom from there.",
    };
  }
  const permission = await env.Notification.requestPermission();
  if (permission !== "granted") {
    return {
      ok: false,
      error: "Notifications are blocked for loom. Allow them in the phone's Settings, under Notifications, then loom.",
    };
  }
  const registration = await activeRegistration(env);
  if (!registration) {
    return { ok: false, error: "loom isn't ready for notifications yet. Reload the app and try again." };
  }
  const key = await act("GET", "/api/v1/push-key");
  if (!key.ok) return { ok: false, error: "" };
  if (!key.data || typeof key.data.key !== "string") {
    return { ok: false, error: "loom sent no push key." };
  }

  let subscription;
  try {
    // A fresh subscription each time: after "expired" the old one is dead.
    const old = await registration.pushManager.getSubscription();
    if (old) await old.unsubscribe();
    subscription = await registration.pushManager.subscribe({
      userVisibleOnly: true,
      applicationServerKey: keyBytes(key.data.key),
    });
  } catch (err) {
    return { ok: false, error: "The phone refused the subscription: " + (err && err.message ? err.message : err) };
  }

  const stored = await act("POST", "/api/v1/device/push", subscription.toJSON());
  if (!stored.ok) {
    // The daemon never learned of it, so keep none the daemon cannot use.
    try {
      await subscription.unsubscribe();
    } catch (err) {
      // Nothing more to undo.
    }
    return { ok: false, error: "" };
  }
  return { ok: true, error: "" };
}

// checkPush runs once per launch, after the first snapshot (app.mjs's
// launched). Apple answers 201 to a subscription the phone has dropped, and
// drops the push, so loom cannot learn of a lost one from the push service:
// the phone tells it. While loom's state (device.push) is "ok" or none, the
// phone's subscription is posted again, which changes nothing when it is the
// same and gives loom back one it lost. When the phone holds none, or may not
// notify, while loom holds one ("ok" or "failing: <status>"), loom drops its
// copy (DELETE /api/v1/device/push, which it records as expired) and the
// person is told. A subscription loom knows is bad ("expired" or "failing")
// is left for the button to replace. post is the app's call, which never
// throws. It resolves what it did, for the check.
export async function checkPush(device, toast, post, env = browser()) {
  if (!pushSupported(env)) return "unsupported";
  let subscription;
  try {
    const registration = await activeRegistration(env);
    if (!registration) return "unknown";
    subscription = await registration.pushManager.getSubscription();
  } catch (err) {
    return "unknown";
  }
  const state = (device && device.push) || "";
  if (subscription && env.Notification.permission === "granted") {
    if (state !== "ok" && state !== "") return "kept";
    const res = await post("POST", "/api/v1/device/push", subscription.toJSON());
    return res.status >= 200 && res.status < 300 ? "posted" : "failed";
  }
  if (state !== "ok" && !state.startsWith("failing")) return "none";
  const res = await post("DELETE", "/api/v1/device/push");
  if (res.status < 200 || res.status >= 300) return "failed";
  toast("Notifications stopped on this phone. Turn them on again in Settings.", true);
  return "dropped";
}

// pushView decides what the button area shows from the device the daemon
// has (device.push is "", "ok", "expired" or "failing: <status>"), what the
// phone knows (pushState, null while it loads) and whether the person just
// turned it on here.
export function pushView(device, local, done = false) {
  if (!local) return "loading";
  if (!local.supported) return "unsupported";
  if (local.permission === "denied") return "denied";
  if (done && local.subscribed) return "on";
  const state = (device && device.push) || "";
  if (state === "ok" && local.subscribed) return "on";
  if (state === "expired" || state.startsWith("failing")) return "again";
  return "off";
}

export function PushButton({ device, act, toast }) {
  const [local, setLocal] = useState(null);
  const [busy, setBusy] = useState(false);
  const [done, setDone] = useState(false);
  const state = (device && device.push) || "";

  // Read what the phone knows when the screen opens, and again whenever the
  // daemon's idea of the subscription changes.
  useEffect(() => {
    let live = true;
    setDone(false);
    pushState().then((s) => {
      if (live) setLocal(s);
    });
    return () => {
      live = false;
    };
  }, [state]);

  async function turnOn() {
    setBusy(true);
    const result = await enablePush(act);
    if (result.error) toast(result.error, true);
    else if (result.ok) toast("Notifications are on.", false);
    setDone(result.ok);
    setLocal(await pushState());
    setBusy(false);
  }

  const view = pushView(device, local, done);
  if (view === "loading") return null;
  if (busy) {
    // subscribe() can take minutes: after an unsubscribe iOS took over two.
    return html`<div>
      <button class="sc-btn sc-primary sc-block" type="button" disabled>Turning on…</button>
      <p class="sc-small sc-dim">The phone can take a minute or two.</p>
    </div>`;
  }
  if (view === "unsupported") {
    return html`<p class="sc-small sc-dim">Notifications need loom on the Home Screen. Tap Share, then Add to Home Screen, and open loom from there.</p>`;
  }
  if (view === "denied") {
    return html`<p class="sc-small sc-dim">Notifications are blocked for loom. Allow them in the phone's Settings, under Notifications, then loom.</p>`;
  }
  if (view === "on") {
    return html`<p class="sc-small sc-dim">Notifications are on for this phone.</p>`;
  }
  return html`<button class="sc-btn sc-primary sc-block" type="button" onClick=${turnOn}>
    ${view === "again" ? "Turn notifications on again" : "Turn on notifications"}
  </button>`;
}
```

In `internal/web/static/js/app.mjs`, import the check after Story 9's last import. Current:

Then, in `internal/web/static/js/app.mjs`, fill Story 9's `launched` hook. Current:

```js
// launched runs once per launch, after the first snapshot, with the app's
// toast: the place for work that needs the device's state once a launch.
function launched(snap, toast) {}
```

Replace with:

```js
// launched runs once per launch, after the first snapshot: the push check
// tells loom of a subscription the phone has lost (push.mjs).
function launched(snap, toast) {
  checkPush(snap.device, toast, call);
}
```

## Story 13: The docs

- **Developer:** junior-dev
- **Files:** `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `USAGE.md`, `internal/web/CLAUDE.md`, `internal/web/README.md`, `core/README.md`, `internal/daemon/README.md`, `internal/daemon/CLAUDE.md`, `config/README.md`, `docs/claude/web-layer.md`, `docs/claude/INDEX.md`
- **Interfaces:** none
- **Verify:** `CGO_ENABLED=0 go test ./tools/claudemd/ && CGO_ENABLED=0 go run ./tools/claudemd -v`
- **Review:** light (docs only)

The web layer's rules go in `internal/web/CLAUDE.md`, appended to the text Story 6 wrote and changing none of it, and in a guide, `docs/claude/web-layer.md`, listed in `docs/claude/INDEX.md` and linked from a rule (the lint fails a guide that is neither); core's own `CLAUDE.md` has no room, so no rule goes there. The layer's facts go in `internal/web/README.md` (appended the same way), the daemon's new listener, `web.json`, the hello's role and the stop order in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (and `web.json` in the global dir's list in `config/README.md`), the `core` files and the `Web` view in `core/README.md`, `loom web`, the tunnel and the systemd user unit with its environment in `USAGE.md`, and the layer `daemon.Serve` starts after the mod socket, closes first when the daemon stops and survives the failure of, in `internal/daemon/README.md` (the fact) and `internal/daemon/CLAUDE.md` (the rule). Every command, flag, file name, limit and message below is the spec's, the names contract's or Story 5's; the rules name a test only where another story's draft pins it (Story 11's `TestEncryptWith_RFC8291Example`, Story 9's `TestPreactHTM_IsThePinnedFile`, `TestStatic_IndexHasNoInlineScriptOrStyle` and `TestStatic_ModulesSetNoStyleAttribute`; the lint fails a test name that does not exist, so a rename in those stories must be followed here), and say **Convention**, with the silent wrong result, everywhere else. The lint (`tools/claudemd`) checks structure only: a package `CLAUDE.md` of at most 100 lines and 20000 bytes, each rule under a `## Rules…` heading marked **Enforced** or **Convention**, no line over 1200 bytes, no phrase that sends the reader elsewhere instead of stating the rule, every backticked path and test name resolving, and every guide listed in the index and linked from a rule. The story has six tasks: the rules and the guide; the layer's README; the architecture; the usage; the core README; the daemon's README and rule.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing check**

Create `docs/claude/web-layer.md`:

````markdown
# Changing the web layer

`internal/web` is a `core.Core` client inside the daemon that serves a phone app, a JSON API, a server-sent stream and Web Push behind device pairing. Read this before adding a route, a stream event or a push kind, before changing the JSON the app reads, and before trying a change on a phone. The package's rules are in `internal/web/CLAUDE.md`; this is the procedure and the traps.

## Adding an API route

1. Register it in `routeAPI` (`internal/web/api.go`) on the mux the registrar gets, wrapped in `l.authed(…)`: only `POST /api/v1/pair` is open. A handler takes the device name `authed` hands it and logs the device, action, session and result (`subsystem=web`).
2. Make the request through the layer's `core.Core` client and wait with `l.reqs.next()` and `l.reqs.wait(ctx, req, device)`. Answer 200 with `{"notices": [...]}`; a `Reply` that refuses is 409 with the model's text, a stopping daemon (`TakeRefused`) 503, a request still running after `replyWait` 202 `{"pending": true}`. Never copy the model's preconditions into the handler: `admit` decides, and the app's buttons only follow the session's `status`.
3. Check what the request carries as the spec's Limits say (a body of at most 1 MiB, ids decimal and served by the model, a reply of 1 byte to 64 KiB, a launch override from `config.ClaudeModelAliases()`, `config.ClaudePermissionModes` and `config.ClaudeEfforts`). An unknown session is 404, an unknown workspace 400.
4. Add the call to the app (`internal/web/static/js/`), through `act(method, path, body)` so its notices and errors become toasts. A request that can lose work (kill, push, merge) gets a confirmation sheet (`ConfirmSheet`, `internal/web/static/js/sheets.mjs`) that names exactly what happens.
5. Add the route to the API table of `internal/web/README.md`, and to `e2e/web_test.go` when it is a lifecycle action.

## Adding a stream event

1. Publish it from a sink (`apply(evs []core.Event)`) through `l.hub.publish(event, data)` or `l.hub.sendTo(device, event, data)`. A sink never blocks and does no network I/O: the drain hands it every `Sync`'s events on one goroutine, and a stalled sink stalls every stream.
2. If a reconnecting phone needs the state, put it in the `snapshot` too: every connect starts with one and nothing is replayed.
3. Add the event's handler where `internal/web/static/js/app.mjs` calls `connect(handlers)` (`internal/web/static/js/api.mjs`), and keep in mind that `internal/web/static/js/store.mjs` saves the last snapshot for the next start.
4. Document the event in the stream section of `internal/web/README.md`.

## Adding a push kind

1. Work the trigger out in `internal/web/triggers.go` from the projection's transitions, one push per transition. The first projection after the layer starts is a baseline and pushes nothing, and workspace terminals never push.
2. Add the kind to the payload (`kind`, `session`, `title`, `body`, `badge`, `url`), to the device's notification settings (`core.NotifySettings`, `PUT /api/v1/device`) and to the settings screen.
3. Choose `Urgency` and a per-session `Topic` so an offline phone gets only the latest.
4. Show it in `internal/web/static/sw.js`: the notification is tagged by session, because iOS requires every push to show something and loom cannot clear a stale one.

## Changing the JSON the app reads

The API is `/api/v1` and an installed app may be older than the daemon for a while. Add fields; never rename, retype or drop one in place. The service worker updates the phone when the build ID differs from the snapshot's `build`, so a daemon upgrade upgrades the phone, but only after it has loaded the old shell once.

## Changing the app

- Modules are `.mjs` (Node 22's `node --check` catches syntax errors in those and misses them in `.js`): `for f in internal/web/static/js/*.mjs; do node --check "$f"; done`. CI has no Node, so no Go test depends on it.
- No inline script and no `style` attribute: the Content-Security-Policy is `default-src 'self'`, and a blocked handler fails silently on the phone.
- The Preact + htm module is one pinned file, `internal/web/static/lib/preact-htm.mjs`: replace it and its pinned SHA-256 together, with the version and source URL in its header comment and the licence in `NOTICE.md`. It sits in `lib/`, not `vendor/`, because Go's module zips drop files below a nested `vendor/` and a `go install` build would embed nothing.
- `sw.js` and `index.html` carry placeholders (`__LOOM_BUILD__`, `__LOOM_SHELL__`) that `routeStatic` fills when serving; the precache list therefore follows the embedded files, and the worker caches the shell only, never `/api/`.

## Trying a change

Go tests cover the layer over the production path (a booted model, the real `rpc.Server`, a client over `net.Pipe`, `httptest`); the JavaScript has no harness, so its logic stays in Go. For the phone, run a dev build through the `loom-dev` skill (`go run ./tools/loomdev`), never `./loom` or `loom serve stop` in a loom pane, and work through the plan's checklist: install, pair, turn on notifications, receive both push kinds with the app open and closed, Open in Claude, reply, kill with its confirmation, revoke.

## Traps

- **iOS keeps a Home Screen app's storage apart from Safari's, and sends Web Push only to Home Screen apps (iOS 16.4 and later).** A pairing done in Safari does not carry over to the installed app.
- **Every request is checked against the configured origin.** The `Host` must equal the origin's host and port (421 otherwise), and a request other than GET or HEAD must carry an `Origin` equal to it (403). A test that builds its own request sets both.
- **Pairing codes live in the model's memory.** A daemon restart voids them, five wrong codes void every pending one, and a code is bound to the device name it was made for.
- **A `web.json` that fails to load is never overwritten.** Unreadable, a newer version, or a stored `listen`, `origin`, device name, token hash or VAPID key that fails validation all count. The web stays off, `ConfigureWeb` and `RevokeDevice` refuse, `PairDevice` answers that the web is off, and a notice says why; fix or move the file by hand.
- **A push service answers 201 to any ciphertext it accepts and cannot read it.** A wrong encryption shows only as a phone that stays quiet, which is why `internal/web/webpush` is checked against RFC 8291's own example.
- **Cloudflare terminates TLS, so it can read the traffic and the device token.** Revocable tokens limit the damage; never log a token, cookie, pairing code or push subscription.
- **The layer holds no session state.** A copy of a view that outlives its replica goes stale: derive from the client's replica and the model's device records.
````

Append to the end of `internal/web/CLAUDE.md`, directly after its last line (Story 6's last rule bullet; no blank line and no heading, so these continue the list under Story 6's `## Rules when modifying this package`; Story 6's text above them stays as it is). Story 6's rules already say that `authed` wraps every route but the pairing one and that a sink never blocks, so this list repeats neither:

```markdown
- **Add a route, a stream event or a push kind by the checklist in [`docs/claude/web-layer.md`](../../docs/claude/web-layer.md).** A piece missed (the app's call, the snapshot's field, the README's table) fails nothing on the server and shows only on the phone. **Convention** — a route the app never calls, an event it ignores, a push the worker does not show.
- **Answer a refused action 409 with the model's own text and a stopping daemon 503, and never copy `admit`'s or `precondition`'s checks here.** A copy drifts, and the phone then offers what the TUI would refuse or withholds what it allows; the app's buttons only follow the session's `status`. **Convention** — a button that does nothing, or an action missing.
- **Put a request's notices in its response, send a later one to the requesting device's stream, and have the app always show Kill's and Resume's stash notices.** They name a SHA that may be the only record of the work, so a notice dropped loses it silently; the daemon also hands a notice tied to the web client's request to its other clients, or keeps it for the next TUI, since the phone may never see it. **Convention** — work that cannot be found again.
- **Start every stream with a `snapshot`, replay nothing, ping every `pingEvery` (20 s), give the stream no write timeout, and hold at most `maxStreamsPerDevice` per device and `maxStreams` in all.** Cloudflare closes a stream idle for 100 s; a replay shows the phone a state that no longer holds; no cap lets one phone or one bug take every stream. **Convention** — a phone that looks live and is stale, or a stream that dies every 100 s.
- **Treat the first projection after the layer starts as a baseline that pushes nothing, push each transition once, and never push for a workspace terminal.** A daemon restart would otherwise send one push per waiting session, and a terminal's status is not a user's turn. **Convention** — a flood of notifications after every restart.
- **Post a push only to a subscription `core.ValidatePushSubscription` passed, from the sender's own goroutine, with a 10 s timeout, at most 30 pushes a minute per device and a queue of at most 100.** The daemon connects to whatever host a subscription names, so an unvalidated one makes it a proxy into the user's network; a send on the loop or in a sink stalls the model. **Convention** — a request from the daemon to a host the user never meant.
- **Build the payload encryption from the standard library only, and change `internal/web/webpush` only with RFC 8291's own example vector still passing.** A push service answers 201 to ciphertext it cannot read, so a wrong one shows only as a phone that stays quiet. **Enforced** for the encryption by `TestEncryptWith_RFC8291Example`; **Convention** for the standard-library-only part — a dependency that drifts from the RFC without a failing test.
- **Serve the app's shell public and free of data, with no inline script and no `style` attribute, and let the service worker cache the shell only, never `/api/`.** The Content-Security-Policy is `default-src 'self'`, which drops an inline handler without a word, and a cached response shows a stale fleet as live. **Enforced** for the shell and the modules by `TestStatic_IndexHasNoInlineScriptOrStyle` and `TestStatic_ModulesSetNoStyleAttribute`; **Convention** for the worker's cache — an old fleet shown as current.
- **Write the app's modules as `.mjs`, and keep the Preact file at `internal/web/static/lib/preact-htm.mjs`, never under a `vendor/` dir, with its SHA-256 pin changed in the same commit.** `node --check` misses syntax errors in a `.js` module, and Go's module zips drop files below a nested `vendor/`, so a `go install` build would embed no such file and the app would not load. **Enforced** for the pin by `TestPreactHTM_IsThePinnedFile`; **Convention** for the extension and the location — a broken module that passes every Go test, or an app missing from an installed build.
- **Change the JSON the app reads only by adding fields, and never rename or retype one in `/api/v1`.** An installed app may run against a newer daemon until its service worker updates; an older app reading a renamed field shows nothing and fails no request. **Convention** — a screen that renders empty after a daemon upgrade.
- **Log every action under `subsystem=web` with its device, action, session and result, and never log a token, a cookie, a pairing code or a push subscription.** A log line is read by other users and pasted into bug reports, and these are credentials. **Convention** — a credential in a file that outlives the pairing.
```

```markdown
## The files of the later stories

| File | Holds |
|---|---|
| `internal/web/fleet.go` | the projection: `sessionJSON`, `workspaceJSON`, `deviceJSON`, `snapshotJSON`, `noticeJSON`, `groupOf`, `deviceOf`, `noticeOf` |
| `internal/web/stream.go` | the hub (`publish`, `sendTo`, `closeDevice`), the `/api/v1/stream` handler, recent notices |
| `internal/web/api.go`, `internal/web/reqs.go` | `routeAPI` and the action handlers; `reqs`, which makes the layer's request IDs and waits for each `Reply` |
| `internal/web/static.go` | `routeStatic`: the embedded app, with the build ID and the shell's file list written into `sw.js` and `index.html` as they are served |
| `internal/web/push.go`, `internal/web/triggers.go` | the sender goroutine and `routePush` (`GET /api/v1/push-key`); the waiting and finished triggers |
| `internal/web/webpush/` | VAPID (ES256), RFC 8291 encryption (aes128gcm, one record) and the request to a push service; standard library only |
| `internal/web/static/` | the app: `index.html`, `app.css`, `sw.js`, `manifest.webmanifest`, `icons/`, `lib/preact-htm.mjs` and the ES modules in `js/` |
| `e2e/web_test.go` | a sandbox daemon with the web enabled: pair, stream, create, pause, resume and kill over HTTP |

## Routes and the API

Every route under `/api/v1` except `POST /api/v1/pair` needs the device cookie. The app shell and its static files are public and hold no data.

| Route | Does |
|---|---|
| `POST /api/v1/pair` | `{code}`: sets the cookie, answers `{"device": "<name>"}` |
| `GET /api/v1/stream` | the live stream |
| `POST /api/v1/sessions` | create from `{workspace, title, prompt}` and the optional `account`, `model`, `permission_mode`, `effort`; 201 `{"id", "notices"}` |
| `GET /api/v1/launch-defaults?workspace=<id>` | `{account, accounts, model, models, permission_mode, permission_modes, effort, efforts}`: what the new-session sheet pre-fills (`account` is the default account, or `""`) and the choices it offers, which are the registry's account names, `config.ClaudeModelAliases()`, `config.ClaudePermissionModes` and `config.ClaudeEfforts`, so the app hard-codes none |
| `POST /api/v1/sessions/{id}/send` | `{text}`: `Send` with `core.OriginWeb` |
| `POST /api/v1/sessions/{id}/pause`, `resume`, `kill`, `push` | the request of the same name |
| `POST /api/v1/sessions/{id}/merge` | `{source}`: `Merge` with the session in the URL as the target |
| `PUT /api/v1/device` | `{waiting, finished, pause}`: notification settings, each field optional and a missing one left as it is; `pause` is `"1h"` (until an hour from now) or `"none"` (ends a pause), any other value is 400; answers the device |
| `POST /api/v1/device/push` | the browser's `PushSubscription.toJSON()`; answers the device |
| `GET /api/v1/push-key` | `{"key"}`: the VAPID public key the app subscribes with |
| `DELETE /api/v1/device` | unpairs this phone; 204 |

Responses are JSON. An error is `{"error": "<text>"}` with 400, 401, 404, 409, 429 or 503. A refusal by the model is 409 with its text; a stopping daemon is 503. An action answers `{"notices": [...]}`; a request still running after `replyWait` (30 s) answers 202 `{"pending": true}` and its outcome arrives on the stream. A handler takes the layer's next request ID, makes the request and waits for the `Reply` the drain routes back. Create checks the workspace and the title as `loom session new` does and resolves the launch options as it does.

A session as JSON: `id`, `workspace`, `title`, `branch`, `group`, `label`, `status`, `since`, `wait` (`reason`, `detail`), `last_message`, `diff` (`added`, `removed`), `ahead`, `behind`, `parity_known`, `pr` (`number`, `state`, `checks`, or null), `account`, `agents` and `remote_url` (`https://claude.ai/code/<RemoteSession>`, or empty). IDs are JSON numbers. A workspace is `id`, `name` and `label`.

Groups: Prompting is `needs_you`; a started Ready is `your_turn`; Running, Loading and Deleting are `working`; Paused, Recoverable and an unstarted Ready are `paused`, each with its own label. Workspace terminals are left out.

## The stream

`GET /api/v1/stream` is server-sent events. Every connect starts with a `snapshot` (the build, the host name, the workspaces, the sessions by workspace, the device and the last `recentNotices` notices), so nothing is replayed. After it: `sessions` (one workspace's whole list when it changes, at most four a second: `publishEvery`), `workspaces`, `device`, `notice`, `ping` every `pingEvery` (20 s, since Cloudflare closes a stream idle for 100 s) and `bye` when the daemon stops. The stream sets the device cookie again when it connects, so a device in use never expires, and a revoked device's streams end as soon as the device list is published. At most `maxStreamsPerDevice` (4) are open per device, a fifth closing that device's oldest, and `maxStreams` (32) in all, a 33rd being refused with 503. The stream has no write timeout.

## The app

`index.html` loads `js/app.mjs`, which routes `#` (the fleet), `#s/<id>` (a session), `#new`, `#settings` and `#pair` and renders each screen from `screens` (`internal/web/static/js/screens.mjs`). `js/api.mjs` makes the calls and reconnects the stream with a backoff from 1 s to 30 s; `js/store.mjs` keeps the last snapshot in IndexedDB, drawn at once and marked stale with every action off until the stream is back, and cleared by a 401 or an unpairing. The service worker (`sw.js`) caches the shell only, never the API, and updates when a snapshot's `build` differs from the shell's. Opened in Safari rather than from the Home Screen, the app shows the Add to Home Screen steps instead of pairing. There is no build step and no JavaScript test harness: the modules are checked with `node --check`, and the logic stays in Go. The Preact + htm file is pinned by a SHA-256 in a test, and its licence is in `NOTICE.md`.

## Push

Turned on by a tap in the app: it subscribes with the VAPID public key and posts the subscription. Triggers come from the projection's transitions: *waiting* when a session enters Prompting or stays in it with a new reason or tool summary, *finished* when a started session goes from Running to Ready. The first projection after the layer starts is a baseline and pushes nothing. A device gets the kinds its settings allow, and nothing while paused. The payload (`kind`, `session`, `title`, `body`, `badge`, `url`) is encrypted per RFC 8291; waiting goes with `Urgency: high`, finished with `normal`, both with a one-hour TTL and a `Topic` per session. The sender is a goroutine of its own with a queue of at most 100: it posts with a 10 s timeout, at most 30 a minute per device, retries a 429 or a 5xx up to three times honouring `Retry-After`, drops a subscription answered 404 or 410 and records it as expired, and records any other failure as failing with the status. `loom web devices` shows the state.

## Limits

Bodies are capped at 1 MiB (`maxBody`). A reply is 1 byte to 64 KiB after trimming, a new session's prompt at most 64 KiB, and its title as `core.CheckNewTitle` says. A launch override must be an account the registry holds, a model in `config.ClaudeModelAliases()`, a permission mode in `config.ClaudePermissionModes` and an effort in `config.ClaudeEfforts`. A push subscription is an `https` endpoint of at most 2 KiB on `web.push.apple.com`, `fcm.googleapis.com`, `updates.push.services.mozilla.com` or a host ending in `.notify.windows.com`. Ids in paths are decimal and served by the model: an unknown session is 404, an unknown workspace 400.

## Tests of the routes, the stream, the app and push

Go tests run over the production path: a booted model, the real `rpc.Server`, a client over `net.Pipe`, and the handler on `httptest`. They pin both sides of each guard (the right and a wrong `Host`, the same and a cross `Origin`, a valid and a revoked cookie), the groups, each push transition once, RFC 8291's example and the VAPID token verified with its public key, and the static files (everything `sw.js` precaches is embedded, the manifest parses). `e2e/web_test.go` runs under `-tags e2e` and needs tmux.
```

4. In "Core flow", insert this paragraph directly before the paragraph that starts `**Health tick.** The model's half (`:

```markdown
**The web layer.** `daemon.Serve` starts `internal/web` after the mod socket (`web.Start`) and closes it first when the daemon stops, before `Server.Bye`. It is a client of the daemon's own server: it dials the `rpc.Server` over `net.Pipe` with `rpc.DialAs(…, rpc.RoleWeb)`, so the hello's optional `role` is `web`, and drains the client as the TUI does (`Wakes`, then `Sync`). Only exactly `web` is the role; any other value is an ordinary client. A connection with that role does not count as a client when the server decides whether a `Notice` reached one: a notice is kept while no other connection exists, and a joining web connection never takes the backlog. A notice tied to the web client's own request goes to that connection and is also handled as if no web connection existed, so it reaches the other connections or waits in the backlog; the phone may never see it, and a kill's or resume's stash notice can be the only record of its SHA. The layer listens on loopback only, on the `listen` address `web.json` holds (none while it is empty), rebinding whenever `WebView.Rev` (which every successful `ConfigureWeb` bumps) differs from the one it last acted on, and reporting through `core.WebHost.WebServing`; a Cloudflare tunnel reaches that port. It serves the embedded phone app, a JSON API under `/api/v1` and a server-sent event stream, all behind the device cookie that pairing sets. It holds no session state: its projection (each workspace's sessions as display-ready JSON) derives from its replica, and the device records, the pairing codes and the VAPID key belong to the model, reached through `core.WebHost` and never on the wire. Pushes go to the push service (Apple's, for an iPhone) from a goroutine of its own, encrypted per RFC 8291 with the standard library. A layer that fails to start, or a panic in its goroutines, costs only the web: the layer stops (with a notice for the next TUI in the panic's case) and the daemon goes on.
```

5. In "The daemon's lifetime and environment", insert this bullet directly before the bullet that starts `- **Frozen environment.**`:

```markdown
- **The web app.** The layer above is opt-in: with no `listen` in `web.json` nothing listens. Its HTTP port answers only while the daemon runs, so a machine that should serve the phone after a reboot runs `loom serve` as a systemd user unit, which must import the session's environment (`PATH`, `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` and the rest), because the daemon passes its environment to every agent it launches ([`USAGE.md`](../USAGE.md) has the unit). A stop says `bye` on every stream first, and a daemon restart ends them: the app reconnects with a backoff from 1 s to 30 s. Pairing codes live in the model's memory, so a restart voids them.
```

2. Insert this section directly before the heading `### Session Recovery (orphaned worktrees)`:

````markdown
### Use Loom from Your Phone

The daemon can serve a web app, an installable page for an iPhone, that
shows your fleet across every workspace, why a session waits and what it
said last. From it you reply to a session, start, pause, resume, kill,
push and merge, and get a notification when a session starts waiting on
you or finishes. It is off until you enable it, listens on loopback only,
and answers only phones you have paired.

- **The fleet.** Sessions are grouped by what needs you: **Needs you**
  (longest wait first, with the reason and, for a permission request, the
  command, file or address), **Your turn** (a dot when the turn ended after this
  phone last opened the session), **Working**, and **Paused** (folded
  away). Chips filter by workspace; the phone remembers the filter. A
  session leads with why it waits, then its last message, its changes,
  branch, pull request and account, the actions, and a reply box.
- **Replying.** The reply goes to Claude through the loom mod, as
  `loom session send` does, queued behind the running turn. It is refused,
  with the reason, for a session with no live mod or another agent than
  Claude.
- **Approvals and the full chat.** Loom answers no permission prompt
  itself. **Open in Claude** links to the session's Remote Control page on
  claude.ai, where you approve and read the whole conversation; it shows
  only while Remote Control is connected for that session, and otherwise
  the card says Remote Control is off for it.
- **Actions.** Kill, push and merge ask first, and the sheet names what
  happens (kill names the worktree, tmux session and branch it removes;
  push commits first; merge asks which session's branch to merge in).
  Pause and resume do not ask. An action the daemon refuses shows the
  daemon's reason. A stash that kill or resume reports is always shown: it
  can be the only record of your work.
- **Notifications.** Turn them on from the app's settings with a tap (iOS
  requires the tap, iOS 16.4 or later, and only for an app installed to the
  Home Screen). Each device chooses *waiting*, *finished* or both, and can
  pause them for an hour. A notification is tagged by session, so a session
  shows at most one; the Home Screen badge counts the sessions that need
  you. Loom cannot clear a notification that went stale: tapping it shows
  the current state. If a push service stops accepting a device, `loom web
  devices` shows `expired` or `failing: <status>` and the app says
  "Notifications stopped; turn them on again".

#### Set it up

1. **Reach the daemon.** The daemon listens on a loopback port, and a
   Cloudflare tunnel run by a `cloudflared` service on the same machine
   carries your host name to it. With a tunnel named `loom` (made once
   with `cloudflared tunnel login`, `cloudflared tunnel create loom` and
   `cloudflared tunnel route dns loom loom.example.com`), the tunnel's
   `config.yml` routes the host name to the port:

   ```yaml
   tunnel: <tunnel id>
   credentials-file: /home/<you>/.cloudflared/<tunnel id>.json
   ingress:
     - hostname: loom.example.com
       service: http://127.0.0.1:7420
     - service: http_status:404
   ```

   Run `cloudflared tunnel run loom` as a service under its own user. Leave
   the Host header as the browser sent it: loom answers 421 to a request
   whose Host is not the origin's host and port.
2. **Turn it on.**

   ```bash
   loom web enable --listen 127.0.0.1:7420 --origin https://loom.example.com
   ```

   `--listen` is a loopback address (`127.0.0.1:<port>` or `[::1]:<port>`),
   not a host name; `--origin` is `https://<host>[:<port>]` with no path
   (for development only, `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` or
   `http://localhost:<port>`). The daemon applies them at once, and the
   command waits a few seconds to say whether it serves or why not (the
   port is taken, say). The settings are kept either way. Running
   `loom web enable` again with the same values while the web serves changes
   nothing and says so; after a bind that failed, it retries the bind: free
   the port, then run it again.
3. **Install the app.** Open the origin in Safari on the iPhone and use
   Share, then Add to Home Screen. Opened in Safari, the app shows these
   steps instead of pairing; the installed app keeps its storage apart from
   Safari's.
4. **Pair.** On the machine, run `loom web pair --name iPhone`; it prints a
   code such as `K7QM-2XPA`, when it expires and the origin. Enter the code
   in the installed app. A code works once and for ten minutes; a second
   `pair` for the same name replaces its code; at most four codes wait at a
   time; five wrong codes void every waiting one (run `loom web pair`
   again); sixteen devices at most. A daemon restart voids the codes.
5. **Notifications.** In the app's settings, tap to turn them on.

`loom web devices` lists each device, when it paired and was last seen, its
notifications and its push state; `loom web revoke <name>` (or `--all`)
unpairs one: its stream ends, its push subscription is dropped and the app
returns to pairing. `loom web disable` turns the web off and keeps the
devices and the origin; `loom web status` shows the settings, whether it
serves and why not, and the device count. The settings live in `web.json`
in the global dir, which only the daemon writes; a `web.json` that fails to
load (unreadable, a newer version than this build, or a stored address,
origin, name or key that is not valid) is left alone: the web stays off,
`enable`, `disable` and `revoke` refuse, `pair` says the web is off, and a notice says
why; fix or move the file by hand.

**What the tunnel means.** Cloudflare ends TLS at its edge and reaches loom
over plain HTTP on loopback, so Cloudflare can read the traffic, the device
token included. A token is revocable per device, and Cloudflare Access can be
put in front of the host name with no change to loom.

#### Keep the daemon up (a systemd user unit)

The app answers only while the daemon runs. To have it after a reboot, run
`loom serve` as a systemd user unit, `~/.config/systemd/user/loom.service`:

```ini
[Unit]
Description=loom daemon

[Service]
Type=simple
ExecStart=%h/.local/bin/loom serve
Restart=on-failure
RestartSec=5
# The login environment, which a user service does not inherit (below).
EnvironmentFile=-%h/.config/loom/serve.env

[Install]
WantedBy=default.target
```

Enable it with `systemctl --user enable --now loom`, and
`loginctl enable-linger $USER` so it starts at boot without a login.

**The unit needs your session's environment.** The daemon passes its own
environment to every agent it launches, and a user service starts with
almost none: `PATH` must reach `claude`, `git`, `tmux` and your tools, and
`SSH_AUTH_SOCK` (and `DISPLAY`, and the rest of what your agents use) must
be set, or every session the daemon starts runs without them. Either import
them from a login shell before the unit starts, with
`systemctl --user import-environment PATH SSH_AUTH_SOCK`, or write them to
the file the unit reads, `~/.config/loom/serve.env`, as `NAME=value` lines
(`PATH=/home/you/.local/bin:/run/current-system/sw/bin`, for example). Either
way, restart the unit after changing them (`systemctl --user restart loom`):
a daemon keeps the environment it started with.

After installing a new loom build, restart the unit too. A `loom` you start by
hand that finds an older daemon replaces it with a detached daemon of its own,
outside the unit.

#### When something is wrong

| You see | Cause |
|---|---|
| The app says "Can't reach loom" and when it last updated; the browser shows 502 | The daemon is not running, or the tunnel cannot reach the port |
| 421 | The Host header is not the origin's host and port: the tunnel changed it, or `--origin` is not the address the phone opens |
| 403 on an action | The request's `Origin` is not the configured origin |
| "loom is restarting" | The daemon stopped; the app reconnects by itself |
| Pairing says the code is wrong, used or expired | Run `loom web pair` again; a code works once, for ten minutes |
| `loom web enable` says it is not serving | `loom web status` shows the reason, usually a port that is taken; the settings are kept. Free the port and run the same `loom web enable` again: it retries the bind |
| The fleet is greyed with every action off | The app shows its last snapshot until the stream is back |
| No notifications | The app is not installed to the Home Screen, notifications are off or paused for the device, or `loom web devices` shows `expired` or `failing` |
````

```markdown
### Web Subcommands

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| `web enable --listen <addr> --origin <url>` | Turn the web app on, or change it: `--listen` is a loopback `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `[::1]:<port>`, `--origin` the `https://<host>` the phone opens. Both are required and checked by the daemon, which applies them at once; the command waits up to 5 s and says whether it serves or why not. Running it again with the same values changes nothing while the web serves, and retries a bind that failed (the port was taken) |
| `web disable` | Turn the web app off; paired devices and the origin are kept |
| `web pair [--name <device>]` | Print a one-time code (`XXXX-XXXX`, default name `iPhone`), when it expires and the origin to enter it at. Refused while the web is off, for a name already paired, and at sixteen devices |
| `web devices [--json]` | Each device: name, when paired, last seen, notifications, push state (`ok`, `expired`, `failing: <status>`, or none) |
| `web revoke <name>` or `web revoke --all` | Unpair a device, or every device: its app returns to pairing and its push subscription is dropped |
| `web status` | The settings, whether the web app serves or why not, and the number of devices |
```

4. Insert this section directly before the heading `## Wire errors`:

```markdown
## The web settings and devices

The web layer (`internal/web`, in the daemon) is a client of the server, but what it must keep lives in the model. `web.json` (global dir, mode 0600, `WebFile`) holds the `listen` and `origin`, the VAPID key pair (made when the web is first enabled) and each device (the SHA-256 of its token, its notification settings, its push subscription and that subscription's state); the model reads it at boot and writes it through `config.AtomicWriteFile`. A file that fails to load is never overwritten: unreadable, a newer version than the build knows, or holding a `listen`, `origin`, device name, token hash or VAPID key that fails its validator (stricter than a version check alone). The model keeps the reason (`loadErr`): the web stays off, `WebView.Error` says why, `ConfigureWeb` and `RevokeDevice` refuse, `PairDevice` answers `ErrWebOff`, and `Boot` raises a notice for the next TUI.

`ConfigureWeb(listen, origin)` validates both (`ValidateWebListen`: a loopback IP literal and a port from 1 to 65535; `ValidateWebOrigin`: `https://<host>[:<port>]`, or `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` / `http://localhost:<port>` for development, at most 255 bytes) and writes; an empty `listen` turns the web off and keeps the origin. Every successful `ConfigureWeb` bumps `WebView.Rev` and clears what the layer last reported (`Serving`, `Addr`, `Error`), and the layer rebinds whenever `Rev` differs from the one it last acted on, so it reports afresh through `WebServing` and no stale error outlives the call; `WebServing` itself does not bump `Rev`, so a failed bind is retried only by the next `ConfigureWeb`. `PairDevice(name)` makes a code of `PairingCodeLen` characters of `PairingAlphabet`, bound to the trimmed name (`ValidateDeviceName`: 1 to 32 characters, no control characters, unique, at most `MaxWebDevices` devices), and keeps it in memory only: it works once for `PairingTTL`, at most `PairingMaxPending` wait at a time, and `PairingMaxMisses` wrong codes void them all. `RevokeDevice(name)` deletes the device and its push subscription. A refusal is one of the sentinels (`ErrWebOff`, `ErrPairingCode`, `ErrPairingLocked`, `ErrDeviceExists`, `ErrTooManyDevices`, `ErrNoDevice`), wrapped with the name when it names a device; over the wire only the message survives (see "Wire errors").

`Web() WebView` is `rpc:local`: the settings, whether the listener serves (`Serving`, `Addr`, or `Error`) and each device without its token hash, published in `ModelView`, so no event of its own exists. `core.WebHost`, implemented by `*Loop` in `loop_web.go` and not in `Core`, is the layer's side: `RedeemPairing` (consumes a code and returns the name and a new token once), `DeviceByToken`, `TouchDevice` (written at most once a minute), `SetNotify` (takes a `NotifyChange`, whose nil fields are left as they are, and returns the device as stored), `SetPushSubscription` (validated by `ValidatePushSubscription`: an `https` endpoint of at most 2 KiB on a push service's host, a 65-byte P-256 point and 16 bytes of auth; returns the device), `PushTargets`, `PushResult`, `VAPIDKey` and `WebServing`, which sets `Serving`, `Addr` and `Error` and raises one `Notice` per distinct error. `OriginWeb` lets `Send` carry a reply from the phone through the mod as the user's own words: it follows `OriginCLI`'s refusals (empty, a program other than Claude, no live mod) and adds no "From" line.
```

- **Start the web layer (`web.Start`) after `modserver.Listen`, close it (`Layer.Close`) first when `Options.Stop` closes, before `Server.Bye`, and keep running when it fails.** The layer is a client of this server over `net.Pipe`: closed after the bye, its streams would end with the connection instead of saying `bye`, so a phone shows a dead connection and not "loom is restarting"; and the daemon owns every session, so a taken web port or a panic in the layer may cost the phone and nothing else. **Convention** — a phone told nothing when the daemon stops, or a daemon that exits, and every TUI with it, because the web port was busy.
